2026-08-22 08:16:44 PDT, fix asymmetric one-sprite scheduler collapse

A new independent C host model copies the enhanced-asymmetric allocator's
15-band P0, 12-band P1, and directional 1..5-band cross-lane conflict rules and
Monte-Carlo tests random X/Y layouts.  It reproduces the user's "only one
sprite" failure with identity priority at X={50,34,100,133,115,97},
Y={16,20,20,18,18,20}.  The greedy allocator blindly seeded the first sprite on
P0; a second sprite 1..5 bands above is then blocked on both lanes and a narrow
pile can cascade to one accepted sprite.

ChooseLane now looks at priority[1] only when placing the first accepted sprite:
for a +1..+5 Y gap the first sprite seeds P1, otherwise it retains P0.  This
small repair guarantees the first two priority candidates a lane for every
legal Y0/Y1 pair without a second allocation pass.  Five million deterministic
random layouts have minimum two accepted sprites and the fairness simulation's
worst observed omission gap is four frames.  A forced-priority Stella witness
also changed from one visible sprite to two.  The focused asymmetric regression
passes and the public 4K image is 3770/4090 ROM, 115/128 RAM.

`vcs_enhanced_multisprite_asymmetric.pl` now compiles and runs the independent
scheduler model for 250,000 deterministic layouts and pins hist[1]==0.  The
remaining scheduler work is distinct: brute-force host comparison still finds
avoidable greedy omissions, and solid three-sprite same-Y clusters require true
horizontal same-line reuse rather than only two-lane reassignment.

2026-08-22 10:38:30 PDT, fix multi-omission scheduler fairness

The asymmetric persistent-priority policy was only fair when a frame had one
omission.  It remembered and promoted the last omitted sprite, so two independent
three-way piles at Y={76,76,76,42,42,42} produced six-frame visibility counts
5,5,2 / 5,5,2.  The renderer now accumulates a six-bit omission mask during
VBLANK and stable-partitions all omitted ids ahead of all shown ids in overscan,
using dead event_order[] as temporary storage.  The same witness becomes exactly
4,4,4 / 4,4,4 without adding VBLANK work.

The host Monte Carlo's deterministic 250k fairness sweep improves from worst
consecutive omission gap 4 to 2 with the single-frame acceptance histogram
unchanged.  `vcs_frame_timing` gained `--require-visible-spread`; the focused
real-CPU regression holds two independent three-way piles for 97 checked frames
and requires all six actual nonzero-GRP visibility counts to differ by at most
one.  Public accounting is 3842/4090 ROM and 115/128 RAM; the focused asymmetric
regression still passes at stable 262-line timing.

Validation checkpoint for this repair: the 250,000-layout host scheduler model,
the focused asymmetric real-CPU regression, source-tree hygiene, context-history
format, example-assembly allowlist, and the four shared multisprite renderer tests
all pass.  The complete compile phase is 500/500.  A full 319-case E2E run did
not finish inside the execution window; its first 78 completed cases passed, with
no fairness-related failure observed.  Do not treat that interrupted run as a
completed full-suite certification.

2026-08-22 16:17:13 PDT, fix persistent top-edge frame overrun

The exact interactive scene X={0,0,62,88,114,140},
Y={95,95,82,74,66,58} reproduces the reported steady 265-line frame in
Stella 7.0 with the old 51/25 extended blanking split.  Persistent fairness
changes the priority order until asymmetric VBLANK scheduling takes about
3391 cycles before its first timer poll, beyond the 3328-cycle deadline of a
51 TIM64T preload.

Keep the total blanking budget at 76 quanta but use 57/19 for extended VBLANK
and overscan.  The same scene is 262 lines / 60.0 Hz in Stella 7.0 and the
existing Y=90 lane-stability witness remains stable.  A new focused regression
pins the scene but deliberately leaves game_priority[] persistent so the old
51/25 split fails after the fairness order evolves.

The focused timing/contract/hygiene checks pass, and the complete E2E suite
finishes 319/319.

    | VCSC PROJECT CONTEXT
    | ====================
    | 
    | Purpose and reading order
    | -------------------------
    | This is the compact durable handoff for continuing VCSC after a chat restart.
    | It records only current state, durable invariants, active problems, and the next
    | work. It is not a chronological diary.
    | 
    | When asked to read `.../context.txt` and continue in a **new chat**:
    | 
    | 1. Read this file once near the start of the chat. This is bootstrap state, not a
    |    per-turn checklist. Do not reread the whole file on later turns unless it has
    |    materially changed and the current task genuinely requires the new contents.
    |    For later verification, prefer a targeted search, section, line range, or tail.
    |    Reinjecting the same handoff text every turn wastes the conversational context
    |    this file is intended to save.
    | 2. Inspect the current tree and verify that it agrees with this record.
    | 3. Follow the user's newest instruction. Otherwise continue the first item under
    |    "Immediate TODO."
    | 4. Read focused internal documents only when relevant:
    | 
    |        .../roadmap.txt                 detailed main-project roadmap
    |        .../bankswitching.txt           bankswitching design and sub-roadmap
    |        .../enhanced.txt                enhanced multisprite side-quest handoff
    |        .../enhanced_asymmetric.txt     asymmetric-PF enhanced WIP handoff
    |        .../ram_optimization.txt         focused RIOT-RAM optimization roadmap
    |        .../instruction.txt             bounded-work and packaging procedure
    |        .../context-history/YYYY-MM-DD.txt
    |                                        historical daily development log
    | 
    | 5. Do not read all of `context-history/` during an ordinary handoff. Search or
    |    open only the day needed to investigate an old decision or regression.
    | 
    | Keeping this file small
    | -----------------------
    | The point of this file is to reduce conversational context, not consume it.
    | Keep it below 50 KiB when practical and never allow it to exceed 100 KiB.
    | 
    | * Keep only facts needed to continue present or likely near-term work.
    | * Update existing current-state text instead of appending another version of it.
    | * Move completed-work narratives into the appropriate daily file under
    |   `.../context-history/`; use one file per local calendar date.
    | * Keep detailed acceptance criteria in `.../roadmap.txt` or the relevant focused
    |   design document. Point to that document instead of duplicating it here.
    | * Do not paste full compiler output, test logs, file inventories, or long lists
    |   of touched paths. Record the command, concise result totals, and only relevant
    |   failures or caveats.
    | * Remove superseded plans and stale measurements once a newer authoritative
    |   value exists. History already preserves the old version.
    | * When this file begins growing through repeated additions, stop and consolidate
    |   it immediately rather than postponing cleanup.
    | 
    | History-entry policy
    | --------------------
    | Every meaningful completed change or decision gets an entry in that day's file:
    | 
    |     .../context-history/YYYY-MM-DD.txt
    | 
    | Create the file when the first entry is written that day. Entries are oldest
    | first. Each entry may contain any number of continuation/detail lines, but its first
    | line is a strictly formatted ASCII header:
    | 
    |     YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS PDT, short description
    | 
    | The description is mandatory and at most 50 characters. The complete header line is
    | at most 72 characters and contains ASCII only. Continuation/detail lines are not
    | subject to those length or ASCII restrictions. Do not leave a timestamp-only header
    | and do not use roadmap-item/task numbers as the entry-title shorthand.
    | 
    | Generate the timestamp with:
    | 
    |     TZ=America/Vancouver date "+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S %Z"
    | 
    | During the project's current summer work this must produce `PDT`; the history-format
    | regression deliberately requires `PDT` in entry headers.
    | 
    | Never put a growing changelog back into this file. Update the current-state
    | sections here and append the historical narrative only to the daily history.
    | 
    | Single-chat source-lineage policy
    | ---------------------------------
    | A VCSC source lineage must have only one active development chat at a time. Parallel
    | chats can each start from the same archive, make valid but different changes, and then
    | return tarballs that silently omit the other chat's work. A later timestamp does not
    | prove that one archive is a descendant of another.
    | 
    | * Treat the exact archive supplied by the user in the current chat as the authoritative
    |   starting tree for that chat. Do not substitute a working tree or tarball from another
    |   chat, even if it appears newer or familiar.
    | * Normal handoff is serial: finish the current chat's work, obtain its returned tarball,
    |   then use **that returned tarball** as the input archive when continuing in a new chat.
    | * Do not continue modifying the same project lineage in two chats at once. If another
    |   chat is still doing VCSC work, finish or abandon one lineage before starting new work
    |   in the other.
    | * If concurrent work happened accidentally, stop feature/bug work and reconcile the
    |   divergent trees first. Diff both descendants against their known common input, identify
    |   every unique change, and explicitly merge or choose changes. Never resolve divergence by
    |   simply picking the archive with the newest filename or timestamp.
    | * Before packaging a result, make sure the working tree descends from the archive supplied
    |   for the current task. Never overwrite newer user work with a stale tree carried over from
    |   another chat.

    | General workflow invariants
    | ---------------------------
    | * The repository archive has no wrapper directory. Top-level entries such as
    |   `COPYING`, `Makefile`, `compiler/`, and `test/` remain at archive root.
    | * Preserve `.../instruction.txt`; it is intentional durable guidance.
    | * `.../bankswitching.txt` is the sole bankswitching design record; do not create a duplicate elsewhere.
    | * Use the top-level `make tar` target for all future VCSC source-tarball
    |   creation and returned source archives. Do not hand-roll source tarballs with
    |   direct `tar` commands; `make tar` owns file selection and the UTC-timestamped
    |   archive name.
    | 
    | * Use the term "renderer," never "kernel," for VCSC display implementations.
    | * Do not change the top-level README unless the task actually requires it.
    | * Do not leave a half-modified tree. Test, update current state and daily
    |   history, clean generated residue, then package.
    | 
    | Project goal
    | ------------
    | VCSC is a brutally pared-down Atari VCS/2600 C-like compiler and companion 6502
    | toolchain. It favors explicit, analyzable, cycle-aware behavior over general C
    | compatibility. Work proceeds in small vertical slices with focused regressions.
    | 
    | Current source lineage
    | ----------------------
    | Initial archive recorded by the project:
    | 
    |     vcsc.20260716_140727.tar.gz
    | 
    | Input archive for the recursive CC0 licensing pass:
    | 
    |     vcsc.20260806_211356.tar.gz
    | 
    | Input archive for the animated-artwork restoration:
    | 
    |     vcsc.20260806_220322.tar.gz
    | 
    | Current public tools are `vcsc`, `vcsc-cc1`, `vcsc-as`, `vcsc-ld`, `vcsc-ar`,
    | and `vcsc-sim`. Source uses `.c26`, maintained assembler source uses `.s26`,
    | relocatable objects use `.o26`, and archives use `.l26`. `vcsc-cc1` assembly
    | output defaults to stdout and `-o -` explicitly means stdout. The top-level
    | `vcsc` driver supports that convention for `-S -o -`; object and linked
    | outputs deliberately require filenames, so `vcsc -c -o -` and linked
    | `vcsc -o -` are rejected instead of creating literal dash-named files. Driver
    | `-v` command traces go to stderr so streamed `-S -o -` assembly stays clean;
    | `-###` remains a stdout-only dry-run command listing.
    | 
    | Core language and ABI invariants
    | --------------------------------
    | * Target CPU/machine: 6507 on the Atari VCS/2600. Multibyte data is always
    |   little-endian.
    | * Stock ordinary integer types are signed and unsigned 8, 16, 24, and 32 bits.
    |   Stock packed-decimal types are unsigned `bcd8_t`, `bcd16_t`, `bcd24_t`, and
    |   `bcd32_t`, storing two decimal digits per byte.
    | * `int`, `char`, and `bool` are ordinary identifiers, not reserved built-in type
    |   names. Programs may typedef them. There is no float or double.
    | * Binary literals may use `.` for zero and `X`/`x` for one after `0b`/`0B`.
    | * `__builtin_ntsc_rgb(r,g,b)` is a compile-time uint8_t expression selecting the
    |   nearest meaningful even NTSC TIA color; exact ties choose the lower TIA byte.
    | * Strings are NUL-terminated `uint8_t` arrays in ROM. Pointer access qualifiers
    |   are enforced: `const` rejects writes and `writeonly` rejects reads and hidden
    |   read-modify-write operations.
    | * Pointers are 16-bit little-endian values. Compatible pointer subtraction
    |   yields an `int16_t` element count. Aggregate and array returns are forbidden.
    | * Functions have ordinary lexical parameters and locals, but storage is static
    |   and non-reentrant. Parameters, automatic locals, expression scratch, and the
    |   return object belong to one linker-overlayable activation record.
    | * Calls are directly named only. Function pointers, indirect calls, variadic
    |   functions, direct recursion, mutual recursion, and callback re-entry are
    |   rejected so the complete call graph remains visible.
    | * Value returns use exact-sized callee-owned zero-page objects named
    |   `function$__return`; source writes them through `$$`. Every epilogue is plain
    |   `RTS`. A, X, and Y are caller-clobbered and do not carry language returns.
    | * Direct-call arguments are evaluated left-to-right into pooled caller scratch,
    |   then copied to callee-owned parameter symbols before `JSR`.
    | * There is no software argument/local stack, heap, `sbrk`, `malloc`, or `free`.
    |   `test/software_stack_inventory.pl` guards against restoration of removed
    |   `_vcsc_sp`, `_vcsc_fp`, `_pushN`, `_popN`, and related helpers.
    | * The linker overlays sibling activations using the complete acyclic call graph.
    |   RIOT RAM and the 6502 hardware return stack share the physical $80-$FF pool.
    |   `callstack=callgraph` reserves two bytes per active JSR node plus declared
    |   hidden assembly use through `.callstackextra` metadata.
    | * The discard pseudo-object `_` supports write-only hardware idioms such as
    |   `WSYNC := _;`; assigning to `_` evaluates and discards the expression. A bare
    |   directly addressed one-byte `foo := _;` lowers to exactly one `STA` regardless
    |   of fixed placement (zeropage, ordinary RAM, activation storage, or absolute
    |   hardware address), so A/X/Y/S/P are unchanged.
    | * Direct ordinary `uint8_t` prefix/postfix `++` and `--` truth tests lower in
    |   place without generic expression scratch. Postfix tests branch on the old
    |   value while still applying the side effect when that old value is zero; this
    |   keeps idioms such as `while (i--) { WSYNC := _; }` within one 6507 scanline.
    | * Source `ref` objects may have distinct read/write addresses. Inline assembly
    |   resolves supported official and recognized unofficial opcode operands by
    |   access type and rejects missing, mismatched, or unknown access semantics.
    | * Compiler-generated builtins and metadata use reserved VCSC namespaces. Do not
    |   revive obsolete N/n65 compatibility spellings or formats.
    | 
    | BCD arithmetic constraints
    | --------------------------
    | Packed BCD addition and subtraction use decimal mode. BCD multiplication,
    | division, remainder, and compound forms are accepted only for a positive
    | constant decimal power `10^n`; multiplication accepts the constant on either
    | side, while division and remainder require it on the right. Lowering uses byte
    | moves or four-bit shifts/masks and does not call general multiply/divide helpers.
    | Representation-changing runtime casts between binary and packed BCD remain
    | unsupported; constants convert numerically.
    | 
    | Renderer and example state
    | --------------------------
    | * Public examples are nested by renderer/display architecture. Build and test
    |   discovery must recurse; never assume one directory below `examples/`.
    | * `examples/01_basic` is ordered as `01_blank_screen`, `02_blank_noasm`,
    |   `03_ode_to_joy`, `04_score`, `05_fingerprint`, and `06_wide_score`. The
    |   source-only blank example keeps VSYNC and its 192-line visible region as
    |   constant nonzero `uint8_t` `for` countdowns such as
    |   `for (uint8_t i := 192; i; i--)`; the compiler keeps those lexical counters
    |   entirely in X when the straight-line body is proven X-safe. VBLANK and
    |   overscan instead demonstrate RIOT timer-owned game-logic budgets: `TIM64T=42`
    |   and `TIM64T=34`. After useful work, each phase performs one `WSYNC` to
    |   normalize CPU phase before polling `TIMINT.7`; this prevents variable-length
    |   timer expires, then uses one deadline-alignment `WSYNC`; overscan uses three
    |   fixed blanked boundaries after expiry for the calibrated Stella frame
    |   boundary. Useful logic can therefore consume blanking time without a
    |   per-scanline WSYNC loop. Bare one-byte
    |   discard stores `foo := _;` are generally eligible because fixed-address
    |   lowering is a register/flag-transparent `STA`; `WSYNC := _;` is only the
    |   hardware instance of that rule. The maintained source-only build is
    |   505 ROM / 18 RAM. Its CPU harness interval is 264 raw lines, calibrated to
    |   Stella's authoritative stable 262-line NTSC display.
    | * `examples/01_basic/03_ode_to_joy/ode_to_joy.c26` is also source-only: it
    |   contains no inline assembly and uses timer-owned VBLANK/overscan budgets.
    |   VBLANK uses the stable `TIM64T=42` deadline form from `02_blank_noasm`.
    |   `music_tick()` runs inside a separately calibrated `TIM64T=35` overscan
    |   budget, followed by one phase-normalizing `WSYNC`, the `TIMINT.7` wait, and
    |   two final alignment `WSYNC` boundaries. This keeps short and long
    |   `music_tick()` paths on the same Stella 262-line frame boundary. Its
    |   behavioral regression compiles the actual public example rather than a stale
    |   golden copy.
    | * Every public leaf example has one editable cartridge source, one Makefile, and
    |   one README. Source/cartridge names are descriptive and independent of local
    |   directory numbering.
    | * Maintained renderer components live under `libraries/vcs/renderers/`. Their
    |   local READMEs and timing artifacts are authoritative for exact raster, RAM,
    |   ROM, stack, color, and integration contracts.
    | * Current maintained families include faithful legacy player colors; 192- and
    |   181-line player-color and all-five variants; official and retained unofficial
    |   181-line variants; `standard_4k_ntsc`; and
    |   `standard_4k_ntsc_playercolors`.
    | * The legacy BASIC renderer source tree is intentional reference material and
    |   must not be pruned merely because it is not part of the current build.
    | * Everything recursively under `libraries/` and, by default, `examples/` is
    |   dedicated to CC0-1.0. The authoritative default legal texts are
    |   `libraries/LICENSE.txt` and `examples/LICENSE.txt`; every source/text file
    |   names its governing license. The sole exception is
    |   `examples/03_player_color_192/02_animated_sprites/`, whose local `LICENSE.txt`
    |   covers the complete subexample under CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 and preserves Quick's
    |   attribution. The compiler, linker, tests, and build/tooling code outside those
    |   trees remain under the top-level GPL.
    | * The animated-sprite example uses 29 attributed four-frame sets and the
    |   source's sole three-frame set from Quick's PICO-8 Free 8x8 Sprites cartridge.
    |   Blank source sprite 16 remains pixel-exact in the table. Set 03 uses a dedicated
    |   modulo-3 phase packed beside the normal modulo-4 phase in the existing frame
    |   byte, and plays 13,14,15 without selecting the blank slot. Preserve its local
    |   license and attribution, four hard pages, exact 960-byte art occupancy, and
    |   480-byte packed source-row color table. Each source row chooses its dominant
    |   nontransparent PICO-8 color with deterministic center/lower-index tie breaks,
    |   then maps it to the nearest NTSC TIA color. The selected frame expands into
    |   mutable eight-byte player color tables so colors bob with the source artwork.
    |   The gallery uses the renderer's mutable P0 delayed-color path, P0 Y=55, and the
    |   color-safe X=16..140 interval; it completes all 15 pairs in 1,875 frames while
    |   retaining controls, the 192-line renderer, and exact 262-line frames.
    | * Shared score fonts live under `libraries/vcs/fonts/`; each family exports the
    |   common `score_font` symbol. Do not duplicate font tables inside examples.
    |   Each of the eight families has decimal, hexadecimal, and printable-ASCII
    |   modules. The separate 8x16 `big_ascii.c26` module covers printable ASCII in
    |   one 1520-byte non-page-contained table and is not an 8-row score-component
    |   drop-in. ASCII covers space (`0x20`) through tilde (`0x7E`), keeps every
    |   glyph bitmap distinct within its family, exactly reuses the family decimal
    |   digits and hexadecimal `A`-`F`, and preserves the source family
    |   blank-row/blank-column cell margins. Font modules use compact three-line
    |   name/coverage/CC0 headers, and `libraries/LICENSE.txt` contains
    |   the local CC0-1.0 text. `logo_font.c26` is special: header changes are
    |   allowed, but its six glyph slices must remain pixel-identical.
    | * The centered and widely spaced six-glyph score displays are separate lifecycle
    |   components. The wide component places 8-pixel glyphs at X=36,52,68,84,100,116
    |   using three medium-spaced copies per player and the compact delayed-player
    |   pipeline: glyph one is staged before each row boundary, glyphs two through six
    |   write at row cycles 0,8,36,39,42, and cycle 45 commits the delayed latch. It
    |   consumes eleven visible scanlines and owns 18 RIOT-RAM bytes,
    |   including a mutable score color. Its public 2K example, reviewed Stella raster
    |   oracle, and interactive official `player_color_181` score-above/score-below
    |   compositions are maintained. The wide compositions use the same Game Select
    |   object cycle, left-joystick selected-object motion, edge-triggered right-joystick
    |   score editing/color changes, reset behavior, sprites, playfield, and per-row
    |   colors as the centered pair. Each measures 2,788 ROM bytes and 53 total RAM
    |   bytes, leaving 75 bytes free.
    | * Beam-critical tables and renderer code may carry object-owned placement,
    |   alignment, page-containment, privacy, and hidden-stack metadata. Do not move
    |   them based on generic packing convenience when their component contract pins
    |   them.
    | 
    | Current cartridge and bankswitching model
    | -----------------------------------------
    | Public C26 topology profiles currently cover 2K, 4K, direct 8K packaging, F8,
    | F6, F4, and their Superchip variants. Profile filenames have no semantics; the
    | actual `cart`, `bank`, and `mem` declarations are authoritative. The reduced
    | `vcs.cfg` retains generic machine/RIOT policy rather than duplicating cartridge
    | topology.
    | 
    | Full-window F8/F6/F4 uses the missing cartridge address lines as a logical bank
    | namespace. VCSC `BANK0` is the startup/home bank and final physical file chunk;
    | logical bank identity, physical/file index, and selector hotspot are distinct.
    | Cross-bank calls use linker-generated, deterministic bridges. Superchip RAM uses
    | direct split aliases and must not trigger bank selection.
    | 
    | Component placement constraints are carried in object metadata through
    | `.segmentregion`, `.segmentalign`, `.segmentprivate`, `.pagecontain`, and
    | `.callstackextra` as applicable. Explicit pins and hard ROM-data ownership are
    | semantic constraints, not heuristic hints. The linker's default
    | `--bank-placement=optimized` mode uses deterministic weighted soft-edge packing
    | plus guarded single-component local moves; `simple` provides stable first-fit
    | capacity placement for comparison and debugging. Neither mode may break a hard
    | component, move a pin, violate replica locality or capacity, or increase
    | weighted hardware-return depth during local optimization.
    | 
    | `--explain-bank-placement` reports component membership, allowed banks,
    | candidate capacity/cut decisions, accepted and rejected local moves, and final
    | byte/cycle/site cut weights plus weighted stack depth. Actual generated bridge
    | entries remain authoritative in the map's `TRAMPOLINES` section.
    | 
    | Main-roadmap Item 42 is complete. Automatic whole-layout CODE/RODATA placement now
    | works across both selector-controlled banks and selector-free direct regions. Explicit
    | region qualifiers remain hard pins; `main` and `_` startup/runtime functions remain in
    | the unique startup/home region, including direct topologies where `$startup` is only a
    | placement marker. Direct cross-region absolute calls/jumps/data references remain plain
    | 16-bit references with no trampoline cost. Candidate placement now dry-runs the actual
    | page/alignment/hole allocator plus fixed DATA-load and startup-table ROM consumers, so
    | raw-byte fits cannot later overflow during final layout. Writable-memory placement was
    | deliberately left explicit: unqualified DATA/BSS/ZEROPAGE stay in the configured RIOT
    | RAM, while mapper-owned cartridge RAM must be explicitly named `cartram` by the
    | programmer across Superchip, FA/RAM Plus, CV/CommaVid, and OMNI profiles. Split
    | RIOT/mapper-RAM startup initialization uses per-object copy/zero records with the
    | correct mapper read/write aliases. Bankswitching Items 32 OMNI, 33 CV/CommaVid, and 34 JANE
    | are complete through 0FA0 Item 37; Item 38 is explicitly deferred by user request, so no further focused mapper item is active.
    | 
    | The consolidated standard-renderer bankswitching diagnostic is under:
    | 
    |     examples/09_bankswitching/02_standard_renderer/
    | 
    | It composes one maintained all-five renderer source with 4K, F8, F6, F4, and
    | F8SC profiles. The renderer and beam-critical data stay in startup bank0; a real
    | overscan/game hook runs in bank1 only while VBLANK is asserted and returns to
    | bank0 before the next frame-critical phase. The last certified implementation
    | uses one 37-cycle cross-bank round trip per frame, produces an exact 20140-cycle
    | frame, and preserves visible raster identity across profiles. Its bank1 overscan
    | hook intentionally changes the initial score from `123456` to `654321` on the
    | first frame and then leaves it there; the resulting static reversed-looking
    | score is a diagnostic behavior, not a renderer regression. Detailed measured
    | bytes and acceptance tests are in `.../bankswitching.txt`, item 28.
    | 
    | Testing policy and last verified baseline
    | -----------------------------------------
    | When removing or changing a feature:
    | 
    | 1. remove obsolete positive tests whose only purpose was the removed feature;
    | 2. retain or add focused negative tests for clean rejection;
    | 3. rewrite still-useful tests to use supported behavior;
    | 4. update documentation and source-tree hygiene checks in the same slice.
    | 
    | The current completely verified baseline is:
    | 
    |     compile-only:             495 passed
    |     end-to-end/generic:       303 passed
    |     staged installed tools:   passed, including all final cartridge builds
    | 
    | The current tree has been verified against all 495 compile-only tests and all
    | 303 end-to-end tests. The end-to-end inventory is executed in ordered capped
    | batches because the container imposes a hard wall-clock limit on one monolithic
    | command; every listed case must run and pass. Exact raster, event-trace, frame-
    | length, bankswitching, installed-profile, assembly-policy, and source-hygiene
    | coverage remain active.
    | 
    | Compact unsigned-byte lowering shortened the compiler-generated tail after a
    | final WSYNC by nineteen cycles. Generic `vcs_ntsc_wait_scanlines()` intentionally
    | keeps that compact, uncalibrated return phase. Beam-sensitive helpers do not rely
    | on it: `vcs_ntsc_wait_component_scanlines()` explicitly preserves the established
    | cycle-3 component entry, and `vcs_ntsc_wait_visible_tail_scanlines()` preserves
    | the established final-visible-tail phase before overscan. Exact historical phase
    | and Stella raster oracles remain authoritative; do not recalibrate them merely
    | because compiler lowering gets shorter.
    | 
    | Important active constraints and open questions
    | -----------------------------------------------
    | * Inline/included assembly stack use is not inferred. Components must declare
    |   known hidden use explicitly; automatic analysis may be considered later.
    | * General cross-statement computed-address reuse remains conservative. One narrow
    |   proven frame-table case now survives across a counted loop: when later page-pointer
    |   offsets are byte-exact doubles of earlier masked/shifted offsets and the loop cannot
    |   modify the pointer or source values, the compiler reuses the low byte with `ASL`.
    | * Runtime binary/BCD representation conversion and general BCD multiply/divide
    |   helpers remain deferred.
    | * Segmented/value-controlled/historical mapper families are not generic variants
    |   of F8/F6/F4. Add a separate model only for a concrete program that requires
    |   one; do not build a universal mapper language speculatively.
    | * Multisprite renderer work remains on the main roadmap and must not be smuggled
    |   into bankswitching placement work.
    | 
    | Enhanced asymmetric top-edge checkpoint (2026-08-22)
    | -----------------------------------------------------
    | The asymmetric 192-line renderer accepts Y=0..95 and prepositions every event
    | whose setup action would begin at/above band 95 during VBLANK.  Y=95 reaches
    | the first visible playfield band; Stella verification covers both lanes with
    | independent X and correct colors.  Last measured public accounting is
    | 3980/4090 ROM and 115/128 RAM.
    | 
    | The current extended NTSC blanking split is 63/13 TIM64T, preserving the same
    | total 76 quanta.  The earlier 51/25 split let the persistent top-left witness
    | X={0,0,62,88,114,140}, Y={95,95,82,74,66,58} settle at 265 lines in Stella;
    | 57/19 fixed that before the later bridge-orientation scheduler work, and 63/13
    | is the current split after budgeting the extra VBLANK scan.
    | 
    | The later 16:41 screenshot exposed a priority-dependent retained-X coloring
    | bug, not legitimate flicker.  For the relevant component, sprites 2 and 4 have
    | the same X and are 12 bands apart, while sprite 3 bridges them seven bands
    | below the upper endpoint/five above the lower.  This component has one safe
    | physical orientation: endpoints 2/4 on P1 and bridge 3 on P0.  The old generic
    | P0 tie-break could omit 4 under identity priority or omit 3 under an inherited
    | priority queue.  `TEMPLATE_bridge_prefers_p1()` now detects this forced
    | orientation whenever both lanes are free.  Focused regressions pin both known
    | bad queues; a one-time real-6502 sweep of all 720 priority permutations rendered
    | all six sprites with stable vertical placement and the calibrated frame length.
    | The earlier 319/319 full-suite result predates this final bridge-orientation
    | change; the user will run the complete suite after this preservation handoff.
    | Detailed state remains in `.../enhanced_asymmetric.txt`.

    | 
    | Source instantiation now uses the hard-cut `instantiate "file.c26" as instance`
    | keyword; the former `template` keyword is intentionally rejected. Directly instantiated
    | source may declare `parameter name;` for a required integer-literal argument or
    | `parameter name := literal;` for an optional/default argument. Callers override with
    | `(name:=literal, ...)` after the instance name. Declared `TEMPLATE_name` identifiers
    | substitute the supplied/default literal while undeclared `TEMPLATE_` names retain
    | ordinary instance-prefix rewriting. Missing required, unknown, duplicate, and
    | non-integer arguments and duplicate parameter declarations are compile-time errors.
    | `instantiate` remains semicolon-free; `parameter` declarations require semicolons.
    | The all-five renderer families prove that parameterized renderer shape in
    | production. `libraries/vcs/renderers/all_five/all_five.c26` and the reviewed
    | stable/common-NMOS twin `libraries/vcs/renderers/all_five_unofficial/all_five_unofficial.c26`
    | both have required `parameter lines;` and support compile-time `lines:=192`,
    | `lines:=181`, and `lines:=170`. The unofficial source must be assembled with
    | `-Wa,--illegals`; each selected profile differs from its official twin only by one
    | same-size/same-cycle zero-page unofficial NOP in dead-flag VBLANK padding. Existing
    | public 192- and 181-line official examples instantiate the unified official source;
    | the maintained unofficial 181 score matrix now instantiates the unified unofficial
    | source with `lines:=181`. `examples/11_all_five_170/` and
    | `examples/12_all_five_170_unofficial/` each compose 11 score + 170 gameplay + 11
    | score = 192 visible lines with the same Select/joystick/Reset controls. Both current
    | dual-score cartridges measure 3507/4090 ROM and exactly 128/128 RAM including the
    | eight-byte hardware-stack reserve, so they fit with zero RAM slack. Profile
    | regressions compare 192/181/170 official/unofficial visible traces and 262-line timing,
    | and staged installcheck compiles all three unofficial line selections. Declared
    | instantiation parameters are also available as integer literals in `#if`/`#elif`,
    | allowing renderer timing profiles to remain compile-time code selection rather than
    | runtime branches.

    | The official player-color family is likewise consolidated into
    | `libraries/vcs/renderers/player_color/player_color.c26` with required `parameter lines;`
    | and compile-time profiles `lines:=192`, `181`, and `170`. The former official
    | `player_color_192/` and `player_color_181/` renderer source directories are removed;
    | existing public groups 03 and 04 select the unified source with 192 and 181 respectively.
    | The 170 profile uses a 40-byte/10-row playfield and the score-composable entry/handoff
    | shape, and `examples/13_player_color_170/` proves 11 score + 170 gameplay + 11 score

    | separate and continues to twin the unified official `lines:=181` profile. Staged
    | installcheck compiles all three unified official line selections.

    | The fixed-address lowering audit is complete. Compile-time-constant offsets into fixed
    | linker symbols use direct operands even beyond offset 255; Y remains reserved for real
    | runtime indexing and pointer dereference. Fixed pointer variables load their own bytes
    | directly before pointee traversal. `ref` pointer slots may be addressed directly, but
    | the referred value stays indirect and directional read/write projection remains intact.
    | Representative savings from this audit include player-color-181 smoke 1432 -> 1422 ROM,
    | all-five-181 smoke 1799 -> 1794, wide-score fixture 926 -> 890, public wide score
    | 1065 -> 969, wide-score-181 compositions 2668 -> 2554, and banked standard-renderer
    | bank0 1848 -> 1827 with ordinary bank1 76 -> 55 and F8SC bank1 115 -> 86. A compile-only
    | regression pins direct automatic-local addressing at byte offset 299 so the removed
    | one-page cutoff cannot silently return.
    | 
    | Main-roadmap item 28 is complete, including the post-integration Stella-visible
    | multisprite timing repair. `libraries/vcs/renderers/multisprite/multisprite.c26` is the
    | single modern source, with required `parameter lines;` and calibrated `lines:=192` /
    | `lines:=181` profiles. The common renderer now owns 67 retained-state bytes plus two public P0 bytes and
    | three bytes of nibble-packed persistent flicker-sort state = **72 module RIOT-RAM
    | bytes**, and declares two bytes of hidden hardware-stack depth. Five immutable P1
    | graphics pointers that formerly consumed ten RIOT-RAM bytes are now represented by
    | fixed ROM low offsets plus the common aligned graphics-page high byte at identical
    | beam timing. The renderer retains
    | stable/common NMOS `LAX` plus the faithful TXS/PHP enable pipeline, so users assemble
    | it with `-Wa,--illegals`. The 192 profile uses logical height 95; the 181 profile uses
    | logical height 89 and a faithful two-line divide-by-15 P0 reposition handoff. The port
    | must preserve the faithful three-cycle mask reads, direct three-cycle HMCLR store,
    | local cycle-balanced reposition decision, and hard `.same` annotations on every
    | beam-critical taken branch. Noncritical setup/sort/overscan branches stay at the
    | normal `.flex` default; one-cycle addressing or branch-page changes can alter the
    | raster.
    | 
    | The repaired P1 raster no longer executes a variable subtract-by-15 horizontal loop
    | inside the visible scanline. VBLANK precomputes a packed HMP1 plus count-minus-one
    | coarse control for each logical P1; draw increments once and runs a bounded countdown,
    | so X=0..159 cannot fall off the scanline near the right edge. Public P1 X is never
    | mutated. Post-completion repairs restore the faithful frame-persistent flicker sort
    | in both profiles: SetupP1 starts from a retained five-byte order and saves its overlap-
    | driven rotation before state[70..74] is reused for packed horizontal controls. This
    | fixes the regression where overlapping sprites permanently hid lower-numbered logical
    | players; six-frame regressions require alternation in 192 and both 181 score orders.
    | The 181 score/P0 handoff used to enter the first/topmost P1 reposition three CPU
    | cycles earlier than every later post-WSYNC reposition. The renderer now spends those
    | three cycles once at raster entry, so the first rank uses the same direct packed X
    | control as every other rank. This removes the wrapped +9 lookup whose alias into the
    | ordinary right-edge bucket made top-ranked X=143..151 jump or wrap. The 181 P0 handoff
    | uses a VBLANK-precomputed wrapped public-X+3 value, and the multiplex loop reloads
    | HMP0=$80 before its late HMOVEs; Stella establishes $80 as neutral at that raster
    | phase, eliminating P0 horizontal dependence on P1 vertical sort order. The P0 Y=0
    | predecrement guard remains and prevents the former broad white stripe.
    | 
    | `examples/14_multisprite/` proves the full-height interactive cartridge and both
    | 181+11 score orders. P0 and all five logical P1 sprites move over X=0..159 and
    | independently over bounded Y ranges: P0/P1 max Y are 95/92 for 192 and 89/86 for 181;
    | the P1 maxima are the highest public coordinates that still reach the first gameplay
    | line. M0/M1/Ball stay off the maintained active raster. The graphics contract is one
    | 145-byte `align(256)` block with bytes 0..95 reserved and P0/P1..P5 at offsets
    | 96/105/113/121/129/137, keeping every legal cycle-critical `(ptr),Y` glyph fetch
    | page-cross free.
    | 
    | The MOS6502/TIA regression exhaustively sweeps 1,521 independent legal X/Y cases for
    | 192 and 1,485 for each 181 score order, all at exact 262-line NTSC timing. It does not
    | infer physical X from RESP1/HMP1; that previous formula was proven capable of declaring
    | a visibly wrong Stella raster correct. Physical placement is independently locked by
    | `test/vcs_multisprite_stella.pl`: actual Stella pixels cover all five P1 rank phases at
    | left/interior/right-edge positions, natural X=159 wrap/clipping, top-edge reach, the
    | 181 top-rank X=143..151 discontinuity (X=148 explicitly locked in both score orders),
    | 181 P0 sort-invariant X, and the P0 Y=0 no-stripe case. The MOS6502/TIA regressions also force logical P1/P2 onto the same vertical band for six
    | consecutive frames in every profile and require the visible winner to alternate every
    | frame; the old identity-reset builds fail this check. The score oracle still locks `123456` above and below gameplay.
    | 
    | A follow-up architectural RAM pass reduces costs outside the renderer without changing
    | its 72-byte private/public contract. Stock startup now selects only `_vcsc_ptr0` and
    | `_vcsc_ptr1`, reducing generic startup workspace from 8 to **4 bytes**; copy/zero records
    | larger than 255 bytes are regression-tested. Direct 1..4-byte constant stores, a
    | scratch-free direct-byte `switch`, and fixed three-line VSYNC lowering reduce the
    | multisprite interactive `main` activation from 4 to **0 bytes** while preserving the
    | scheduler's calibrated post-VSYNC phase. The centered six-glyph display is now one
    | canonical `six_glyph_component.c26`: compact fixed-white mode owns **14 bytes**;
    | `mutable_color:=1` adds one byte for **15 bytes** total; `compact_font:=0` restores
    | six redirectable full pointers plus the historical row byte for arbitrary glyph-page
    | callers. Compact mode uses two one-byte page-contained-font offsets plus four full
    | 16-bit pointers and no row counter while preserving the exact historical 48x8
    | GRP-write raster. The former `six_glyph_color_component.c26` duplicate is removed. Current public sizes are **2613 ROM / 84 RAM** for 192 and **3409 ROM / 99 RAM**
    | for either 181 score composition, leaving **44** and **29** RIOT-RAM bytes free
    | respectively. Relative to the prior 110-byte 181 build, this recovers 11 more bytes:
    | 4 startup + 4 `main` + 3 score. More aggressive score-offset schemes were rejected
    | because exact GRP timing changed even when coarse pixels/frame length looked correct.
    | The same score-specific storage reduction is now propagated to the other six-glyph
    | profiles with their own raster deadlines preserved: left defaults to five full
    | pointers plus one digit-6 offset (one byte saved), right defaults to four full
    | pointers plus two offsets (two bytes saved), and wide uses five full pointers plus
    | one deliberately page-crossing digit-2 offset and no row counter (two bytes saved).
    | Left/right accept optional `compact_font:=0` for applications such as fingerprint
    | that deliberately redirect all six full pointers to another page-contained font.
    | Wide now offers the same compatibility mode: its compact default remains five full
    | pointers plus one offset/no row, while `compact_font:=0` restores six full pointers
    | plus the historical row byte. Ordinary score instances keep the compact defaults.
    | The bankswitching diagnostic no longer uses the normal wide component for its result.
    | Its stable result frame composes `six_glyph_big_wide_component.c26` above
    | `six_glyph_component.c26`: green success shows lowercase `pass` and red failure shows
    | uppercase `FAIL` using exact 16-row glyphs copied from `fonts/big_ascii.c26`; the line
    | below identifies `F8`, `F6`, `F4`, `F8SC`, `F6SC`, or `F4SC` with exact 8-row glyphs
    | from `fonts/default_ascii.c26`, while the poisoned image deliberately shows `??????`.
    | Both compact diagnostic subset tables are page-contained and both components own full
    | six-pointer workspaces. The Stella grader locks both exact text lines and their glyph
    | masks, not merely color or a loose white-pixel area threshold.
    | The Big font family now has exact 16-row decimal and hexadecimal subsets of
    | `fonts/big_ascii.c26`: `big_decimal.c26` is 160 bytes (0-9) and
    | `big_hex.c26` is exactly 256 bytes (0-9,A-F), both `align(256)` and both
    | preserving the Big source glyphs byte-for-byte. `six_glyph_big_wide_component.c26`
    | extends the proven wide six-glyph geometry to 16-row glyphs at X=36,52,68,84,100,116
    | with a **19-visible-scanline** component contract. Public example
    | `examples/01_basic/07_big_wide_score` uses `big_decimal.c26`, budgets 87+19+86=192
    | visible lines, and builds as a 2K cartridge at **1116 ROM / 35 RAM**. The dedicated
    | font-family regression proves decimal/hex are exact Big-ASCII subsets and page-contained;
    | `vcs_six_glyph_big_wide` locks all six writes for the complete **88x16** score raster
    | and exact 262-line frames. Existing 8-row score components are unchanged. The silicon
    | fingerprint example now uses `six_glyph_big_wide_component.c26` with `big_hex.c26` for
    | its central six-digit hexadecimal fingerprint. Its upper-right/lower-left VCSC logos
    | remain the 11-line right/left components; visible placement is 11 + 76 + 19 + 75 + 11
    | scanlines, putting the Big fingerprint on raw lines 127..145 while preserving exact
    | 262-line NTSC timing.

    | Main-roadmap items 25, 26, 29, 30, 31, 32, and 33 and the completed
    | bankswitching sub-roadmap are closed. PAL and SECAM were implemented together via
    | `.../video_standard_roadmap.txt`: one measured 312-line 50 Hz scheduler core (3
    | VSYNC + 45 VBLANK + 228 visible + 36 overscan), distinct PAL/SECAM frame/color/sound
    | front ends, Stella-compatible PAL RGB matching, canonical eight-color SECAM matching,
    | portable-renderer classification, and public blank plus interactive all-five examples.
    | `examples/17_video_standards/` now has exactly two standard subtrees, `pal/` and
    | `secam/`; each is locally numbered `00_blank`, `01_all_five`. Those examples use
    | `__builtin_pal_rgb()` / `__builtin_secam_rgb()` directly so their RGB intent is
    | visible in source rather than hidden behind palette aliases. Both `00_blank`
    | examples target the usual NTSC dark-blue background RGB intent `#12139d`; PAL
    | maps it to TIA `$c0`, while SECAM maps it to canonical blue `$02`. The PAL/SECAM
    | `01_all_five` sources now feed the exact NTSC all-five RGB intents through their
    | standard-specific matchers; PAL selects `$d2/$2a/$9a/$8a` for background/PF/P0/P1,
    | while SECAM necessarily collapses those intents to blue/yellow/cyan/magenta.
    | Stella cannot reliably auto-detect SECAM50 from 50 Hz timing, so all four example
    | `make play` targets explicitly pass `-format PAL` or `-format SECAM`; Stella `-tv`
    | is only the emulated Color/B&W console switch. Legacy full-frame renderers remain
    | explicitly NTSC-only. Current validation is 499/499 compile tests
    | and 305/305 E2E tests; the Stella 7.0 50 Hz all-five phase/snapshot regression and
    | staged installed-tree builds for all four video-standard examples also pass. Item 36 is the first unfinished
    | main-roadmap item. Item 31's focused implementation record is `.../inline_roadmap.txt`, whose
    | subsections 0 through 7 are complete. Normal compilation automatically performs
    | safe single-callsite ref-address and readonly by-value specialization, including
    | fixed-point constant-bound control-flow pruning and effective-callsite recomputation.
    | Whole-program ordinary-function inlining/dead pruning is intentionally opt-in via
    | `-finline-profit` because real speculative final links impose substantial build-time
    | cost; `-finline-profit -v` reports measured accept/reject decisions. Legal inlining
    | preserves activation-overlay lifetimes, obeys page/region and `.same`/`.cross`
    | placement constraints, retains exported/contract/source-inline/assembly identities,
    | and accepts only measured ROM wins (or equal-ROM hardware-stack wins) without an
    | activation-RAM regression. Representative maintained examples are unchanged except
    | the F8SC standard-renderer composition, which saves 25 linked ROM-bank bytes and 2
    | peak RIOT RAM bytes with unchanged stack/raster behavior; focused fixtures prove
    | -2 RAM bytes for an eliminated ref-address formal, -1 byte for a safe readonly byte
    | formal, an exact 8-ROM-byte profitable nested inline, and rejection of an 11-byte
    | ROM loss. Final item-31 validation passes 482/482 compile tests, all 283 E2E
    | behaviors, and the complete staged installcheck recipe in bounded shards.
    | 
    | Windows host-tool packaging is now supported from the top-level Makefile. `make windows`
    | bootstraps native `vcsc-as`/`vcsc-ar` for construction of the target-neutral runtime,
    | then recursively invokes the ordinary `tools` target with an x86-64 MinGW-w64 C/C++
    | toolchain, `.exe` suffixes, and static linker flags. It stages the normal installed
    | runtime/include/share tree together with editable examples and a `vcsc.cmd` launcher,
    | checks the PE imports for MinGW runtime DLL dependencies, and writes a relocatable
    | `vcsc.windows.YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS.zip`. Component Makefiles support `EXEEXT`/`LDFLAGS`,
    | and the driver/compiler/assembler/linker sources have the required Win32 path, process,
    | temporary-file, and case-insensitive-string portability branches. See `WINDOWS.md`.
    | The Windows package path has since been exercised by the user on Void Linux: the
    | MinGW-w64 build completes and the packaged `vcsc.exe -V` runs under Wine and locates
    | all five sibling `.exe` tools successfully.
    | 
    | Native Linux host-tool packaging is also supported from the top-level Makefile.
    | `make linux` recursively invokes the ordinary `tools` target with native C/C++
    | compilers and `-static`, stages the normal installed runtime/include/share tree plus
    | editable examples, rewrites packaged example Makefiles to use `bin/vcsc` and
    | `share/vcs`, strips all six host tools, rejects ELF executables that retain an
    | interpreter or `NEEDED` shared-library entry, smoke-runs the staged driver and
    | packaged blank-screen example, and writes the relocatable artifact
    | `vcsc.linux.YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS.tar.gz`. See `LINUX.md`.
    | 
    | The ordinary installed distribution now includes the editable `examples/` tree at
    | `$(PREFIX)/examples` (default `/opt/vcsc/examples`). `make install` stages the examples
    | with Makefiles rewritten to use installed `bin/vcsc` and `share/vcs`; `make package`
    | contains the same `/opt/vcsc/examples` tree, and `make uninstall` removes it.
    | 
    | GitHub Actions binary-package automation lives at `.github/workflows/build-packages.yml`.
    | Pushes to `main`/`master`, tag pushes, and manual runs execute `make clean`,
    | `make windows`, `make clean`, then `make linux`. Every successful run uploads the
    | Windows zip and Linux tarball as Actions artifacts. Ordinary branch/manual builds
    | retain the timestamped package filenames produced by the Makefile. Tag builds rename
    | the files to `vcsc.windows.<TAG>.zip` and `vcsc.linux.<TAG>.tar.gz`, use the tag in
    | the Actions artifact display names, then create or reuse the matching GitHub Release
    | and attach those exact tag-named packages as release assets.
    | 
    | Version-header generation is centralized in top-level `gen_version_h.pl`. All seven
    | host-tool/helper Makefiles that include `version.h` invoke that script rather than
    | carrying duplicated shell/Git recipes. In a Git worktree the script uses the commit
    | UTC timestamp and 12-digit hash, prefers an exact tag pointing at HEAD over the branch
    | name, falls back to `detached` for an untagged detached HEAD, and preserves tracked-tree
    | `clean`/`modified` state; source archives without Git metadata retain the `nogit
    | user@host clean` form. The binary-package GitHub Actions checkout uses `fetch-depth: 0`
    | so tag refs are available during tagged package builds. `test/version_header_generation.pl`
    | locks branch, annotated-tag, dirty, detached, no-Git, Makefile-delegation, and workflow
    | behavior.
    | 
    | All seven installed host-tool Makefiles generate compiler header dependencies with
    | a separate `DEPFLAGS = -MMD -MP`, compile through `.o` files, include the resulting
    | `.d` files with `-include`, and remove both products on `make clean`. Their tool-local
    | `.gitignore` files ignore `*.o` and `*.d`; source-tree hygiene locks this convention.
    | 
    | The six public command-line tools use a common unsupported-option contract: reject the
    | option with nonzero status, name it on stderr, and print `Try '<invoked-tool> --help'
    | for a list of supported options.` rather than dumping the entire help text. `vcsc-ar`
    | now supports explicit `-h`/`--help`; `test/tool_unsupported_option_help.pl` locks this
    | behavior across driver, compiler, assembler, linker, archiver, and simulator.

    | The completed item-23 `all_five_player_color_192` profile now has a public
    | interactive cartridge at `examples/15_all_five_player_color_192/01_interactive`.
    | Game Select cycles P0/P1/M0/M1/Ball and the left joystick moves the selected
    | object while P0/P1 retain independent eight-row color tables. The example links
    | at 97/128 RAM bytes and 3481/4090 ROM bytes; the renderer itself remains the
    | 83-byte component certified by the item-23 regressions.
    | A score-composable official `all_five_player_color_181` sibling now retains the
    | same five objects and per-row player colors in 181 gameplay lines, composing with
    | one independent 11-line score above or below. Its component RAM contract is 88
    | bytes. Public score examples live under
    | `examples/16_all_five_player_color_181/`; the static score orders use 3754/4090
    | ROM and 114/128 total RAM, while score-above and score-below interactive
    | siblings use 4058/4090 ROM and 123/128 RAM. Game Select cycles P0/P1/M0/M1/Ball
    | and the left joystick moves the selected object in both axes; the score remains
    | fixed at 123456. The visible P0/P1 handoff now precomputes coarse/fine motion in
    | VBLANK and advances RESP by one CPU cycle for residues 13, 14, and 0, removing
    | the inherited four-coordinate horizontal plateau every 15 pixels. The terminal
    | blank-tail entry clears ENABL and immediately writes GRP1, preventing a delayed
    | Ball from reappearing on the final renderer line when positioned on the left. The
    | 181 color examples now reuse the canonical full-width `all_five_181` playfield
    | rows. That richer oracle exposed and fixed a one-cycle optimized boundary PF2
    | handoff; pair 1 now pipelines next-row M1 so pair 6 can prefetch PF2, restoring
    | the cached reflected-half phase. The terminal reflected pair is traced at the
    | canonical PF2@40 / PF1@47 phases.
    | Pairs 2-3 share one exact 152-cycle body;
    | pairs 4-7 remain specialized so their late-row dead cycles can prefetch the
    | opposite seven-byte cache bank. During the visible loop `S` temporarily carries
    | the row base while X is the pair counter; the caller stack pointer is saved and
    | restored and no stack access occurs while it is borrowed. Exact 262-line timing
    | survives a full-byte Y sweep, and Stella full-raster comparisons still match
    | `all_five (lines:=181)` pixel-for-pixel in both score orders.
    | The three-plus-three score has interactive 181-line public compositions under
    | `examples/06_all_five_181/11_three_plus_three_score_above` and
    | `12_three_plus_three_score_below`. They use the official `all_five(lines:=181)`
    | profile, retain five-object movement, and edit all six visible digits with the
    | edge-triggered right joystick: each press acts immediately once, held input is
    | ignored, and neutral re-arms the next press. Both use 111/128 RAM; score-above is 3367/4090 ROM and
    | score-below is 3347/4090 ROM. The temporary static-only 3+3 examples under the
    | 88-byte `all_five_player_color_181` profile were removed.

    | Main-roadmap item 35 is complete as a combined two-paddle/Paddleball workstream.
    | `libraries/vcs/two_paddles.c26` now exposes two raw analog positions and both
    | fire buttons for either controller port, explicitly owns the RC dump/charge
    | lifecycle without violating `frame_ntsc.c26` callback ownership, permits
    | measurements to span frames, and keeps its post-overscan dump path cycle-balanced.
    | `examples/01_basic/09_paddleball/paddleball.c26` is a self-contained cartridge source that
    | composes that input API with the 11-line `three_plus_three_score_component.c26`;
    | its Paddleball-specific beam-critical renderer is kept in the same source rather than
    | polluting `examples/common/`. The raster uses thin M0/M1 paddles and a thin Ball,
    | blue on the physical left and red at the exactly mirrored physical right X,
    | with the maintained-renderer calibrated divide-by-fifteen RESP/HMxx sequence.
    | The score has a black gap above the four-line top wall; the 8-on/8-off center
    | divider has the same one-scanline black gap to the top and bottom walls; PF state
    | is cleared across the score handoff. Ball software X limits stop at the stable
    | visible positioning range instead of allowing a left/right RESP wrap. Paddle Y
    | handling is now symmetric at the renderer rather than compensated per channel:
    | both M0/M1 enable transitions occur in the same raster phase, a small low-end raw
    | input dead zone absorbs normal controller endpoint tolerance, and both channels map
    | to odd rendered top Y=9..159. The corresponding start/end pairs stay inside the
    | visible loop, so a bottom-edge paddle always disables after exactly 16 scanlines
    | instead of leaking through the bottom wall. The game applies each completed raw
    | paddle measurement directly; it deliberately does not add a display slew on top of
    | the controller's unavoidable RC measurement latency. Ball top travel starts at
    | drawable Y=8. The emulator oracle swaps the long/short RC endpoints between channels
    | so both paddles must reach both extremes, checks common M0/M1 raster phase and exact
    | 16-line bottom spans, rejects queued display motion once a completed endpoint is
    | visible, plus fire-to-serve, a deterministic miss/score, Reset, stable Ball ranges,
    | and stable NTSC frame length. A later timing audit found that RC-completion paths
    | are substantially longer than unfinished samples: sampling both channels on one busy
    | scanline could add a displayed scanline at some paddle positions. Paddleball now samples
    | channel 0 on line A and channel 1 on line B, then advances the pair clock only after
    | the line-B WSYNC. Midrange timing probes 200/200 and 200/160 lock the previously
    | failing cases at the scheduler's calibrated 264 raw intervals / Stella 262 displayed
    | scanlines. Paddleball now also uses TIA audio channel 0 for short collision effects:
    | paddle rebounds use a brief square-wave ping and top/bottom wall rebounds use the
    | same square waveform with a 2x frequency divider, exactly one octave lower. Sound
    | state advances in overscan game logic, never in the beam-critical renderer. Wall
    | rebounds trigger as the ball moves into the visible contact Y instead of one update
    | later. Paddle rebounds now use the TIA CXM0FB/CXM1FB M0-Ball/M1-Ball collision
    | latches, cleared before each gameplay raster, instead of guessed software X/Y
    | overlap; only actual rendered-pixel overlap can reverse the ball. The Paddleball
    | oracle models those hardware latches, distinguishes the two square-wave beeps by
    | AUDF, and verifies that audio/collision work does not perturb frame length. The
    | current cartridge build uses 3577 ROM bytes and 87 RAM bytes. That completed
    | work remains the two-player baseline for later controller examples.

    | Main-roadmap item 36 is complete. `libraries/vcs/four_paddles.c26` extends the
    | existing two-paddle RC lifecycle to INPT0..INPT3 and all four fire buttons while
    | keeping the original position0/1, button0/1, validity, sampling, gap-accounting,
    | and dump vocabulary as a strict API subset. The timing-safe schedule observes one
    | RC channel per scanline, alternating 0/1 and 2/3 across adjacent two-line pairs;
    | seven four-line VBLANK cycles consume 28 scanlines and leave scheduler headroom,
    | with explicit gap accounting preserving the common two-scanline elapsed units.
    | `examples/01_basic/10_four_player_paddleball/four_player_paddleball.c26` is an
    | unbanked 4K four-player team cartridge: left-port blue controls P0/M0,

    | by the score then restored below it with fixed-phase coarse RESP strobes only;
    | M0/M1 are likewise coarse-positioned once at startup. No paddle uses HMPx or
    | HMOVE at all; only the moving Ball uses horizontal fine motion. All four
    | defenders use TIA Ball collision latches, either teammate can serve, and team
    | scoring plus the existing Paddleball beeps are retained. A focused size pass
    | made four-paddle `account_gap()` one compact shared callable helper instead of
    | duplicating its four-channel bookkeeping at both call sites. A later renderer
    | timing/size pass removed the aligned PF2-pattern and fine-motion lookup tables:
    | Ball fine motion is precomputed outside the visible raster, and PF2 is computed
    | from the pair index on the preceding line and committed at cycle 3. This also
    | removed paddle-position-dependent visible scanline overruns and moved the
    | top-wall PF2 write from cycle 69 to cycle 24. The current build uses 3617/4090
    | ROM bytes and 101/128 RAM bytes. HMCLR is applied before Ball HMOVE, and the
    | four-player oracle now verifies that every post-startup blanking HMOVE has zero
    | HMP0/HMP1/HMM0/HMM1 so the Ball cannot drag any fixed paddle horizontally. The
    | oracle also includes an independent-position cross-sweep that catches
    | RC-completion plus paddle-transition scanline overruns, covering distinct,
    | simultaneous, and staggered four-channel RC thresholds, all four buttons,

    | Main-roadmap item 37 is complete. `libraries/vcs/keypad_controller.c26` supports
    | one 4x3 twelve-key keypad on either controller port while preserving the opposite
    | SWCHA/SWACNT nibble. It exposes a stable 12-bit `keys` snapshot, snapshot-edge
    | `pressed`/`released` masks, and a first-held `key` code. The controller is treated
    | as a simple active-low switch matrix: pins 1..4 select one LOW row and the three
    | columns are sampled through INPT0/1/4 on the left or INPT2/3/5 on the right after
    | the documented roughly 400-us settle interval. There is no paddle-position
    | measurement loop. The public NTSC example `examples/01_basic/11_keypad/keypad.c26`
    | scans the same row on both ports during overscan, waits seven scanlines before
    | sampling, and commits the full four-row state every four frames. Keeping the scan
    | in overscan removes input-dependent keypad work from the VBLANK-to-visible
    | deadline. It renders a custom 13-glyph 8x10 Big-font subset with P0 centered in the
    | left half and P1 centered in the right half, using an empty rectangle for no key,
    | white glyphs, and the project blue background.
    | 
    | A post-item hardware/emulator correction replaced the original compiler-heavy
    | matrix logic with compact fixed-cycle assembly and corrected all three columns to
    | the active-low software contract. More importantly, current Stella's ROM
    | controller detector classifies keypad INPT signatures plus recognized SWCHA
    | joystick-direction reads as Joy2BPlus rather than Keyboard. The component now
    | keeps the required BIT/BMI INPT signatures but preserves SWCHA with an
    | absolute-indexed X=0 read, which is electrically the same RIOT access yet does
    | not trigger Stella's joystick-direction signature. `vcs_keypad.pl` pins both
    | halves of that detector contract: all required left/right Keyboard signatures
    | must exist and all recognized SWCHA direction-read signatures must be absent.
    | The common two blank top rows and four blank bottom rows were removed from
    | all thirteen keypad glyphs; the surrounding visible blank scanlines preserve
    | vertical placement while the 10-byte glyph stride is computed as key*10. The
    | public cartridge is currently 1647/4090 ROM bytes and 43/128 RAM bytes.
    | `vcs_keypad.pl/.cpp` plus left/right private fixtures cover all twelve keys,
    | press/hold/release semantics, opposite-port isolation, minimum row-settle timing,
    | and invariant scheduler frame length. The example assembly allowlist pins the
    | trimmed keypad source at 47 reviewed beam-critical `asm` statements. Item 38
    | (Indy 500 driving controllers) is complete. `libraries/vcs/driving_controller.c26`
    | supports one controller on either port with a two-bit SWCHA Gray-code decoder.
    | The physical pin mapping is left pins 1/2 -> SWCHA D4/D5 and right pins 1/2 ->
    | SWCHA D0/D1; numeric phase bit 0 is pin 1 and bit 1 is pin 2. The decoder exposes
    | active-low INPT4/INPT5 fire state, a signed most-recent `step`, a signed per-frame
    | `delta`, current `phase`, and remembered unambiguous `direction`. Adjacent Gray
    | states are decoded as +/-1 across wrap; an opposite state after a known direction
    | is preserved as a +/-2 skipped transition, while an opposite state before any
    | direction history is ignored rather than guessed. `begin_frame()` resets `step`
    | and `delta`, and callers can sample repeatedly during timer-owned blanking.
    | 
    | The public `examples/01_basic/12_drive/` cartridge instantiates left and right
    | controllers, samples both three times in VBLANK and three times in overscan, and
    | displays independent centered Big-hex P0/P1 glyphs. Clockwise increments and
    | counterclockwise decrements modulo 16; each glyph is white while its fire button
    | is released and red only while held. The timing-critical controller decoder is
    | compact assembly so all six sample pairs fit the scheduler deadlines; the public
    | cartridge uses 1669/4090 ROM bytes and 41/128 RAM bytes while retaining stable
    | 264-raw / Stella-262 frame timing. Hex-glyph pointer preparation is ordinary VCSC;
    | the remaining 47 source `asm` statements are the reviewed beam-critical player
    | positioning/glyph raster. Current Stella requires the Driving controller
    | type to be selected manually because its ROM detector does not auto-detect this
    | controller family. `vcs_driving.pl/.cpp` plus left/right private 2K fixtures model the actual
    | RIOT pin wiring (unused pins 3/4 held high while pins 1/2 carry Gray code) and cover
    | both ports independently, clockwise/counterclockwise wrap, ambiguous and
    | direction-preserving skipped states, per-frame accumulation/reset, button
    | press/release, opposite-port SWCHA/SWACNT isolation, public hex arithmetic/color,
    | and invariant frame length. Installed-tree checks ship the component and build
    | the public example. Main-roadmap Items 39 (FA/RAM Plus), 41 (low-hanging-fruit mapper survey), and 42 (general automatic multi-region CODE/RODATA placement) are complete. Bankswitching Item 31 adds direct 4KSC with full Superchip RAM and a PASS/FAIL diagnostic. Focused mapper Items 32 OMNI, 33 CV/CommaVid, 34 JANE, 35 0840/EconoBanking, 36 UA/UASW, and 37 0FA0 are complete; Item 38 is explicitly deferred, so that focused mapper queue currently has no active unfinished item. `vcs_omni_32k.c26` defines seven direct `$ro` islands plus one explicitly selected `$rw` island at $1000 using existing DATA/BSS initialization semantics; no `$rwx` capability or automatic RAM spill is planned. `vcs_omni_32k.cfg` gives the simulator the matching selector-free eight-island logical layout, and public `09_bankswitching/05_omni` certifies all seven RO islands plus all 4096 bytes of `cartram`. Every public bankswitching profile now stamps a four-byte mapper signature at logical $xFF8-$xFFB in only the final file bank: `4KSC`, `F8\0\0`, `F8SC`, `F6\0\0`, `F6SC`, `F4\0\0`, `F4SC`, `FA\0\0`, `CV\0\0`, `OMNI`, `JANE`, `0840`, `UA\0\0`, `UASW`, or `0FA0`. The optional C26 `$signature:` cartridge flag is carried in `__cartmeta$V2$`; the linker still accepts legacy V1 cartridge records without a signature. Stella 7.0 autodetects the 4KSC diagnostic from the resulting `SC` bytes at $xFFA-$xFFB. The convention is documented for users in the top-level README, `libraries/vcs/README.md`, `examples/09_bankswitching/README.md`, and the compiler/linker references. The compile suite passes 500/500; focused OMNI/CV/JANE profile, example, simulator, disassembler, installation, and hygiene gates pass. JANE uses physical/file bank order 0/1/2/3 with selectors $1FF0/$1FF1/$1FF8/$1FF9 and hardware startup bank 1; VCSC logical `bank0` is therefore file index 1. Its replicated vector bridge lives at $FEE0-$FEF1 to avoid the $FFF0/$FFF1 selector pair, while the standard $FF00-$FFDF trampoline corridor remains available.
