2026-08-22 20:43:02 PDT, archive pre-cleanup bankswitching.txt

    Cold snapshot of .../bankswitching.txt immediately before the context-trimming pass.
    The maintained hot copy was then reduced to durable current state and unfinished work.

    | VCSC BANKSWITCHING AND SUPERCHIP PLAN
    | =====================================
    | 
    | Status
    | ------
    | This is the detailed design and implementation roadmap for the broad project
    | roadmap items covering 8K-and-larger cartridge bankswitching and Superchip RAM.
    | It is an internal planning document, not installed user documentation. The
    | compact `.../context.txt` handoff selects the active workstream, while
    | `.../roadmap.txt` owns the detailed main-project checklist. This file owns the
    | detailed order within the bankswitching milestones.
    | 
    | The first implementation family is the ordinary full-window Atari schemes:
    | 
    |     F8      2 x 4K banks   8K total
    |     F6      4 x 4K banks  16K total
    |     F4      8 x 4K banks  32K total
    | 
    | Their Superchip variants follow after ordinary banking is stable:
    | 
    |     F8SC    2 x 4K banks plus shared 128-byte cartridge RAM
    |     F6SC    4 x 4K banks plus shared 128-byte cartridge RAM
    |     F4SC    8 x 4K banks plus shared 128-byte cartridge RAM
    | 
    | Segmented and unusual schemes such as 3F, 3E, E0, E7, FE, UA, and 0840 are not
    | part of the first implementation. The common model should avoid blocking them,
    | but it must not pretend that they are merely differently sized F8 cartridges.
    | DPC, DPC+, CDF, and coprocessor-assisted formats are separate projects.
    | 
    | Core model: use the missing address lines as the bank namespace
    | ---------------------------------------------------------------
    | The 6507 has a 16-bit internal program counter but exposes only thirteen address
    | lines to the cartridge. A13-A15 do not reach the cartridge. VCSC can therefore
    | assign every physical 4K bank a distinct ordinary 16-bit logical range while
    | the cartridge sees every one of those ranges as the same $1000-$1FFF window.
    | 
    | Three bank identities must be kept separate.  Using the bare phrase "bank 0"
    | without saying which identity is meant is prohibited in this document and
    | should be avoided in diagnostics and user documentation:
    | 
    | 1. VCSC logical bank name
    | 
    |    This is the source/linker namespace.  BANK0 is deliberately the home bank at
    |    $F000, BANK1 is at $D000, and so on.  `main`, startup, and the mandatory
    |    runtime live in VCSC BANK0.
    | 
    | 2. Physical/file bank index
    | 
    |    This is the zero-based 4K chunk number in the emitted cartridge image.
    |    Physical/file bank 0 is the first 4096 bytes on disk.  Because VCSC writes
    |    banks in ascending logical-address order, physical/file bank 0 is the
    |    highest-numbered VCSC logical bank, not VCSC BANK0.  VCSC BANK0 is the last
    |    physical/file bank.
    | 
    | 3. Mapper selector hotspot
    | 
    |    F8/F6/F4 selectors increase with physical/file bank index.  The first
    |    selector chooses the first 4K chunk on disk, the next selector chooses the
    |    second chunk, and so forth.  Since VCSC logical bank numbers run in the
    |    opposite direction, selector addresses decrease as VCSC BANKn increases.
    | 
    | Use the established descending VCSC logical convention:
    | 
    |     VCSC logical bank    linker range
    |     -----------------    ------------
    |     BANK0                $F000-$FFFF
    |     BANK1                $D000-$DFFF
    |     BANK2                $B000-$BFFF
    |     BANK3                $9000-$9FFF
    |     BANK4                $7000-$7FFF
    |     BANK5                $5000-$5FFF
    |     BANK6                $3000-$3FFF
    |     BANK7                $1000-$1FFF
    | 
    | Equivalently:
    | 
    |     logical_base(BANKn) = $F000 - (n * $2000)
    | 
    | F8 uses VCSC BANK0 and BANK1. F6 uses BANK0 through BANK3. F4 uses BANK0
    | through BANK7. Ordinary linker symbols and relocations remain 16-bit. No far
    | pointer or bank-qualified C pointer is required for this family.
    | 
    | The cartridge file is written in ascending logical-address order, beginning
    | with the lowest-address bank and ending with the highest-address bank. Therefore
    | VCSC BANK0 is always the final 4K chunk in the file:
    | 
    |     mapper  file index  VCSC bank  linker range  selector hotspot
    |     ------  ----------  ---------  ------------  ----------------
    |     F8      0           BANK1      $D000-$DFFF   $1FF8
    |             1           BANK0      $F000-$FFFF   $1FF9
    | 
    |     F6      0           BANK3      $9000-$9FFF   $1FF6
    |             1           BANK2      $B000-$BFFF   $1FF7
    |             2           BANK1      $D000-$DFFF   $1FF8
    |             3           BANK0      $F000-$FFFF   $1FF9
    | 
    |     F4      0           BANK7      $1000-$1FFF   $1FF4
    |             1           BANK6      $3000-$3FFF   $1FF5
    |             2           BANK5      $5000-$5FFF   $1FF6
    |             3           BANK4      $7000-$7FFF   $1FF7
    |             4           BANK3      $9000-$9FFF   $1FF8
    |             5           BANK2      $B000-$BFFF   $1FF9
    |             6           BANK1      $D000-$DFFF   $1FFA
    |             7           BANK0      $F000-$FFFF   $1FFB
    | 
    | The equivalent formulas for these full-window schemes are:
    | 
    |     file_index(BANKn) = bank_count - 1 - n
    |     hotspot(BANKn)    = first_mapper_hotspot + file_index(BANKn)
    | 
    | where the first mapper hotspot is $1FF8 for F8, $1FF6 for F6, and $1FF4 for
    | F4.  Thus the startup/home hotspot is always the last hotspot in the mapper's
    | range: $1FF9 for F8 and F6, and $1FFB for F4.
    | 
    | Cartridge signature convention
    | ------------------------------
    | Every public VCSC bankswitching profile carries a four-byte mapper signature at
    | physical offsets `$xFF8-$xFFB` of the final 4K bank written to disk. The source
    | profile spells the meaningful ASCII bytes with `$signature:TEXT`; signatures
    | shorter than four bytes are padded with ASCII NUL, never spaces or underscores.
    | Current signatures are `4KSC`, `F8\0\0`, `F8SC`, `F6\0\0`, `F6SC`,
    | `F4\0\0`, `F4SC`, `FA\0\0`, and `OMNI`.
    | 
    | Only the final physical/file bank receives the signature. Earlier banks retain
    | their normal ROM/fill bytes at the same offsets. Hotspot overlap is intentional
    | and harmless: file-based mapper detection can inspect these bytes before any
    | cartridge object exists, and real mapper hardware reacts to an access at a
    | hotspot address rather than to the data value stored there. On the 6507, the
    | last two signature bytes occupy the otherwise-unused NMI-vector location; reset
    | and IRQ/BRK vectors remain ordinary vectors. Profile allocator regions reserve
    | `$xFF8-$xFF9` so the signature never overwrites ordinary linked program data.
    | 
    | The linker emits those banks contiguously without the unused logical gaps
    | between $1FFF and $3000, $3FFF and $5000, and so on. File position, VCSC logical
    | address, and selector hotspot are three separate facts. The currently implemented cfg records all three relationships explicitly and
    | the linker validates the complete table rather than guessing from a name or
    | relying on declaration order. Roadmap items 24 through 27 replace that duplicated
    | cfg description with ordinary C26 topology metadata while preserving these same
    | three independent identities.
    | 
    | This convention also matches the common cartridge layout in which the final
    | chunk contains the $F000 home/reset code.  Existing ROMs are not normative for
    | VCSC naming: some assemble both physical banks at $F000, some use other mirrors,
    | and external documentation may call the first chunk "bank 0".  In VCSC,
    | uppercase BANK0 always means the $F000 home bank, never merely the first bytes
    | on disk.
    | 
    | For the first public F8/F6/F4 family, VCSC BANK0 is the required startup/home
    | bank and `main` always resides there. A later mapper family may revisit the
    | startup-bank rule as a separate decision.
    | 
    | Current cfg implementation model
    | --------------------------------
    | The implemented system has separate public linker configurations for the common schemes:
    | 
    |     vcs_8k_f8.cfg
    |     vcs_16k_f6.cfg
    |     vcs_32k_f4.cfg
    |     vcs_8k_f8sc.cfg
    |     vcs_16k_f6sc.cfg
    |     vcs_32k_f4sc.cfg
    | 
    | The first implemented cfg spelling uses `CARTRIDGE` and `BANKS` blocks plus a
    | `bank=NAME` property on cartridge-output `MEMORY` regions:
    | 
    |     CARTRIDGE {
    |         mapper = F8;
    |         fillval = $FF;
    |         trampoline = $0F00;
    |         trampolinesize = $00E0;
    |         vectorbridge = $0FE0;
    |     }
    | 
    |     BANKS {
    |         BANK0: start=$F000, size=$1000, hotspot=$1FF9, startup=yes;
    |         BANK1: start=$D000, size=$1000, hotspot=$1FF8, startup=no;
    |     }
    | 
    |     MEMORY {
    |         bank1:              start=$D000, size=$0F00, type=ro, bank=BANK1;
    |         BANK1_TRAMPOLINE:    start=$DF00, size=$00E0,          bank=BANK1;
    |         BANK1_VECTOR_BRIDGE: start=$DFE0, size=$0012,          bank=BANK1;
    |         BANK1_TAIL:          start=$DFF2, size=$0008,          bank=BANK1;
    |         BANK1_VECTORS:       start=$DFFA, size=$0006,          bank=BANK1;
    |         ROM:                 start=$F000, size=$0F00, type=ro, bank=BANK0;
    |         BANK0_TRAMPOLINE:    start=$FF00, size=$00E0,          bank=BANK0;
    |         BANK0_VECTOR_BRIDGE: start=$FFE0, size=$0012,          bank=BANK0;
    |         BANK0_TAIL:          start=$FFF2, size=$0008,          bank=BANK0;
    |         BANK0_VECTORS:       start=$FFFA, size=$0006,          bank=BANK0;
    |         /* shared RAM regions omitted */
    |     }
    | 
    | A bank description tells the linker the VCSC logical bank name and 16-bit range,
    | its mapper selector hotspot, whether it is the startup bank, and the full 4K
    | physical-bank size.  The output writer derives physical/file index from
    | ascending logical address, not from the numeric suffix in `BANKn`.  `MEMORY`
    | entries subdivide each full physical unit into allocatable ROM and
    | non-allocatable holes such as hotspots, vectors, and the common trampoline
    | corridor. `CARTRIDGE trampoline` gives the corridor's low-twelve-bit start and
    | `trampolinesize` gives its reserved capacity. Both are required for banked
    | profiles, must fit inside one 4K bank, and may not overlap the vector bridge,
    | vectors, or any selector hotspot.
    | 
    | The cfg parser rejects unknown blocks and properties. Silently ignoring a
    | misspelled bank or hotspot property would create a plausible-looking but dead
    | cartridge. MEMORY, SEGMENTS, and BANKS storage is dynamically sized rather than
    | limited to sixteen entries.
    | 
    | Every selector hotspot is reserved at the same low twelve-bit offset in every
    | physical bank, not merely in the bank selected by that hotspot. The linker
    | rejects a configuration when any ordinary allocatable code/data segment region
    | covers any declared selector byte. Vector-only ownership may use fixed bytes
    | which overlap mapper hotspots where the mapper requires it, but ordinary code
    | must never be placed there.
    | 
    | The implementation should model a physical bank as one 4K output unit even when
    | its allocatable ROM is split into ordinary code, a bridge area, reserved hotspot
    | bytes, and vectors. A bank may contain non-allocatable holes, but its emitted
    | file chunk remains exactly 4096 bytes.
    | 
    | Planned C26 topology model and cfg retirement
    | ------------------------------------------------
    | The cfg files are a historical implementation mechanism, not the desired final
    | source of cartridge truth. They currently repeat facts already present in C26
    | `mem` declarations and hide physical image topology in a second syntax. The
    | planned model moves the complete public cartridge description into ordinary C26
    | metadata and leaves cfg support only as a temporary compatibility path and,
    | possibly, a future escape hatch for unusual non-VCS targets.
    | 
    | The design separates two declarations with deliberately different jobs:
    | 
    | * `bank` describes one independently emitted cartridge-image unit, its location
    |   in the output file, the part of that unit mapped into logical CPU space, and,
    |   when applicable, the selector which makes that mapping active. A bank may be
    |   selector-controlled or directly mapped.
    | * `mem` describes only bytes which are available to the allocator. This adopts
    |   the strict "allocatable bytes only" interpretation: vector bytes, generated
    |   bridges, mapper-reserved bytes, RAM-port prefixes, and other holes are not
    |   included in a `mem` region merely because they lie inside an output bank.
    | 
    | `bank` and `mem` use separate namespaces. The same spelling may be used in both:
    | 
    |     bank bank1 { /* output and mapping topology */ };
    |     mem  bank1 { /* source-placement region */ };
    | 
    | A source declaration modifier such as `bank1 void logic(void)` always resolves
    | in the `mem` namespace. A bank is not a source placement qualifier. Neither
    | namespace gives semantic meaning to the spelling `bank0`, `bank1`, `ram`, or any
    | other name. Matching names are convenient for people, but the linker must derive
    | behavior entirely from declared values. For example, `bank fred` may contain
    | `mem bank1` when their ranges establish that relationship.
    | 
    | Do not add a magic declaration such as `cart f8;`. A file named `f8.c26` or
    | `f8sc.c26` may be a convenient installed library file, but its filename and
    | included path have no linker semantics. The file must contain the actual bank,
    | allocation, output, and generated-corridor declarations. A programmer or build
    | may include that file or compile it as a configuration-only C26 input; identical
    | declarations from several translation units coalesce, while conflicting
    | same-name declarations are fatal.
    | 
    | The exact parser spelling is locked by roadmap item 24, but the intended facts
    | are illustrated by this F8SC description:
    | 
    |     cartridge {
    |         $fill:                  0xff
    |         $trampoline_offset:     0x0f00
    |         $trampoline_size:       0x00e0
    |         $vector_bridge_offset:  0x0fe0
    |         $vector_bridge_size:    0x0012
    |         $vectors_offset:        0x0ffa
    |         $vectors_size:          0x0006
    |     };
    | 
    |     bank bank0 {
    |         $image_size:    0x1000
    |         $file_index:    1
    |         $image_offset:  0x0100
    |         $link_start:    0xf100
    |         $cpu_start:     0xf100
    |         $map_size:      0x0f00
    |         $select_access: 0x1ff9
    |         $startup
    |     };
    | 
    |     bank bank1 {
    |         $image_size:    0x1000
    |         $file_index:    0
    |         $image_offset:  0x0100
    |         $link_start:    0xd100
    |         $cpu_start:     0xf100
    |         $map_size:      0x0f00
    |         $select_access: 0x1ff8
    |     };
    | 
    |     mem bank0 {
    |         $start:    0xf100
    |         $size:     0x0e00
    |         $ro
    |         $priority: 2
    |     };
    | 
    |     mem bank1 {
    |         $start: 0xd100
    |         $size:  0x0e00
    |         $ro
    |     };
    | 
    |     mem cartram {
    |         $read_start:  0xf080
    |         $write_start: 0xf000
    |         $size:        0x0080
    |         $rw
    |     };
    | 
    | The fields have these intended meanings:
    | 
    | * `$image_size` is the complete physical file chunk for that bank. F8 and F8SC
    |   both emit 4096-byte chunks.
    | * `$file_index` is the physical chunk number in the output image. It is explicit;
    |   declaration order, numeric suffixes, and logical-address sorting are not hidden
    |   substitutes for it.
    | * `$image_offset` is the byte offset within the physical chunk at which the
    |   logical mapping begins.
    | * `$link_start` and `$map_size` define the synthetic linker range which uniquely
    |   distinguishes the bank's contents during linking.
    | * `$cpu_start` and `$map_size` define where those bytes appear to the 6507.
    | * `$select_access`, when present, is an any-bus-access selector hotspot matching
    |   the current F8/F6/F4 hardware and the linker's state-preserving `STA hotspot`
    |   bridges. A later mapper may require separately described read, write, masked,
    |   or value-qualified selectors; that must extend the topology language rather
    |   than overload names.
    | * Absence of `$select_access` means the bank is directly mapped. Its logical
    |   mapping is simultaneously available with every other nonoverlapping direct
    |   bank, and references to it never acquire selector or trampoline code.
    | * `$startup` marks the one selector-controlled bank whose reset/runtime/main
    |   placement contract is used by the current full-window family. A direct-only
    |   topology does not require a startup-bank marker; ordinary vectors and placement
    |   priority determine its entry layout.
    | * The `cartridge` block gives output-wide fill and explicit locations/capacities
    |   for linker-generated replicated material. Direct-only topologies which need no
    |   mapper trampolines or reset bridges may omit those reservations and retain only
    |   the output properties they actually use.
    | 
    | Plain F8 and F8SC therefore have genuinely different bank declarations. Plain
    | F8 maps the complete physical bank as ROM:
    | 
    |     bank bank0 {
    |         $image_size:    0x1000
    |         $file_index:    1
    |         $image_offset:  0x0000
    |         $link_start:    0xf000
    |         $cpu_start:     0xf000
    |         $map_size:      0x1000
    |         $select_access: 0x1ff9
    |         $startup
    |     };
    | 
    |     mem bank0 { $start:0xf000 $size:0x0f00 $ro $priority:2 };
    | 
    | F8SC leaves the first $100 physical bytes outside the switched-ROM mapping and
    | begins ROM at `$x100`. Its allocatable region begins at `$x100` and still ends at
    | `$xEFF`, because the final `$100` bytes are reserved for generated bank-call and
    | vector machinery. Thus the model records all three distinct extents:
    | 
    |     physical F8SC chunk:        image offsets $000-$FFF
    |     switched F8SC ROM mapping:  image offsets $100-$FFF
    |     ordinary allocatable ROM:   image offsets $100-$EFF
    | 
    | This distinction is how the linker knows that Superchip accesses do not require
    | hotspot code. Output ownership is derived from allocator-region containment in a
    | bank's synthetic mapped link range, not from a numeric CPU address and not from
    | a region name. For each `mem` region with an allocatable link range, the linker
    | finds containing bank ranges using:
    | 
    |     bank.link_start <= mem.start
    | 
    | and
    | 
    |     mem.start + mem.size <= bank.link_start + bank.map_size
    | 
    | Exactly one containing bank gives the region one output owner. No containing
    | bank makes it shared/unpackaged by that bank table. More than one containing bank
    | is an ambiguous target configuration and is rejected. The inference uses the
    | synthetic link range, never the CPU-visible mirror, because selector-controlled
    | banks may share the same CPU window.
    | 
    | For the F8SC example:
    | 
    |     mem bank0:       $F100-$FEFF  is inside bank0 $F100-$FFFF
    |     mem bank1:       $D100-$DEFF  is inside bank1 $D100-$DFFF
    |     mem cartram:   read $F080-$F0FF, write $F000-$F07F
    | 
    | The Superchip aliases lie outside every switched-ROM link range and therefore
    | have no owning bank. They are one shared split-address allocation. Relocations
    | to that region remain ordinary direct reads/writes from every selected bank;
    | they never generate a selector access.
    | 
    | Output ownership and switch behavior are separate facts:
    | 
    | * A region owned by a directly mapped bank is always directly addressable. Calls,
    |   jumps, and data references between distinct direct banks use ordinary absolute
    |   addresses and never use bridges.
    | * A region owned by a selector-controlled bank participates in the current
    |   selected-bank state. Transfers between different selector-controlled banks use
    |   the declared selector and generated bridge machinery.
    | * A region with no output-bank owner is shared/nonbanked and never causes a bank
    |   transition by itself.
    | 
    | A direct-bank topology can therefore use the same declaration machinery purely
    | for deterministic image packaging. For example:
    | 
    |     bank bank1 {
    |         $image_size:   0x1000
    |         $file_index:   0
    |         $image_offset: 0x0000
    |         $link_start:   0x3000
    |         $cpu_start:    0x3000
    |         $map_size:     0x1000
    |     };
    | 
    |     bank bank0 {
    |         $image_size:   0x1000
    |         $file_index:   1
    |         $image_offset: 0x0000
    |         $link_start:   0xf000
    |         $cpu_start:    0xf000
    |         $map_size:     0x1000
    |     };
    | 
    |     mem bank1 { $start:0x3000 $size:0x1000 $ro };
    |     mem bank0 { $start:0xf000 $size:0x0ffa $ro $priority:2 };
    | 
    | This emits two fixed 4K chunks in declared file order. The first chunk represents
    | logical `$3000-$3FFF`; the second represents `$F000-$FFFF`, with its final six
    | bytes reserved for ordinary vectors. Both mappings are simultaneously available.
    | A call from `$Fxxx` to `$3xxx` is an ordinary `JSR $3xxx`, not a cross-bank call.
    | The output format may remain a flat concatenation when the consumer already knows
    | the target profile; a future self-describing container may add a range table
    | without changing the linker-address or placement model.
    | 
    | No explicit `$bank` property is needed on `mem`. The linker validates that every
    | owned allocation lies wholly inside exactly one mapped link range, that mapped
    | link ranges of simultaneously available direct banks do not overlap, that
    | selector-controlled synthetic ranges are unambiguous, that
    | `image_offset + map_size <= image_size`, that file indices are unique and dense,
    | and that selector triggers are valid and unique for the profile. It requires
    | exactly one startup bank only when selector-controlled banks are present. It also
    | maps allocator and generated ranges back to physical image offsets and rejects
    | any collision between ordinary allocation, RAM-port prefixes, trampoline tables,
    | vector bridges, vectors, selector bytes, or other declared reservations.
    | 
    | Once `mem` is authoritative, ordinary segment routing is mechanical rather than
    | cfg-authored duplication:
    | 
    |     CODE.region and RODATA.region -> mem region
    |     BSS.region                    -> writable mem region
    |     DATA.region                   -> load in default read-only mem,
    |                                      run in writable mem region
    | 
    | Unqualified CODE/RODATA/DATA/BSS/STARTUP placement is derived from region access,
    | priority, startup-bank ownership where applicable, and existing language rules.
    | The linker must continue to preserve private movable layouts, hard pins,
    | alignment/page constraints, split read/write aliases, and deterministic
    | placement. The map reports both the `mem` region and its inferred output bank,
    | including whether that bank is direct or selector-controlled, so the inference
    | is visible rather than mystical.
    | 
    | Renderer and hand-written assembly constraints are not cartridge topology and
    | must not be moved into a renderer x mapper matrix of target files. Segment
    | region requirements, alignment/page constraints, and hidden hardware-stack use
    | belong in object metadata emitted by the source or assembler. For example, the
    | standard renderer should carry its own bank-local segment and 256-byte alignment
    | requirements, while the cartridge C26 file supplies only cartridge topology and
    | allocatable regions. This is the composition mechanism required before a banked
    | renderer profile is added.
    | 
    | The current cfg implementation remains normative until roadmap items 24 through
    | 27 are completed and differential tests prove identical images, placements,
    | bridges, maps, simulator behavior, and Stella behavior. Completed roadmap items
    | which mention cfg describe the implementation that was actually certified; they
    | are not retroactively rewritten as though the C26 topology model already exists.
    | 
    | Per-bank vectors and reset bridges
    | ----------------------------------
    | Per-bank vectors and reset bridges are an early foundation item, not cleanup at
    | the end. Power-up bank state must not be assumed.
    | 
    | The implemented `CARTRIDGE vectorbridge` property gives one low-twelve-bit
    | corridor offset. The first implementation reserves eighteen bytes there in every
    | physical bank and emits three byte-identical six-byte entries:
    | 
    |     NMI entry:      BIT BANK0_HOTSPOT; JMP __nmi
    |     RESET entry:    BIT BANK0_HOTSPOT; JMP __reset
    |     IRQ/BRK entry:  BIT BANK0_HOTSPOT; JMP __irqbrk
    | 
    | Every physical bank also receives the same six vector bytes at its final six
    | physical offsets. All three vector words use BANK0's logical mirror of the
    | common entries, for example $FFE0, $FFE6, and $FFEC when `vectorbridge=$0FE0`.
    | The currently selected physical bank ignores those upper logical address bits,
    | fetches the identical entry at the same cartridge offset, and the entry selects
    | BANK0 before jumping to the ordinary runtime handler. BANK0 runs the same
    | sequence; selecting the already selected bank is harmless.
    | 
    | Using one identical vector image is deliberate. F4's $1FFA and $1FFB selector
    | hotspots overlap the NMI vector bytes.  Under the table above, the low-byte
    | fetch at $1FFA selects physical/file bank 6, which is VCSC BANK1; the high-byte
    | fetch at $1FFB then selects physical/file bank 7, which is VCSC BANK0.  Every
    | bank contains the same vector bytes, so the word is deterministic despite the
    | mid-vector bank changes, and the NMI fetch itself finishes with BANK0 selected.
    | The common NMI entry selects BANK0 again defensively before entering `__nmi`.
    | RESET and IRQ/BRK follow the same general bridge path, so BRK remains safe even
    | though the 6507 package exposes no ordinary NMI/IRQ inputs.
    | 
    | Ordinary placement must never consume the vector corridor, the six final vector
    | bytes, or any mapper hotspot. The linker rejects an allocatable code/data region
    | covering the configured eighteen-byte corridor or any selector byte, and it
    | rejects generated bridge/vector output which would overwrite placed material.
    | The normal DATA/BSS initialization and `main` entry live only in BANK0 rather
    | than being duplicated wholesale.
    | 
    | Bank-aware relocation rules
    | ---------------------------
    | A declared output-bank identity, not just an arithmetic test on upper address
    | bits, determines whether two ROM locations share one selector-controlled bank.
    | The current implementation obtains that identity from cfg ownership; roadmap
    | item 25 derives it from C26 `mem` containment within C26 `bank` mapped link
    | ranges. Direct banks remain simultaneously addressable and therefore do not use
    | this selected-bank test. This also handles Superchip banks whose allocatable ROM
    | begins at $x100.
    | 
    | References from banked ROM obey these rules:
    | 
    | * A reference to ROM in the same bank is emitted normally.
    | * A reference to RIOT RAM, TIA, RIOT registers, or another explicitly shared
    |   nonbanked address is emitted normally from every bank.
    | * A direct cross-bank JSR is redirected through a generated call bridge.
    | * A direct cross-bank JMP is redirected through a generated one-way bridge.
    | * A cross-bank conditional branch is an error.
    | * A cross-bank ROM load, address constant relocation, pointer initializer, or
    |   other data reference is an error.
    | * Programmer-authored indirect cross-bank control flow remains unsupported.
    |   The linker-generated call/jump machinery may use an indirect JMP internally,
    |   but VCSC still rejects function pointers, so ordinary compiled calls remain
    |   visible to the linker.
    | 
    | The assembler/object format identifies direct control-transfer relocations as
    | JSR, JMP, or relaxed conditional branch. The two otherwise-unused low bits of
    | the o26 relocation type carry this intent without changing relocation
    | arithmetic or the version-2 object format. The linker does not infer semantics
    | by peeking at an opcode byte next to a generic word relocation. Existing
    | low-byte, high-byte, and word data relocations remain ordinary data references
    | and therefore cannot cross banks. Ordinary two-byte relative branches remain in
    | the B26 branch table and are checked from their final source and target
    | addresses after placement.
    | 
    | Roadmap items 5 and 6 install direct cross-bank JMP and JSR trampolines. A
    | proven direct transfer is rewritten to the source bank's logical mirror of a
    | deduplicated entry in the common replicated table. JMP entries are deduplicated
    | by final target and destination hotspot. JSR entries also include the statically
    | known source hotspot because their embedded return stub must restore that bank.
    | The linker never emits a raw mirrored cross-bank target address. This generated
    | control-flow support is intentionally different from the permanent prohibition
    | on cross-bank ROM data and conditional branches.
    | 
    | A programmer can always defeat relocation checks with a manually written raw
    | numeric address. That does not make such access supported; diagnostics cover
    | symbolic relocations the toolchain can prove.
    | 
    | Cross-bank call and jump bridges
    | --------------------------------
    | The complete linker-generated bridge table must occupy the same low twelve-bit
    | cartridge offsets and contain exactly the same bytes in every physical bank.
    | It is one common replicated image, not a separately generated per-bank table.
    | After a hotspot access changes the selected bank, instruction fetching continues
    | at the next byte in the newly selected bank; byte-for-byte identity is therefore
    | what makes every continuation safe.
    | 
    | The 6502 has no indirect JSR instruction, but a normal JSR can call a fixed
    | entry whose own JSR creates the synthetic return address before the entry
    | ultimately performs an indirect JMP. That is the generic cross-bank call
    | mechanism. The implemented fifteen-byte entry is:
    | 
    |     generated entry for source BANKs and target $DABC:
    |         JSR  body
    |     return_to_source:
    |         STA  source_hotspot
    |         RTS
    |     body:
    |         STA  destination_hotspot
    |         JMP  (BANK0-mirror address of inline_target)
    |     inline_target:
    |         .word $DABC
    | 
    | The call site's ordinary JSR first places its real return address on the
    | hardware stack. The generated entry's JSR then places the address immediately
    | before `return_to_source` above it, without modifying A, X, Y, or the processor
    | flags. `body` selects the target bank and enters the callee with the indirect
    | JMP. The target function's RTS increments the synthetic address and reaches the
    | identical return stub. That stub stores A to the caller's hotspot, preserving A
    | and flags while restoring the source bank, and executes a second RTS through the
    | call site's original return address.
    | 
    | Both the internal JSR operand and indirect-JMP pointer are encoded through
    | BANK0's logical mirror of the common table. The CPU may therefore change its
    | upper logical address bits while the cartridge continues to see the same low
    | twelve-bit offset. Every physical bank contains the same bytes at that offset,
    | so the entry remains valid before and after either bank switch. Entries are
    | deduplicated by source hotspot, destination hotspot, and final target. Nested
    | cross-bank calls naturally nest the two return addresses in LIFO order.
    | 
    | The implemented direct-JMP entry does not need a shared RAM pointer. Each
    | specialized eight-byte table entry carries its own immutable target word:
    | 
    |     generated entry for target $DABC:
    |         STA  destination_hotspot
    |         JMP  (BANK0-mirror address of inline_target)
    |     inline_target:
    |         .word $DABC
    | 
    | `STA` is deliberate: F8/F6/F4 switch on an access to the hotspot, and a store
    | changes neither A nor the processor flags. A direct JMP therefore preserves the
    | machine state that the original instruction would have preserved. The indirect
    | pointer operand always uses BANK0's logical mirror of the inline word. A13-A15
    | do not reach the cartridge, so that address reads the same low-twelve-bit table
    | offset from whichever physical bank is selected after the hotspot store. The
    | complete entry bytes are consequently identical in every bank.
    | 
    | The linker deduplicates entries by final target address and destination hotspot.
    | Every call site to one target jumps to its own source bank's logical mirror of
    | the same entry offset. The table allocator inserts a fill byte when needed so an
    | inline target word never begins at page offset $FF and cannot trigger the NMOS
    | 6502/6507 indirect-JMP page-wrap bug. Corridor exhaustion is a link error which
    | reports the reserved range, target, entry count, and consumed bytes.
    | 
    | A cross-bank JMP never restores the source bank. It selects the destination and
    | continues at the final logical target. A cross-bank JSR restores the source bank
    | through its embedded synthetic return stub.
    | 
    | The linker reserves the common bridge corridor at the same physical offset in
    | every bank and emits one byte-identical table into every bank. Eight-byte JMP
    | entries and fifteen-byte source-aware JSR entries share that corridor. The map
    | reports occupied and total replicated bytes, separate entry counts and sizes,
    | and each transfer's source/destination identity. Exhausting the corridor is a
    | clear link error. The allocator inserts fill when either entry's inline target
    | word would begin at page offset `$FF`, avoiding the NMOS indirect-JMP wrap bug.
    | 
    | A cross-bank call keeps one additional hardware-stack return address live while
    | the destination function runs. For compiler-described call edges, the linker
    | computes both the ordinary longest function depth and a weighted depth which
    | adds one return-address slot for every simultaneously active cross-bank edge.
    | `callstack = callgraph` reserves two bytes per weighted slot and exports
    | `__call_stack_weighted_depth` and `__call_stack_bank_extra_slots` alongside the
    | existing stack symbols. Calls hidden in hand-written or separately assembled
    | code remain the integration's responsibility through `callstack_extra`.
    | 
    | Cross-bank data policy
    | ----------------------
    | The first implementation deliberately has no far ROM data reads. Code and the
    | ROM tables it directly uses must live in the same bank. Data shared by multiple
    | banks may be duplicated or deliberately placed with each caller. A relocation
    | from one bank to a const object in another bank is a hard error, not an implicit
    | bank switch.
    | 
    | Ordinary C pointers stay 16-bit and bank-local for ROM. Taking the address of a
    | ROM object in another bank is rejected when represented by a relocation. No
    | hidden bank byte is added to pointer objects or function parameters.
    | 
    | Bank placement policy
    | ---------------------
    | VCSC already has named `mem` regions and uses a region name as a declaration
    | modifier for objects. The same mechanism is now the source spelling for explicit
    | function placement; do not add special `bank0` through `bank7` language keywords.
    | For an ordinary F8 bank:
    | 
    |     mem bank1 { $start:0xD000 $size:0x0F00 $ro };
    | 
    |     bank1 void some_function(void) {
    |         /* ... */
    |     }
    | 
    | For a Superchip profile, the source `mem` declaration describes the allocatable
    | ROM interval, not the complete emitted physical bank. The RAM-port prefix and
    | the common `$xF00` trampoline corridor are excluded, leaving `$x100-$xEFF`:
    | 
    |     mem bank1 { $start:0xD100 $size:0x0E00 $ro };
    | 
    | The current cfg/image model still emits the complete 4K physical bank and fills
    | its reserved `$D000-$D0FF` prefix separately. The planned C26 `bank` declaration
    | expresses the same full image size, mapped-ROM offset, and mapped-ROM size. A
    | `$size:0x0F00` region would collide
    | with the trampoline corridor; `$size:0x1000` would run beyond the logical bank.
    | 
    | Named region modifiers work on function definitions and produce private
    | layouts such as `CODE.bank1.__vcsc_function$some_function`. The assembler keeps
    | procedures in any `CODE` or `CODE.*` segment independently movable, and the
    | linker resolves the longest matching segment rule so `CODE.bank1` controls that
    | private layout. A declaration and its definition must use the same named region.
    | An inline function cannot use a region modifier because inline expansion has no
    | independently placeable linker layout.
    | 
    | Named `$ro` regions also pin constant ROM objects. For example:
    | 
    |     bank1 const uint8_t level_table[4] := { 1, 2, 3, 4 };
    | 
    | is emitted as `RODATA.bank1.__vcsc_object$level_table`. A definition in a `$ro`
    | region must be `const` and must have a link-time initializer; it cannot fall back
    | to DATA/BSS plus startup writes. This keeps the source declaration, o26 private
    | layout, and linker bank affinity honest. Ordinary writable named regions retain
    | their existing DATA/BSS behavior.
    | 
    | Unmarked private `CODE.__vcsc_function$...` and
    | `RODATA.__vcsc_object$...`/`RODATA.__vcsc_page$...` layouts are candidates for
    | automatic placement. Source-level `main` normally remains unqualified: the
    | linker pins its private layout to the unique BANKS entry marked `startup=yes`
    | and chooses that bank's ordinary allocatable ROM region. An explicit read-only
    | `mem` modifier on `main` is permitted only when its MEMORY region belongs to
    | that same configured startup bank. The compiler does not interpret names such
    | as `bank0`; only the linker has the BANKS/MEMORY relationship needed to enforce
    | the rule. Startup/non-private runtime material and private runtime functions
    | whose reserved implementation names begin with `_` are pinned by the same
    | startup-bank property. The public full-window profiles happen to call that bank
    | BANK0, but neither placement nor diagnostics depend on that spelling.
    | 
    | The implemented placement policy is constrained and deterministic:
    | 
    | * Named `mem` modifiers are hard pins for functions and ROM objects. A pinned
    |   private layout keeps the exact named MEMORY region, not merely the bank.
    | * Cross-bank ROM data is forbidden. Every relocation between ROM layouts other
    |   than a trampoline-eligible direct JSR/JMP, plus every retained branch edge,
    |   forms a hard same-bank relation. The linker collapses the transitive closure
    |   into indivisible placement components before assigning addresses.
    | * A hard component inherits any explicit or mandatory bank pin carried by one
    |   of its members. Conflicting pins in one component are a fatal diagnostic that
    |   names the component, layout, object, and incompatible banks.
    | * Direct JSR and direct JMP edges are soft relations because the replicated
    |   trampoline table can implement them. Repeated static relocation sites
    |   accumulate deterministic weights equal to the bridge payload and one-execution
    |   penalty: 15 bytes and 25 extra cycles for JSR, or 8 bytes and 6 extra cycles
    |   for JMP. Sites remain separate weights even when final trampoline
    |   deduplication can share an entry. These are relocation-derived priorities, not
    |   invented dynamic call frequencies or a prediction of final replicated bytes.
    | * Before packing movable components, the linker charges each ordinary ROM
    |   region for fixed initialized-data images and generated copy/zero/init tables.
    |   The largest `type=ro` MEMORY region owned by each bank is that bank's default
    |   automatic-placement region; named pins still use their exact region.
    | * Both placement modes assign pinned components first in stable input order and
    |   preserve every hard component, pin, replica-locality mask, and capacity rule.
    |   `optimized`, the default, orders unpinned components by decreasing byte size,
    |   decreasing soft-edge byte degree, then stable object/layout order. Among banks
    |   with enough preliminary capacity, it minimizes incremental byte weight,
    |   then cycle weight and cut-site count. A deterministic single-component local
    |   pass then scans stable order and accepts a move only when it improves the
    |   incident cut without increasing weighted hardware-stack depth, or leaves the
    |   cut unchanged while reducing that depth. It repeats to a fixed point.
    | * `simple` ignores the soft-edge graph, takes unpinned components in stable input
    |   order, and chooses the first bank under the normal deterministic preference
    |   which has enough capacity. It is a debugging/reproducibility baseline, not a
    |   fallback that weakens semantics. Bank ties prefer the startup bank, then the
    |   higher logical bank address, then the bank name.
    | * The preliminary budget catches clear capacity failures early. The normal
    |   layout allocator remains authoritative for alignment, page containment,
    |   branch-page constraints, and any holes those rules create.
    | * If no bank can hold a component, the diagnostic reports its size and the free
    |   ordinary-ROM capacity in every candidate bank. The linker never breaks a hard
    |   component, moves a hard pin, duplicates a function, or invents a cross-bank
    |   ROM-data access to make the image fit.
    | 
    | There is still no second placement language, explicit same-bank-group attribute,
    | translation-unit preference, or profile-guided dynamic call frequency. Current
    | objects contain no runtime frequency evidence, so assigning guessed frequencies
    | would merely replace linker astrology with numerology. Existing hard data edges
    | already express the most important same-bank grouping, while named `mem`
    | modifiers give programmers a direct steering wheel for timing-critical or
    | architectural decisions.
    | 
    | The call graph alone is insufficient: ROM-data edges, explicit pins, reserved
    | trampoline/vector space, generated startup tables, and per-bank capacity all
    | matter. Conversely, requiring programmers to place everything manually throws
    | away whole-program information the linker already has. The hybrid policy keeps
    | normal use sane while preserving precise control for timing-critical code,
    | fixed architecture, debugging, and reproducible experiments.
    | 
    | A ROM table used by functions in several banks pulls those functions and the
    | table into one hard component unless the table is explicitly duplicated. If
    | that component carries incompatible pins or cannot fit, the link fails. The
    | linker never silently synthesizes a far ROM read. Functions and objects are not
    | duplicated automatically in this implementation.
    | 
    | Archive selection remains normal symbol-driven selection. Placement runs only
    | after the final object/archive-member set is known. The map's `BANK PLACEMENT`
    | section reports the selected mode and every component, pinned versus automatic
    | assignment, owning bank and concrete MEMORY region, component byte cost,
    | incident cut weight, and originating object. `--explain-bank-placement` writes
    | component membership and allowed banks, candidate capacity rejections and costs,
    | chosen assignments, accepted local moves, final byte/cycle/site cut weights, and
    | weighted stack depth to standard error. The `TRAMPOLINES` section then reports
    | the actual generated bridges. This makes placement reproducible and auditable
    | rather than linker astrology.
    | 
    | Superchip RAM through split-address named memory
    | -----------------------------------------------------
    | Superchip storage is allocator-owned named memory, not an absolute external
    | alias. The SC machine/profile header defines the region conceptually as:
    | 
    |     mem cartram {
    |         $read_start:  0xF080
    |         $write_start: 0xF000
    |         $size:        0x80
    |         $rw
    |     };
    | 
    | Applications declare ordinary owned objects with the region modifier:
    | 
    |     cartram uint8_t byte;
    |     cartram uint8_t buffer[32];
    | 
    | Loads relocate through the read alias, stores and initialization through the
    | write alias, and map accounting counts the physical bytes once across all ROM
    | banks. This split-address contract also gives the disassembler a mapper-inference
    | invariant: a 6502 read-modify-write uses one effective address for both its read
    | and write phases, so an RMW targeting either alias of a split RAM region cannot
    | represent an ordinary read-modify-write of that RAM byte. Such a reachable RMW
    | is negative evidence for any mapper whose RAM uses those aliases. Pure reads of
    | the read alias are neutral evidence because a plain ROM mapping may expose ROM
    | at the same CPU address. Superchip, FA/RAM Plus, CommaVid CV, and WD all use this
    | rule; each profile supplies its own read/write ranges.
    | 
    | The current SC cfg profiles reserve each bank's $x000-$x0FF RAM-port prefix and
    | begin allocatable ROM at $x100 while still emitting complete 4K physical banks.
    | In the planned C26 topology model, the `bank` mapping begins at image offset
    | `$0100` and CPU/link address `$x100`; this explicit mapping, not the word
    | `cartram`, keeps the RAM aliases shared and outside selector-controlled ROM.
    | 
    | A non-owning `@[read/write]` absolute external binding may not overlap these or
    | any other allocator-managed windows. The linker compares the binding's complete
    | readable extent with managed read/ordinary windows and its complete writable
    | extent with managed write/ordinary windows. Raw hardware bindings remain valid
    | only in addresses deliberately left outside linker-managed `MEMORY`; storage
    | inside a named region must be declared through that region so the allocator can
    | reserve it.
    | 
    | The source header supplies the region name and aliases so an application does
    | not repeat mapper constants. The linker treats the region as one shared
    | 128-byte physical allocation, not BANK0-owned storage merely because the
    | canonical aliases use the `$Fxxx` mirror. Code in every selected bank may use
    | the same allocated object without a bank switch.
    | 
    | Normal cold-start startup initializes automatically allocated SC DATA/BSS just
    | as it initializes RIOT RAM, but stores through the write window. Bank switching
    | must not disturb SC RAM contents. Warm-reset behavior should follow the normal
    | VCSC startup contract unless a later explicit no-reinitialize entry point is
    | added.
    | 
    | Map, listing, and diagnostics
    | -----------------------------
    | A banked map should report at least:
    | 
    | * mapper/profile name and exact output size;
    | * each bank's logical range, selector hotspot, startup status, physical file
    |   offset, used bytes, free bytes, and reserved bytes;
    | * output order, proving that the lowest logical bank is written first and BANK0
    |   is written last;
    | * every per-bank vector and reset bridge;
    | * the byte-identical common trampoline table, its physical offset, reserved
    |   capacity, occupied and replicated size, inline target-passing ABI, direct-JMP
    |   entries, and later source entries/return stubs;
    | * every generated call/jump target entry, its source bank, destination bank,
    |   target, logical addresses, and byte cost;
    | * bridge corridor occupancy;
    | * weighted hardware-stack depth including cross-bank call edges;
    | * every placement component, its pinned or automatic status, capacity cost,
    |   and weighted call edges cut by the placer;
    | * every rejected or unresolved cross-bank data relocation with source and
    |   target symbols;
    | * Superchip RAM physical occupancy and read/write aliases when present.
    | 
    | Listings and symbol sidecars should retain ordinary 16-bit logical addresses.
    | Where ambiguity matters, they should additionally show the bank name and output
    | file offset. The raw `.bin` remains a contiguous cartridge image in the
    | ascending-logical-address bank order defined above.
    | 
    | Testing strategy
    | ----------------
    | Every implementation slice needs both structural and execution tests.
    | 
    | Structural tests should verify exact image size, bank order, fill bytes, vectors,
    | hotspot reservations, logical symbol addresses, physical file offsets, bridge
    | bytes, deterministic placement, map output, and useful errors. Tests must prove
    | that BANK0 is the final physical bank in F8, F6, and F4 output.
    | 
    | Stella is the authoritative end-to-end execution environment for bank
    | switching and Superchip behavior.  `vcsc-sim` now consumes the public cfg
    | `CARTRIDGE`/`BANKS` descriptions and executes raw F8/F6/F4 images, which gives
    | fast deterministic RAM-signature and forced-start-bank diagnostics.  That
    | VCSC-owned mapper model is not independent evidence: finished cartridge images
    | must also run in Stella's F8/F6/F4 and later F8SC/F6SC/F4SC implementations.
    | 
    | Create a visible diagnostic cartridge, parameterized or separately built for
    | F8, F6, and F4, which:
    | 
    | * is run once with every physical/file bank forced as the initial power-on bank
    |   and also in repeated developer-mode runs with randomized startup-bank state;
    | * resets correctly from every initial bank, enters BANK0, and calls `main` there;
    | * exercises every possible ordered source-bank to destination-bank transition,
    |   including self-selection where useful, through direct selector accesses,
    |   cross-bank JMP, cross-bank JSR/return, and nested cross-bank calls;
    | * writes known source-bank, destination-bank, transition-number, and phase
    |   values into RIOT RAM before and after each switch, and verifies those values,
    |   the restored source bank, and the hardware-stack balance after each return;
    | * renders a distinctive PASS or FAIL sprite for every transition in a stable
    |   frame.  A failure indication should identify at least the source bank,
    |   destination bank, and failed phase so a screenshot is diagnostically useful;
    | * emits a final unmistakable all-pass frame only after every transition has
    |   completed, allowing the existing Stella screenshot/oracle machinery to grade
    |   the actual cartridge;
    | * retains exact frame length and performs mapper changes outside critical
    |   visible timing.
    | 
    | The Superchip versions of the diagnostic must additionally write distinct
    | patterns through the write window, read them back through the read window,
    | carry them across every tested bank transition, and display separate PASS/FAIL
    | results for alias direction, persistence, initialization, and bounds.  Stella
    | must be used to test both bank switching and Superchip operation; a local
    | interpreter that repeats VCSC's own assumptions is not sufficient certification.
    | 
    | Negative tests should cover cross-bank ROM reads, pointers, low/high-byte data
    | relocations, conditional branches, indirect control flow, bridge exhaustion,
    | invalid or duplicate hotspots, overlapping banks, malformed output order,
    | missing per-bank vectors, unknown cfg properties, and ROM placement in an SC
    | RAM-port prefix.
    | 
    | Detailed roadmap
    | ----------------
    | The first unfinished leaf in this file is the next bankswitching-specific action.
    | Main-roadmap Item 42 is complete. OMNI Item 32 can use its generalized automatic
    | CODE/RODATA placement across compatible direct regions; RAM placement remains
    | explicit by design.
    | 
    | [x] 0. Record and lock the initial full-window design decisions.
    | 
    |        * Use descending mirrored logical ranges with BANK0 at $F000.
    |        * Keep ordinary 16-bit symbols and bank-local ROM pointers.
    |        * Reject cross-bank ROM data references.
    |        * Write physical banks from lowest logical address to highest, placing
    |          BANK0 last in the cartridge file.
    |        * Use existing split-address refs as the explicit Superchip baseline.
    | 
    | [x] 1. Extend existing named `mem` region modifiers to function code.
    | 
    |        * Emit a function marked `bank1` into `CODE.bank1` and preserve each
    |          procedure as an independent private o26 layout.
    |        * Resolve `CODE.bank1.__vcsc_function$NAME` through the exact
    |          `CODE.bank1` linker segment rule before falling back to `CODE`.
    |        * Emit and validate the same source mem-region metadata used by objects.
    |        * Require declarations and definitions to agree on their region.
    |        * Reject region modifiers on inline functions and explicit numbered
    |          nonzero-bank placement of `main`.
    |        * Test ordinary and Superchip-sized function regions, including the
    |          correct `$D100+$0E00` allocatable SC interval.
    | 
    | [x] 2. Extend the cfg parser and linker image model for multiple full 4K banks.
    | 
    |        * Add explicit CARTRIDGE mapper/fill metadata, BANKS
    |          start/size/hotspot/startup metadata, and MEMORY `bank=NAME` ownership.
    |        * Replace fixed MEMORY and SEGMENTS tables with dynamic storage and add a
    |          dynamic BANKS table.
    |        * Reject unknown cfg properties, malformed entries, unsupported or
    |          incomplete F8/F6/F4 bank sets, conflicting banks, overlapping bank
    |          MEMORY regions, and non-binary banked output.
    |        * Validate VCSC logical bank name/range, physical file index, and hotspot
    |          as three distinct identities.  The first mapper hotspot selects file
    |          chunk 0; VCSC BANK0 is the final chunk and therefore uses $1FF9 for
    |          F8/F6 and $1FFB for F4.
    |        * Reserve every selector hotspot at the same physical offset in every
    |          bank by rejecting any ordinary allocatable segment region which covers
    |          one; hotspot bytes may not become code or ordinary ROM data.
    |        * Group allocatable regions, reserved holes, and vectors into exact 4K
    |          physical output units.
    |        * Emit bank units in ascending logical-address order with no logical gaps
    |          in the file; prove BANK0 is last.
    |        * Preserve the current unbanked `vcs_4k.cfg` output byte-for-byte.
    | 
    | [x] 3. Add per-bank vectors and same-offset reset bridges.
    | 
    |        * Require a `CARTRIDGE vectorbridge` low-twelve-bit offset and reserve the
    |          same eighteen-byte NMI/RESET/IRQ corridor in every bank.
    |        * Emit byte-identical `BIT BANK0_HOTSPOT; JMP handler` entries and
    |          byte-identical vector words in every selected physical bank.
    |        * Route all vectors through BANK0 before entering `__nmi`, `__reset`, or
    |          `__irqbrk`; keep startup and `main` in BANK0.
    |        * Test all three vectors from every possible initial bank under F8, F6,
    |          and F4, including F4's NMI-vector/selector overlap.
    |        * Reject missing, vector-overlapping, selector-overlapping, or
    |          ordinary-code-overlapping bridge corridors.
    | 
    | [x] 4. Add bank-aware relocation classification and validation.
    | 
    |        * Preserve JSR, JMP, and conditional-branch relocation intent in o26.
    |        * Identify same-bank, shared-memory, and cross-bank references from cfg
    |          bank identities.
    |        * Continue emitting same-bank ROM and shared RAM/hardware references
    |          normally.
    |        * Reject cross-bank branches, data reads, address constants, pointer
    |          initializers, and low/high/word data relocations with source-located
    |          diagnostics.
    |        * This validation slice originally rejected cross-bank direct JMP/JSR
    |          with distinct pending-trampoline diagnostics rather than emitting a
    |          wrong raw mirror address or misclassifying the relocation as data.
    |          Items 5 and 6 now rewrite direct JMP and JSR through the common table.
    |        * Diagnose object, movable source layout, final source address/bank,
    |          target symbol/layout, final target address, and target bank.
    | 
    | [x] 5. Add the byte-identical common trampoline table and cross-bank JMP.
    | 
    |        * Require `CARTRIDGE trampoline` and `trampolinesize`; reserve that
    |          deterministic low-twelve-bit corridor in every bank and reject overlap
    |          with ordinary allocation, vector bridges, vectors, or hotspots.
    |        * Use an eight-byte state-preserving target ABI per deduplicated entry:
    |          `STA destination_hotspot; JMP (inline_target); .word target`, with the
    |          indirect operand encoded through BANK0's logical mirror.
    |        * Redirect each direct cross-bank JMP to the source bank's mirror of the
    |          shared entry offset. Copy the exact occupied table bytes into every
    |          physical bank and leave the rest of the reserved corridor filled.
    |        * Avoid the NMOS indirect-JMP page-wrap bug, deduplicate equivalent
    |          targets, report reserved/occupied/replicated and per-entry costs, and
    |          diagnose corridor exhaustion.
    | 
    | [x] 6. Add JSR-to-indirect-JMP cross-bank calls and return stubs.
    | 
    |        * Redirect a direct cross-bank JSR to a fifteen-byte source-aware target
    |          entry in the common replicated table.
    |        * Use an internal JSR to create the synthetic return address without
    |          clobbering A, X, Y, or flags; select the target bank and enter the
    |          callee with `JMP (inline_target)`.
    |        * Restore the caller's bank with an embedded identical `STA hotspot; RTS`
    |          stub, then RTS through the original call-site return address.
    |        * Deduplicate by source bank, destination bank, and final target; support
    |          nested cross-bank calls and avoid the NMOS indirect-JMP page-wrap bug.
    |        * Add one weighted hardware-stack slot for every active compiler-described
    |          cross-bank edge, reserve two bytes per slot, and report/export the
    |          ordinary and weighted depths. Assembly-hidden calls still require
    |          `callstack_extra`.
    | 
    | [x] 7. Add placement constraints and deterministic automatic bank placement.
    | 
    |        * Pin `main`, startup/non-private runtime layouts, and reserved-name
    |          private runtime functions to BANK0; keep named `mem` modifiers as hard
    |          function/object pins.
    |        * Emit initialized `const` objects in named `$ro` regions as private
    |          `RODATA.region` layouts. Reject mutable definitions and runtime-only
    |          initializers in read-only regions.
    |        * Build indivisible hard components from every cross-bank-forbidden ROM
    |          relocation and retained branch, propagating explicit and mandatory
    |          pins and diagnosing contradictions before final layout.
    |        * Treat direct JSR/JMP relationships as soft edges weighted by their
    |          15-byte/8-byte bridge payload. Reserve fixed ROM/startup-table capacity,
    |          place pins first, then use deterministic size/degree/stable ordering and
    |          minimum incremental cut cost with documented bank tie-breaks.
    |        * Report component identity, pinned versus automatic members, concrete
    |          bank/MEMORY assignment, byte cost, incident cut weight, and resulting
    |          trampoline entries. Diagnose per-bank capacity when a component cannot
    |          fit; preserve final allocator checks for alignment and page constraints.
    |        * Cover deterministic repeated links, capacity spill, hard data affinity,
    |          contradictory pins, `main` residency, generated bridge count, and a
    |          source-compiled named `$ro` object whose unpinned reader follows it.
    | 
    | [x] 8. Add and certify `vcs_8k_f8.cfg`.

    | [x] 10. Make archive selection, listings, map output, simulator execution,

    |        * Teach `vcsc-sim` to parse the same F8/F6/F4 `CARTRIDGE` and `BANKS`

    |        * Add the reusable `bankswitching_diagnostic_suite.c26` plus the editable
    |          `examples/09_bankswitching/01_diagnostic` wrapper.  Emit one cartridge
    |          per mapper rather than one cartridge per transition: F8, F6, and F4.
    |          Each cartridge internally exercises every possible ordered source-bank
    |          to destination-bank transition through direct JMP, performs a same-bank
    |          JSR/RTS check from every source, adds a nested BANK0-to-BANK1 call and
    |          return, writes known RIOT-RAM signatures, verifies stack balance, and
    |          settles on a stable final green-background/white-PASS or dark-red-
    |          background/white-FAIL frame. PASS and FAIL use exact letters copied
    |          from the default ASCII font and the reusable six-glyph-wide score
    |          component, with blank outer cells for horizontal centering and a
    |          90/11/91 visible-line split for vertical centering. The public example
    |          also emits one deliberately poisoned F8SC image and every displayed
    |          result frame is exactly 262 scanlines.

    | [x] 11. Add explicit-binding Superchip profiles.
