    | [x] 12. Add Automatic allocation of variables into Superchip RAM.
    | 
    |        * Define `mem cartram { $read_start:0xF080 $write_start:0xF000
    |          $size:0x80 $rw };` through the SC machine/profile header and allocate
    |          objects with ordinary named-region syntax such as
    |          `cartram uint8_t foo;` and `cartram uint8_t buffer[32];`.
    |        * Lock the existing split-address order: explicit bindings use
    |          `@[read_address/write_address]`, so the first SC byte is equivalent to
    |          `uint8_t foo@[0xF080/0xF000];`.  Loads use the read alias; stores,
    |          initializer copies, and BSS clearing use the write alias.
    |        * Preserve both aliases through compiler metadata, o26, linker layout,
    |          loads, stores, initialization, and map output while treating the region
    |          as shared across all selected banks rather than owned by BANK0.
    |        * Start with persistent globals and arrays.
    |        * Reject address-taking, pointer decay, and unsupported `ref` passing.
    |        * Count physical occupancy once and provide deterministic overflow
    |          diagnostics.
    |        * Implemented compiler metadata carries both aliases through private
    |          `BSS.cartram`/`DATA.cartram` layouts. Unsuffixed direct accesses
    |          retain absolute addressing, direct and runtime array indexing select
    |          the correct alias, and invalid address-taking, decay, `ref`, local,
    |          parameter, function, split-address bitfield-write, and bank-owned
    |          split-region uses fail explicitly.
    |        * The linker allocates shared split storage once at the read alias,
    |          translates startup DATA copies and BSS clearing to the write alias,
    |          exempts both aliases from cross-bank ROM rewriting, validates all cfg
    |          properties, reports both addresses, and names the first overflowing
    |          object deterministically.
    |        * F8SC, F6SC, and F4SC simulator regressions start from every physical
    |          bank and prove initialized DATA, zeroed BSS, dynamic indexing, alias
    |          direction, persistence across calls in every logical bank, exact image
    |          sizes, and one 34-byte physical occupancy report. Authoritative Stella,
    |          installed-toolchain, and reset-lifecycle certification remains item 23.
    |        * Parameters and function return storage remain implementation staging
    |          restrictions, not intended final language semantics. Items 14, 15, and 20
    |          remove those restrictions. Item 13 has removed the local-object
    |          restriction, including function-scope static storage.
    | 
    | [x] 13. Add Superchip-backed local variables.
    | 
    |        * Permit ordinary automatic and function-scope `static` scalars, arrays,
    |          and other already-supported local object forms to select a split-address
    |          writable region, for example `cartram uint8_t scratch;` or
    |          `static cartram uint8_t calls;` inside a function body.
    |        * Keep the existing non-reentrant, symbol-backed automatic-local model.
    |          Reads use the region's read alias; writes and function-entry initializers
    |          use its write alias. No runtime stack or new lifetime mechanism is
    |          required. Function-scope static objects instead use persistent
    |          `BSS.<region>` or `DATA.<region>` storage and the existing one-time
    |          startup initialization machinery.
    |        * Reuse the existing alias-aware lowering for assignment, compound
    |          assignment, increment/decrement, and indexed access. Extend the formerly
    |          rejected split-address bitfield update so it evaluates the lvalue once,
    |          loads through the read alias, and stores through the write alias; never
    |          emit a single-address 6502 read-modify-write for a split-address object.
    |        * Preserve region identity and both aliases through inline-expansion-private
    |          locals where inline locals are otherwise legal, and count each physical
    |          allocation only once in map and overflow reporting.
    |        * Test direct and indexed reads/writes, initializers, nested calls, inline
    |          expansions, cross-bank use, exact map occupancy, deterministic overflow,
    |          and rejection only where the underlying local object form is itself
    |          unsupported.
    | 
    |        Completed 2026-08-04:
    | 
    |        * Automatic scalars, arrays, structures, packed bitfields, and inline-
    |          expansion-private locals may use a split writable region. They retain
    |          fixed non-reentrant backing symbols in function activation layouts.
    |        * Function-entry declaration initializers and every generated store use the
    |          write alias; generated reads use the read alias. The existing alias-aware
    |          assignment/index/increment paths are reused.
    |        * Split bitfield writes now evaluate the destination once, copy the physical
    |          storage byte through the read alias, edit it in compiler scratch, and
    |          store it through the write alias.
    |        * Linker activation zeroing now translates split activation destinations to
    |          the write window. Map accounting counts each physical local byte once.
    |        * F8SC, F6SC, and F4SC execution tests cover direct/runtime indexing,
    |          initializers, compound assignment, increment, bitfields, inline locals,
    |          repeated nested cross-bank calls, exact eleven-byte occupancy, every
    |          physical startup bank, and deterministic activation overflow.
    |        * Function-scope `static` split objects use persistent `BSS.cartram` or
    |          `DATA.cartram`, never activation overlays. Uninitialized storage is
    |          cleared at startup, link-time initializers use the DATA copy path, and
    |          nonconstant initializers run once in the translation-unit startup
    |          initializer; all destinations use the write alias. F8SC/F6SC/F4SC tests
    |          prove persistence across repeated calls and every physical startup bank.
    |        * A mapper-independent regression uses `banana`, `pair`, and `orange`
    |          split regions with different sizes, unaligned and noncontiguous aliases,
    |          and both read-above-write and read-below-write layouts. It proves that
    |          compiler metadata, linker allocation, activation/static storage, startup
    |          initialization, bitfield lowering, simulator aliasing, and overflow
    |          reporting are driven by `mem`/cfg attributes rather than the region name
    |          or a fixed `$F000/$F080` ordering.
    | 
    | [x] 14. Add Superchip-backed value parameters.
    | 
    |        * Permit a value parameter to select the split-address writable region, for
    |          example `void consume(cartram uint16_t value)`.
    |        * Allocate the existing callee-owned parameter object in that region. The
    |          caller writes the argument through the write alias before `JSR`; the
    |          callee reads it through the read alias, and assignments to the parameter
    |          write through the write alias.
    |        * Carry the parameter region and both aliases in function ABI metadata so
    |          calls work identically within one translation unit, across separate
    |          compilation, and through generated cross-bank trampolines. Preserve the
    |          existing non-reentrancy and call-cycle rules for symbol-backed parameters.
    |        * Do not conflate value parameters with ordinary `ref` parameters.
    |          Items 17 through 19 add directional one-address pointers and `ref`
    |          binding rules without introducing a built-in fat-pointer ABI.
    |        * Test one- through four-byte values, BCD values, caller-side expression
    |          evaluation, callee reads and writes, repeated and nested calls, separate
    |          compilation, map accounting, and overflow diagnostics.
    | 
    |        Completed 2026-08-04:
    | 
    |        * Any named split-address writable `mem` region may back a value parameter;
    |          no compiler or linker path recognizes the spelling `cartram`. The
    |          parameter remains an exact-sized callee-owned symbol in the function's
    |          region-local activation layout.
    |        * Direct calls evaluate argument expressions left to right and use
    |          selective caller staging. A converted argument is copied immediately to
    |          its callee-owned object unless it must survive a function call in a later
    |          argument; only those earlier values remain staged until all evaluations
    |          finish. Split parameters use their configured write alias for either
    |          copy path. Callee loads use the read alias and parameter assignments use
    |          the write alias. Inline-expansion-private parameters use the same alias-
    |          aware storage rule without gaining a callable ABI.
    |        * Function signatures and ABI fingerprints now include each value
    |          parameter's region identity; linker-visible fingerprints additionally
    |          carry the read and write starts plus ordinary type representation.
    |          Declarations and definitions therefore cannot disagree about split
    |          storage either within one translation unit or across separate builds,
    |          even when their value widths match.
    |        * Split-address `ref` parameters remain rejected pending item 19, with a
    |          focused diagnostic: a normal pointer cannot represent distinct load and
    |          store addresses.
    |        * F8SC, F6SC, and F4SC tests cover one- through four-byte ordinary and BCD
    |          values, caller-side calls during argument evaluation, callee mutation,
    |          inline expansion, nested and cross-bank calls, every physical startup
    |          bank, exact twenty-one-byte activation occupancy, and deterministic
    |          overflow. A separately compiled mapper-independent test uses `banana`,
    |          `pair`, and `orange` regions with different sizes, noncontiguous aliases,
    |          and both alias orderings; it also proves linker-visible ABI mismatch
    |          diagnostics name the conflicting parameter regions.
    |        * `call_selective_staging.pl` additionally proves call-free argument lists
    |          reserve only one reusable slot sized for the largest argument, while a
    |          nested-call list reserves exactly the values which cross a later call
    |          plus that reusable slot. Existing sibling-overlay execution coverage
    |          proves the reduced staging remains safe.
    | 
    | [x] 15. Add Superchip-backed function return storage.
    | 
    |        * Permit a non-void function's existing hidden `function$__return` object
    |          to select the split-address region, using syntax such as
    |          `cartram uint8_t function(void)`. This extends the existing memory-return
    |          ABI; it does not introduce register returns or a second calling convention.
    |        * Lower `return expression;` to stores through the write alias. Callers read
    |          the result through the read alias after the call. Preserve exact-sized
    |          return storage for every already-supported return width and type which
    |          fits in the selected region.
    |        * Keep `$$` as the source-level lvalue for that same return object. Reads
    |          from `$$` must use the read alias; assignments and compound assignments to
    |          `$$` must use the write alias. A write followed by a read of `$$` inside
    |          the callee must observe the value just written.
    |        * Carry the result region and both aliases through declarations, definitions,
    |          ABI metadata, separate compilation, generated cross-bank calls, map output,
    |          activation layout, and deterministic overflow diagnostics. Item 20 handles
    |          combining result storage with an explicit code-placement region.
    |        * Add execution tests which explicitly write `$$`, read it back, modify it,
    |          use a bare `return;`, and verify the caller receives the final value. Cover
    |          at least one-, two-, three-, and four-byte returns, ordinary and BCD values,
    |          same-bank and cross-bank calls, and all three SC profiles.
    | 
    |        Completed 2026-08-04:
    | 
    |        * Any named writable split-address `mem` region may back the existing
    |          exact-sized hidden `function$__return` object. The implementation derives
    |          the read symbol, write expression, size, and region-local activation
    |          segment from ordinary `mem` metadata; no compiler or linker path recognizes
    |          the spelling `cartram` or assumes a particular alias order or distance.
    |        * `return expression;`, direct assignment to `$$`, and compound assignment to
    |          `$$` store through the configured write alias. Reads of `$$` inside the
    |          callee and result copies in the caller use the configured read alias. A bare
    |          `return;` preserves the value already stored in the object. The epilogue
    |          remains an ordinary `RTS`, and ignored results require no caller copy.
    |        * Split return objects use absolute import/export rather than zero-page
    |          import/export. Function contracts and linker ABI fingerprints carry the
    |          result region plus both window starts, so declarations and definitions
    |          cannot silently disagree within one translation unit or across separate
    |          compilation. A writable result modifier on a `void` function is rejected.
    |        * The return object participates in the selected region's ordinary call-graph
    |          activation overlay and map accounting. Sibling result objects reuse the
    |          same physical bytes, caller/callee paths remain disjoint, read and write
    |          windows count once, and an over-capacity path produces the same deterministic
    |          region-overflow diagnostic as locals and parameters.
    |        * `superchip_function_returns.pl` covers one- through four-byte ordinary and
    |          BCD returns, explicit `$$` write/read/update and bare-return paths, same-bank
    |          calls, generated cross-bank JSR trampolines, every physical starting bank,
    |          both aliases, exact four-byte physical occupancy, and deterministic overflow
    |          under F8SC, F6SC, and F4SC. `split_memory_function_returns.pl` separately
    |          compiles callers and definitions using `banana`, `pair`, and `orange`, with
    |          different sizes, reversed window ordering, noncontiguous aliases, ordinary
    |          integer and pointer results, map/symbol checks, result-region ABI mismatch,
    |          and per-region overflow coverage.
    |        * A split result modifier currently selects only return storage; the function
    |          body's code remains automatically placed. Item 20 adds the independent
    |          code-region/result-region partition needed to combine writable result
    |          storage with an explicit read-only code-placement modifier.
    | 
    | [x] 16. Make `@[read_address/write_address]` imply an absolute external
    |        object binding without `ref`.
    | 
    |        * Accept declarations such as `uint8_t VSYNC @[none/0x00];`,
    |          `uint8_t CXM0P @[0x30/none];`, and
    |          `uint8_t SWCHA @[0x0280/0x0280];`. The address annotation itself means
    |          that the object names pre-existing storage: allocate no bytes, emit no
    |          initializer, and preserve the supplied read and write locations exactly.
    |        * Reserve `ref` for parameter-passing semantics. Migrate the installed
    |          hardware headers, examples, tests, and documentation away from
    |          `ref T object @[read/write]`, then diagnose `ref` on an object definition
    |          or declaration rather than retaining two spellings for the same binding.
    |        * Preserve `none` independently on either side and carry every absolute
    |          binding through the same generic lvalue metadata used by split `mem`
    |          objects. Reads require a read location, writes require a write location,
    |          and read-modify-write operations require both; do not special-case
    |          hardware-register names or the `ref` spelling.
    |        * Reject initializers, allocation modifiers, and other contradictory storage
    |          requests on absolute external bindings. Require compatible redeclarations
    |          and separate-compilation metadata to agree on type and both addresses.
    |        * Add migration and diagnostic tests for readable-only, writable-only,
    |          same-address read/write, and differing-address bindings, including array
    |          and aggregate object declarations where those forms are already legal.
    |        * Implemented file-scope and block-scope `@...` declarations as non-owning
    |          absolute bindings without `ref`; installed TIA, RIOT, and Superchip
    |          headers and all examples now use the shorter spelling. Object-level and
    |          function-level `ref` are diagnosed, while parameter `ref` remains intact.
    |        * Absolute bindings allocate no segment bytes, reject initializers and
    |          storage/linkage/use-contract modifiers, preserve `none` independently,
    |          and retain the existing generic direct/index/member/inline-assembly
    |          read/write lowering. Aggregate members cannot themselves carry an
    |          absolute binding, but aggregate and array objects may.
    |        * Same-translation-unit redeclarations compare canonical numeric addresses
    |          as values rather than spellings. Linker-visible ABI fingerprints record
    |          the object type plus canonical read and write addresses, so separately
    |          compiled declarations with different locations fail deterministically.
    |        * Because installed hardware headers now contribute ABI records for every
    |          absolute binding, the assembler's `.o26` export writer now honors the
    |          format's existing 16-bit export count instead of imposing an unrelated
    |          255-symbol ceiling. This preserves complete metadata even in large generated
    |          overflow fixtures and ordinary translation units which include `vcs.c26`.
    |        * Coverage includes global and local bindings, readable-only, writable-only,
    |          same-address and split-address objects, arrays and aggregate objects,
    |          legacy `ref` diagnostics, contradictory modifiers, forbidden
    |          initialization, compatible redeclarations, and cross-object ABI mismatch.
    |        * Absolute bindings now emit linker-visible range metadata for their full
    |          object size. Before layout, `vcsc-ld` rejects a readable range which
    |          overlaps any configured allocator-managed read/ordinary window and a
    |          writable range which overlaps any configured allocator-managed
    |          write/ordinary window. The check is generic, directional, and covers
    |          block-scope bindings as well as separately compiled file-scope bindings.
    |        * Convert the bankswitching diagnostic's complete Superchip probe from a
    |          non-owning absolute alias to `cartram uint8_t ...[128]`, so the probe
    |          owns and reserves the entire physical region. Keep TIA and RIOT hardware
    |          bindings valid because their addresses lie outside the VCS profiles'
    |          allocator-managed RAM and ROM windows.
    |        * Add ordinary/split/reversed/noncontiguous overlap tests, complete-array
    |          boundary checks, local-binding coverage, and an external-hole cfg for
    |          tests that intentionally model raw helper buffers outside managed memory.
    | 
    | [x] 17. Add `writeonly` as the write-side access qualifier.
    | 
    |        * Define `writeonly T *` as a one-address pointer through which the pointed-
    |          to object may be written but not read. Permit copying and the ordinary
    |          pointer comparison/arithmetic operations VCSC already supports, indexing
    |          used as a store destination, and direct assignment through the pointer.
    |        * Reject every operation which obtains a value through a `writeonly`
    |          lvalue, including ordinary loads, compound assignment, increment/
    |          decrement, and packed-bitfield updates which must preserve surrounding
    |          bits. Keep `const` and `writeonly` mutually exclusive on the same pointee.
    |        * Permit an ordinary readable/writable `T *` to convert implicitly to
    |          `writeonly T *`, just as it may lose write capability when converted to
    |          `const T *`. Reject implicit conversion from either restricted pointer
    |          back to ordinary `T *` or between `const T *` and `writeonly T *`.
    |        * Carry access qualification through declarations, assignments, casts if
    |          supported, function signatures, ABI fingerprints, structure layout, and
    |          separate compilation. The qualifier changes permitted access, not pointer
    |          width: ordinary, `const`, and `writeonly` pointers remain one-address
    |          pointers.
    |        * Add positive store/index/arithmetic tests and focused diagnostics for
    |          hidden reads, forbidden conversions, bitfield writes, qualification
    |          mismatch across declarations, and attempts to combine `const` with
    |          `writeonly`.

    |        Completed 2026-08-04:
    | 
    |        * `writeonly T *` is a real one-address pointer capability. Pointer values
    |          remain two bytes and retain ordinary copy, comparison, indexing, and
    |          arithmetic behavior, while dereference is accepted only as a pure store
    |          destination.
    |        * Generic lvalue access checks reject loads through `writeonly`, stores
    |          through `const`, and hidden reads caused by compound assignment,
    |          increment/decrement, or packed-bitfield preservation. The rules follow
    |          pointer-derived lvalue metadata rather than symbol or region names.
    |        * Ordinary read/write pointers implicitly convert to either `const` or
    |          `writeonly`. Restricted pointers cannot implicitly regain full access or
    |          convert between the read-only and write-only forms. `const writeonly`
    |          and non-pointer `writeonly` declarations are diagnosed.
    |        * Access capability is preserved through locals, globals, expressions,
    |          assignments, initializers, returns, value parameters, aggregate members,
    |          declarations, and separate-compilation ABI fingerprints. Function and
    |          object redeclarations must agree independently of the unchanged pointer
    |          width.
    |        * Compile-only coverage exercises stores, indexing, arithmetic, comparison,
    |          every forbidden read/conversion, aggregate and function mismatches, and
    |          file-scope pointer expressions. End-to-end execution proves generated
    |          stores and the two-byte representation; linker failures prove pointer and
    |          aggregate ABI qualification mismatches across translation units.

    | [x] 18. Add explicit read- and write-address projection operators `&<` and `&>`.
    | 
    |        * Define `&<lvalue` to return the lvalue's read address as `const T *`, and
    |          `&>lvalue` to return its write address as `writeonly T *`. Evaluate the
    |          lvalue location once and do not load from or store to the object merely to
    |          form either address.
    |        * Lex `&<` and `&>` as indivisible multi-character unary operators with
    |          the same precedence and associativity as ordinary unary `&`. Require the
    |          characters to be adjacent: `& <foo` and `& >foo` are not alternate
    |          spellings, and neither spaced form creates a competing unary-address
    |          interpretation.
    |        * Drive both operators entirely from generic lvalue read/write metadata.
    |          `&<` is an error when the read location is `none`; `&>` is an error when
    |          the write location is `none`. This applies equally to named `mem` objects,
    |          absolute bindings such as `uint8_t VSYNC @[none/0x00];`, array elements,
    |          structure members, and dereferenced lvalues.
    |        * Make no assumption about region spelling, window order, contiguity,
    |          distance, alignment, or size. For an ordinary one-address object the two
    |          projected pointer values may be numerically equal while retaining their
    |          different access qualifications.
    |        * Keep plain `&` for objects which have one conventional address compatible
    |          with the resulting ordinary pointer type. Reject plain `&` when distinct
    |          read and write locations cannot be represented by one pointer, and direct
    |          the diagnostic to `&<` and `&>`. Keep implicit decay of a split-address
    |          array invalid; use an explicit directional projection such as `&<array[0]`
    |          or `&>array[0]`. Expressions such as `&<*p` and `&>*p` remain valid
    |          compositional spellings when the dereferenced lvalue has the required
    |          access path.
    |        * Do not add a built-in split/fat pointer in this slice. A program which must
    |          retain both addresses may place the two projections in an ordinary user-
    |          defined structure. Leave a language-defined paired-pointer type deferred
    |          until a concrete use case justifies its ABI and optimizer cost.
    |        * Test ordinary, read-only, write-only, same-address, reversed-window, and
    |          noncontiguous split objects using unrelated fruit-named regions and
    |          explicit absolute bindings. Verify `none` diagnostics and ensure the
    |          generated pointer is the exact configured read or write address.

    |        Completed 2026-08-04:
    | 
    |        * Add adjacent unary `&<` and `&>` tokens at ordinary address-of precedence.
    |          `&<` returns the selected lvalue read address as `const T *`; `&>`
    |          returns its write address as `writeonly T *`. Spaced `& <` and `& >`
    |          forms remain ordinary tokens and are rejected rather than reinterpreted.
    |        * Lower both operators through generic lvalue metadata for ordinary,
    |          split-region, absolute, indexed, member, and pointer-derived lvalues.
    |          Runtime location expressions are evaluated once, and constant
    |          initializers retain symbolic alias relocations and offsets without an

    | [x] 19. Extend one-address `ref` parameters with directional access capability.

    | [x] 29. Improve automatic placement heuristics without changing semantics.
    | 
    |        * Keep `optimized` as the default and add `simple` as an explicit stable
    |          input-order/capacity baseline. Both modes preserve pins, hard ROM-data
    |          components, replicated-data locality, and deterministic bank tie-breaks.
    |        * Extend soft-edge measurements with static site counts and bridge-form
    |          weights: each JSR site contributes the 15-byte payload and 25-cycle
    |          one-execution penalty; each JMP site contributes 8 bytes and 6 cycles.
    |          Optimize lexicographically by byte weight, cycle weight, then site count.
    |          Runtime-frequency profiles were not invented because no such evidence
    |          exists in o26. Final actual entries remain authoritative in `TRAMPOLINES`.
    |        * Add a deterministic fixed-point local pass. It moves only one automatic
    |          hard component at a time, never exceeds capacity, and refuses any cut
    |          improvement which increases weighted hardware-return depth. Equal-cut
    |          moves are accepted only when they reduce that depth.
    |        * Add `--explain-bank-placement`, including component members, pins and
    |          allowed banks, every candidate's capacity/cut result, chosen assignments,
    |          accepted or rejected local candidates, and final byte/cycle/site cut
    |          weights and stack metrics. The map records `mode=optimized` or
    |          `mode=simple`.
    |        * The ordering fixture measures 2 JSR bridges, byte weight 30, cycle
    |          weight 50, and weighted depth 6 in simple mode versus 1 bridge, byte
    |          weight 15, cycle weight 25, and depth 5 when optimized. The local-move
    |          fixture measures 4 bridges with byte/cycle weights 60/100 versus 1
    |          bridge with weights 15/25, with weighted depth unchanged at 5 and all
    |          pins intact. A third fixture rejects a 60-to-30 byte-weight improvement
    |          because it would raise weighted depth from 5 to 7. Repeated optimized
    |          links are byte-for-byte and map-for-map deterministic, and omitted mode
    |          exactly matches explicit `optimized`.
    |        * The maintained standard-renderer F8/F6/F4 builds are byte-identical under
    |          simple and optimized modes, retain one cross-bank JSR and weighted depth
    |          4, and retain 30/60/120 replicated bridge bytes respectively. That is the
    |          correct result: item 28 deliberately hard-pins its architectural split,
    |          leaving no heuristic freedom to improve.

    | 30. Mapper-family policy gate — not an unfinished implementation item.
    | 
    |        This entry is a trigger condition and a list of guardrails. There is no
    |        current action while no concrete program requires unsupported hardware
    |        semantics.
    | 
    |        * Do not build a universal mapper-description language merely to reproduce
    |          every historical circuit. Full-window selector-controlled banks, direct
    |          banks, shared memory, and explicit replication are the maintained model.
    |        * If a real program eventually requires independently switched windows,
    |          value-controlled selection, banked RAM, or another unsupported behavior,
    |          open a new scoped roadmap item for that program and design that
    |          family separately rather than distorting the current bank abstraction
    |          in advance.
    |        * Reuse logical mapping, vector, output, relocation, and reporting pieces
    |          where honest; add a true window or device model only when the chosen
    |          hardware semantics actually require one.
    |

    |          from either physical bank, certify nested calls/returns, explicitly test
    |          alias read/write switching, and verify byte-exact disassembly round trip.
    |        * Added `vcsc-disas` UA/UASW inference and exact selector-flow semantics.
    |          VCSC `UA\0\0` and `UASW` signatures disambiguate the swapped variant;
    |          established legacy UA access patterns infer ordinary UA.
    |        * Added profile-matrix, installation, editable-example, assembly-review,
    |          documentation, and source-hygiene coverage.
    | 
    | [x] 37. Add 0FA0.
    | 
    |        * Reuse below-window selector support for the two-bank masked/aliased 0FA0
    |          selector behavior. Keep address-mask semantics explicit rather than
    |          expanding every alias into an ad-hoc hotspot list.
    | 
    |        Completed 2026-08-17:
    | 
    |        * Added `vcs_8k_0fa0.c26` and `vcs_8k_0fa0.cfg`. Physical/file bank 1 is
    |          the hardware startup bank, so VCSC logical `bank0` maps to file index 1
    |          and canonical `$0FC0`; logical `bank1` maps to file index 0 and `$0FA0`.
    |        * Modeled the actual masked selector decoder explicitly: `(A & $16E0)`
    |          equal to `$06A0` selects physical bank 0 and `$06C0` selects physical
    |          bank 1. A11, A8, and A4-A0 remain aliases rather than being expanded into
    |          an ad-hoc selector list. Reads and writes retain their underlying
    |          console-side access before the mapper side effect.
    |        * Reused the state-preserving absolute-NOP read for generated below-window
    |          bank transitions. Replicated reset bridges recover to startup bank 1
    |          even when a simulator/debugger begins with physical bank 0 selected.
    |        * Added public `09_bankswitching/10_0fa0`, with big-wide `pass`/`FAIL`, a
    |          small `0FA0` label, nested cross-bank call/return certification, and a
    |          non-taken Stella detector sequence. Focused tests exercise both initial
    |          banks and masked read/write aliases including `$07A7` and `$0ECF`.
    |        * Added `vcsc-disas` 0FA0 inference, startup-bank and selector-flow
    |          semantics, byte-exact round trip, profile/install/example/assembly-review
    |          coverage, and documentation. The regression pins `$16E0` so the stale
    |          `$16A0` prose found in one external header cannot replace the executable
    |          hardware model accidentally.
    | 
    | [ ] 38. Reconsider the next tier: F0, FA2, and FC. **DEFERRED.**
    | 
    |        * Deferred by user request on 2026-08-17; do not schedule this item after
    |          Item 37 unless the user explicitly reopens it.
    |        * Evaluate F0 sequential next-bank switching, FA2's larger FA-like bank/RAM
    |          layout plus flash-save hardware, and FC's staged selector protocol after
    |          the simpler descriptor-level families above are complete. Open separate
    |          implementation items only for the parts whose hardware contracts fit VCSC
    |          cleanly; do not hide new mechanisms behind the existing hotspot model.
    | 
    | 
    | [ ] 39. Backfill an E0 public diagnostic cartridge.
    | 
    |        * Add the minimum compiler/linker/simulator profile support needed to
    |          build E0 deliberately rather than hand-authoring a disassembler-only
    |          fixture.
    |        * Add a self-checking `examples/09_bankswitching` PASS/FAIL diagnostic
    |          that exercises all three selectable 1K windows plus the fixed top 1K.
    |        * Force Stella's E0 mapper in `make play`, and require simulator/Stella
    |          execution plus byte-exact `vcsc-disas` round trip.
    | 
    | [ ] 40. Backfill a 3F public diagnostic cartridge.
    | 
    |        * Add a real variable-size 3F output profile using the value-written TIA
    |          selector semantics already modeled by `vcsc-disas`.
    |        * Exercise at least two lower 2K banks and the fixed final 2K in a
    |          self-checking public PASS/FAIL cart; force `-bs 3F` for Stella play.
    |        * Pin exact disassembly/reassembly and mapper-detection behavior.
    | 
    | [ ] 41. Backfill a 3E public diagnostic cartridge.
    | 
    |        * Build on the 3F profile, adding `$3E` RAM-bank selection and the split
    |          lower-window RAM aliases without pretending `$3F` alone proves 3E.
    |        * Exercise ROM->RAM->ROM transitions in a public PASS/FAIL cart and test
    |          both simulator behavior and exact disassembly round trip.
    | 
    | [ ] 42. Backfill an FE / SCABS public diagnostic cartridge.
    | 
    |        * Add a profile capable of expressing FE's delayed `$01FE` stack-bus
    |          latch rather than faking it as an F8-style hotspot.
    |        * Use the released-cart JSR switching idiom in a self-checking diagnostic,
    |          force Stella `-bs FE`, and certify return-to-caller bank behavior.
    | 
    | [ ] 43. Backfill a WD public diagnostic cartridge.
    | 
    |        * Add/build the WD four-segment arrangement profile and 64-byte split RAM
    |          semantics as first-class output support, including the known dump-order
    |          distinction where applicable.
    |        * Exercise multiple selector arrangements and RAM in a public PASS/FAIL
    |          diagnostic with simulator/Stella and byte-exact disassembler coverage.
    | 
    | [ ] 44. Backfill a DPC public diagnostic cartridge.
    | 
    |        * Add a buildable DPC profile only after the data-fetcher/register model
    |          needed by a meaningful diagnostic is available; do not ship a ROM that
    |          merely has DPC's file size.
    |        * Exercise real DPC data-ROM/data-fetcher behavior in a self-checking
    |          PASS/FAIL diagnostic and require simulator/Stella plus exact
    |          disassembly/reassembly coverage.
    | 
    | 
    | Decisions intentionally deferred
    | --------------------------------
    | The following are not settled by this document:
    | 
    | * whether cfg remains a supported generic escape hatch after all public VCS
    |   profiles migrate, or becomes test-only compatibility code;
    | * whether a future self-describing image container is warranted beyond the
    |   current deterministic flat concatenation of declared output-bank chunks;
    | * the exact size/location of the common bridge corridor beyond the currently
    |   certified full-window values;
    | * whether non-public or future mapper profiles may use a startup bank other
    |   than BANK0 while still keeping public full-window profiles and `main` in BANK0;
    | * whether a concrete use case eventually justifies a built-in split/fat
    |   pointer rather than explicit `&<`/`&>` projections stored in a user-defined
    |   structure;
    | * whether future measured programs justify profile-guided dynamic edge weights,
    |   explicit same-bank groups, or translation-unit preferences beyond the completed
    |   deterministic greedy-plus-local optimizer.
    | 
    | Those choices should be made in the roadmap slice that can implement and test
    | them, not guessed here and fossilized without evidence.


2026-08-22 20:43:03 PDT, archive pre-cleanup disassembler.txt

    Cold snapshot of .../disassembler.txt immediately before the context-trimming pass.
    The maintained hot copy was then reduced to durable current state and unfinished work.

    | VCSC DISASSEMBLER CONTEXT AND ROADMAP
    | =====================================
    | 
    | Purpose
    | -------
    | Create a standalone Atari 2600 cartridge disassembler named `vcsc-disas`.
    | The implementation will live in a new top-level `disassembler/` directory.
    | Start that directory by copying `stego/` and then rename/adapt the program and
    | Makefile rather than inventing another command-line/program skeleton from
    | scratch.
    | 
    | The primary output is VCSC assembly source (`.s26`). Given a cartridge binary,
    | `vcsc-disas` must emit source that the existing `vcsc-as` assembler can consume
    | to reproduce the original cartridge image byte-for-byte exactly.
    | 
    | Human readability, reverse-engineering usefulness, and good inference are major
    | goals, but exact reconstruction is the governing invariant. When those goals
    | conflict, exact bytes win.
    | 
    | This file is the focused design/context document and ordered roadmap for the
    | side project. It is intentionally separate from `.../roadmap.txt`; work here
    | does not advance or reorder the main VCSC roadmap.
    | 
    | Status legend: [ ] not started, [~] partial, [x] complete.
    | 
    | Current implementation status (2026-08-20)
    | ------------------------------------------
    | The first implementation is now usable and exact-round-trip driven. It builds
    | `disassembler/vcsc-disas` and the standalone `disassembler/roundtrip.pl`. The
    | implemented foundation includes all 256 opcode bytes, exact addressing-mode
    | emission, mandatory branch `.same`/`.cross`, recursive bank-aware control flow,
    | unbanked 1K/2K/4K, F8/F6/F4 plus Superchip variants, FA, DPC and WD, per-bank origin inference, overlapping
    | instruction streams, code-as-data, established/speculative confidence, and
    | definite/possible/unclassified ROM-use roles,
    | canonical and mirrored TIA/RIOT symbolization, conservative sprite rows, static
    | video/controller inference, manual mapper/origin/entry/code/data/video/controller
    | hints, deterministic output, and exact raw-byte fallback for uncertain regions.
    | A disassembly that finds zero instructions is a hard error; a 100%-data dump is
    | not considered successful analysis.
    | 
    | For Superchip cartridges, the physical low `$100` bytes of each 4K bank remain in
    | the emitted image exactly but are marked as hidden by the RAM window and excluded
    | from ordinary ROM code/data discovery. Mapper overrides control this behavior: a
    | forced SC mapper enables it and a forced plain F8/F6/F4 mapper suppresses it.
    | 
    | Permanent regressions cover all 256 opcodes, all eight branch opcodes in both page
    | classes, forward-branch relaxation edges, deterministic arbitrary-ROM fuzz,
    | BIT-skip and generic overlaps, instruction-as-data, known indirect reads,
    | unreferenced ROM, TIA/RIOT mirrors and RMW accesses, mapper/Superchip inference,
    | video/controller fixtures, manual overrides, deterministic output, and exact
    | reassembly. A 16-ROM real VCSC corpus including PAL, SECAM, controller, score,
    | F8/F6/F4, F8SC/F6SC/F4SC, FA, DPC and WD cartridges also round-trip exactly through the
    | standalone verifier.
    | 
    | Items still marked partial are intentionally broader than the current first
    | implementation. Speculative instruction-island discovery now rejects newly
    | speculative paths that can reach HLT/JAM/KIL, records three-rejected-start
    | sequential-flow barriers, and uses straight-line JAM endpoints plus the mature
    | `stego` credibility filters before promoting a lower-confidence island; graphics
    | recognition can still grow beyond strong GRP/PF provenance; video inference
    | now covers maintained and arbitrary known TIM64T values, parameterized timer-helper
    | call sites, broad counted-WSYNC kernels, and filename region hints, but can still
    | grow into a lightweight dynamic frame probe for kernels whose scanline count is
    | not statically recoverable; richer
    | pointer/color/table recognition remains useful; manual known-pointer/table hints
    | are not yet present; and final hardening still needs broad arbitrary-ROM
    | fuzz/corpus coverage and an exercised Windows cross-build. These are improvements
    | to analysis breadth, not exceptions to byte-exact output.
    | 
    | Core invariant: exact binary round trip
    | ---------------------------------------
    | For every accepted ROM there must be a mechanically testable path:
    | 
    |     original.bin
    |         -> vcsc-disas
    |     original.s26
    |         -> vcsc-as direct assembly
    |     reconstructed.bin
    |         -> byte-for-byte comparison with original.bin
    | 
    | The comparison includes file length and every byte. A SHA-256 comparison may be
    | reported as a convenience but does not replace a `cmp`-equivalent comparison.
    | 
    | If a real ROM contains `JMP ($xxFF)`, preserve those three bytes as raw data in
    | the emitted source. `vcsc-as` intentionally rejects that mnemonic form because
    | of the NMOS 6502/6507 page-wrap hazard; raw spelling is therefore required for
    | exact reconstruction of cartridges that deliberately rely on the silicon bug.
    | 
    | Inference must never break this invariant. If a mapper, origin, instruction,
    | data type, label, pointer interpretation, controller type, video standard, or
    | other semantic interpretation is uncertain, preserve the original bytes and
    | state the uncertainty in comments. A completely unfamiliar cartridge must be
    | able to degrade to an exact `.byte` representation rather than becoming
    | undisassemblable.
    | 
    | The disassembler must be deterministic: identical input plus identical command
    | line must produce byte-for-byte identical `.s26` output.
    | 
    | Do not normalize ROM images. Duplicated banks, padding, unused fill, inaccessible
    | physical bytes, unusual vector contents, and other oddities are part of the
    | input image and must survive the round trip exactly.
    | 
    | Generated-file header
    | ---------------------
    | Every generated `.s26` file should begin with deterministic comments summarizing
    | at least:
    | 
    |     input filename
    |     input byte size
    |     input SHA-256
    |     inferred mapper/bankswitching scheme and confidence
    |     physical bank layout
    |     inferred logical origin for each bank and confidence/evidence
    |     inferred power-on/reset bank
    |     inferred video standard and confidence
    |     inferred controller type for each port and confidence
    |     important ambiguities or unsupported hardware assumptions
    |     vcsc-disas version
    | 
    | For 50 Hz software, report PAL-versus-SECAM ambiguity honestly when the ROM does
    | not contain enough evidence to distinguish them. Stable 50 Hz timing by itself
    | is PAL-family evidence, not proof of PAL rather than SECAM.
    | 
    | Video inference should accumulate evidence rather than depend on a short list of
    | VCSC scheduler constants. In particular, use arbitrary statically-known RIOT
    | timer values, timer arguments passed through small helper subroutines, broad
    | counted-WSYNC loop lengths, and explicit NTSC/PAL/SECAM filename tokens as
    | secondary evidence. Timer/scanline ranges are heuristics and must retain honest
    | confidence levels. Filename hints must use token boundaries and must never outrank
    | contradictory strong timing evidence. A future dynamic frame probe may execute a
    | small bounded model of the cartridge when static timing remains inconclusive; it
    | must not become necessary for exact-byte disassembly.
    | 
    | Output-address model
    | --------------------
    | Keep physical cartridge-file position separate from the 6507 logical/runtime
    | address. This is the same distinction required by `.../bankswitching.txt`.
    | 
    | Use assembler `.org` to describe physical output position in the reconstructed
    | cartridge image and `.rorg`/`.rend` to describe the inferred logical CPU address
    | for a physical bank. Multiple physical banks can therefore be emitted in file
    | order while each is analyzed in its runtime address space.
    | 
    | Conceptually:
    | 
    |     .org $0000
    |     .rorg $F000
    |         ; physical bank 0
    |     .rend
    | 
    |     .org $1000
    |     .rorg $F000
    |         ; physical bank 1
    |     .rend
    | 
    | The origins above are examples only. Do not impose VCSC's own bank origins on a
    | foreign ROM. Historical cartridges often map all physical banks to the same
    | runtime window, but other layouts exist.
    | 
    | The 6507 exposes only A0-A12 to the cartridge. A13-A15 can therefore differ in
    | encoded 16-bit operands without changing the physical cartridge-bus address.
    | Preserve the original 16-bit operand value. Do not replace a mirrored address by
    | a prettier canonical address if doing so changes either operand byte.
    | 
    | Origin inference
    | ----------------
    | Infer the most plausible logical origin independently for every physical bank.
    | Use all available constraints together rather than treating the reset vector as
    | the only source of truth.
    | 
    | Strong evidence includes:
    | 
    | * RESET/NMI/IRQ vector values and whether they land at plausible offsets in a
    |   physical bank.
    | * Absolute `JMP $hhhh` targets.
    | * Absolute `JSR $hhhh` targets.
    | * Resolved indirect-JMP destinations.
    | * Absolute ROM data references whose physical target can be identified.
    | * Bank-switch transitions followed by control transfer into the selected bank.
    | * Internal consistency of branches, labels, tables, and decoded control flow.
    | * Common cartridge-window mirrors, used as priors rather than proof.
    | 
    | Absolute JMP opcodes deserve aggressive use as origin evidence. If a `$4c`
    | occurs at file offset O and its encoded destination can plausibly refer to offset
    | T in a bank, the destination immediately constrains that bank's runtime origin.
    | Do not require the JMP itself to have already been proven RESET-reachable before
    | using it as an origin hypothesis. A coincidental `$4c` in data is evidence only,
    | not proof, so score hypotheses by the number of independent constraints they
    | satisfy.
    | 
    | Apply the same logic to direct JSR destinations. For `JMP (addr)`, the operand is
    | the pointer address, not the destination. Only a statically resolved indirect
    | JMP destination constrains the destination bank's origin.
    | 
    | When resolving NMOS 6502/6507 `JMP ($xxFF)`, model the original silicon page-wrap
    | behavior: the high destination byte is fetched from `$xx00`, not `$xx+1:00`.
    | 
    | Do not compare raw counts of unresolved/dynamic control exits between mapper
    | hypotheses. A wrong mapper can make the graph look deceptively clean by mapping
    | execution into an early RTS or otherwise truncating reachable code. Exit counts
    | remain useful diagnostics within one analysis, but they are not positive mapper
    | evidence. When multiple mapper hypotheses remain viable without discriminating
    | selector/flow evidence, retain the ordinary size/signature default rather than
    | choosing whichever hypothesis decoded less code.
    | 
    | A bankswitch access followed by a JMP/JSR into the newly selected bank is
    | particularly valuable because it can constrain the destination bank directly.
    | Solve mapper state, reset bank, and per-bank origins together rather than
    | assuming they are independent.
    | 
    | All supported cartridge bank placements preserve 256-byte page alignment. That
    | is a durable invariant for branch annotation: uncertainty in a bank's absolute
    | high-byte origin cannot change whether a relative branch is same-page or
    | cross-page, because the origin shift cannot alter the low eight address bits.
    | 
    | If several complete origin assignments remain equally plausible, report the
    | ambiguity and choose a deterministic representation that still reassembles
    | exactly. Origin ambiguity is never a reason to omit `.same`/`.cross` on a branch.
    | 
    | Mapper inference
    | ----------------
    | Infer bankswitching from behavior and layout rather than file size alone. File
    | size is useful evidence, not proof.
    | 
    | Initial mapper support should cover the ordinary full-window family already
    | understood by VCSC:
    | 
    |     unbanked 2K / 4K
    |     F8 / F8SC       8K
    |     F6 / F6SC      16K
    |     F4 / F4SC      32K
    |     FA             12K + 256-byte RAM
    |     WD              8K corrected image + 64-byte RAM
    |     WDSW            8K+3 historical swapped-bank preservation dump
    | 
    | Use a scored/evidence-based classifier. Evidence includes:
    | 
    | * File size and candidate 2K/4K bank boundaries.
    | * Exact duplicated/padded-bank patterns, without stripping them.
    | * Executed or referenced F8/F6/F4 selector hotspots.
    | * Which physical bank contains a plausible reset path.
    | * Per-bank vectors and reset/vector bridge patterns.
    | * Superchip read/write-window accesses. Neither half alone is sufficient:
    |   semantic promotion requires established resolved use of both the write alias
    |   `$x000-$x07F` and the read alias `$x080-$x0FF`. The accesses need not use the
    |   same RAM-byte offset. Speculative islands do not vote on mapper hardware.
    |   Explicit VCSC SC signatures and the canonical per-bank duplicated hidden
    |   window remain independent raw-image evidence.
    | * Whether the resulting bank-aware control-flow graph is coherent.
    | * For every size-compatible supported mapper, trace only RESET-established code
    |   under that mapper's real selector semantics until the established graph reaches a
    |   fixed point. Detached speculative islands are excluded from mapper viability,
    |   selector counts, ranking, and contradictions. Eliminate hypotheses whose possible
    |   established execution reaches HLT/JAM/KIL, and prefer surviving hypotheses that
    |   explain established decoded selector accesses. Raw detector byte strings are supporting or
    |   tie-break evidence only; bytes spanning unrelated instructions must not select a
    |   mapper when executable control flow contradicts that interpretation.
    | * Whether JMP/JSR/data-reference constraints agree with the mapper's bank
    |   windows and inferred origins.
    | 
    | Track the power-on/reset bank separately from mapper type. Knowing that a ROM is
    | F8 does not by itself identify which physical bank supplies RESET.
    | 
    | Mapper state is part of control-flow state. An access to a selector hotspot can
    | change which physical bank supplies subsequent instruction fetches at the same
    | runtime address. Reads, writes, and read-modify-write operations must be modeled
    | according to the mapper's actual hotspot semantics. A physical old-bank byte after
    | the selector is not the successor instruction if the access selected another bank;
    | for example, `LDA $hotspot` followed by old-bank JAM/KIL is a valid execution line
    | when the new bank supplies a valid instruction at the continuation PC. This rule
    | applies equally to primary tracing and speculative-island validation.
    | 
    | FA is an explicit CBS RAM Plus model rather than an F8 variant. A 12288-byte
    | image consists of three 4K physical banks selected by `$1FF8-$1FFA`; physical
    | bank 2 is the hardware power-on bank. The 256-byte cartridge RAM write port
    | occupies `$1000-$10FF` and the read port `$1100-$11FF`, so the first `$200`
    | physical bytes of each bank are preserved exactly but excluded from ROM
    | code/data discovery. The 6507's 13-bit external address bus means valid mirrored
    | bank origins need not be `$F000`; commercial FA code may intentionally use
    | origins such as `$3000`, `$5000`, and `$7000`.
    | 
    | WD is an explicit Wickstead Design model for Pursuit of the Pink Panther rather
    | than an F8 variant. Runtime ROM is eight 1K banks arranged four at a time across
    | the cartridge window. Reads from TIA `$30-$3F` request one of eight fixed bank
    | arrangements; writes to those TIA addresses remain ordinary TIA writes. WD also
    | has 64 bytes of cartridge RAM, read at `$1000-$103F` and written at
    | `$1040-$107F`. Power-on arrangement 0 maps logical banks `0,0,1,3` into
    | `$1000,$1400,$1800,$1C00`. Stella distinguishes the two known dump layouts by
    | name while instantiating the same hardware model: `WD` is the corrected 8192-byte
    | image, detected by the `A5 39 4C` (`LDA $39; JMP`) signature; `WDSW` is the
    | historical 8195-byte 8K+3 preservation image. In WDSW, logical 1K banks 2 and 3
    | are reversed in the file and the last three bytes are outside the emulated 8K
    | ROM. Analysis must use the corrected logical ordering while source emission
    | preserves the original file ordering and all three trailing bytes exactly.
    | Because the selector change is hardware-delayed, static control flow should
    | conservatively retain both the old and requested successor arrangements when
    | exact cycle placement is not proven.
    | 
    | E0 is an explicit segmented Parker Brothers model. An 8K image is eight physical
    | 1K banks. Runtime `$1000-$13FF`, `$1400-$17FF`, and `$1800-$1BFF` independently
    | select any physical bank via `$1FE0-$1FE7`, `$1FE8-$1FEF`, and `$1FF0-$1FF7`;
    | `$1C00-$1FFF` is permanently physical bank 7 and supplies the vectors. The
    | deterministic reset configuration is physical banks 4,5,6,7. E0 configuration
    | must travel with the CFG state, and both established tracing and speculative
    | island validation must fetch the instruction after a selector from the newly
    | selected physical bank. Detached E0 islands are promoted conservatively only
    | when their speculative path actually exercises an E0 selector, because a bare
    | physical 1K chunk has no unique runtime segment/configuration. 2026-08-20 corpus
    | refinement: on 8K images, established E0 detector evidence keeps Stella's E0-before-E7
    | precedence over an otherwise unsignatured E7 hypothesis. This prevents a wrong E7
    | mapping from manufacturing `$FFE4-$FFE6` selector traffic in Gyruss/Popeye-family
    | images whose real startup path begins with a static-analysis-resistant RAM-clear loop.
    | An explicit VCSC E7 signature or genuine E7 detector evidence still identifies E7.
    | 
    | Do not rank mapper hypotheses by raw hotspot count. Decoder alias breadth differs
    | radically among mapper families (0840 is the concrete failure case), so a false
    | mapper may appear to have more selector evidence merely because more addresses
    | alias its hotspots. A bank transition that avoids an old-mapping HLT/JAM/KIL and
    | continues with valid code in the new mapping is strong positive evidence.
    | 
    | Do not pretend that unrelated schemes are variants of F8/F6/F4. 3F, 3E, E7,
    | FE, DPC+, CDF, and coprocessor cartridges need separate mapper models. DPC has its own explicit model: its distinctive 10240/10495-byte layout
    | contains two 4K F8-style executable banks followed by 2K of DPC data ROM and, in
    | the standard 10495-byte dump, a 255-byte RNG table. File size is decisive evidence
    | for this historical format.
    | 
    | A successful disassembly must identify at least one executable instruction. If
    | analysis finds zero instructions, emit an error and do not create an `.s26` file.
    | Do not claim that an unsupported mapper was successfully disassembled merely
    | because its bytes can be reproduced as `.byte` directives. Raw-byte emission is
    | still the exactness fallback for uncertain regions of a supported, nonempty code
    | analysis.
    | 
    | Analysis model: byte roles are non-exclusive
    | --------------------------------------------
    | Do not classify each ROM byte with a single `code` versus `data` enum. Real 6502
    | software can deliberately use the same physical bytes in multiple ways.
    | 
    | A byte may simultaneously be:
    | 
    |     start of one decoded instruction
    |     operand of one or more decoded instructions
    |     part of another overlapping instruction stream
    |     read as ROM data
    |     sprite/font data
    |     pointer/vector/table data
    |     mapper metadata/hotspot-adjacent data
    |     possibly read through an unresolved dynamic access
    |     otherwise unreferenced
    | 
    | Store semantic roles independently and retain provenance/evidence for each role.
    | At minimum distinguish definite versus heuristic executable reachability, direct
    | versus possible data reads, overlapping instruction participation, and truly
    | unreferenced bytes.
    | 
    | Physical ROM layout has exactly one emitted representation. Analysis may attach
    | multiple semantic interpretations to those bytes, but the `.s26` source must not
    | emit the same physical bytes twice merely to show two views of them. Use labels,
    | aliases, and comments to expose alternate interpretations.
    | 
    | Control-flow discovery
    | ----------------------
    | Seed definite executable control flow from plausible vectors and user-supplied
    | entry hints, then recursively follow:
    | 
    |     fallthrough
    |     all eight conditional branches
    |     JMP
    |     JSR and RTS
    |     resolvable indirect JMP
    |     provable RTS/stack dispatch idioms
    |     mapper-changing accesses and their successor bank states
    | 
    | Reuse/adapt useful abstract-analysis machinery from `stego`, including constant
    | register tracking, zero-page pointers, stack-built destinations, RTS-based
    | dispatch, and table-driven indirect control flow. Do not change `stego`'s public
    | behavior as part of this work; copied code can be factored later only if doing so
    | simplifies both tools and preserves independent regression coverage.
    | 
    | If control flow enters RIOT RAM, Superchip RAM, or another writable region, do
    | not invent ROM instructions. Follow dynamically generated/copied code only when
    | both the written bytes and execution destination are provable. Otherwise end
    | that static path with an explanatory annotation.
    | 
    | A second-pass instruction-island detector may identify plausible unreachable
    | utility code, but heuristic islands must remain lower-confidence than vector- or
    | control-flow-reachable code. Random data happens to decode as legal 6502 very
    | often; legal decoding alone is not meaningful evidence of execution.
    | 
    | Speculative island validation and negative evidence
    | ---------------------------------------------------
    | Use HLT/JAM/KIL opcodes as strong negative evidence when discovering otherwise
    | unreferenced instruction islands. This is a speculative-code rule, not a claim
    | that a real cartridge can never intentionally execute a CPU-locking opcode. If
    | normal vector/control-flow analysis has already proved a HLT/JAM/KIL reachable,
    | preserve it as executable code. Do not invalidate established code merely
    | because it deliberately halts the processor.
    | 
    | For each otherwise unknown candidate instruction-start address in each mapped
    | 4K cartridge bank/window, perform a speculative control-flow decode beginning at
    | that byte. At minimum:
    | 
    |     ordinary fallthrough       -> continue at the next instruction
    |     conditional branch         -> explore every statically reachable successor
    |     JSR                        -> explore the call target and return continuation
    |     RTS / RTI                  -> successful path termination
    |     unconditional JMP          -> successful transfer/termination for the local
    |                                   linear island; follow the target as appropriate
    |     previously proven code     -> successful path termination
    |     newly speculative HLT/JAM/KIL
    |                                -> reject the candidate flow
    | 
    | Abstract-state facts matter. The shared analyzer tracks A/X/Y plus C/Z/N/V/D
    | where they are statically known, so every conditional branch can prune an arm
    | when its controlling flag is proved. If one arm is provably unreachable, a
    | HLT/JAM/KIL on that impossible arm does not reject the candidate.
    | If both outcomes remain possible, every possible executable arm must be safe; a
    | possible path into HLT/JAM/KIL rejects that speculative candidate.
    | 
    | Do not turn rejection into an exclusive byte classification. A candidate start
    | can be rejected while a later byte in the same range is independently proven to
    | be an instruction start by a branch, JMP, JSR, vector, mapper transition, or
    | other established entry. This remains compatible with overlapping instruction
    | streams and code-as-data.
    | 
    | Retain rejected candidate starts as first-class negative evidence. The longest
    | 6502/6507 instruction is three bytes, so three consecutive addresses that have
    | all been proven not to be instruction starts form a sequential-flow barrier:
    | no ordinary fallthrough instruction stream can cross that barrier. Explicit
    | control transfers may still jump over the barrier or enter code on either side,
    | so a barrier does not by itself prove that all later bytes are data.
    | 
    | Use these barriers to strengthen second-pass island and unused-ROM analysis.
    | For performance, first build one physical-ROM speculative control-flow graph and
    | reverse-walk it from every HLT/JAM/KIL byte. Mapper-selector edges cross to the
    | selected physical bank before the next opcode fetch; ordinary branch/JSR edges
    | remain bank-local. This cheaply marks the only unknown
    | starts that can structurally reach a halt. Run the more expensive stateful C/Z/N/V
    | validation only for those starts; a start outside that reverse-reachable set cannot
    | be rejected by the halt rule. Branch-state analysis still makes the final decision,
    | so a structurally reachable but provably dead halt arm remains safe. This prefilter
    | must preserve the same established-code/JMP/JSR/local-termination semantics as the
    | stateful walk.
    | 
    | The implementation bounds one stateful speculative candidate walk by the physical
    | bank size in static CFG-node visits, replacing the old arbitrary 512-node constant.
    | This is not emulated CPU time: loops are cycle-detected, so a runtime loop that
    | executes for thousands of frames may consume only a handful of graph-node visits.
    | Hitting the cap is explicitly reported, is inconclusive, and can never supply positive
    | evidence for speculative-code promotion. The strictly linear JAM/KIL endpoint scan
    | has no artificial node cap because it always advances and is bounded by the physical
    | bank. A future state-keyed worklist/fixed-point validator should replace the remaining
    | computational guard entirely. Every three consecutive rejected starts are a
    | sequential-flow barrier.
    | For the stronger claim that an entire interval through a JAM/KIL endpoint is
    | non-code, require all three rejected starts to reach their halt through pure
    | straight-line fallthrough (a proved not-taken branch may remain on that line).
    | Only that stronger barrier span supplies an automatic island boundary.
    | 
    | A candidate after such a span is still not code merely because it avoids JAM.
    | Adapt `stego`'s second-pass credibility checks: reject conflicts with established
    | code/data roles, require a coherent multi-instruction trace with a credible
    | terminal or join, require at least 80% official NMOS 6502 instructions with very
    | few unofficial opcodes, and treat an unresolved indirect JMP as weak rather than
    | a sufficient terminal. Prefer inbound branch/JMP/JSR references or meaningful
    | internal control flow; random legal-opcode runs must remain raw. Never let this
    | heuristic override definite control flow, overlaps, or definite code-as-data.
    | 
    | Overlapping instruction streams
    | -------------------------------
    | The analyzer must support branch/JMP/JSR targets that enter the middle of an
    | instruction decoded along another path. That does not invalidate either path.
    | Both instruction instances can be real and reachable.
    | 
    | A canonical example is the common three-byte BIT-skip trick:
    | 
    |     CheckStatus:
    |         LDA STATUS_REG
    |         BNE.same LoadAlternative
    |         LDX #$01
    |         .byte $2c
    |     LoadAlternative:
    |         LDX #$02
    |         STX player_speed
    | 
    | On the linear path the bytes `$2c $a2 $02` execute as `BIT $02A2`, consuming the
    | `LDX #$02` bytes. On the taken branch, execution begins at `$a2`, so those same
    | bytes execute as `LDX #$02`.
    | 
    | Internally this must be represented as two overlapping instruction instances,
    | not as a decoder error. A control-flow target landing inside an already decoded
    | instruction is strong evidence for an additional instruction start.
    | 
    | For generated source, prefer a representation such as `.byte $2c` immediately
    | before the alternate-entry label when spelling the outer `BIT` mnemonic would
    | prevent placing a label on the inner opcode byte. Annotate the fallthrough
    | interpretation in comments. General support must not be hard-coded only for
    | `$2c`; hand-written 6502 can overlap streams in other ways.
    | 
    | Instruction bytes and operands read as data
    | -------------------------------------------
    | Cartridges may intentionally read their own instruction bytes or operand bytes
    | as data. The analyzer must preserve both meanings simultaneously.
    | 
    | For example, an immediate operand may be used as a table constant elsewhere:
    | 
    |     Foo:
    |         LDA #$42
    |         RTS
    | 
    |     ...
    |         LDA Foo+1
    | 
    | `Foo+1` remains an operand of executable code and is also a definite ROM-data
    | read target. Data evidence must never cause the executable interpretation to be
    | removed, and executable decoding must never hide a proven data reference.
    | 
    | When possible, generated labels/aliases should make such self-references clear
    | without duplicating physical bytes. If no elegant source spelling exists, keep
    | the exact primary byte representation and explain the additional data use in a
    | comment.
    | 
    | ROM-read and unused-byte analysis
    | ---------------------------------
    | Track reads from cartridge ROM separately from instruction fetches. Direct
    | absolute/indexed accesses with known bounds can mark definite data ranges.
    | Abstract-state analysis should narrow indexed and indirect accesses as far as it
    | can.
    | 
    | Use at least these usage states for reporting:
    | 
    |     definitely executed/fetched on discovered paths
    |     definitely observed/provable read as data
    |     executed and read as data
    |     possibly referenced/read by unresolved dynamic accesses
    |     unclassified by the analysis performed so far
    | 
    | Do not call the residual set `unreferenced` merely because current control-flow and
    | data-flow analysis failed to classify it. Concrete RESET exploration may miss
    | input-dependent paths, and unresolved indexed/indirect accesses may cover broad
    | mapper-dependent ranges. If an unresolved access could reach a region, mark it
    | `possible`; otherwise the residual category is `unclassified`, not a proof of
    | non-use. A future proof strong enough to establish truly unused ROM may add a
    | separate `proven-unused` role, but absence of evidence is not that proof.
    | 
    | Concrete execution must retain physical ROM reads that are not opcode/operand fetches
    | and feed them back into the definite data-read role. Mapper-aware unresolved reads
    | must conservatively map their possible logical address ranges into physical ROM. Do
    | not force-decode residual bytes merely because their bit patterns form legal opcodes.
    | Emit exact `.byte` data for bytes not represented as established code.
    | 
    | The analysis summary reports byte counts for executed, data-read, exec+data, overlap,
    | possibly referenced, and unclassified regions. These roles are evidence classes, not
    | a claim that every cartridge byte has been proven reachable or unreachable.
    | 
    | Instruction emission
    | --------------------
    | For bytes represented primarily as code, emit normal 6502 mnemonics whenever the
    | current `vcsc-as` opcode table and addressing-mode syntax reproduce the exact
    | original opcode and operands.
    | 
    | Support all 256 opcode bytes. For unofficial/ambiguous encodings where a normal
    | mnemonic would not uniquely preserve the byte, use the assembler's exact `opXX`
    | spelling and the correct addressing-mode suffix/operand form. Never silently
    | replace one opcode encoding by a semantically equivalent different opcode.
    | 
    | Preserve the original addressing mode explicitly when symbolization or normal
    | relaxation could cause a zero-page/absolute choice to change. Avoid redundant
    | suffix noise: ordinary named zero-page instructions normally omit `.z`, `.zx`,
    | and `.zy` because resolved 8-bit operands naturally select those modes. An
    | original absolute/absolute-indexed instruction whose resolved operand is below
    | `$0100` must retain `.a`, `.ax`, or `.ay` so it cannot relax to the shorter
    | encoding. Raw `opXX` spellings retain whatever explicit mode suffix the assembler
    | requires for an otherwise ambiguous operand shape. Other suffixes such as `.i`,
    | `.ix`, and `.iy` remain available where needed for exact reconstruction.
    | 
    | Branch page annotations
    | -----------------------
    | Every emitted conditional branch instruction must carry an explicit hard timing
    | annotation:
    | 
    |     .same   when the taken branch stays on the page containing PC+2
    |     .cross  when the taken branch crosses that page boundary
    | 
    | Never emit a bare branch or `.flex` from `vcsc-disas`.
    | 
    | Emit the exact numeric runtime target as the branch operand, even when analysis
    | has also generated a label at that destination. Put the deterministic target
    | label in a comment when useful. This avoids a `vcsc-as` forward-label relaxation
    | corner at original positive displacements +125 through +127: the assembler's
    | provisional long-branch form temporarily moves a forward label three bytes away,
    | which can otherwise prevent relaxation back to the original short branch. A
    | numeric target is fixed during relaxation and therefore preserves the original
    | two-byte branch exactly without changing the assembler.
    | 
    | This classification is always determinable for a decoded branch. Relative
    | branch displacement gives the target low-byte relationship directly, and every
    | supported cartridge mapping/origin shift preserves 256-byte page alignment.
    | Absolute origin uncertainty therefore cannot change `.same` versus `.cross`.
    | 
    | The comparison is between the page of PC after the two-byte branch instruction
    | and the page of the branch target, matching NMOS 6502/6507 taken-branch timing.
    | Test all eight branch opcodes in both same-page and cross-page cases, including
    | branches near `$xxFE/$xxFF` boundaries and bank origins that differ by page-
    | aligned amounts.
    | 
    | TIA and RIOT symbolic register accesses
    | ---------------------------------------
    | Generated assembly should use the project's canonical Atari hardware register
    | names rather than unexplained numeric addresses whenever doing so can preserve
    | the original instruction bytes exactly.
    | 
    | The canonical vocabulary is currently defined by:
    | 
    |     libraries/vcs/tia.c26
    |     libraries/vcs/riot.c26
    | 
    | TIA examples include:
    | 
    |     VSYNC VBLANK WSYNC RSYNC
    |     NUSIZ0 NUSIZ1 COLUP0 COLUP1 COLUPF COLUBK CTRLPF
    |     PF0 PF1 PF2 RESP0 RESP1 RESM0 RESM1 RESBL
    |     AUDC0 AUDC1 AUDF0 AUDF1 AUDV0 AUDV1
    |     GRP0 GRP1 ENAM0 ENAM1 ENABL
    |     HMP0 HMP1 HMM0 HMM1 HMBL HMOVE HMCLR CXCLR
    |     CXM0P CXM1P CXP0FB CXP1FB CXM0FB CXM1FB CXBLPF CXPPMM
    |     INPT0 INPT1 INPT2 INPT3 INPT4 INPT5
    | 
    | RIOT examples include:
    | 
    |     SWCHA SWACNT SWCHB SWBCNT INTIM TIMINT
    |     TIM1T TIM8T TIM64T T1024T
    | 
    | The current assembler expands `.include` source text but does not make ordinary
    | constant assignments from an included file available afterward in the way a
    | generated disassembly would need. Do not change assembler semantics merely for
    | the disassembler. `vcsc-disas` therefore emits the canonical TIA/RIOT equates it
    | actually needs into each generated `.s26`, derived from the same canonical names
    | in `libraries/vcs/tia.c26` and `riot.c26`. The output remains self-contained and
    | round-trips with the existing assembler.
    | 
    | Symbolization must preserve exact encoded addressing mode and operand value. For
    | example, if `COLUBK` is `$09`:
    | 
    |     original two-byte zero-page access -> use `COLUBK` with `.z` as needed
    |     original three-byte absolute $0009 -> use `COLUBK` with `.a`
    | 
    | Do not let the assembler shorten an original absolute access into zero page.
    | 
    | TIA and RIOT are mirrored in the VCS address space. If the ROM intentionally
    | uses a noncanonical mirror, do not rewrite the encoded address to the canonical
    | one. Prefer an exact symbolic expression such as a canonical register plus the
    | appropriate mirror offset when the assembler can encode that exact numeric
    | operand; otherwise retain the numeric operand. In either case, every preserved
    | noncanonical mirror access must carry an end-of-line or immediately adjacent
    | comment naming the canonical hardware register actually selected by the decoded
    | bus address and access direction, for example `; mirror of COLUBK ($09)` or
    | `; mirror of SWCHA ($0280)`. The original operand bytes remain authoritative.
    | 
    | Choose symbols according to access semantics, not address alone. TIA read and
    | write register sets differ. Loads should name the appropriate readable TIA
    | register; stores should name the appropriate writable register. Read-modify-
    | write instructions or unusual mirrored/open-bus accesses may not have one clean
    | symbolic name; preserve the exact operand and annotate both effects rather than
    | inventing a misleading symbol.
    | 
    | Hardware-register recognition feeds higher-level analysis. SWCHA/INPT accesses
    | are controller evidence; VSYNC/VBLANK/WSYNC/timer accesses are video/frame
    | evidence; GRP0/GRP1/PF0/PF1/PF2 data flow is graphics/sprite evidence; mapper
    | hotspots remain mapper semantics rather than ordinary hardware-register data.
    | 
    | Data emission
    | -------------
    | Bytes not primarily represented as instructions must still be readable and
    | exact. Use labels for proven ROM-data targets and deterministic names for
    | anonymous targets.
    | 
    | Emit `.word` only when a two-byte region is proven to be a little-endian pointer
    | or vector and symbolic assembly reproduces the exact original 16-bit value.
    | Otherwise use bytes. Never rewrite a mirrored pointer value to a prettier
    | bus-equivalent address.
    | 
    | A symbolic reference may point into the interior of an emitted source container.
    | This includes instruction operands and the high byte of a two-byte vector `.word`.
    | Such an interior byte cannot own a standalone source label. Promote the label to
    | the emitted container boundary and reference the exact byte as `label + offset`.
    | For example, an IRQ vector containing `$FFFF` in a 4K `$F000` cartridge may be
    | emitted as `L_FFFE: .word L_FFFE + 1`; never emit an unresolved `L_FFFF` merely
    | because analysis marked `$FFFF` as a target.
    |
