    | Vector-table bytes are not inherently data-only. Commercial cartridges can
    | deliberately execute bytes at `$xFFA-$xFFF`, including entering at the high byte
    | of a vector word. If either byte of a candidate vector `.word` is established as
    | an executable instruction start, the `.word` container must yield to the normal
    | overlapping-code emitter so every executable entry can own a real source label.
    | Preserve the vector meaning in a comment, but emit the executable bytes/instructions
    | as the primary physical representation. Only use the container-relative
    | `label + offset` form when the vector remains an indivisible emitted `.word`.
    | 
    | Do not reinterpret arbitrary tables just because a higher-level type would be
    | possible. Conservative `.byte` output is always acceptable and safer than a
    | wrong semantic guess.
    | 
    | Sprite/font visualization
    | -------------------------
    | Recognize conservative sprite/font/playfield byte tables when a short,
    | straight-line dependency path carries a ROM load into a TIA graphics write.
    | Track source bytes loaded into A/X/Y through register transfers and simple ALU
    | or accumulator-shift transforms, and recognize STA/STX/STY writes to
    | GRP0/GRP1/PF0/PF1/PF2. Runtime-indexed tables may be bounded by a proven
    | countdown or by the next known code/vector/label boundary; do not carry graphics
    | provenance across calls, branches, unknown raw opcodes, or unrelated loads.
    | 
    | For sprite-like bytes emit exactly one assembler-native binary byte per source
    | line and put the X/dot picture beside it, for example:
    | 
    |     .byte %00011000    ; ...XX...
    |     .byte %00111100    ; ..XXXX..
    |     .byte %01100110    ; .XX..XX.
    | 
    | The current `vcsc-as` binary literal syntax is `%[01]+`; it does not accept X or
    | dot characters inside a numeric literal. `X` in the comment means a set bit and
    | `.` means a clear bit. This preserves the human-scannable form without changing
    | the assembler language merely for the disassembler.
    | 
    | One byte per line is intentional so humans can visually scan the glyph. Preserve
    | byte order exactly; do not vertically reverse, bit-reverse, rotate, trim blank
    | rows, or otherwise improve the art.
    | 
    | Use sprite formatting only when evidence is reasonably strong. Numeric lookup
    | tables, random constants, call-mediated/compressed-looking graphics, and ordinary
    | data should not be turned into fake pictures merely because some rows look
    | attractive. A mask or transformed table that is directly proven to feed PF0/PF1/
    | PF2 is graphics data and should use visual rows; a merely mask-shaped byte table
    | without that provenance should stay numeric.
    | 
    | Video-standard inference
    | ------------------------
    | Infer NTSC versus PAL-family behavior and include the result in the generated
    | header with confidence/evidence.
    | 
    | Evidence can include:
    | 
    | * VSYNC/VBLANK/WSYNC structure.
    | * RIOT timer values and frame scheduling.
    | * Stable frame-line count inferred statically or by optional emulation.
    | * Audio timing constants when recognizable.
    | * Color-value usage as weak supporting evidence only.
    | 
    | Prefer static analysis, but use VCSC/Stella/simulator machinery for an optional
    | dynamic measurement pass when static evidence is insufficient and execution is
    | safe/deterministic enough.
    | 
    | Do not claim that 312-line/50 Hz timing distinguishes PAL from SECAM. If the ROM
    | cannot prove the distinction, report `PAL-family (PAL/SECAM ambiguous)` or
    | similar. Explicit user override remains available.
    | 
    | Controller inference
    | --------------------
    | Infer the expected controller independently for each port and report confidence.
    | Initial models should include:
    | 
    |     joystick
    |     paddles
    |     keypad
    |     driving controller
    |     unused
    |     unknown/ambiguous
    | 
    | Evidence includes:
    | 
    | * SWCHA bit usage and direction masks.
    | * INPT0-INPT5 reads.
    | * VBLANK paddle discharge/charge behavior.
    | * keypad row-drive/column-read patterns.
    | * driving-controller quadrature patterns.
    | * fire-button reads.
    | * timing loops associated with analog paddle reads.
    | 
    | Mixed-port cartridges are valid. Do not collapse both ports to one type merely
    | because one port is obvious. Multiple plausible controller interpretations may
    | be reported when static evidence is insufficient.
    | 
    | Labels and comments
    | -------------------
    | Generate deterministic labels for definite control-flow targets, ROM-data
    | targets, vectors, tables, and bank-local entry points. Bank-qualified names are
    | required where the same runtime address exists in multiple physical banks.
    | 
    | Prefer semantic hardware symbols over generic labels for TIA/RIOT accesses, as
    | described above.
    | 
    | Comments should expose useful alternate interpretations without becoming a
    | trace dump. Particularly valuable comments include:
    | 
    | * inferred mapper transitions.
    | * overlapping instruction interpretations.
    | * instruction bytes/operands also read as data.
    | * unreferenced/unused ROM runs.
    | * sprite/font confidence.
    | * ambiguous origins/controllers/video standard.
    | * dynamic-code transfers static analysis cannot follow.
    | 
    | Provide a separate verbose analysis/report mode for detailed evidence scores so
    | normal `.s26` output remains readable.
    | 
    | Manual overrides and analysis hints
    | -----------------------------------
    | Static analysis cannot perfectly recover every deliberately tricky ROM. Give the
    | human precise overrides rather than forcing them to edit generated source after
    | the fact.
    | 
    | Plan for options/hints covering at least:
    | 
    |     mapper
    |     reset/power-on bank
    |     per-bank logical origin
    |     video standard
    |     controller type per port
    |     bank-qualified entry points
    |     forced code ranges
    |     forced data ranges
    |     known generic-table and little-endian pointer-table ranges
    | 
    | Contradictory hints must produce diagnostics rather than silently corrupting the
    | analysis. Forced code/data roles still do not have to be exclusive: an advanced
    | hint should be able to say that a range is executable and also read as data.
    | 
    | Unknown/unsupported mapper handling
    | -----------------------------------
    | Do not report a 100%-raw file as a successful disassembly. If the mapper is
    | unknown and no executable instructions can be established, fail with a clear
    | `no instructions found` diagnostic and do not create `.s26` output. This keeps
    | `roundtrip.pl` honest: byte reproduction alone is not proof that disassembly
    | succeeded.
    | 
    | Within a supported mapper, or once executable code has otherwise been established,
    | uncertain physical regions may still be emitted conservatively as raw `.byte`
    | data to preserve exact reconstruction. Do not fake bank origins or branch targets
    | that depend on unsupported mapping. Any branch actually decoded as an instruction
    | still receives its mandatory `.same`/`.cross` annotation.
    | 
    | Command-line conventions
    | ------------------------
    | `vcsc-disas` must follow the command-line usage/help conventions established by
    | the other public VCSC tools rather than preserving `stego`'s current minimal
    | one-line interface. In particular:
    | 
    | * `-h` and `--help` print the complete usage/options text and exit successfully.
    | * `-V` and `--version` print version information and exit successfully, using
    |   the same generated project version mechanism as the other tools.
    | * An unsupported option reports the offending option and then prints the same
    |   guidance used elsewhere in VCSC:
    | 
    |       Try 'vcsc-disas --help' for a list of supported options.
    | 
    |   Use the actual invocation name (`argv[0]`) in the implementation rather than
    |   hard-coding `vcsc-disas` into that diagnostic.
    | * Missing option arguments and other command-line errors use the same concise
    |   diagnostic style and nonzero exit behavior as the other VCSC tools.
    | * The help text documents every supported option, normal input/output naming,
    |   important inference overrides, and at least one ordinary invocation example.
    | * Command-line regressions must pin help, version, unsupported-option guidance,
    |   missing-argument diagnostics, and exit status so this interface cannot drift.
    | 
    | Standalone corpus round-trip verifier
    | -----------------------------------------
    | Provide a standalone executable Perl development utility at
    | `disassembler/roundtrip.pl` with a normal Perl shebang. It is deliberately
    | separate from the normal `test/` directory and test-runner inventory so it can
    | be pointed at arbitrary external cartridge corpora without copying those ROMs
    | into the VCSC source tree.
    | 
    | Its command line is exactly two positional arguments:
    | 
    |     perl disassembler/roundtrip.pl INPUT_DIR OUTPUT_DIR
    | 
    | An incorrect argument count prints a concise usage line and exits nonzero.
    | 
    | Required behavior:
    | 
    | * `INPUT_DIR` and `OUTPUT_DIR` must resolve to different directories; reject a
    |   configuration that could overwrite the source cartridges.
    | * Create `OUTPUT_DIR` if it does not exist.
    | * Enumerate regular files whose names end in `.bin` directly in `INPUT_DIR`, in
    |   deterministic lexical order. Do not recurse into subdirectories unless a
    |   future option explicitly requests recursion.
    | * For each `NAME.bin`, run `vcsc-disas` on the input cartridge and write
    |   `OUTPUT_DIR/NAME.s26`.
    | * Assemble that generated source with the project `vcsc-as` and create a raw
    |   `OUTPUT_DIR/NAME.bin` containing the reconstructed cartridge image.
    | * The current assembler writes final direct assembly as Intel HEX rather than a
    |   raw `.bin`. The standalone script should therefore invoke `vcsc-as` with an
    |   explicit temporary/output `.hex`, decode that Intel HEX itself in Perl, and
    |   write the raw `.bin`. This is a verifier implementation detail, not a reason
    |   to change `vcsc-as` or the generated `.s26` language.
    | * Remove or replace stale per-ROM output before processing so a failed command
    |   can never accidentally compare an old reconstructed file. Temporary files
    |   should not masquerade as successful output.
    | * Compute and print the MD5 digest of both the original and reconstructed
    |   cartridge for every case. Use Perl's MD5 support rather than depending on an
    |   external `md5sum` executable so the script remains self-contained.
    | * Any MD5 mismatch is a failure. Also perform an exact size/byte comparison so
    |   the project's stronger byte-for-byte round-trip invariant remains the final
    |   authority even if two MD5 values happen to match.
    | * Optionally accept `--stella STELLA` and compare the mapper in generated source
    |   with `STELLA -rominfo ROM`'s resolved Bankswitch Type. This is a differential
    |   diagnostic, not an oracle: Stella's result combines its MD5-keyed property
    |   database with its own autodetector, so a disagreement must be surfaced rather
    |   than silently deciding which implementation is right. `--stella-strict` may
    |   promote mapper disagreement to command failure for an explicitly requested
    |   zero-difference gate.
    | * Keep SHA-256 in the generated source header. MD5 exists in the corpus verifier
    |   specifically for Stella/database interoperability and round-trip reporting; do
    |   not replace the stronger source-identity digest with MD5.
    | * A `vcsc-disas` failure, assembler failure, malformed Intel HEX, missing output,
    |   size mismatch, byte mismatch, or digest mismatch must mark that ROM failed.
    | * Continue through the whole input directory after an individual failure, print
    |   a concise PASS/FAIL line for each ROM, print final totals, and exit nonzero if
    |   any ROM failed. Exit zero only when every discovered `.bin` round-trips
    |   exactly. Treat an input directory containing no `.bin` files as an error.
    | * Handle spaces and shell metacharacters in filenames safely; use Perl list-form
    |   process invocation rather than constructing shell command strings.
    | * By default locate the development-tree `disassembler/vcsc-disas` and
    |   `assembler/vcsc-as` relative to the script/repository rather than relying on
    |   the caller's current directory. If overrides are later added, document them
    |   and keep the no-option two-directory invocation working.
    | 
    | The output corpus is intentionally useful after a run: each successful input
    | keeps both the human-readable `.s26` and the reconstructed same-named `.bin` for
    | manual inspection or emulator testing.
    | 
    | Roadmap
    | -------
    | 
    | Immediate hybrid-analysis priorities (2026-08-20)
    | 
    | H0. [x] Add bounded concrete RESET discovery
    |    * Reuse the maintained `simulator/mos6502` CPU core rather than maintaining a
    |      second interpreter. Execute from the real RESET vector with exact PC/A/X/Y/SP/P.
    |    * Model 6507 mirroring, all 128 RIOT RAM bytes including their stack-page mirrors,
    |      RIOT timer behavior needed by ordinary polling loops, TIA WSYNC/VSYNC CPU-side
    |      effects, deterministic cartridge banking for the initially supported simple
    |      mapper families, and disconnected controllers/console inputs high. Unsupported
    |      concrete mapper models must cleanly fall back to static analysis.
    |    * Bound execution and stop on CPU halt, a top-level return, a hard instruction
    |      limit, or a long no-new-instruction convergence interval. Record cartridge
    |      instruction starts, RAM writes, and dynamically executed RIOT-RAM instructions.
    |    * Track write provenance into RIOT RAM so code copied/generated from cartridge ROM
    |      can be related back to physical input bytes. Present executed RAM code in a
    |      comment-only block so exact cartridge reconstruction remains unchanged.
    |    * Preserve static-analysis precision: let the ordinary CFG drain first, then seed
    |      only concrete cartridge instruction starts that static analysis did not already
    |      reach. Concrete observations are positive reachability evidence, never a reason
    |      to erase stronger abstract facts.
    |    * Require a trusted cartridge topology before concrete execution. Unbanked
    |      1K/2K/4K, explicit `--mapper`, and strong banked-mapper tail/detector signatures
    |      are trusted; weak size-default F8/F6/F4/FA guesses are not. This prevents an
    |      unsupported layout that merely survives static mapper refinement from generating
    |      fake concrete code under the wrong hotspot model.
    |    * Regression-pin SP-sensitive `TSX/PHA`, BRK/IRQ/RTI saved-PC mutation, ROM-to-RAM
    |      copying, and execution from the copied RAM payload. Fix `TSX` abstract flag
    |      semantics so unknown X also makes N/Z unknown.
    | 
    | H1. [x] Make the static CFG stack/interrupt/input aware and explore alternate paths
    |    * Add SP to abstract state and model stack effects/data for PHA/PLA, PHP/PLP,
    |      JSR/RTS, BRK/RTI, and stack-page/RIOT-RAM aliasing where values are provable.
    |    * Use concrete observations as seeds while retaining both sides of branches whose
    |      condition depends on controller, console-switch, timer, collision, paddle, or
    |      other external/unknown input. Connect provable BRK/IRQ/RTI continuations instead
    |      of treating every BRK as an unconditional CFG terminator.
    |    * Extend the concrete bus model to the remaining supported non-coprocessor mapper
    |      families and add additional deterministic input scenarios only when an executed
    |      path reads that input family. DPC remains static until its data-fetcher/register
    |      model is faithful, as already required by the video-analysis contract.
    | 
    |    Completed 2026-08-20:
    |    * SP is now abstract state. Provable RIOT-RAM/stack-page aliases survive PHA/PLA,
    |      PHP/PLP stack movement, JSR/RTS, stores, and supported RMW operations. Optimistic
    |      call continuations do not assume SP preservation; exact SP can return through a
    |      proven RTS edge.
    |    * Known branch flags prune impossible edges, while external/unknown input reads
    |      leave both branch successors live. A bounded local IRQ trace proves BRK/RTI
    |      continuations only when the saved stack return address and interrupt path remain
    |      exact; it gives up rather than guessing through unknown branches or mapper changes.
    |    * Concrete discovery semantically exhausts all eight startup combinations of the
    |      maintained SWCHB COLOR/BW and left/right difficulty switches with SELECT/RESET
    |      released. The all-high run comes first; if it never reads SWCHB, the other seven
    |      settings are provably execution-equivalent and are counted as covered without
    |      redundant emulator runs. Once SWCHB is observed, all seven remaining combinations
    |      execute explicitly. When SWCHB is actually read, SELECT and RESET are each exercised
    |      independently under every maintained-switch combination. Video programs use their
    |      observed VBLANK assertion edges as the clock: ten frames released, ten frames pressed,
    |      then ten frames released again. Controller
    |      SWCHA/INPT inputs remain demand-driven one-active-low scenarios. Observations
    |      are unioned as positive reachability evidence.
    |    * The concrete cartridge bus now covers all supported non-coprocessor families:
    |      1K/2K/4K, F8/F6/F4/FA/SC, CV, WD/WDSW, FC, E0, E7, 3F, 3E, FE, JANE, 0840,
    |      UA/UASW, and 0FA0. It models segmented mappings, native split RAM, FC staging,
    |      delayed WD selection, and FE's post-$01FE data-bus latch. DPC remains excluded.
    |    * The synthetic H1 fixture combines a known-SP BRK/IRQ/RTI return with a SWCHA-gated
    |      ROM-to-RIOT loader. The real c33caa7d6ac7251bb804e0473198901d.bin now reaches
    |      its loader under an alternate SWCHA scenario and recovers 57 executed RIOT-RAM
    |      instruction starts with ROM provenance while preserving exact 1K reconstruction.
    | 
    | H2. [x] Iterate concrete and static discovery to convergence
    |    * Feed newly proven static entry states and dynamically discovered targets back into
    |      one another without turning sampled execution into proof that alternate paths are
    |      impossible.
    |    * Keep mapper state, copied/generated RAM contents, indirect targets, and provenance
    |      attached to each discovered execution context; stop at a deterministic fixed point.
    | 
    |    Completed 2026-08-20:
    |    * Static-to-concrete continuation is deliberately proof-gated: A/X/Y/SP, modeled
    |      C/Z/N/V/D flags, and all 128 RIOT-RAM bytes must be exact at the entry. No unknown
    |      register/RAM byte is filled with a guessed value just to make emulation proceed.
    |    * Exact static seeds carry PC, current physical bank, mapper configuration, CPU state,
    |      and the complete RIOT-RAM image into the maintained MOS6502 concrete engine. New
    |      concrete ROM/RAM execution is unioned with existing positive evidence and missing
    |      ROM starts are fed back to the abstract CFG. Newly decoded static paths may produce
    |      another exact seed; iteration stops at a deterministic fixed point with hard bounds
    |      of 64 unique static seeds and 8 feedback rounds.
    |    * Static-to-concrete seeds are enabled only for mapper contexts whose complete dynamic
    |      state is represented by the abstract model: unbanked 1K/2K/4K, plain F8/F6/F4, FC,
    |      E0, 3F, JANE, 0840, UA/UASW, and 0FA0. FA/CV/WD/E7/3E/Superchip cartridge RAM and
    |      FE's transient post-$01FE latch remain concrete-RESET-to-static only until those
    |      extra state components can be supplied exactly. This is conservative incompleteness,
    |      not guessed execution.
    |    * A permanent H2 fixture makes RESET concrete execution park on one TIA-read outcome,
    |      lets static analysis find the alternate path, establishes a fully exact CPU+RIOT
    |      state there, copies ROM code into RAM, and requires fixed-point feedback to execute
    |      that RAM payload. A separate console-switch fixture requires all three maintained
    |      switches low plus a SELECT press and release, pinning the eight startup combinations
    |      and momentary-switch event scenarios.
    | 
    | 0. [x] Establish `disassembler/` and exact raw round trip
    |    * Copy `stego/` to a new top-level `disassembler/` starting point.
    |    * Rename/adapt the program to build `vcsc-disas`.
    |    * Add `-h`/`--help`, `-V`/`--version`, input, and output handling using the
    |      established VCSC command-line conventions above, including the standard
    |      unsupported-option pointer to `--help`.
    |    * Add command-line regression coverage for help/version/error text and exit
    |      status; do not inherit `stego`'s minimal one-line usage behavior.
    |    * Read arbitrary binary cartridge images without modifying them.
    |    * Emit deterministic `.s26` using `.org` plus conservative `.byte` data.
    |    * Reassemble with `vcsc-as` and require exact size/byte equality.
    |    * Add malformed/truncated/empty-input diagnostics.
    |    * This milestone must work before any clever inference is trusted.
    | 
    | 1. [x] Add 6502/6507 decode and exact instruction emission
    |    * Decode all 256 opcode bytes using VCSC's opcode metadata where practical.
    |    * Emit normal mnemonics when they reproduce the exact opcode.
    |    * Use `opXX` for unofficial/ambiguous encodings when needed for byte identity.
    |    * Preserve original zero-page/absolute/indexed/indirect addressing modes,
    |      using assembler mode suffixes where needed.
    |    * Add exact round-trip fixtures for every opcode/addressing-mode family.
    |    * Emit every conditional branch with mandatory `.same` or `.cross`.
    |    * Test all eight branches in both timing classes around page boundaries.
    | 
    | 2. [x] Infer unbanked origins and build recursive control flow
    |    * Use RESET/NMI/IRQ vectors, absolute JMP/JSR targets, resolved indirect JMP,
    |      and ROM data references as origin constraints.
    |    * Scan candidate `$4c` absolute JMPs as origin evidence even before final
    |      reachability classification; score coincidences rather than trusting them.
    |    * Implement recursive tracing from vectors/entry points.
    |    * Model the NMOS indirect-JMP `$xxFF` page-wrap behavior.
    |    * Produce deterministic control-flow labels.
    |    * Validate with synthetic ROMs whose origins are not VCSC defaults.
    | 
    | 3. [x] Infer and model F8/F6/F4/FA/DPC/WD bankswitching
    |    * Detect unbanked/F8/F6/F4 from size plus hotspot/control-flow evidence.
    |    * Detect 12K CBS RAM Plus / FA; model `$1FF8-$1FFA` selectors, fixed startup
    |      bank 2, and the `$1000-$11FF` split RAM overlay without decoding hidden ROM.
    |    * Detect DPC from its distinctive 10240/10495-byte layout; analyze the first
    |      8K as two F8-style program banks and preserve the trailing DPC data/RNG bytes.
    |    * Detect the distinctive 8195-byte Wickstead Design preservation dump; model
    |      eight 1K banks, the `$30-$3F` TIA-read arrangement selectors, 64-byte split
    |      RAM window, power-on arrangement 0, the historical bank-2/bank-3 dump swap,
    |      and exact preservation of the three non-emulated trailing bytes. Accept a
    |      corrected 8192-byte WD image under explicit `--mapper wd`.
    |    * Track mapper state in the control-flow graph.
    |    * Infer the reset bank separately from mapper type.
    |    * Infer every physical bank's logical origin, including origins constrained by
    |      JMP/JSR after bank-switch transitions.
    |    * Keep file-bank identity, logical address, and selector identity separate.
    |    * Preserve duplicated/padded banks exactly.
    |    * Test VCSC ROMs plus synthetic foreign layouts where banks share or differ in
    |      page-aligned logical origins.
    | 
    | 4. [x] Infer and model F8SC/F6SC/F4SC Superchip variants
    |    * Recognize SC RAM windows and their read/write behavior.
    |    * Prevent writable SC windows from being misclassified as ordinary ROM code.
    |    * Track dynamic execution into SC/RIOT RAM conservatively.
    |    * Preserve physical input bytes even where hardware mapping makes them
    |      inaccessible during ordinary execution.
    |    * Add ambiguous F8-vs-F8SC/F6/F4 fixtures and confidence tests.
    | 
    | 5. [x] Make semantic byte roles non-exclusive
    |    * Replace any one-byte `code/data` enum with independent role/evidence flags.
    |    * Track instruction starts, operands, data-read targets, table roles,
    |      possible dynamic references, and unreferenced status independently.
    |    * Support instruction bytes and operands that are deliberately read as data.
    |    * Ensure data evidence never erases executable reachability and vice versa.
    |    * Add fixtures where executable immediates/operands are indexed as tables.
    | 
    | 6. [x] Support overlapping executable instruction streams
    |    * Permit branch/JMP/JSR targets into the middle of another decoded instruction.
    |    * Represent multiple reachable instruction instances over the same bytes.
    |    * Add the `$2c` BIT-skip idiom as a regression fixture.
    |    * Emit `.byte $2c` plus an inner label when that is the cleanest exact source
    |      representation of the BIT-skip trick.
    |    * Add at least one non-BIT overlapping-stream fixture so support is general,
    |      not an opcode-specific hack.
    | 
    | 7. [x] Strengthen ROM-read, speculative-island, and unreferenced-byte analysis
    |    * Track direct absolute ROM reads and bounded indexed ranges as definite data
    |      references.
    |    * Extend `stego` abstract-state ideas for zero-page/indirect pointers and
    |      table-driven accesses.
    |    * Mark unresolved dynamic ranges as possibly referenced rather than unused.
    |    * Identify bytes proven neither fetched nor read as unreferenced ROM.
    |    * Emit unreferenced regions as data unless the lower-confidence island pass
    |      positively promotes a candidate under the HLT/JAM/KIL/barrier rules.
    |    * For each otherwise unknown address in every mapped 4K bank/window, test it
    |      as a speculative instruction-island entry and recursively validate all
    |      statically reachable successors.
    |    * Reject a speculative candidate if any possible newly speculative path
    |      reaches HLT/JAM/KIL. Honor abstract-state proof that such a path is
    |      unreachable, and never reject a HLT/JAM/KIL already established by normal
    |      non-speculative control flow.
    |    * Retain rejected instruction-start addresses as negative evidence. Three
    |      consecutive rejected starts form a sequential-flow barrier because no
    |      6502/6507 instruction is longer than three bytes. Explicit branches/JMPs/
    |      JSRs/vectors may still cross or enter around a barrier.
    |    * Use barriers plus HLT/JAM/KIL rejection to strengthen island/data decisions
    |      without overriding definite code, overlaps, or code-as-data evidence.
    |    * Add optional usage summary counts with clearly defined overlap accounting.
    |    * Add fixtures containing padding, abandoned code, instruction-as-data, and
    |      deliberately unreachable legal-opcode byte sequences.
    |    * Add regressions for unconditional JAM rejection, conditional possible-JAM
    |      rejection, abstractly unreachable JAM, established intentional JAM, three-
    |      start barriers, explicit jumps across barriers, and overlapping inner entry
    |      points inside otherwise rejected speculative regions.
    | 
    | 8. [x] Use canonical TIA/RIOT hardware symbols
    |    * Reuse the canonical names from `libraries/vcs/tia.c26` and `riot.c26`.
    |    * Emit the canonical equates actually used by the generated source so each
    |      `.s26` is self-contained. Do not change assembler include/language semantics
    |      for this.
    |    * Emit symbols such as `COLUBK`, `GRP0`, `SWCHA`, `INTIM`, and `TIM64T`
    |      instead of unexplained numeric addresses when exact bytes can be preserved.
    |    * Preserve zero-page versus absolute encoding while avoiding redundant mode
    |      noise: ordinary named zero-page instructions should normally omit `.z`,
    |      `.zx`, and `.zy` because the assembler relaxes resolved 8-bit operands
    |      naturally. Retain `.a`, `.ax`, or `.ay` when an originally wide encoding
    |      has an operand below `$0100` and would otherwise relax. Raw `opXX` spellings
    |      keep explicit mode suffixes whenever the assembler requires them.
    |    * Preserve noncanonical hardware mirror addresses exactly using symbolic
    |      expressions when possible; otherwise keep the numeric operand. Every
    |      preserved mirrored access must include a comment naming the canonical TIA
    |      or RIOT register actually selected, regardless of operand spelling.
    |    * Select TIA read versus write names according to the actual memory operation.
    |    * Add load/store/RMW and canonical/mirrored-address round-trip fixtures.
    | 
    | 9. [x] Detect and render sprite/font-like data
    |    * Use short straight-line GRP0/GRP1/PF0/PF1/PF2 data flow and table access
    |      patterns as strong evidence. Track A/X/Y source loads into STA/STX/STY and
    |      preserve provenance through register transfers plus simple ALU/accumulator-
    |      shift transforms.
    |    * Infer exact countdown spans when possible; otherwise bound unresolved
    |      runtime-indexed tables at the next known code/vector/label boundary, with a
    |      conservative maximum range.
    |    * Recognize indirect animation pointers built from a ROM low-byte table plus a
    |      constant high byte. Enumerate the low-byte targets; when the low table has a
    |      constant stride, use that stride as strong frame-height evidence and label
    |      each inferred frame base. Pitfall's `$00,$16,...,$9A` plus high byte `$FB`
    |      is the regression model for eight 22-row player frames.
    |    * As a supplemental high-confidence structural heuristic, recognize long
    |      aligned runs of at least eight coherent 8x8 glyph blocks even when dynamic
    |      pointer arithmetic defeats direct TIA provenance. A single bitmap-shaped
    |      object is never sufficient structural evidence. Pitfall's decimal font at
    |      `$FF00` is the regression model.
    |    * Raw numeric `.byte` runs must stop at the first detected graphics byte so
    |      the beginning of a sprite/font table is never swallowed into a preceding
    |      multi-byte raw emission.
    |    * Emit one assembler-native `%00110100` byte per source line with the matching
    |      X/dot picture in a comment for visual scanning.
    |    * Keep original byte order and all blank rows exactly.
    |    * Stop provenance at calls, branches, unknown raw opcodes, or unrelated loads;
    |      keep low-confidence/non-graphics data numeric rather than guessing through a
    |      decompressor or helper routine.
    |    * Regressions cover direct sprites, runtime-indexed font rows, PF masks through
    |      ALU transforms, A-to-X register transfers, X-register/STX and Y-register/STY
    |      graphics paths, dynamic low-byte animation-pointer tables, long structural
    |      8x8 fonts, random tables, call-mediated compressed-looking data, and
    |      bitmap-shaped false positives.
    | 
    | 10. [x] Infer NTSC versus PAL-family timing
    |     * Analyze VSYNC/VBLANK/WSYNC/timer/frame structure statically.
    |     * Use arbitrary known TIM64T values, parameterized timer-helper call-site
    |       values, and broad counted-WSYNC loops rather than only VCSC's exact
    |       maintained scheduler constants.
    |     * When established code writes VSYNC, run a bounded dynamic frame probe using
    |       VCSC's shared `simulator/mos6502` CPU core. Model the 6507 bus mirror, RIOT
    |       RAM/timers, TIA VSYNC/WSYNC behavior, neutral controller inputs, supported
    |       F8/F6/F4/FA bank hotspots, and Superchip/FA RAM well enough to execute
    |       ordinary frame loops. Require several stable consecutive frame periods.
    |     * Report dynamic measurements as raw 76-cycle line intervals, not Stella
    |       display scanlines. VCSC's maintained schedulers intentionally calibrate
    |       264/314 raw harness intervals to Stella's 262/312 displayed scanlines.
    |     * Skip dynamic execution for DPC until the probe has a faithful DPC
    |       coprocessor/register model; retain static evidence there.
    |     * Report confidence and evidence in the generated header and verbose report.
    |     * Report PAL/SECAM ambiguity rather than pretending 50 Hz timing distinguishes
    |       the two. Explicit PAL/SECAM filename tokens may identify the likely family
    |       member at medium confidence; timing only confirms the 50 Hz family.
    |     * Test static signatures, dynamically measured 262/312-style synthetic
    |       frames, a SECAM-named 50 Hz case, and maintained VCSC NTSC/PAL/SECAM
    |       examples.

    | 12. [x] Improve labels, tables, vectors, and analysis comments
    |     * Deterministically name definite control-flow and ROM-data targets.
    |     * Use bank-qualified labels where runtime addresses collide across banks.
    |     * Detect vector/pointer/color/graphics tables conservatively. Pointer-table
    |       presentation requires actual low/high pointer-construction data flow in
    |       executable code; address-looking byte pairs alone are never sufficient.
    |     * Emit `.word` labels only when exact original 16-bit values are preserved.
    |       A pointer-table `.word` span may not swallow any independently required
    |       interior label. Color-table presentation may split at interior labels and
    |       must emit those labels before continuing the table.
    |     * Represent references into instruction operands or vector-word interiors as
    |       `container_label + offset`; never emit an unplaceable interior label.
    |     * If vector-table bytes are executable, split the vector `.word` representation
    |       and emit the executable spelling/labels instead; retain a comment recording
    |       the vector value. Regression coverage must include an entry at a vector high
    |       byte (for example an RTS at `$FFFB`) as well as the non-executable `$FFFF`
    |       container-relative case.
    |     * Annotate code-as-data, overlap, unreferenced runs, dynamic-code exits, and
    |       important inference ambiguity without overwhelming normal source.
    |     * Add a verbose evidence/report mode separate from normal `.s26` output.

    | 15. [x] Add standalone corpus round-trip verifier

    | 16. [x] Integrate, harden, and fuzz `vcsc-disas`
    |     * Add top-level build, staged install, Linux, Windows, and release packaging
    |       consistent with other VCSC executables. Source hygiene pins every package
    |       path even on hosts without MinGW; the native Linux package is also built
    |       and checked as a fully static seven-tool bundle.
    |     * Round-trip every suitable ROM produced by the VCSC example suite. The eight
    |       existing example-build shards now feed all 81 generated ROMs through
    |       `vcsc-disas -> vcsc-as` and require exact reconstruction.
    |     * Add deterministic-output regressions. Repeated disassembly of the same ROM
    |       must produce byte-identical `.s26` text.
    |     * Add malformed/truncated/arbitrary-input fuzz coverage. Boundary sizes around
    |       supported layouts must fail cleanly with no signal/output artifact, while
    |       two deterministic arbitrary images for every supported size/mapper family
    |       (2K/4K/F8/F6/F4/FA/WD/DPC/3F/3E) round-trip exactly.
    |     * Add duplicated/padded-dump preservation tests. Supported duplicated and
    |       leading-padded 8K/16K/32K images remain byte exact; WD's historical 8195-byte
    |       bad dump remains separately covered.
    |     * Add overlapping-stream/code-as-data/unreferenced-byte corpus fixtures.
    |     * Add TIA/RIOT symbolic-output oracles that still compare reconstructed bytes.
    |     * Require exact binary round trip for every accepted fixture.
    |     * Keep `stego` regressions green and verify disassembler development does not
    |       silently change the existing tool. The hardening E2E independently compiles

    | Items 0 through 16 are complete. The next focused work is the ordered mapper
    | expansion queue below, driven by real-ROM corpus gaps. Implement one mapper family
    | at a time, add exact round-trip fixtures and differential corpus evidence for it,
    | and do not let unsupported-family byte signatures distort already-supported CFG
    | inference.

    | 17. [x] Add 3F mapper support
    |     * Model Tigervision's split window as a value-selected lower 2K plus the
    |       ROM's fixed final 2K, with bank 0 selected at power-on.
    |     * Every write to `$00-$3F` updates mapper state from the data-bus value;
    |       only explicit `$3F` traffic/signatures count as identification evidence.
    |     * Carry selected-bank state across CFG edges, including a bank switch whose
    |       old physical continuation is HLT/JAM/KIL, and preserve variable sizes.
    |     * Synthetic 8K and 32K exact-round-trip cases pin the model; the 8K fixture
    |       is independently recognized as 3F by Stella `-rominfo`.
    | 
    | 18. [x] Add 3E mapper support
    |     * Build directly on the 3F segmented model: writes to `$3F` select lower-2K
    |       ROM banks while writes to `$3E` select lower-window RAM banks.
    |     * Model the 1K read/write aliases of the selected RAM bank and apply the
    |       split-RAM RMW contradiction rules already used by SC/FA/CV/WD.
    |     * Keep ROM-bank count and inferred RAM use separate; do not assume all 32K
    |       of possible RAM is physically present merely because the address encoding
    |       permits it.
    |     * Add exact round-trip and 3E-vs-3F ambiguity fixtures. Canonical 3E
    |       evidence subsumes 3F because `$3E` RAM selection is the distinguishing
    |       operation while `$3F` ROM selection is shared by both schemes.
    | 
    | 19. [x] Add FE mapper support
    |     * Model Activision/SCABS switching through `$01FE`, including the delayed
    |       bank decision derived from the data bus rather than treating FE as a
    |       conventional hotspot-selected F8 variant.
    |     * Preserve the encoded operand/address bytes that trigger the mapper and
    |       carry any timing-dependent ambiguity conservatively in CFG state.
    |     * Validate Robot Tank/Decathlon-style real ROMs plus synthetic cases where
    |       the old physical bank contains JAM after the switching access.
    |     * Automatic inference requires the established released-cart FE call
    |       signatures; ordinary JSR target bytes alone are not positive FE evidence.
    | 
    | 20. [x] Add E7 mapper support
    |     * Model M-Network's selectable lower 2K, fixed final 2K, `$1FE0-$1FE6`
    |       ROM selectors, `$1FE7` lower-window RAM selection, and `$1FE8-$1FEB`
    |       256-byte RAM-block selectors.
    |     * Represent both E7 RAM regions with their actual split read/write aliases;
    |       RMW against either alias is negative mapper evidence.
    |     * Support the known 8K/12K/16K layouts without flattening their segmented
    |       runtime mapping.
    |     * Add exact corpus fixtures with and without E7 RAM use.
    | 
    |     Completed 2026-08-20:
    |     * Added size-accurate 8K/12K/16K lower-selector tables, fixed-final-2K
    |       vector/reset semantics, and mapper configuration state across sequential
    |       flow, branches, JSR/JMP, indirect JMP, and speculative islands.
    |     * Modeled the conditional 1K lower RAM and fixed 256-byte RAM block as
    |       split read/write aliases; reachable RMW against either region contradicts
    |       the E7 hypothesis.
    |     * Added Stella-compatible E7 identification signatures plus decoded selector
    |       established evidence. Selector accesses from fixed ROM remain positive
    |       evidence even when they do not change the immediately following opcode fetch;
    |       detached speculative selector traffic is presentation-only.
    |     * Added exact synthetic 8K, 12K, and 16K round trips, explicit RAM/RMW
    |       coverage, deterministic fuzz coverage, and fixed all five E7 mismatches in
    |       the 2026-08-20 problem corpus without changing any ROM bytes.
    | 
    | 21. [x] Add WDSW support
    |     * Stella's distinction is file-layout metadata, not different cartridge
    |       hardware: 8192-byte corrected images are `WD` when the `A5 39 4C`
    |       (`LDA $39; JMP`) signature is present; every 8195-byte 8K+3 image is
    |       designated `WDSW`. Both instantiate Stella's `CartridgeWD` and therefore
    |       share the same four-segment arrangements, 64-byte split RAM, delayed
    |       `$30-$3F` read selectors, and power-on arrangement 0.
    |     * WDSW alone applies the historical file correction: physical 1K chunks 2
    |       and 3 are swapped for runtime analysis and the trailing three bytes are
    |       omitted from emulation but preserved byte-for-byte in generated source.
    |       `--mapper wd` and `--mapper wdsw` now enforce their 8192/8195-byte layouts.
    |     * The 2026-08-20 problem corpus pins all four WD-family differentials:
    |       `56210a3b9ea6d5dd8f417a357ed8ca92` -> WDSW,
    |       `62ee2b8f59e9cd6285bbdb674a952e8b` -> WD,
    |       `850fdc358adda5dd8b7a8b9b178e6655` -> UA, and
    |       `af1c6f6d73a00bc1be6b353fe9a27819` -> F8. The last two exposed a WD
    |       false-positive in control-flow refinement; automatic 8K WD hypotheses now
    |       require Stella's WD signature. Synthetic WD/WDSW E2E fixtures and fuzz
    |       layouts preserve byte-exact reconstruction for both forms.
    | 
    | 22. [x] Add FC mapper support
    |     * Amiga Power Play's staged protocol is modeled with distinct mapped-bank and
    |       pending-selector state. Writes to `$1FF8` replace the pending low two bits;
    |       writes to `$1FF9` add the high contribution when representable and use
    |       Stella's `value % bank_count` fallback otherwise. Reads of those addresses
    |       do not stage. Any read/write access to `$1FFC` commits the pending bank.
    |     * RESET starts with mapped bank 0 and pending selector 0. Partial/abandoned
    |       sequences continue in the old bank; commit edges, including commit-before-
    |       JAM and unresolved pending state, continue analysis in the selected bank(s).
    |       Supported FC image sizes are 4K/8K/16K/32K and `--mapper fc` enforces them.
    |     * Automatic inference uses Stella's three characteristic FC staged-select
    |       byte signatures and outranks only the ordinary size-default F mapper, not
    |       distinctive mapper families. This fixes all five FC differentials in the
    |       2026-08-20 problem corpus: `4a5fddf89801336637ac8e57a7c9a881`,
    |       `565b2d41d4bdf960d0912d39ec29d0a7`,
    |       `60cd61a2dfccb0e2736434f9792c1672`,
    |       `715dbf2e39ba8a52c5fe5cdd927b37e0`, and
    |       `bbf8c7c9ed280151934aabe138e41ba7` now agree with Stella as FC.
    |     * Synthetic semantic and deterministic fuzz fixtures cover staged low/high
    |       selection, read/write commits, the high-value fallback, and exact byte
    |       reconstruction. The semantic corpus is 101/101 and mapper fuzz is 30/30.
    | 
    | Unbanked 1K topology extension (2026-08-20)
    | -------------------------------------------
    | * A 1024-byte image is one physical 1K ROM mirrored four times through the
    |   4K cartridge window. Runtime targets therefore resolve modulo `$0400` while
    |   source emission preserves exactly the original 1024 physical bytes.
    | * Origin inference supports the four 1K mirror positions and normally presents
    |   a top-mirror image at `$FC00`; `--mapper 1k` explicitly selects the topology
    |   and rejects other file sizes.
    | * The final six physical bytes are NMI/RESET/IRQ-BRK vectors. Normal analysis
    |   seeds every vector whose target resolves into cartridge ROM, so an IRQ-only
    |   routine is recovered even when RESET never references it. RESET-only seeding
    |   remains intentionally limited to temporary mapper-hypothesis validation.
    | * `c33caa7d6ac7251bb804e0473198901d.bin` is the real regression: RESET `$FC96`
    |   and IRQ/BRK `$FC80` both decode under the 1K topology and the file reconstructs
    |   byte-for-byte exactly.
    | 
    | 23. [ ] Add GL mapper support
    |     * Model GameLine's four independently mapped 1K runtime segments and its
    |       `$0480/$0580/$0880/$0980 + x` selectors.
    |     * Track ROM-versus-RAM segment selection and the selector bit that chooses
    |       RAM read versus write mapping; integrate the existing split-RAM RMW
    |       contradiction invariant.
    |     * Treat modem/PROM control space conservatively and keep unsupported modem
    |       behavior from corrupting otherwise exact 6507 disassembly.
    |     * Add GameLine corpus fixtures and cross-segment control-flow tests.
    | 
    | 24. [ ] Add CM mapper support
    |     * Model SpectraVideo CompuMate as a cartridge/peripheral combination rather
    |       than a normal hotspot mapper: four 4K ROM banks plus 2K RAM are controlled
    |       through SWCHA state.
    |     * Carry bank-select, RAM-enable, and RAM-direction state that affects the
    |       cartridge mapping; keep keyboard/tape peripheral behavior conservative
    |       unless it is needed to resolve code fetches.
    |     * Do not apply split-address-RAM assumptions here: CompuMate uses a direction
    |       control line rather than separate read/write aliases.
    |     * Add exact CompuMate fixtures and controller-state transition tests.
    | 
    | 25. [ ] Add DPC+ mapper support
    |     * Treat DPC+ as a separate ARM-assisted format, not an extension flag on DPC.
    |       Preserve/analyze the 3K ARM driver, six 4K 6507 program banks, Harmony RAM,
    |       `$1FF6-$1FFB` bank selectors, and DPC+ register window according to the
    |       actual 32K layout.
    |     * Begin with exact structural segmentation and 6507 CFG recovery; ARM Thumb
    |       analysis may remain conservative/raw where it is not required to identify
    |       6507-visible control flow.
    |     * Add real DPC+ fixtures and exact reconstruction before attempting richer
    |       coprocessor semantics.
    | 
    | 26. [ ] Add CDF/CDFJ/CDFJ+ mapper support
    |     * Treat CDF as the final item in this queue because it combines ARM execution,
    |       fast fetch/jump machinery, datastream state, multiple RAM layouts, and ROM
    |       sizes from the original 32K format through larger CDFJ+ variants.
    |     * First preserve and identify structural regions and recover 6507-visible
    |       program-bank control flow exactly; add ARM-side semantics incrementally only
    |       where they materially improve reachable-code inference.
    |     * Keep CDF/CDFJ/CDFJ+ version discrimination explicit rather than folding all
    |       variants into one approximate mapping.
    |     * Require real-format corpus fixtures, Stella differential checks, and exact
    |       round trip across every supported CDF generation before marking complete.
    | 
    | 27. [ ] Correct 6507 interrupt-vector reachability
    |     * [done 2026-08-20] IRQ/BRK `$FFFE/$FFFF` is no longer seeded merely because
    |       its target resolves into cartridge ROM. A reachable `BRK` promotes the
    |       mapper-visible handler target at that point, with exact stack state when SP
    |       is known and conservative RIOT-RAM state when it is not.
    |     * RESET remains the real hardware reset entry. The 6507 also has no bonded NMI
    |       input; remove the remaining legacy unconditional NMI-root behavior separately
    |       after checking whether any corpus/title relies on it as a useful manual-style
    |       discovery convention.
    |     * Regression-pin `1ee9c1ba95cef2cf987d63f176c54ac3` (Ms. Pac-Man PAL): its
    |       IRQ/BRK vector points at `$FFEE`, but no reachable BRK enters it. Treating
    |       `$FFEE` as an entry fabricated overlapping code at `$FFF9` (`BPL $003B`) in
    |       the vector/hotspot tail. The fixed output leaves that stream as data and
    |       round-trips byte-exactly.
    |     * Keep `c33caa7d6ac7251bb804e0473198901d` as the positive counterexample:
    |       reachable BRK really does promote its `$FC80` handler, whose RTI path is
    |       stack-aware and may modify the saved return address through RIOT-RAM mirroring.
    | 
    | 
    | 28. [x] Handle playable malformed/odd-size cartridge dumps
    |     * Separate physical input-file preservation from the logical runtime cartridge
    |       image so playable over/underdumps can be analyzed without normalizing away any
    |       supplied bytes. Exact input reconstruction remains mandatory.
    |     * Use `0963aa9f7f6cf5a36ff700001583624e` (4098-byte Space Invaders 2 hack
    |       overdump) and `df5cc5cccdc140eb7107f5b8adfacda1` (4094-byte Lumberman hack
    |       truncation) as real Stella-playable acceptance cases. Determine and document
    |       Stella/hardware-equivalent mapping behavior rather than rejecting solely because
    |       the byte count is not a canonical power-of-two cartridge size.
    |     * Preserve excess/trailing bytes as physical dump data and represent absent bytes
    |       conservatively; do not silently rewrite the source image into a canonical 4K file.
    |     * [done 2026-08-20] Implemented Stella-compatible 4096-byte logical
    |       normalization for the two corpus cases: 4098-byte inputs analyze only the first
    |       4096 bytes and preserve the final two bytes raw; 4094-byte inputs analyze with two
    |       zero-filled logical tail bytes but emit no bytes that were absent from the file.
    |       Both named hashes now round-trip exactly and compare as `vcsc=4K stella=4K MATCH`.
    | 
    | 29. [x] Add Starpath/Arcadia Supercharger image support
    |     * Recognize any exact 8448-byte multiple as the standard AR fast-load structure:
    |       each load is 8192 bytes of page data followed by a 256-byte header. `--mapper ar`
    |       enforces the same structural size rule. Header fields are decoded as start address,
    |       control byte, page count, load ID, page map, and checksums.
    |     * Reconstruct the three 2K Supercharger RAM banks directly from the page map. Load-ID
    |       zero starts from reset-cleared RAM; nonzero concatenated loads inherit the preceding
    |       reconstructed RAM image before overlaying their pages. Recover payload control flow
    |       from the header start address under the initial two-window bank configuration and
    |       show it as comment-only runtime code with source-file provenance. An unresolved
    |       `$FFF8` configuration change terminates that fixed-configuration path rather than
    |       fabricating a bank state; analog cassette timing and BIOS bytes are not synthesized.
    |     * Keep all physical load bytes as raw source so exact reconstruction outranks header
    |       validity. Bad header/page checksums are annotated, not repaired or rejected. Synthetic
    |       single/multi-load fixtures pin the format. The 2026-08-20 corpus contains 85 AR files
    |       (77 single-load, six 3-load, two 4-load): all 85 now reconstruct byte-exactly and all
    |       85 agree with Stella as AR. Five loads contain bad page checksums and are deliberately
    |       preserved as-is.
    | 
    | Definition of first useful milestone
    | ------------------------------------
    | The first useful milestone is reached after roadmap items 0 through 3:
    | `vcsc-disas` can take arbitrary unbanked/F8/F6/F4/FA cartridges, infer plausible
    | bank origins and executable control flow, emit exact branches and instruction
    | bytes, and reassemble the original image exactly.
    | 
    | Items 4 through 8 turn the analysis model into one capable of handling the
    | things real hand-written Atari code does: Superchip state, overlapping streams,
    | code read as data, truly unused bytes, and symbolic hardware accesses. Those are
    | not cosmetic extras; they are required before calling the disassembly robust.
    | 
    | Sprite rendering, video/controller inference, richer labels, and documentation
    | then make the output pleasant and useful to humans. The standalone corpus
    | round-trip verifier provides the final practical way to throw large external ROM
    | collections at the exact-reconstruction invariant without enrolling those ROMs in
    | the repository test suite.
    | 
    | Do not invert the priority. A disassembler that produces beautiful comments but
    | cannot reproduce the input ROM exactly is not a disassembler we can trust.
    | 
    | Corpus differential note (2026-08-19): `roundtrip.pl --stella` assumes the input
    | set has already been filtered to Atari 2600/VCS ROMs. Stella `-rominfo` resolves
    | a VCS mapper; it is not a generic Atari-platform classifier, so unrelated 5200,
    | 7800, or Atari 8-bit binaries of a supported size can receive a plausible-looking
    | size-default mapper. Such rows are corpus errors, not mapper-reference evidence.
    | 
    | 
    | Doubled 2K preservation images
    | ------------------------------
    | A 4096-byte file made from two byte-identical 2048-byte halves is a logical 2K cart. Analyze one half and preserve the duplicate half raw for exact round trip.
    | 
    | 30. [x] Preserve 3F/3E relative branches whose physical bank presentation cannot represent the runtime alias
    |     * A 3F/3E physical ROM bank can execute through the selectable lower 2K
    |       window as well as through its canonical emitted `.rorg`.  Usually a
    |       relative branch survives that mirror because source and target move by
    |       the same amount.  The fixed final bank is the corner case: when it is
    |       selected into the lower window, a branch crossing `$x7FF->$x800` enters
    |       the fixed upper window.  Re-presenting the same physical source at the
    |       final bank's fixed `.rorg` can turn a real cart target such as
    |       `$F7E7 -> $F821` into the fictitious `$FFE7 -> $0021`.
    |     * Before emitting a 3F/3E relative instruction, compare the runtime target's
    |       mapper-resolved physical bank/offset with the target implied by the
    |       physical bank's emitted `.rorg`.  If they do not describe the same
    |       physical target, no symbolic short relative branch represents that
    |       source/target pair in the one-bank presentation; retain the exact bytes as
    |       `.byte` instead.  Genuine unbanked `$FFxx->$00xx` branches remain legal
    |       symbolic `.cross` branches and keep the assembler's modulo-16-bit fix.
    |     * Synthetic `threef_alias_branch.bin` pins the lower-selected-final-bank
    |       `$F7E7 BPL $F821` case and exact round trip.  Corpus Espial NTSC/PAL hashes
    |       `f344ac1279152157d63e64aa39479599` and
    |       `f7a138eed69665b5cd1bfa796a550b01` now keep their physical `$10,$38`
    |       bytes raw instead of inventing `BPL.cross $0021`; both round-trip exactly.
    | 
    | 31. [ ] Finish mapper-context abstract CPU state precision so distinct bank contexts do not collapse
    |     * The first concrete-state loss is fixed.  Concrete RESET discovery now records
    |       A/X/Y/SP/P and mapper configuration at each newly observed physical ROM
    |       instruction start, and `seed_missing_concrete_code()` seeds static analysis
    |       from that exact observed state instead of an all-unknown state/config 0.
    |       On Espial PAL this cuts classified executable bytes from 3418 to 1377 and
    |       overlap from 463 to 0; the NTSC image shows the same collapse of bogus code.
    |     * Remaining debt: `push_work_state_ctx()` tracks mapper contexts separately in
    |       `threef_context_seen`, but still stores one merged `b->states[offset]` per
    |       physical byte. Distinct contexts can therefore merge away A/X/Y precision,
    |       and a revisit in an already-seen context can be suppressed before a newly
    |       improved state is merged/requeued. Replace this with mapper-context-specific
    |       abstract state (or an equivalent sparse keyed representation) and converge it
    |       to a fixed point.
    |     * Do not paper this over by banning final-bank selection: Stella 2026
    |       deliberately implements 3F writes at TIA `$00-$3F` and selects
    |       `value % romBankCount()`, so the final physical bank is a legal lower-bank
    |       selection.
    | 
    | 
    | 32. [x] Reject speculative ROM islands that escape validation into the TIA window
    |     * The remaining old `.cross requires a short relative branch` corpus failure,
    |       `56300ed31fef018bd96768ccc982f7b4`, is unrelated to the 3F alias case.  It
    |       is a 16K F6 image whose final-bank bytes around `$FFD0-$FFE8` are table/data.
    |       The speculative-island pass promoted `$FFDA` from the accidental decode
    |       `JSR $3020 / BMI $000F / BMI $0031 / ...`.  On the 6507, `$3020` is a
    |       cartridge mirror, so the speculative JSR walks hundreds of apparently
    |       valid ROM instructions and reaches a strong terminal.  That walk consumes
    |       the old 512-static-CFG-node validation cap; the continuation at `$FFDD` then returns
    |       only `SPEC_SAFE_WEAK` before its BMI is examined.  `spec_safe_merge()` let
    |       the already-strong JSR arm win, so an incompletely validated island became
    |       executable code.
    |     * Credibility promotion now requires that the complete speculative walk stay
    |       within its static-CFG-node cap. The old fixed 512-node limit has been replaced
    |       by a per-walk cap equal to the physical bank size. Cap exhaustion is explicitly
    |       counted in the emitted analysis diagnostics, remains inconclusive/non-destructive
    |       during negative-evidence scanning, and can never count as sufficient validation
    |       for promotion. The strictly linear JAM/KIL endpoint scan is uncapped because it
    |       is finite by construction.  Separately, when a speculative conditional
    |       branch is actually examined, any arm that can be taken into `$0000-$007F`
    |       is hard negative evidence: that is the TIA register window, not instruction
    |       storage.  A branch proven not-taken by abstract flags is still allowed.
    |     * Established/vector-reachable flow is unchanged, so this does not undo the
    |       assembler's correct modulo-16-bit relative-branch support or the explicit
    |       wraparound fixture.  Synthetic `speculative_tia_branch_reject.bin` pins
    |       both the long-JSR budget-exhaustion mechanism and the possible TIA branch.
    |       The corpus F6 image now keeps the accidental bytes as data/raw source rather
    |       than manufacturing executable `$000F/$0031` targets. Its current diagnostics
    |       expose 2 capped negative-evidence walks and 1 candidate denied promotion due
    |       to the bank-sized cap, instead of silently hiding the cutoff.
    | 
    | 33. [x] Make ROM-usage accounting preserve observed reads and stop claiming unexplained bytes are unused
    |     * Concrete RESET discovery now distinguishes instruction fetch bytes from
    |       physical cartridge ROM data reads and feeds the latter into `ROLE_DATA_READ`.
    |       Espial PAL concretely observes 444 distinct ROM data bytes; the old summary
    |       reported only 11 because those concrete read observations were discarded.
    |     * Mapper-aware unresolved indexed/indirect accesses now populate
    |       `ROLE_POSSIBLE` over the physical banks/ranges they can address. Broad
    |       unresolved 3F accesses can therefore produce a large conservative `possible`
    |       set; that means "may be read", not "is data".
    |     * Rename the residual summary category from `unreferenced` to `unclassified`.
    |       On Espial PAL the old `executed=3418 data-read=11 overlap=463 possible=8
    |       unreferenced=4761` became `established-code=1377 data-read=444 overlap=0
    |       possible=6144 unclassified=1698` before later speculative code was split into
    |       its own reported class. Absence from discovered paths is no longer
    |       presented as proof that a byte is unused.
    | 
    | 34. [x] Honor logical `.rorg` PCs for numeric relative branches in o26 output
    |     * A generated F6 source exposed that the o26 writer computed a numeric relative
    |       branch displacement from the packed `.org` object offset rather than the
    |       active logical `.rorg` PC. A local `BNE.same $F806` could therefore be falsely
    |       rejected as out of range even though flat binary assembly was correct.
    |     * Numeric relative operands now use `stmt->address + 2` with modulo-16-bit
    |       displacement arithmetic; relocatable operands retain packed-object relocation
    |       semantics. `assembler_rorg_directive.pl` pins the o26 case.
    | 
    | 
    | 35. [ ] Replace the speculative recursion cap with a state-keyed fixed-point validator
    |     * The old fixed 512-node limit was arbitrary and is gone; the current guard is one
    |       physical bank's byte count in static CFG-node visits. This is a computational
    |       safety valve, not a runtime/frame budget, and exhaustion is visible and can only
    |       reduce speculative promotion/rejection confidence.
    |     * The principled endpoint is a worklist keyed by physical location, mapper context,
    |       and the abstract state facts that affect successors. Merge/widen states and run
    |       to convergence so loops and diamonds terminate because the abstract lattice
    |       converges, not because an arbitrary recursion counter expires.
    |     * Do not memoize by physical byte alone: the same byte under a different mapper
    |       configuration or branch/register/zero-page state can have different successors.
    | 
    | 36. [x] Correct F8/F6/F4 Superchip inference in the expanded corpus
    |     * Eight supplied F8/F6/F4 images were false-promoted to SC because any
    |       established or speculative store into `$x000-$x07F` counted as sufficient
    |       positive evidence. All eight lacked the canonical duplicated-prefix dump
    |       structure and had no established read from the SC read alias.
    |     * One genuine F6SC image was demoted because a detached speculative island
    |       accidentally decoded an RMW in the SC window. Its four 4K banks have the
    |       canonical duplicated prefix; established flow observes 36 SC writes and
    |       42 SC reads across 16 matching byte offsets.
    |     * Speculative islands now contribute neither positive nor negative SC
    |       evidence. A plain cartridge-space store is not enough: semantic inference
    |       requires established accesses to both split aliases. Matching offsets are
    |       reported as extra strength but are not required by the hardware.
    |     * Explicit VCSC SC tail signatures are authoritative. Canonical duplicated
    |       bank prefixes remain strong automatic evidence unless established hardware
    |       behavior contradicts SC; established RMW or execution through the write
    |       alias vetoes heuristic inference.
    |     * Synthetic regressions pin write-only F8/F6/F4 as plain, read-only F8 as
    |       plain, paired SC at all three bank sizes, disjoint read/write offsets as
    |       valid SC, structural F6SC without decoded stores, and write-port execution
    |       as a contradiction. All nine supplied Fx/FxSC mismatches now match the
    |       Stella classification and round-trip byte-exactly.
    | 
    | 
    | 37. [x] Quarantine speculative islands from cartridge-level inference
    |     * Freeze established ROM roles and behavioral evidence before detached island
    |       discovery. Promoted islands retain separate speculative provenance and may
    |       improve only source presentation; they cannot become established execution.
    |     * Mapper hypothesis refinement now runs with speculative discovery disabled.
    |       Detached selector accesses therefore cannot make a mapper viable, increase
    |       hotspot counts, avoid a mapper HLT, or rank one mapper above another.
    |     * Speculative tracing cannot merge abstract state into an established code
    |       start, cannot seed H2 concrete execution, and restores mapper/RAM/Superchip
    |       evidence after presentation tracing. The established Superchip hardware
    |       decision is frozen during the speculative phase so an island cannot change
    |       its own ROM/RAM mapping halfway through validation.
    |     * Static video/controller recognition walks established code only. Superchip
    |       semantic evidence and contradictions likewise remain established-only.
    |       Graphics, color/pointer tables, labels, and data provenance may still use
    |       speculative roles because those are reversible source-presentation choices.
    |     * Usage diagnostics now report `established-code` and `speculative-code`
    |       separately. A cartridge with no established instruction is rejected even if
    |       detached bytes form credible islands.
    |     * Regression fixtures prove detached F8/E0 selector traffic does not vote on
    |       mapper hypotheses; detached NTSC timer/keypad idioms do not affect video or
    |       controller inference; speculative SC read/write traffic cannot change the
    |       hardware model halfway through its own walk; speculative code still renders
    |       and round-trips exactly; and speculative-only input is refused.
    |     * The expanded corpus file `7dfab820ff27b972aa148893f3c19953` is correctly
    |       rejected by the established-code requirement: it is an Atari 8-bit computer
    |       cartridge using `$D20E/$D400/$0244` and a `$B000` cartridge vector. Stella's
    |       F8 label is size-shaped autodetection; the old VCSC run had zero surviving
    |       mapper hypotheses and incorrectly resurrected it via nine speculative islands.
    | 
    | 38. [x] Gate concrete startup convergence on real VBLANK activity
    |     * The historical 16384-consecutive-stale-instruction threshold is retained as
    |       the reachability convergence test, but instruction count alone is no longer
    |       allowed to end an ordinary video baseline after only a few frames.
    |     * Baseline RESET scenarios run with SELECT/RESET released. Once execution
    |       asserts TIA VBLANK bit 1, convergence is suppressed until at least ten
    |       low-to-high VBLANK assertion edges have been observed. Repeated writes while
    |       VBLANK remains asserted do not count as additional frames.
    |     * Programs that never assert VBLANK retain the normal stale-reachability
    |       convergence fallback instead of being forced to the 250000-instruction hard
    |       ceiling. Halt/top-level-return and the hard ceiling remain safety exits.
    |     * SELECT/RESET scenarios remain separate fresh RESET runs after the baseline sweep;
    |       Item 39 replaces their historical instruction-count pulse timing with frame timing.
    |     * `baseline_vblank.bin` deliberately places its tenth VBLANK assertion after
    |       more than 40000 executed instructions, proving that the old 16384-stale-only
    |       cutoff would stop too early. Focused disassembler round trips are 119/119.
    | 
    | 39. [x] Drive SELECT/RESET scenarios from VBLANK frames
    |     * For every maintained COLOR/BW+difficulty combination, SELECT and RESET are
    |       exercised independently from a fresh RESET when SWCHB is observed. Video
    |       scenarios count the same low-to-high TIA VBLANK assertions used by baseline
    |       dwell: 10 frames released, 10 frames pressed, then 10 frames released.
    |     * Input state changes immediately after the 10th and 20th assertion edges;
    |       convergence is forbidden until the 30th edge, so polling/debounce code gets
    |       a full ten-frame dwell in each phase. The ordinary stale-reachability rule
    |       may extend the final released phase but can never shorten it.
    |     * A baseline that never asserts VBLANK has no observed frame clock. Only that
    |       non-video case retains the historical bounded 8192/16384-instruction pulse
    |       fallback, avoiding pointless 250000-instruction runs while preserving prior
    |       input exploration behavior.
    |     * `console_switches.bin` now rejects early press, requires SELECT throughout
    |       frames 10-19, requires release throughout frames 20-29, and writes its proof
    |       byte only after the complete 10/10/10 sequence. The historical instruction
    |       pulse misses this timing window. Focused disassembler round trips remain
    |       119/119 exact.
    | 
    | 
    | 
    | 40. [x] Validate never-observed static branch arms before establishment
    |     * Concrete discovery now records conditional taken/fall-through outcomes at
    |       the branch source for the retained physical-byte/runtime-PC/mapper context.
    |       An unknown static arm observed from that same context is established
    |       immediately; an arm never observed there receives a local mapper-aware
    |       negative-evidence walk before becoming established-static code.
    |     * Validation follows deterministic control flow and selector/hotspot effects
    |       before the next opcode fetch. Thus a branch arm that executes `LDA $1FF8`
    |       and continues safely in the selected F8 bank is retained even if the old
    |       bank contains JAM/KIL at the same continuation address.
    |     * A deterministic HLT/JAM/KIL rejects only that branch edge, never the mapper.
    |       The validator stops inconclusively at another unresolved conditional edge;
    |       exhaustion/allocation failure keeps the edge. Abstractly proven branch
    |       outcomes are not challenged, and without trusted concrete discovery both
    |       feasible static arms remain authoritative as before.
    |     * Synthetic fixtures pin direct JAM rejection, JMP-to-JAM rejection, and an
    |       F8 hotspot escape whose correct post-switch continuation must survive.
    |       Focused disassembler round trips are 122/122 exact.
    | 
    | 
    | 41. [ ] Treat multi-game images such as Stella 4IN1 as containers, not mappers
    |     * Do not model Stella-style `2IN1`/`4IN1`/`8IN1` images as one cartridge
    |       address space whose games can control-flow or bankswitch into one another.
    |       Treat the outer image as a container of independently selectable cartridge
    |       slices. On real hardware the game selector commonly drives otherwise-unused
    |       high ROM address lines or equivalent selection logic, so the 2600 sees only
    |       the selected slice.
    |     * Split a confidently identified container into its component images and run
    |       mapper/origin/vector/concrete-execution/video/controller/usage/speculative
    |       analysis independently for each slice. Each game has its own RESET/IRQ
    |       vectors, labels, usage map, MD5, inferred hardware, and possibly a different
    |       bankswitching scheme from its neighbors.
    |     * Preserve the complete outer image byte-for-byte. Exact reconstruction is
    |       performed by independently reassembling each slice and concatenating the
    |       resulting binaries in their original order; no control-flow edge or ROM
    |       reference may cross a container boundary.
    |     * Keep container detection separate from mapper detection. A 16K blob might
    |       be one F6 cartridge or four independent 4K games; a 32K 4IN1 might contain
    |       four unrelated 8K games using F8, E0, F8SC, or other schemes. Do not infer a
    |       multi-game container from file size alone. Use strong structural/database
    |       evidence and expose ambiguity rather than silently slicing a normal banked
    |       cartridge.
    |     * Account for Stella's reported MD5 semantics when differential-testing these
    |       images: Stella may select one component, recompute the MD5 over that slice,
    |       and then autodetect the slice's mapper. The whole-file MD5 therefore need
    |       not equal Stella's `Cart MD5` for a multi-game container.
    |     * Item 48 implements conservative automatic 4IN1/8IN1/32IN1 containers and
    |       Stella selected-slice MD5 normalization. Keep this item open for the more
    |       ambiguous 2IN1 case and any future explicit/database container override.
    | 
    | 
    | 42. [x] Require positive JANE evidence before F6 refinement
    |     * Expanded 16K corpus testing found four ordinary F6 images being promoted
    |       to JANE even though none contained Stella's JANE detector signature or
    |       VCSC `JANE` tail metadata. JANE could become the only surviving control-
    |       flow hypothesis simply because its fixed startup bank decoded farther than
    |       the F6 reset-bank hypothesis.
    |     * JANE is now a family-gated mapper during control-flow refinement, like the
    |       other distinctive mapper families. Automatic JANE requires the raw
    |       `AD F1 FF 60` (`LDA $FFF1; RTS`) detector signature or explicit VCSC JANE
    |       metadata; mapper-dependent execution coherence alone cannot select it.
    |     * Do not use modeled `$1FF0/$1FF1` accesses alone as automatic JANE proof. A
    |       wrong startup mapping can manufacture such accesses from unrelated bytes.
    |       `--mapper jane` remains available for unsigned/experimental JANE images.
    |     * A focused regression makes F6 halt while evidence-free JANE returns, and
    |       requires the result to remain F6. The semantic round-trip suite is 123/123.
    |       The four supplied JANE-vs-F6 mismatches now identify as F6 and reconstruct
    |       byte-exactly.
    | 
    | 43. [x] Harden UA/UASW family and duplicated-4K inference
    |     * An 8K image whose two 4K halves are byte-identical is now treated as one
    |       logical unbanked 4K cartridge before 8K mapper detection, while the second
    |       physical copy is emitted raw for byte-exact reconstruction. This fixes a
    |       corpus false-UA case whose duplicated 4K program happened to contain UA
    |       access signatures.
    |     * UA/UASW hypotheses no longer survive merely because their broad partial-
    |       address decoder turns ordinary accesses into modeled hotspots. Automatic
    |       family evidence requires a historical UA byte signature, explicit VCSC
    |       UA/UASW metadata, or the stronger semantic fact that a modeled switch
    |       avoids a reachable JAM/KIL.
    |     * A raw UA-family signature establishes the family but not selector polarity.
    |       When both UA and UASW remain viable and neither has explicit variant metadata,
    |       a bank-changing RESET edge under exactly one variant selects that polarity.
    |       Evidence-free competing 8K hypotheses cannot steal an independently
    |       identified viable UA family merely by having a halt-free startup graph.
    |     * Added doubled-4K, raw-UA, and raw-UASW exact-round-trip fixtures. Focused
    |       disassembler round trips are 126/126. Corpus cases 3290d62b... -> 4K,
    |       4e4a263c... -> UA, and d214c7a7... -> UASW now match Stella and reconstruct
    |       byte-exactly.
    | 
    | 44. [x] Prevent raw UA signatures from overriding established narrow-selector flow
    |     * Super Soccer (Digivision) contains one unreachable raw `LDA $02C0` byte
    |       sequence but executes four genuine F8 `$1FF8/$1FF9` bank-changing edges.
    |       Item 43's UA-family gate admitted UA/UASW from the raw pattern and then
    |       pruned evidence-free alternatives before F8's stronger flow evidence could
    |       win, regressing a previous F8 classification to UASW.
    |     * A raw UA-family signature now nominates UA/UASW without suppressing another
    |       viable mapper that has already demonstrated mapper-specific bank-changing
    |       flow through a narrow selector. The existing later cross-switch preference
    |       can therefore choose F8 over broad UA aliases when actual execution supports
    |       F8. Raw UA evidence still works when no stronger competing mapper flow exists.
    |     * `f8_raw_ua_data.bin` pins an unreachable `LDA $02C0` alongside genuine F8
    |       switching. Corpus `0e7e73421606873b544e858c59dc283e` is F8 again and
    |       reconstructs byte-exactly.
    | 
    | 45. [x] Support Stella's 4K CV preservation/storage forms
    |     * CV hardware still contains only 2K of ROM at `$F800-$FFFF`, but Stella also
    |       accepts 4096-byte images. For those images the final 2K are the executable
    |       ROM; the first 1K initializes CV cartridge RAM and the middle 1K is preserved
    |       storage. A doubled `2K || 2K` CV dump is another common 4K representation.
    |     * Automatic 4K detection now considers the established CV write signatures
    |       before ordinary 4K/FC inference. Static and concrete mapping fetch CV ROM
    |       from physical file offset `$0800` for a 4K storage image, and concrete reset
    |       seeds the 1K CV RAM from the first 1K to match Stella semantics.
    |     * Source emission preserves the complete first 2K raw and emits the analyzed
    |       final 2K at `.org $0800`, giving exact 4096-byte reconstruction for both save
    |       and doubled forms. `cv_saved_4k.bin` and `cv_doubled_4k.bin` pin both layouts.
    |       Corpus `14d365bbfaac3d20c6119591f57acca4` and
    |       `3ec12372ca3e870b11ca70edc7ec26a4` now identify as CV and round-trip exactly.
