    | IMPLEMENTED CHECKPOINT -- TRUE DUAL RESP PROOF + HYBRID FALLBACK -- 2026-08-21
    | ==============================================================================
    | This section supersedes the pair-classifier-only status above for the current
    | progress tree.  It is a timing-stable proof checkpoint, NOT final horizontal
    | calibration.
    | 
    | WHAT NOW WORKS
    | --------------
    | * The zero-RAM same-Y/same-coarse classifier now feeds a real same-scanline
    |   dual-RESP raster path.  Pair metadata is staged on its own PF-aware line and
    |   the marked P0 setup vector selects one of eleven pair landing stubs; the
    |   selected group emits consecutive RESP0/RESP1 strobes while both target lanes
    |   are blank, then rejoins the existing P0 setup/HMOVE pipeline.
    | * A deterministic same-slot pair (logical sprites 2/3, X=31/32, Y=74) exercises
    |   a true dual-RESP physical line.  The timing harness observes RESP0 at
    |   instruction-end phase 25 and RESP1 at 28 for that slot.
    | * Combined randomized X/Y stress remains frame-stable.  In addition to the
    |   asymmetric regression's seeded runs, four independent 10,000-frame runs
    |   completed at 262 displayed NTSC lines while requiring at least one physical
    |   line containing both RESP0 and RESP1.
    | * A discovered top-edge pair bug is currently avoided by falling back to the
    |   proven two-one-RESP schedule for the exact marked-pair case that enters through
    |   the action-95 top trigger.  Public Y=89 is NOT intrinsically incompatible with
    |   dual RESP: Y=89 merely selects the special top-entry path, while horizontal
    |   slot/phase determines whether a particular pair is feasible.  The exact former
    |   crash state remains pinned as a regression until the top dispatcher is made
    |   pair-aware.
    | * `vcs_frame_timing` gained repeated `--set-zp`, `--require-resp-phases`, and
    |   `--require-dual-resp` controls so the asymmetric test can require the ordinary
    |   eleven-phase lattice while also proving that a true pair path was exercised.
    | 
    | CURRENT BUILD
    | -------------
    | The current public asymmetric example builds at approximately:
    | 
    |     ROM 3905 / 4090
    |     RAM 119 / 128
    | 
    | The increase from the classifier checkpoint is the experimental shared dual-
    | RESP machinery.  It still fits the 4K target without adding RAM.
    | 
    | IMPORTANT STELLA FINDING -- X TABLE IS STILL PROVISIONAL
    | -------------------------------------------------------
    | Stella 7.0 pixel snapshots disproved the source comment that the 160-entry
    | `TEMPLATE_position_table` had already been calibrated for this renderer.  A
    | normal, non-dual P0/P1 case requesting X=31 and X=100 rendered at roughly X=10
    | and X=73.  The deterministic dual case requesting X=31/32 rendered its two
    | players around X=13 and X=23.  Thus this is not merely a special pair-table
    | problem: the ordinary asymmetric position table is still inherited from the
    | older RESP timing and must be recalibrated against the measured asymmetric
    | phase lattice before horizontal placement can be called correct.
    | 
    | The observed ordinary displacement closely tracks the phase difference between
    | this renderer's 14,19,24,28,33,36,42,47,50,56,61 lattice and the maintained
    | symmetric renderer's later 21,26,31,36,41,46,51,56,61,66,71 lattice.  Use that
    | as a calibration lead, but certify the resulting table with Stella pixels rather
    | than trusting arithmetic alone.
    | 
    | HYBRID SCHEDULER / VDEL DESIGN DECISION
    | ---------------------------------------
    | Do not classify pair scheduling by Y alone and do not require literal same-line
    | dual RESP for every overlap.  Classify each accepted P0/P1 pair by the complete
    | feasibility tuple `(X0, X1, Y)` (plus the derived coarse slots/RESP phases).  Y
    | selects the available vertical entry/setup windows; X0/X1 determine the RESP
    | landing phases inside those windows.  Choose the least expensive correct class:
    | 
    | 1. Ordinary independent one-RESP positioning when the two events do not need a
    |    coupled setup window.
    | 2. Adjacent-scanline split positioning when both lanes are blank and one RESP can
    |    be issued a scanline early.  Give each lane its ordinary calibrated RESP phase
    |    on its own physical line, preload both HMP values, then apply them together at
    |    the later HMOVE.  This should be the preferred paired case because it reuses
    |    the proven one-RESP X calibration.
    | 3. Literal same-line dual RESP only for `(X0, X1, Y)` geometries where the two
    |    strobes and surrounding PF/setup work fit cleanly and where the dual path has
    |    its own Stella-verified horizontal calibration.
    | 4. VDELP0/VDELP1-assisted split when graphics-data handoff, rather than RESP, is
    |    the limiting deadline.  VDEL can let GRPx be prepared on the preceding line,
    |    but it does not make RESP cheaper and its cross-coupled GRP latch semantics
    |    require deliberate use.
    | 5. Fair flicker/omission only when no non-flicker feasibility class fits.  Keep
    |    persistent rotating allocation priority so omission is distributed rather
    |    than starving one sprite.
    | 
    | Public Y=89 is therefore only a top-entry case, not a blanket split-only rule.
    | The current implementation still falls back for the known marked-pair top-entry
    | state because that dispatcher is not pair-aware yet; future scheduling should
    | allow any Y=89 `(X0, X1)` combination whose measured cycle window is safe.
    | 
    | NEXT WORK
    | ---------
    | 1. Recalibrate the ordinary P0/P1 `TEMPLATE_position_table` for public X=0..159
    |    against the actual eleven asymmetric RESP phases; verify edges/wraps in
    |    Stella, not just the timing harness.
    | 2. Prototype the adjacent-scanline split pair path so it can reuse those ordinary
    |    calibrated positions.  Build a feasibility classifier over `(X0, X1, Y)`; do
    |    not encode Y=89 as a permanent split-only exception.
    | 3. Make the action-95 top dispatcher pair-aware, then compare split versus literal
    |    dual RESP across same-slot, adjacent-slot, moderate, and far-separated X pairs
    |    including Y=89.  Retain literal dual RESP only where it genuinely reduces
    |    scheduling pressure and its measured phases are safe.
    | 4. Use VDELP0/VDELP1 only if the resulting graphics handoff, rather than RESP,
    |    is the limiting cycle budget.
    | 5. Finish the stale per-event color handoff after the pair raster structure and
    |    X calibration are stable; current draw color still caches the first compact
    |    event's color.
    | 6. Keep the maintained symmetric enhanced-multisprite regression and all branch,
    |    hygiene, branding, example-build, and asymmetric timing gates green.

    | IMPLEMENTED CHECKPOINT -- 12-PHASE ORDINARY X CALIBRATION -- 2026-08-21
    | ==============================================================================
    | This checkpoint supersedes the provisional ordinary-X status above.  The passing
    | renderer was restored after rejecting the first far-right late-family experiment.
    | 
    | PASSING ORDINARY RESP LATTICE
    | -----------------------------
    | Ordinary P0 and P1 positioning now uses the exact instruction-end phase set:
    | 
    |     14,19,24,28,33,37,42,47,50,53,58,63
    | 
    | The extra phase relative to the prior eleven-slot checkpoint closes ordinary
    | coarse-position holes without destabilizing frame timing.  The asymmetric timing
    | regression passes combined randomized X/Y stress at 262 displayed NTSC lines and
    | still requires the true dual-RESP proof path to execute.  Current public example
    | accounting at this checkpoint is approximately 3915/4090 ROM and 119/128 RAM.
    | 
    | STELLA CALIBRATION FINDINGS
    | ---------------------------
    | Stella 7.0 pixel checks confirm that the revised ordinary mapping is exact for
    | checked middle/left positions.  Verified examples include P0 public X=31 and X=55
    | and P1 public X=20 and X=100.  X=55 was one of the former arithmetic-hole cases,
    | so the phase-37 correction is doing real useful work rather than merely changing
    | the timing harness.
    | 
    | Do NOT extrapolate the simple `(RESP phase delta) * 3 color clocks` arithmetic
    | through HBLANK.  Provisional far-right entries around public X=140 and X=159
    | wrapped back toward the left edge in Stella.  The remaining ordinary-X problem is
    | therefore specifically the far-right/HBLANK timing class, not a generally bad
    | position table.  Full X=0..159 certification is still incomplete.
    | 
    | REJECTED FIRST LATE-X FAMILY
    | ----------------------------
    | An experimental expanded late-RESP family attempted to carry the continuing
    | graphics byte across WSYNC so HMOVE could remain at cycle 0 while supporting
    | far-right phases.  It was rejected and is NOT present in this checkpoint.  The
    | first arrangement disturbed shared dispatch control flow: the static scene
    | collapsed to RESP0 phases 28/61 and emitted no RESP1 at all.  This was a routing
    | regression, not evidence that late horizontal positioning is impossible.  The
    | known-good 12-phase renderer was restored before this checkpoint.
    | 
    | SCHEDULER CONSEQUENCE
    | ---------------------
    | Continue using the `(X0, X1, Y)` feasibility paradigm.  Add far-right/HBLANK as
    | an explicit ordinary-positioning class rather than pretending all X values share
    | one modulo phase model.  Prefer adjacent-scanline split RESP for paired sprites
    | when it lets each lane reuse a Stella-certified ordinary position.  Retain literal
    | same-line dual RESP only where its complete geometry is measured safe.  VDELP0/1
    | remains an option when GRPx handoff, rather than RESP itself, is the limiting
    | deadline; fair rotating flicker remains the last-resort class.
    | 
    | NEXT WORK
    | ---------
    | 1. Design a safe far-right/HBLANK ordinary-X path without rearranging or corrupting
    |    the existing P0/P1 dispatch families.
    | 2. Stella-sweep the complete public X=0..159 range for both lanes and pin the
    |    resulting mapping in regression data.
    | 3. Feed that certified ordinary mapping into the `(X0,X1,Y)` feasibility classifier
    |    and implement the preferred adjacent-scanline split-pair path.
    | 4. Revisit literal dual-RESP calibration only for geometries where it is actually
    |    cheaper than the split path.

    | IMPLEMENTED CHECKPOINT -- 152-CYCLE PHASE-73 SPLIT PROOF -- 2026-08-21
    | ===============================================================================
    | This supersedes the literal-dual pair proof as the preferred pair architecture.
    | The maintained ordinary one-RESP paths are still on the provisional 12-phase
    | lattice; this checkpoint proves the new two-line scheduler at the hardest coarse
    | position before migrating every ordinary X entry.
    | 
    | HARDWARE-DERIVED FINAL COARSE LATTICE
    | -------------------------------------
    | Treat each two-scanline logical band as one 152-CPU-cycle scheduling unit.  The
    | beam still wraps every 76 CPU cycles, but there is no need to execute WSYNC at
    | the internal A/B boundary when the path is cycle exact.
    | 
    | The final target coarse RESP write phases and HMP coverage are:
    | 
    |     slot   line-A RESP   line-B RESP   coarse X   HMP-covered hardware X
    |       0         23            99            6             -1 .. 14
    |       1         28           104           21             14 .. 29
    |       2         33           109           36             29 .. 44
    |       3         38           114           51             44 .. 59
    |       4         43           119           66             59 .. 74
    |       5         48           124           81             74 .. 89
    |       6         53           129           96             89 .. 104
    |       7         58           134          111            104 .. 119
    |       8         63           139          126            119 .. 134
    |       9         68           144          141            134 .. 149
    |      10         73           149          156            149 .. 164
    | 
    | Five CPU cycles move RESP by 15 color clocks, while the sixteen HMP values span
    | one overlapping 16-pixel interval.  Eleven slots therefore cover the complete
    | 0..159 hardware range continuously.  Cycle 75 is the wrap cliff; the last useful
    | coarse write is phase 73, with one slot of fine-motion reach still available.
    | 
    | FINAL X MAPPING IS A LOOKUP TABLE, NOT RUNTIME MATH
    | ---------------------------------------------------
    | Do not add divide-by-15, modulo, shift-bucket, or correction arithmetic to the
    | renderer.  The final implementation should keep one direct 160-byte packed table
    | indexed by public X.  Each byte carries HMP in the high nibble and coarse slot
    | 0..10 in the low nibble, exactly like the current provisional table.
    | 
    | VCSC public X uses the existing multiplexed-player convention: public X selects
    | hardware origin X-1 modulo 160.  With the hardware-derived lattice, the target
    | packed table is therefore:
    | 
    |     0xda,0x60,0x50,0x40,0x30,0x20,0x10,0x00,0xf0,0xe0,0xd0,0xc0,0xb0,0xa0,0x90,0x80,
    |     0x61,0x51,0x41,0x31,0x21,0x11,0x01,0xf1,0xe1,0xd1,0xc1,0xb1,0xa1,0x91,0x81,0x62,
    |     0x52,0x42,0x32,0x22,0x12,0x02,0xf2,0xe2,0xd2,0xc2,0xb2,0xa2,0x92,0x82,0x63,0x53,
    |     0x43,0x33,0x23,0x13,0x03,0xf3,0xe3,0xd3,0xc3,0xb3,0xa3,0x93,0x83,0x64,0x54,0x44,
    |     0x34,0x24,0x14,0x04,0xf4,0xe4,0xd4,0xc4,0xb4,0xa4,0x94,0x84,0x65,0x55,0x45,0x35,
    |     0x25,0x15,0x05,0xf5,0xe5,0xd5,0xc5,0xb5,0xa5,0x95,0x85,0x66,0x56,0x46,0x36,0x26,
    |     0x16,0x06,0xf6,0xe6,0xd6,0xc6,0xb6,0xa6,0x96,0x86,0x67,0x57,0x47,0x37,0x27,0x17,
    |     0x07,0xf7,0xe7,0xd7,0xc7,0xb7,0xa7,0x97,0x87,0x68,0x58,0x48,0x38,0x28,0x18,0x08,
    |     0xf8,0xe8,0xd8,0xc8,0xb8,0xa8,0x98,0x88,0x69,0x59,0x49,0x39,0x29,0x19,0x09,0xf9,
    |     0xe9,0xd9,0xc9,0xb9,0xa9,0x99,0x89,0x6a,0x5a,0x4a,0x3a,0x2a,0x1a,0x0a,0xfa,0xea
    | 
    | FIRST IMPLEMENTED 152-CYCLE CLASS -- X=159/X=159
    | -------------------------------------------------
    | The current renderer now routes only an exact same-Y public X=159/X=159 pair
    | through the new split path.  This is deliberately narrow: it proves the hardest
    | coarse phase while every other pair geometry retains the known-good independent
    | one-RESP behavior.
    | 
    | Physical line A is cycle exact with no terminal WSYNC:
    | 
    | * HMP0 is loaded before the strobe.
    | * Left PF writes complete first; right PF0/PF1 remain in their safe windows.
    | * Paired-P1 metadata is staged before right PF2 so PF2 lands at cycle 65.
    | * RESP0 writes at cycle 73.
    | * A three-cycle BIT consumes the remainder and execution falls into line B at
    |   absolute cycle 76.
    | 
    | Physical line B repeats the PF schedule, writes RESP1 at local cycle 73
    | (absolute 149), then immediately executes STA HMOVE so the HMOVE write lands at
    | absolute cycle 152 / cycle 0 of the following physical scanline.  There is no
    | WSYNC at the A/B boundary and no WSYNC before this HMOVE.  The pair then writes
    | GRP1 and spends the ordinary HMOVE-equivalent three cycles on a JMP, rejoining
    | the existing Setup0 path at exactly cycle 6; subsequent PF phases are unchanged.
    | 
    | The old provisional ordinary table encodes public X=159 with HMP nibble $c.
    | The hardware-derived phase-73 slot requires $e.  This narrow proof uses EOR #$20
    | on the two pair HMP bytes under the beam.  That is a temporary proof device, not
    | the final mapping method: migration to the direct 160-byte target table above
    | must remove this special correction.
    | 
    | VALIDATION
    | ----------
    | * Public asymmetric example: 3814/4090 ROM, 119/128 RAM at this checkpoint.
    | * `vcs_enhanced_multisprite_asymmetric.pl` passes combined randomized X/Y frame
    |   stress and pins an exact X=159/X=159 same-Y pair.  The timing harness now has
    |   `--require-adjacent-resp`; the pinned case also requires RESP phase 73 on both
    |   lanes.  Same-line dual RESP is no longer a completion requirement.
    | * Stella 7.0 pixel snapshot of the exact pair shows the expected wrapped glyph
    |   extent at hardware X=158..159 and X=0..5.  That matches the logical sprite-0
    |   shape at public X=159 and proves the phase-73 coarse position plus HMP result
    |   is pixel-correct, not merely frame-stable.
    | * The earlier phase-33 split experiment also passed timing and Stella for public
    |   X=30/X=41 before this harder phase-73 class replaced it in the maintained tree.
    |

    | IMPLEMENTED CHECKPOINT -- ORDINARY EDGE REMAP AND MOTION SWEEP -- 2026-08-21
    | =============================================================================
    | This checkpoint temporarily retires the experimental adjacent-line/same-line
    | pair raster paths from the active visible scheduler while ordinary positioning,
    | playfield timing, color, and frame stability are made correct first.  Pair
    | metadata may remain in preparation code, but no adjacent-line RESP proof is a
    | current completion contract.
    | 
    | CURRENT ORDINARY RESP LATTICE
    | -----------------------------
    | The active ordinary positioner now uses these twelve measured RESP write phases
    | on both hardware lanes:
    | 
    |     23,28,33,37,42,47,50,53,58,63,68,73
    | 
    | The three former provisional left-edge slots were repurposed rather than adding
    | new dispatch entries:
    | 
    | * slot 0 -> phase 23 for public X=1..15;
    | * slot 1 -> phase 68 for public X=136..150;
    | * slot 2 -> phase 73 for public X=0 and X=151..159.
    | 
    | Their HMP nibbles use the hardware-derived final edge mapping.  Middle X values
    | still use the proven provisional slots 3..11 and therefore this is NOT yet the
    | final eleven-slot 23+5n lattice.  The final target remains
    | 23,28,33,38,43,48,53,58,63,68,73 with one direct packed X->{HMP,slot} lookup.
    | 
    | MOVEMENT-SENSITIVE FRAME REGRESSION
    | -----------------------------------
    | Static independent-X tests were insufficient: the user observed frame-count
    | changes while physically moving sprite 0.  `vcs_frame_timing` therefore has a
    | `--sweep-zp ADDR MIN MAX` mode that mutates one zero-page byte once per
    | synchronized frame in a triangular sequence.  The asymmetric regression now
    | moves game_x[0] continuously 0..159..0 and checks every resulting frame.  The
    | current edge-remapped tree is stable at 262 displayed lines for that sweep and
    | combined randomized X/Y stress.
    | 
    | The regression also asserts the exact active twelve-phase set above and pins
    | phase 73 on both hardware lanes.  Obsolete requirements for phases 14/19/24 and
    | for the retired adjacent-line pair proof were removed; those assertions described
    | an earlier experimental architecture rather than the current renderer.
    | 
    | RECOVERED VISUAL FIXES -- 2026-08-21
    | --------------------------------------
    | The visual fixes from the interrupted work window have been reconstructed and
    | revalidated.  The resulting cartridge is 3932/4090 ROM bytes and 119/128 RAM
    | bytes, matching the last known-good accounting before the rejected PF-pad
    | experiment.
    | 
    | Three concrete defects are fixed without moving the proven RESP phases:
    | 
    | * Per-event color handoff now advances `pair_y` from the compact `lane_for[]`
    |   color stream on each lane-specific setup path.  P0 pays for the indexed color
    |   fetch with the old padding/CLC cycles and uses the still-set carry with
    |   `ADC #5`; P1 advances Y/setup_index together and uses an absolute gfx-index
    |   store to keep its line at exactly 76 cycles.  Simulator instrumentation saw
    |   the configured event colors individually rather than rank 0 being reused.
    | * A newly repositioned P1 is kept blank on its first post-setup line.  The old
    |   path could write `current_gfx+1` from the previous P1 occupant, producing the
    |   one-row horizontal fragment seen above sprite 2.  The post-setup line now
    |   reloads the continuing P0 row and waits for P1's setup sentinel to advance.
    | * The late phase-53/58/63 PF family now writes right PF2 four cycles later while
    |   preserving RESP, by moving the existing DEX+NOP ahead of the PF2 store.
    |   Phase 63 additionally writes left PF1 three cycles earlier, then spends those
    |   three cycles on a JMP into the common tail.  The intended write phases are
    |   therefore approximately 50/55/60 for right PF2, with phase-63 left PF1 near
    |   cycle 25 instead of about 28.
    | 
    | `vcs_enhanced_multisprite_asymmetric.pl` still passes the static raster,
    | 12-phase randomized X stress, phase-73 edge case, continuous 0..159..0 movement,
    | and combined randomized X/Y stress.  Its structural checks now pin all three
    | recovered fixes so the exact regressions cannot silently return.
    | 
    | PF SEAMS CLOSED -- 2026-08-21
    | --------------------------------
    | Framebuffer comparison of the two physical rows that form each logical PF band
    | isolated two remaining three-cycle timing errors, and both are fixed without
    | lengthening a scanline or moving the proven RESP phases.
    | 
    | * P0 entered `PostSetupLineBBody` three cycles earlier than the equivalent P1
    |   path because P0 had already staged `event_code`.  Moving the existing
    |   `LDY gfx_index+1` ahead of the right-half PF0/PF1 rewrite spends the same three
    |   cycles in the same line but delays those right-half stores by three cycles.
    |   Right PF2 and the line endpoint are unchanged.  This fixes the seam where the
    |   tail of left PF1 (`$A5`) was being replaced by right PF1 (`$66`).
    | * P1 had a separate seam in `Setup1LineB`.  Moving the existing
    |   `STY setup_index` until after the right PF1 store advances the affected left
    |   PF writes and right PF0/PF1 by three cycles, while leaving right PF2 and the
    |   76-cycle endpoint unchanged.
    | 
    | A Stella framebuffer oracle comparing paired physical rows now reports zero PF
    | mismatches in the default scene and in three substantially different X/Y
    | arrangements.  The static raster, twelve-phase randomized X stress, phase-73
    | edge case, continuous 0..159..0 movement, and combined randomized X/Y timing
    | regression all remain stable at 262 displayed lines.  The public example remains
    | 3932/4090 ROM bytes and 119/128 RAM bytes.
    | 
    | TOP BAND PLAYFIELD PRELOAD -- 2026-08-21
    | -----------------------------------------
    | The remaining corruption in the first visible playfield scanline had a different
    | cause from the setup-path PF seams above.  `game_draw()` enters the first visible
    | scanline about nine CPU cycles after its left-edge PF deadline, so band 95's
    | PF0/PF1/PF2 cannot be established from inside `TEMPLATE_draw()` before the beam
    | has already emitted pixels.
    | 
    | The maintained fix preloads band 95's left PF0/PF1/PF2 at the end of
    | `TEMPLATE_vblank_worker()` while VBLANK is still asserted.  The first visible
    | line therefore no longer tries to establish its left PF half after the beam has
    | already started.  Its right-half PF0/PF1 are moved three cycles earlier by
    | relocating one existing three-cycle BIT pad until after PF1; total line length
    | is unchanged.  Physical line B now includes the ordinary line-B graphics-state
    | work and center PF2 pad before its right-half rewrite, closing the separate
    | second-line seam without changing the following position/setup schedule.
    | 
    | A Stella 7.0 1x snapshot of the default scene shows zero PF/background pixel
    | differences between the two physical rows of the top band, and the next bands
    | also pair exactly.  `vcs_enhanced_multisprite_asymmetric.pl` remains stable at
    | 262 displayed lines through static raster, twelve-phase randomized X stress,
    | phase-73 edge coverage, the continuous 0..159..0 movement sweep, and combined
    | randomized X/Y stress.  The public 4K diagnostic still happens to fit at
    | 3967/4090 ROM bytes and 119/128 RAM bytes, but 4K is no longer a design
    | constraint.  This checkpoint intentionally contains only the playfield repair;
    | the unfinished Y=90..95 sprite-preload prototype is not present.
    | 
    | TOP-EDGE SPRITE PRELOAD -- WORKING CHECKPOINT -- 2026-08-21
    | ------------------------------------------------------------
    | The public Y range is now 0..95.  Sprites whose ordinary visible-raster setup
    | would begin above band 95 are detected as a descending event prefix and are
    | pre-positioned during VBLANK.  The VBLANK helper performs RESP/HMOVE with the
    | renderer's public X convention, loads the correct per-event color, and seeds the
    | graphics-state chain so Y=90..95 enters the visible raster at the correct clipped
    | row.  Y=95 reaches the first visible playfield band instead of stopping six
    | logical bands short.
    | 
    | The public asymmetric example is now F8SC/8K, as explicitly allowed by the user;
    | there is no longer a 4K packing requirement for this renderer.  The top preload
    | needs a larger share of the unchanged total blanking budget, so the example
    | defines `VCS_NTSC_EXTENDED_VBLANK`: VBLANK uses 51 TIM64T quanta and overscan 25
    | (instead of 42/34).  This 51/25 split was the experimentally sufficient worst
    | case for two simultaneous Y=95 sprites.
    | 
    | Before this checkpoint, Stella snapshots verified Y=95 on both hardware lanes
    | with independent X positions and correct colors.  The top-band playfield preload
    | repair remains intact.  The current generic `vcs_frame_timing` helper does not
    | model F8SC and therefore cannot yet serve as the timing oracle for this 8K
    | example; the old asymmetric Perl regression also still assumes a 4K image and
    | Y<=89.  Regression migration to F8SC/Y<=95 remains follow-up work, not a reason
    | to remove the working top-edge implementation.
    |

    | F8SC REGRESSION MIGRATION -- 2026-08-21
    | ----------------------------------------

    | checkpoint.  It now builds with `-DVCS_NTSC_EXTENDED_VBLANK -T vcs.cfg`, requires
    | the 8192-byte F8SC image, certifies two simultaneous Y=95 sprites, and sweeps one
    | sprite through Y=89..95.

    | `test/vcs_frame_timing.cpp` now accepts either the historical 4K image or an 8K
    | F8/F8SC image.  For 8K it models $1FF8/$1FF9 bank selection, the 6507's 13-bit
    | cartridge mirroring (so linker-logical $Dxxx/$Fxxx addresses reach the same
    | physical window), and the shared 128-byte Superchip RAM ports.  All 14 tests
    | that compile this helper pass, preserving the existing 4K timing oracles.
    | 
    | The example assembly allowlist was deliberately refreshed for the F8SC source
    | change; its old rationale no longer claims this diagnostic is 4K.
    | 
    | A stronger experiment exposed one timing edge not yet closed: changing the
    | existing six-sprite combined randomized Y range from 0..89 to 0..95 can rarely
    | miss the 51-TIM64T VBLANK deadline.  One reproducible busy layout has two
    | accepted top events at Y=93.  The explicit two-Y=95 regression and the Y=89..95
    | sweep are stable.  Keep those top-edge tests and fix the worst-case VBLANK path
    | before broadening the six-way randomized range; do not hide the miss by merely
    | increasing a test timeout.

    | F8 VISIBLE-BANK HANDOFF FIX -- 2026-08-21
    | ------------------------------------------
    | The two thin/corrupt scanlines still visible above the intended top playfield
    | were not another PF-phase error.  They appeared only after the diagnostic moved
    | to F8SC.  `run_asymmetric_frame()` was left in startup `bank0` while
    | `game_draw()` and its PF tables lived in `bank1`; `vcs_ntsc_end_vblank()` cleared
    | VBLANK first, then the linker-generated cross-bank JSR bridge ran with the beam
    | visible.  Stella consequently exposed two bridge-time scanlines before the
    | 192-line renderer started.  A native 1x snapshot contained 194 nonblank rows;
    | the first row repeated the preloaded left PF state across both halves and the
    | second repeated the stale right PF state.
    | 
    | The example now pins `run_asymmetric_frame()` to `bank1`, co-resident with
    | `game_draw()`, so the only frame-loop bank handoff occurs while VBLANK is still
    | asserted.  Removing the visible bridge shortened the CPU harness frame by one
    | raw scanline, so the example adds one final blanked WSYNC after
    | `vcs_ntsc_end_overscan()` to preserve the existing Stella-calibrated 262-line
    | frame budget.  This WSYNC occurs with VBLANK still asserted and cannot affect
    | the visible raster.
    | 
    | Stella 7.0 1x now shows exactly 192 nonblank rows, and the first four rows have
    | identical intended PF/background pixel counts.  The asymmetric timing regression
    | again passes all static, edge, top-Y, continuous-X, and combined X/Y cases.  The
    | regression also checks the link map so `run_asymmetric_frame -> game_draw` cannot
    | silently regain a bank bridge.
    | 
    | NEXT WORK UPDATE

    | 4K RECOVERY + FULL-Y DEADLINE CLOSEOUT -- 2026-08-21
    | -----------------------------------------------------
    | The F8SC detour is no longer required.  The public asymmetric diagnostic is back
    | on an ordinary unbanked 4096-byte cartridge and uses no Superchip RAM.  The key
    | packing win is an example-specific 28-byte reset stub: this diagnostic explicitly
    | initializes every writable DATA value and has no constructors, so it can clear
    | RIOT RAM/TIA directly and tail-enter `main` instead of linking the generic
    | 328-byte copy/zero/init runtime.  The resulting public image is 3752/4090 ROM
    | bytes and 115/128 RIOT RAM bytes, leaving 338 ROM bytes and 13 RAM bytes free.
    | The Makefile deliberately uses `-nostdlib`; the startup file warns that it is not
    | suitable for programs that rely on nonzero DATA initializers or constructors.
    | 
    | The last known full-Y timing failures are also closed at their root causes:
    | 
    | * `ChooseLane` now rejects prior sprites at |dy|>=15 before loading lane metadata
    |   or running lane-specific conflict checks.  Busy six-sprite frames therefore
    |   avoid paying the expensive close-pair path for the overwhelmingly common far
    |   pairs.
    | * More importantly, every event whose setup `action_y` is >=95 is now consumed
    |   by the VBLANK top-preload prefix.  The earlier implementation used >=96, so a
    |   P1 at Y=92 (and P0 at Y=89) could still start setup on the first visible band.
    |   The special visible action-95 path then reached its second WSYNC seven cycles
    |   into the following scanline, producing a one-line frame extension.  VBLANK
    |   preloading now includes those events.  An extra six-state blank predecessor
    |   chain in otherwise unreachable `graphics_next` entries supports P0 Y=89.
    |   With no action-95 event left for visible time, the obsolete `TopTrigger` path
    |   is removed and the top line-B tail uses the ordinary graphics continuation.
    | 
    | The formerly failing static layout X={14,97,9,53,99,15},
    | Y={79,21,92,56,84,84} is stable.  More importantly, the timing harness passes
    | 4997 checked randomized frames with all six X values in 0..159 and all six Y
    | values in 0..95.  `vcs_enhanced_multisprite_asymmetric.pl` now uses that full
    | Y range for all three combined randomized seeds, builds the actual 4K/nostdlib
    | public cartridge, and pins the >=95 VBLANK-preload contract so the visible
    | action-95 regression cannot return.  The example assembly allowlist and source
    | tree hygiene checks are updated and passing.
    | 
    | CURRENT NEXT WORK
    | -----------------
    | 1. Stella-certify every public X=0..159 on both lanes, especially the transition
    |    boundaries 159<->0<->1, 15<->16, 135<->136, and 150<->151.
    | 2. Migrate the middle X range from the current twelve measured phases to the
    |    intended final eleven-slot lattice 23,28,33,38,43,48,53,58,63,68,73 if the
    |    pixel oracle confirms it.  Do not sacrifice the now-proven 4K/full-Y timing.
    | 3. Reintroduce the richer `(X0,X1,Y)` pair-feasibility scheduler only after the
    |    ordinary positioner is pixel-certified; use independent/adjacent RESP where
    |    feasible and fair flicker only as the last fallback.
    | 4. Finish with the full repository suite and a final Stella torture pass.

    | The source-adjacent
    | `enhanced_multisprite_192_asymmetric_startup.s26` is now part of the handoff.
    | `test/vcs_examples_build.pl` also has a general public-example convention:
    | when `<stem>_startup.s26` exists beside an editable `<stem>.c26`, the smoke build
    | uses `-nostdlib` and links that startup after the C source.  This makes the
    | recursive example corpus exercise the same runtime topology as the public
    | Makefile rather than silently substituting the generic startup.

    | startup was omitted by `make tar`'s temporary `git add -A` even though it existed
    | in the working tree.  The asymmetric startup now has an explicit negated
    | `.gitignore` entry, matching the existing faithful-multisprite data exception.

    | DIAGNOSTIC PLAYFIELD REWORK -- 2026-08-22
    | ------------------------------------------
    | The public asymmetric diagnostic now uses twelve 16-scanline visual rows (eight
    | logical two-scanline bands each) instead of six 32-scanline rows.  Its full
    | 40-bit asymmetric playfield is a set of one-bit diagonals with exactly five
    | blank playfield bits between diagonals; successive rows shift by one bit.  This
    | makes stale PF bytes, half-screen timing errors, and row-boundary mistakes much
    | easier to spot while preserving the established raster timing.
    | 
    | The focused asymmetric regression remains stable.  This checkpoint does not yet
    | change sprite arbitration: the 0/1/2 same-Y cluster still uses the current
    | two-of-three policy, and the reported disappearance of sprite 5 remains the
    | immediate scheduler bug to root-cause.  Fix sprite 5 before extending same-line
    | horizontal reuse for the 0/1/2 cluster.
    | 
    | SCHEDULER MONTE CARLO + ONE-SPRITE COLLAPSE FIX -- 2026-08-22
    | -------------------------------------------------------------
    | The user's report that a busy layout can collapse to only one visible logical
    | sprite is reproduced independently of the raster.  New host model
    | `test/vcs_enhanced_multisprite_asymmetric_scheduler.c` mirrors the renderer's
    | P0/P1 Y-conflict rules and runs millions of random X/Y layouts.  The historical
    | greedy allocator has a deterministic one-sprite witness with identity priority:
    | 
    |   X={50,34,100,133,115,97}
    |   Y={16,20,20,18,18,20}
    | 
    | The root cause is lane orientation, not frame timing.  The first candidate was
    | always seeded on P0.  When priority[1] is 1..5 logical bands above it, that P0
    | choice leaves candidate two blocked on P0 by the 15-band same-lane setup window
    | and blocked on P1 by the asymmetric opposite-lane setup hazard.  In a narrow
    | pile the same bad seed can strand the second hardware player and cascade into a
    | one-sprite frame.
    | 
    | `ChooseLane` now looks only at priority[1] when placing the first accepted
    | sprite.  For a +1..+5 gap it seeds the first sprite on P1; all other cases retain
    | the historical P0 seed.  Exhaustive Y0/Y1 testing proves the first two priority
    | candidates are always accepted under the current conflict model.  Five million
    | deterministic Monte Carlo layouts report no one-sprite result (minimum two),
    | and fairness simulation reports a worst observed consecutive omission gap of
    | four frames.  A Stella version of the deterministic witness visibly changed
    | from one sprite to two, confirming that the host model found a real renderer
    | failure rather than a modeling artifact.
    | 
    | The focused asymmetric raster/X/Y regression still passes and the 4K public
    | example remains comfortably inside ROM/RAM at 3770/4090 ROM and 115/128 RAM.
    | The Perl regression compiles and runs a deterministic 250,000-layout copy of the
    | host scheduler model so the one-sprite failure cannot silently return.
    | 
    | This is deliberately a bounded repair, not the final scheduler.  Brute-force
    | comparison of the host model against all legal two-lane assignments confirms
    | that the priority-order greedy allocator can still omit more sprites than
    | necessary for some geometries.  Also, three exact-same-Y logical sprites cannot
    | all be represented by the current one-logical-sprite-per-hardware-lane model;
    | the requested solid 0/1/2 cluster requires the planned horizontal same-line
    | reuse/pair-feasibility work, not merely a different greedy lane choice.
    | 
    | CURRENT NEXT WORK
    | -----------------
    | 1. Use the host model as the cheap scheduler oracle while replacing avoidable
    |    greedy omissions with bounded lane rebalancing that preserves persistent
    |    priority/fairness and VBLANK timing.
    | 2. Reintroduce horizontal pair/same-line reuse so sufficiently separated
    |    same-Y triples such as the diagnostic 0/1/2 cluster need not flicker.
    | 3. Keep the 12x16 diagonal PF diagnostic and the full-Y/full-X timing sweeps
    |    active while scheduler changes are made.
    | 4. Stella-certify all X=0..159 on both lanes and finish the intended eleven-slot
    |    RESP lattice after scheduler correctness is stable.
    | 
    | SCHEDULER/Raster DIVERGENCE + PERSISTENT-MISSING-SPRITE FIX -- 2026-08-22
    | -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    | The user's clarification is important: the reported sprite 5 was not merely
    | absent in one captured flicker phase; it never appeared.  The earlier inference
    | that the screenshot represented a 3/4/5 same-Y triple was wrong.  In the public
    | diagnostic sprite 5 remains at Y=16; sprites 1/2 are at Y=76, sprites 3/4 are at
    | Y=42, and sprite 0 had been moved down near the bottom.  A permanently absent 5
    | therefore had to be treated as a scheduler/raster correctness failure, not as an
    | expected two-lane arbitration result.
    | 
    | Two concrete root causes were found.
    | 
    | 1. The 6502 allocator did not actually implement the host C model's close-above
    |    test.  `@TEMPLATE_ChooseAboveClose` correctly saved
    |    `candidate_y-other_y` and restored it into X, then loaded the prior sprite's
    |    lane into A and jumped to `@TEMPLATE_ChooseCandidateAtOrAbove`.  That label
    |    immediately executed `TAX`, replacing the real delta with the prior lane
    |    value (0 or 1).  Every close-above pair was therefore classified with a fake
    |    distance of zero or one.  The host Monte Carlo was modeling the intended
    |    algorithm and could not expose this assembly-only divergence.  The stray
    |    `TAX` is removed; X now remains the saved Y delta.
    | 
    | 2. The opposite-lane hazard was one band too narrow.  The scheduler allowed a
    |    P1 sprite six logical bands above a P0 sprite.  P1 action_y is Y+3 while P0
    |    action_y is Y+6, so that geometry produces only three action bands between
    |    the P1 event and the following P0 event.  The visible P1 post-setup pipeline
    |    does not return to the ordinary trigger test until action-4, so the following
    |    P0 event is swallowed.  The only supported three-band handoff is the opposite
    |    ordering produced by exact-same-Y P0 then P1.  All allocator/model
    |    opposite-lane checks and first/rank-4 lookaheads now use the correct +1..+6
    |    hazard rather than +1..+5.
    | 
    | A real 6502 witness pins the failure:
    | 
    |   X={143,82,135,107,49,159}
    |   Y={9,18,67,73,60,23}
    |   priority={5,1,2,0,3,4}
    | 
    | Before the repair the allocator emitted five draw records (scheduled mask $3e)
    | but the raster produced nonzero player graphics for only sprite 3 (visible mask
    | $08); missing mask $36 includes sprite 5.  A forced Stella snapshot showed the
    | same one-visible-sprite failure.  With both fixes the witness has a safe schedule
    | and every accepted sprite produces graphics.
    | 
    | `test/vcs_frame_timing.cpp` now has two test-only facilities needed to keep this
    | class of bug pinned: `--released-inputs` leaves SWCHA/SWCHB high so RESET is not
    | accidentally held during the run, and
    | `--verify-asymmetric-visibility Y COLOR DRAW COUNT` compares the scheduler's
    | compacted draw records against actual nonzero GRP0/GRP1 writes using the
    | example's per-sprite colors.  Y=0 is exempt because the maintained multisprite
    | contract defines it as the fully clipped bottom position.  `--require-visible-mask`
    | and `--expect-memory` support fixed-scene assertions.  This also exposed a
    | weakness in the older timing-only stress: its zero-initialized RIOT inputs held
    | RESET low, so the example reinitialized `game_priority` every overscan and never
    | exercised persistent fairness.
    | 
    | `vcs_enhanced_multisprite_asymmetric.pl` now includes both the five-scheduled /
    | one-visible 6502 witness above and a screenshot-family scene with
    | X={18,36,62,88,114,140}, Y={12,76,76,42,42,16}.  In the repaired renderer the
    | latter schedules six and all six actually appear.  A Stella 7.0 snapshot of that
    | scene visibly shows 0,1,2,3,4,5 together, including sprite 5 at its original
    | Y=16.
    | 
    | The C Monte Carlo is retained as the cheap allocation oracle, but it now also
    | checks that every accepted lane assignment produces a raster-safe action stream.
    | The mistaken screenshot-specific 3/4/5 fairness reconstruction was removed;
    | there is only a generic exact-same-Y fairness witness.  At 250,000 deterministic
    | layouts the maintained allocator reports:
    | 
    |   min=2, worst fairness gap=4
    |   histogram 2:205, 3:7172, 4:56665, 5:123611, 6:62347
    |   exact-search prefix=20000, avoidable greedy omissions=7188, worst optimal gap=3
    | 
    | The existing rank-4 one-candidate lookahead remains because it is provably
    | non-regressive and cheap.  A rank-3 safe lookahead was also prototyped in the C
    | model; it reduced the 20k exact-prefix avoidable count from 7188 to 6918 and was
    | non-regressive in a 500k-layout comparison.  The direct 6502 implementation was
    | rejected, however: its extra close-case scan exceeded the already-tight VBLANK
    | budget and produced a 265-raw-line frame at combined-stress frame 259 for seeds
    | 0x6d2b79f5/0x1234567b.  That experiment was fully reverted.  Future lane
    | rebalancing must reuse/compact existing scheduler work rather than simply adding
    | another accepted-list scan.
    | 
    | Current public size after the correctness repair is 3794/4090 ROM bytes and
    | 115/128 RIOT RAM bytes.  The focused asymmetric regression passes, including the
    | full-X/full-Y timing stress and the new scheduler-to-raster visibility checks.
    | 
    | CURRENT NEXT WORK
    | -----------------
    | 1. Treat the persistent missing-sprite bug as fixed and keep the new real-CPU
    |    visibility oracle active; do not infer sprite presence from draw records alone.
    | 2. Use the exact host search to find a VBLANK-cheap, non-regressive lane
    |    rebalancing scheme.  7188/20000 exact-prefix layouts still have avoidable
    |    greedy omissions, so unnecessary flicker remains measurable.
    | 3. Reintroduce horizontal same-line reuse/pair feasibility for the genuinely
    |    impossible one-sprite-per-lane cases (notably the 0/1/2 same-Y cluster).
    | 4. Preserve the 264-raw-line timing budget while doing scheduler work; the
    |    rejected rank-3 experiment demonstrates there is essentially no room for an
    |    extra close-pair scan.
    | 5. Continue Stella X=0..159 pixel certification and the final eleven-slot RESP
    |    lattice only after scheduler correctness remains stable.
    | 
    | ALL-OMISSION FAIRNESS REPAIR -- 2026-08-22
    | -------------------------------------------
    | The user's phosphor-on screenshot exposed a separate persistent-priority defect:
    | some logical sprites visibly flickered more often than peers even though no sprite
    | was permanently lost.  The old policy remembered only the last omission in a
    | frame and promoted that one logical id to the front of `priority[]`.  That policy
    | is fair for one isolated contention group, but not when two or more independent
    | groups omit sprites in the same frame.
    | 
    | A deterministic host witness makes the bias obvious without relying on visual
    | judgment:
    | 
    |   Y={76,76,76,42,42,42}
    | 
    | This is two independent exact-same-Y three-way piles far enough apart to reuse the
    | hardware lanes.  Four logical sprites can be shown each frame.  Over six frames,
    | the historical single-last-omission policy produces service counts:
    | 
    |   sprites 0..5: 5,5,2,5,5,2
    | 
    | so logical sprites 2 and 5 receive less than half the service of their peers.  A
    | fair omitted-first queue must instead produce exactly:
    | 
    |   sprites 0..5: 4,4,4,4,4,4
    | 
    | The scheduler now records every omission in a six-bit mask in `setup_code[6]`.
    | That replaces the old last-rank/last-id bookkeeping and is slightly cheaper on
    | the VBLANK omission path.  At the end of VBLANK the mask is preserved in
    | `setup_sprite`.  After the visible raster, overscan uses dead `event_order[]` as
    | a six-byte temporary queue and stable-partitions `priority[]`: all sprites omitted
    | this frame first, then all sprites shown this frame, preserving relative order in
    | both groups.  Moving the full queue rebuild to overscan avoids the already-tight
    | VBLANK deadline that killed the earlier rank-3 rescan experiment.
    | 
    | `test/vcs_frame_timing.cpp` now supports `--require-visible-spread MASK MAX` on
    | top of the existing asymmetric visibility oracle.  The focused regression runs
    | the two-pile witness on the real 6502 model for 97 checked frames and requires the
    | actual nonzero-GRP visibility counts of all six sprites to differ by no more than
    | one frame.  The host Monte Carlo also pins the exact six-frame 4/4/4/4/4/4 result.
    | At 250,000 deterministic layouts the scheduler still has minimum two accepted
    | sprites and the single-frame histogram is unchanged, while the worst observed
    | consecutive omission gap over the 12-frame fairness sweep improves from four to
    | two frames.
    | 
    | This repair addresses fairness only; it does not claim the greedy allocator is
    | optimal.  The exact host oracle still reports 7188/20000 layouts where a better
    | legal lane assignment would draw more sprites in the current frame.  Same-line
    | horizontal reuse is also still needed to eliminate genuinely unavoidable
    | one-sprite-per-lane flicker such as a single three-way exact-Y pile.
    | 
    | Current public accounting after the fairness repair:
    | 
    |   ROM: 3842/4090 bytes
    |   RAM: 115/128 bytes
    | 
    | CURRENT NEXT WORK
    | -----------------
    | 1. Keep the all-omission stable priority queue and the real-GRP visibility-spread
    |    oracle active while changing the allocator.
    | 2. Use the exact host search to find VBLANK-cheap lane rebalancing that reduces
    |    the remaining 7188/20000 avoidable greedy omissions without sacrificing the
    |    264-raw-line timing budget.
    | 3. Reintroduce horizontal same-line reuse/pair feasibility for geometries where
    |    two hardware lanes are intrinsically insufficient.
    | 4. Continue Stella X=0..159 pixel certification and final RESP-lattice cleanup
    |    after scheduler correctness remains stable.
    | 
    | VALIDATION CHECKPOINT -- 2026-08-22
    | -----------------------------------
    | The all-omission fairness repair passes the 250,000-layout host scheduler model,
    | the focused asymmetric real-CPU regression, source-tree hygiene, context-history
    | format, example-assembly allowlist, and the four shared multisprite renderer

    | STAGGERED 0/1/2 FLICKER DIAGNOSIS -- 2026-08-22
    | ------------------------------------------------
    | The user's phosphor-on screenshot around 10:55 is not an intrinsically
    | three-way visible overlap.  Using unchanged sprites 3/4 at Y=42 and sprite 5
    | at Y=16 as screen-coordinate anchors, the moved cluster is approximately (and
    | at joystick integer resolution effectively) X={62,72,62}, Y={86,82,76} for
    | sprites 0/1/2.  The eight bitmap bands are therefore:
    | 
    |   sprite 0: 86..79
    |   sprite 1: 82..75
    |   sprite 2: 76..69
    | 
    | At no logical band are all three live.  The maximum visible occupancy is two,
    | and sprites 0 and 2 do not overlap at all.  They are also horizontally aligned
    | in the screenshot.  A hardware-lane coloring 0+2 on one player and 1 on the
    | other should therefore be solid; the user's expectation is correct.
    | 
    | The current flicker comes from a renderer scheduling limitation, not from the
    | new fairness queue and not from a shortage of two TIA players.  The allocator
    | uses the complete reposition/setup reservations as its compatibility graph:
    | P0 same-lane reuse requires 15 bands, P1 requires 12, and the normal event
    | stream also rejects the directional P1-above-P0 gap of 1..6 bands.  For
    | Y={86,82,76}, those setup reservations make the current graph require an
    | omission even though the actual bitmap-overlap graph is two-colorable.  With
    | identity priority the current allocator draws 0/1 and omits 2; the all-omission
    | fairness queue then rotates the avoidable loss, so over three frames 0/1/2 each
    | receive exactly 2/3 service.  Phosphor makes that unnecessary 2-of-3 rotation
    | especially obvious.
    | 
    | `test/vcs_enhanced_multisprite_asymmetric_scheduler.c` now contains this exact
    | staggered-chain witness.  It independently verifies that visible occupancy
    | never exceeds two and records the current known deficit/fair 2/2/2 rotation so
    | the next continuation work starts from a deterministic geometry rather than a
    | visual impression.
    | 
    | This changes the priority of the next scheduler work.  Before attempting true
    | same-scanline >2 horizontal multiplexing, add a shorter same-lane continuation
    | path for vertically disjoint sprites.  The important target is the two blank
    | bands between sprite 0's last row (79) and sprite 2's first row (76): the lane
    | is idle during bands 78 and 77, which should be enough to prepare sprite 2
    | without reserving the full 12/15-band normal position/setup interval.  Because
    | 0 and 2 share X in this witness, the first implementation can exploit retained
    | horizontal state and need only hand off color/graphics state; a generalized
    | continuation can later decide when a real RESP/HMOVE reposition also fits.
    | 
    | Do not describe this geometry as unavoidable three-way contention.  The
    | fairness repair is working here; it is fairly rotating an omission that should
    | not exist.
    | 
    | TOP-EDGE LANE JITTER AND 12:04 TWO-COLUMN WITNESS -- 2026-08-22
    | -----------------------------------------------------------------
    | The user's phosphor screenshot exposed a different defect from fairness: sprite
    | 0 visibly hopped by one logical band when persistent priority moved it between
    | P0 and P1.  A real-6502 GRP-write sweep with a fixed logical Y isolated the
    | lane mismatch precisely:
    | 
    |   Y       old P0/P1 first-GRP delta
    |   85..88  0 physical lines
    |   89..91  2 physical lines
    |   92..95  0 physical lines
    | 
    | Only P0 uses the VBLANK top-preload path at Y=89..91.  Its predecessor-state
    | map was one blank state too short there, so P0 published the glyph one logical
    | band before the ordinary P1 path.  `graphics_next[39..44]` now maps the P0-only
    | 89..91 cases through an extra predecessor, with formerly unreachable states
    | 67..72 providing the needed 55..60 chain.  After the change the measured P0
    | and P1 first nonzero GRP line is identical for every Y=85..95.
    | 
    | `test/vcs_frame_timing.cpp` now has
    | `--require-stable-first-visible-line MASK`.  The focused asymmetric regression
    | uses three Y=90 sprites so the fair priority queue repeatedly assigns each
    | logical glyph to both P0 and P1.  The old predecessor table fails with a
    | 56-versus-58 first-line hop; the corrected table passes.  This is deliberately
    | a real-GRP regression rather than a scheduler-table assertion.
    | 
    | The later 12:04 screenshot is also a stronger flicker witness.  Pixel geometry
    | and integer joystick motion resolve it to approximately:
    | 
    |   id       0   1   2   3   4   5
    |   X       34  34  46  46  34  46
    |   Y       90  76  82  68  61  58
    | 
    | The six eight-band glyph intervals have maximum simultaneous occupancy two.
    | Nevertheless two distinct limitations remain in the current implementation:
    | 
    | * With identity priority the greedy allocator accepts only four sprites.
    |   The exact omit/P0/P1 oracle finds a legal current-graph assignment of five.
    |   Therefore at least one omission in this exact scene is purely greedy and can
    |   be removed by better bounded lane choice without changing raster machinery.
    | * The exact oracle still cannot assign all six under today's 15-band P0,
    |   12-band P1, and directional +1..+6 setup constraints.  That final omission
    |   is not caused by bitmap overlap; it is caused by reserving the full normal
    |   horizontal-position/setup pipeline for every logical sprite.  The two
    |   horizontal columns make retained-X continuation the appropriate next raster
    |   primitive.
    | 
    | The host scheduler model now pins this distinction: visible occupancy=2,
    | greedy accepted=4, exact current-graph optimum=5.  This prevents a future
    | allocator change from claiming that a five-sprite result has solved the whole
    | screenshot.
    | 
    | Two tempting threshold-only shortcuts were tested and rejected.  Allowing P1
    | same-lane gap 10 without a new raster path can produce an accepted logical
    | sprite that never emits nonzero GRP.  Allowing candidate-below P0 gap 14 passed
    | the broad randomized focused test but a deterministic two-sprite construction
    | stretched the frame to 264 raw harness lines.  The 15/12 constants therefore
    | encode real pipeline work, not merely conservative allocator policy.
    | 
    | CURRENT NEXT STEP
    | -----------------
    | Implement a real retained-X short continuation event, initially for equal-X,
    | vertically disjoint successors.  It must avoid RESP/HMOVE reposition work and
    | handoff color/graphics state only after the previous occupant is finished; do
    | not simulate it by weakening same-lane distance checks.  In parallel, use the
    | exact host oracle to find a bounded, VBLANK-cheap rebalancing rule that raises
    | the 12:04 witness from greedy four to the current-graph optimum five without
    | making any Monte Carlo layout worse.  Re-run the Y=90 lane-stability GRP oracle
    | on every raster change.

    | RETAINED-X TEST/DEADLINE REPAIR -- 2026-08-22
    | ------------------------------------------------
    | The first retained-X checkpoint compiled and fit 4K but was not yet regression
    | clean.  Two failures were immediate: the renderer branch audit rejected two new
    | `.flex` branches, and the old structural six-band hazard regex no longer matched
    | the retained-P0 exception.  Once those gates were repaired, the focused timing
    | sweep also exposed a real hidden regression: several randomized all-six-normal
    | frames crossed the VBLANK deadline and became 265 raw harness lines.
    | 
    | The branch fixes preserve timing semantics rather than merely silencing the
    | audit.  The below-P0 `cmp #15`/far branch was unreachable because that label is
    | entered only after the earlier abs(dy)<15 test, so it was removed.  The now-long
    | AssignRank back edge is expressed as `beq.same` to a local exit followed by an
    | unconditional `jmp`, which is the explicit form of the assembler's relaxed long
    | branch and leaves no timing-flexible conditional branch in the renderer.
    | 
    | The six-band test was genuinely stale after retained P0 was introduced.  Normal
    | P1-above-P0 separation 1..6 is still illegal, but retained P0 starts one action
    | band later, making the exact-six case a safe four-band event gap.  The Perl
    | contract now checks 1..5 blocking and requires the exact-six escape to be gated
    | by the retained-P0 flag/mask in both traversal directions.
    | 
    | The VBLANK overrun was caused by carrying retained-X tests through every normal
    | accepted record.  The allocator now returns four accepted encodings directly in
    | A: lane 0/1 normally and $80/$81 when retained; 2 is the reject code.  The hot
    | assignment loop tests the sign before `cmp #2`, so retained dispatch shares the
    | existing accept/reject decision.  Normal records then use the old cheap builder.
    | Retained records use a separate short builder and write the dedicated no-RESP
    | landing directly instead of computing a coarse RESP slot only to overwrite it.
    | This recovers enough deadline margin for all three 5000-frame combined X/Y
    | stress seeds while keeping retained-X machinery present.
    | 
    | Validation at this checkpoint:
    | 
    | * `vcs_enhanced_multisprite_asymmetric.pl` passes, including all combined X/Y
    |   stress seeds and the real-GRP visibility/fairness checks already in that test;
    | * `vcs_renderer_branch_contracts.pl` passes and the asymmetric renderer contains
    |   no `.flex` conditional branches;
    | * source-tree hygiene, context-history format, example assembly allowlist, and
    |   branding checks pass;
    | * compile phase is 500/500;
    | * full E2E was interrupted by the execution timeout after 78/319 passing cases,
    |   so a later window should rerun the complete 319-case E2E suite.

    | Current public accounting is 3980/4090 ROM and 115/128 RAM.  Retained-X is still
    | experimental rather than complete: do not claim arbitrary-priority six-sprite

    | TOP-LEFT DUAL-PRELOAD FRAME-LENGTH FIX -- 2026-08-22
    | ------------------------------------------------------
    | The interactive configuration reported at 15:59 is reproducible with
    | X={0,0,62,88,114,140}, Y={95,95,82,74,66,58}.  With the previous 51/25
    | extended VBLANK/overscan split, the persistent fairness queue evolves into a
    | more expensive VBLANK event order.  A cycle trace measures the first timer
    | poll at about 3391 cycles after TIM64T is loaded, but preload 51 underflows
    | after only (51+1)*64 = 3328 cycles.  The scheduler therefore misses the
    | intended phase boundary.  Stella 7.0 directly reproduces the user's steady
    | 265-line / 59.3-Hz frame for this scene.
    | 
    | This is a blanking-budget split bug, not a visible-raster lane-allocation bug.
    | At this checkpoint overscan was comparatively cheap, so
    | `VCS_NTSC_EXTENDED_VBLANK` kept the same total 76 TIM64T quanta and moved the
    | split to 57/19.  A later forced bridge-orientation scan increased worst-case
    | VBLANK work; the current split is 63/13 as recorded below.  Preload 57 gives a
    | 3712-cycle VBLANK deadline, roughly 321 cycles beyond the measured expensive
    | schedule.  Preload 19 gives a 1280-cycle overscan deadline; observed overscan
    | work is about 543 cycles, leaving roughly 737 cycles.  A 56/20 experiment fixed
    | this exact scene but made the existing Y=90 top-edge lane-stability witness one
    | raw line long, so 57/19 is the least aggressive split found that satisfies
    | both constraints.
    | 
    | `vcs_enhanced_multisprite_asymmetric.pl` now includes a deterministic
    | `persistent top-left dual-preload deadline` case with the exact X/Y geometry.
    | It deliberately does not initialize `game_priority[]`; released console inputs
    | allow the real overscan fairness code to evolve priority across frames.  This is
    | essential because identity priority alone does not expose the steady-state
    | cost.  The old 51/25 split fails this witness; 57/19 passed it at this checkpoint
    | together with the Y=90 lane-stability test and the existing randomized X/Y
    | stress.  Current code uses the later 63/13 split.
    | 
    | Real Stella 7.0 verification of the same fixed scene reported 262 lines /
    | 60.0 Hz with 57/19, versus 265 / 59.3 Hz with 51/25.  Keep the total blanking budget
    | unchanged unless a separate frame-policy change is intentional; future
    | scheduler optimizations may recover margin but must not silently depend on the
    | cheaper identity-priority ordering.
    | 
    | Validation after this repair: the focused asymmetric regression, renderer
    | branch contracts, shared NTSC frame scheduler, source-tree hygiene, history
    | format, example assembly allowlist, and branding checks pass; the complete
    | E2E suite is 319/319.

    | 16:41 RETAINED-COLUMN BRIDGE ORIENTATION -- 2026-08-22
    | -------------------------------------------------------
    | The user's later phosphor screenshot showed sprite 3 almost disappearing and
    | the top of sprite 4 malformed.  This was not an unavoidable two-player limit.
    | The relevant deterministic geometry is:
    | 
    |   id       0   1   2   3   4    5
    |   X        0   0  62  72  62  140
    |   Y       95  95  71  64  59   12
    | 
    | Sprites 2 and 4 are equal-X endpoints separated by 12 logical bands.  Sprite 3
    | sits seven bands below the upper endpoint and five above the lower endpoint.
    | The retained-X/event hazards admit one physical coloring for that component:
    | 2 and 4 must use P1, while 3 uses P0.  All three are drawable in the same frame.
    | 
    | The historical generic tie-break chose P0 whenever both lanes were available.
    | That made the result depend on the persistent fairness permutation: identity
    | priority could omit 4, while priority {0,1,2,4,3,5} could omit 3.  The latter is
    | the direct explanation for the user's nearly absent 3 and partial/malformed 4.
    | Fairness itself was not the fault; it merely changed which traversal order hit
    | the orientation bug.
    | 
    | `TEMPLATE_bridge_prefers_p1()` is a VBLANK-only forced-orientation oracle.  For
    | the current candidate it searches for an equal-X endpoint exactly 12 bands away
    | and then for any sprite at the required middle Y (seven below the upper / five
    | above the lower).  When both physical lanes are otherwise free and that pattern
    | is present, `ChooseLane` takes P1 instead of the historical P0 tie-break.  The
    | check is also used for the first candidate, so the result does not depend on
    | which endpoint or bridge happens to lead the priority queue.  Ordinary lane
    | selection is unchanged when the forced component is absent.
    | 
    | The focused Perl regression permanently pins the two previously failing queues:
    | identity {0,1,2,3,4,5} and inherited {0,1,2,4,3,5}.  Both require six scheduler
    | records, actual real-6502 GRP visibility mask $3f, and stable first visible lines
    | for sprites 2/3/4.  During development the exact scene was also swept through
    | all 6! = 720 priority permutations with the real renderer; all 720 scheduled and
    | actually rendered all six sprites at stable vertical positions and the calibrated
    | frame length.  That exhaustive sweep was a development proof, not a 720-case
    | permanent test loop.
    | 
    | The extra forced-orientation scan consumes VBLANK time.  The interim 57/19
    | extended VBLANK/overscan split used by the preceding top-left repair no longer
    | had enough worst-case margin once this scan was added.  The current
    | `VCS_NTSC_EXTENDED_VBLANK` split is therefore 63/13 TIM64T.  Total blanking
    | budget remains 76 quanta; only ownership moves toward VBLANK.  Do not regress
    | this to 57/19 merely because the earlier top-left fixture passes in isolation.
    | 
    | HANDOFF STATE
