    |  __   __ ___  ___   ___
    |  \ \ / // __|/ __| / __|
    |   \ V /| (__ \__ \| (__
    |    \_/  \___||___/ \___|
    | 
    | VCSC RIOT RAM OPTIMIZATION ROADMAP
    | =================================
    | 
    | Purpose
    | -------
    | This document records the RAM problems exposed by the animated-sprite gallery
    | and turns them into an ordered compiler/runtime/renderer roadmap. The gallery is
    | a useful stress case, but the goal is not to special-case one example. The first
    | priority is to stop the compiler and linker from reserving static scratch whose
    | lifetimes do not overlap. Renderer capability must not be removed merely to hide
    | compiler waste.
    | 
    | This is a focused roadmap. `.../roadmap.txt` remains the main project roadmap.
    | The items below should be selected explicitly when RAM optimization becomes the
    | active workstream.
    | 
    | Guiding rules
    | -------------
    | * Measure every change from linker maps and emulator-backed frame tests.
    | * Distinguish persistent application state, renderer-owned state, expression
    |   scratch, activation storage, and hardware-return-stack reservation.
    | * Prefer whole-program lifetime reuse over hand-written assembly in examples.
    | * Public examples should contain little or no assembly; ordinary application logic
    |   must exercise the high-level language and drive compiler improvements.
    | * Do not remove Ball support from the existing player-color renderers merely to
    |   make this gallery fit. First complete the compiler scratch work and remeasure.
    | * Keep the 181-line and 192-line public renderer contracts stable unless a new
    |   renderer profile is deliberately introduced.
    | * Never trade a RAM reduction for unstable visible timing or an unprovable
    |   activation/stack overlap.
    | 
    | Measured animated-gallery baseline
    | ----------------------------------
    | The source-derived-color animated gallery currently fills all 128 bytes of RIOT
    | RAM. The measured ownership is:
    | 
    |     VCSC runtime pointer/argument scratch                 8 bytes
    |     player_color_192 renderer                            69 bytes
    |       public renderer state                              13 bytes
    |       private workspace and object schedule              56 bytes
    |     mutable P0/P1 row-color tables                       16 bytes
    |     persistent animation/control state                    7 bytes
    |     main activation/expression scratch                   20 bytes
    |     hardware return-stack reservation                     8 bytes
    |                                                         --------
    |                                                          128 bytes
    | 
    | The 480-byte packed source-color table is ROM, not RAM. Only the selected P0 and
    | P1 eight-row color tables occupy RAM.
    | 
    | What `game_object_masks` is
    | ---------------------------
    | `game_object_masks` is not the playfield. The gallery's 48-byte playfield is
    | already immutable ROM data.
    | 
    | The renderer's `game_object_masks[48]` is a writable, frame-rebuilt vertical
    | object schedule and preparation workspace. Its layout is sparse and driven by
    | visible-loop timing:
    | 
    | * The twelve bytes at offsets 0, 4, 8, ... 44 hold Ball enable bits for the
    |   renderer's twelve four-byte playfield rows / ninety-six two-scanline pairs.
    | * The remaining addresses are reused during VBLANK for P0/P1 height thresholds,
    |   range endpoints, loop limits, and mask-generation temporaries.
    | * During visible drawing, instructions such as
    | 
    |       lsr.zx game_object_masks,x
    | 
    |   destructively consume the Ball schedule while X simultaneously follows the
    |   four-byte playfield-row stride. The 48-byte shape is therefore partly a
    |   cycle-scheduling choice, not forty-eight independent pieces of game state.
    | 
    | The current array cannot simply be made `const` or moved to ROM: the renderer
    | writes it during VBLANK and shifts it in place during drawing. A different
    | representation or renderer schedule could reduce it, but that is a renderer
    | redesign and must be proven against the beam.
    | 
    | What the faithful legacy renderer proves
    | ----------------------------------------
    | The retained faithful legacy player-color renderer supports arbitrary P0, P1,
    | and Ball Y positions and heights without `game_object_masks` or any equivalent
    | 48-byte object schedule. During visible drawing it destructively counts the
    | three object Y values down, compares them directly with their heights, and emits
    | `GRP0`, `GRP1`, and `ENABL` from those live counters. It saves the public Y
    | values before drawing and restores them afterward.
    | 
    | That implementation uses unofficial `DCP` instructions and has other legacy
    | constraints, so it cannot simply replace the maintained official renderer.
    | Nevertheless, it proves that arbitrary P0/P1/Ball vertical positioning does not
    | inherently require a 48-byte RAM schedule. The maintained renderer's array is an
    | implementation choice, not a TIA requirement.
    | 
    | A serious redesign must therefore investigate an official-opcode direct-countdown
    | schedule before deleting Ball support. The result must preserve the maintained
    | renderer lifecycle, composable handoff, ROM playfield, per-row player colors,
    | legal-opcode contract, arbitrary object positions/heights, and exact beam timing.
    | 
    | Why `main` owns twenty scratch bytes
    | ------------------------------------
    | The animated gallery's current `main` activation record includes approximately:
    | 
    |     vcs_ntsc_wait_scanlines parameter                     1 byte
    |     VSYNC expression scratch                              2 bytes
    |     pause-condition scratch                               1 byte
    |     first inlined next_pair expansion                     6 bytes
    |     control-expression scratch                            4 bytes
    |     second inlined next_pair expansion                    6 bytes
    |                                                         --------
    |                                                           20 bytes
    | 
    | None of those values is useful during the visible renderer. Most are dead after
    | one expression or one VSYNC/overscan path, but VCSC currently gives `main` one
    | static, non-returning activation record and does not overlay scratch whose
    | lifetimes occur at different points inside the function.
    | 
    | The worst symptom is that the two expansions of the same inline `next_pair()`
    | helper receive separate six-byte scratch groups even though those expansions
    | cannot execute simultaneously. Duplicating scratch groups for duplicated inline
    | functions has got to stop.
    | 
    | Why eight bytes are reserved for the hardware stack
    | ----------------------------------------------------
    | The 6507 hardware return stack shares the physical RIOT $80-$FF RAM. The linker
    | therefore reserves real RAM for the deepest possible JSR chain.
    | 
    | The current player-color VBLANK path declares hidden assembly stack use and may
    | execute a chain equivalent to:
    | 
    |     runtime startup -> main -> prepare_object_masks -> prepare_one -> set_range
    | 
    | Four simultaneous return addresses require eight bytes. Most source helpers are
    | already inline; the remaining depth comes largely from handwritten renderer
    | assembly and the deliberately out-of-line `install_frames()` routine.
    | 
    | Inlining everything is not automatically correct. `install_frames()` is large
    | and called repeatedly; blindly duplicating it would waste ROM and may overflow
    | 4K. Stack reduction must use measured whole-program inlining, helper flattening,
    | or renderer redesign only when the linked result improves.
    | 
    | Why `install_frames()` is full of assembly
    | ------------------------------------------
    | `install_frames()` is not beam-critical renderer code. It runs during startup or
    | outside visible drawing and performs ordinary data work:
    | 
    | * choose one of the hard ROM pages for each player's bitmap;
    | * compute the selected frame offset;
    | * choose the packed row-color page;
    | * unpack two four-bit source palette indices from each byte;
    | * translate those indices through `pico8_tia_palette`;
    | * fill the two eight-byte mutable player-color tables; and
    | * install the two graphics pointers.
    | 
    | That work is written in assembly because the current compiler makes the obvious
    | high-level formulation too expensive. Dynamic pointer/page selection, pointer
    | arithmetic, indexed ROM loads, nibble extraction, and small loops currently
    | produce excessive generic expression scratch and/or larger code. The cartridge
    | was already close to both its 4K ROM and 128-byte RAM ceilings, so the example
    | used hand-written 6502 to control every byte. Keeping the routine out of line was
    | then necessary to avoid duplicating the large body at each call site.
    | 
    | This is an implementation failure, not a timing requirement. Examples exist to
    | show users how to write VCSC programs; ordinary startup, animation, table lookup,
    | and color-expansion logic should be VCSC source. Assembly in examples should be
    | reserved for genuinely cycle-exact TIA sequences, explicit hardware idioms not
    | yet expressible by the language, or narrowly documented compiler gaps that have
    | an associated roadmap item and regression.
    | 
    | Ordered roadmap
    | ---------------
    | 
    | [x] 0. Add authoritative RAM-accounting fixtures before optimizing.
    | 
    |     * Build the animated gallery and retain a machine-readable symbol/segment
    |       accounting report that classifies every byte as persistent state,
    |       renderer state, activation scratch, runtime scratch, or hardware stack.
    |     * Pin the current 128-byte baseline and the twenty-byte `main` activation
    |       breakdown so later reductions are measurable rather than anecdotal.
    |     * Add diagnostics capable of explaining why two scratch objects cannot
    |       overlap and why each hardware-stack depth edge exists.
    |     * Keep exact 262-line frame, sprite, source-derived row-color, control, and
    |       animation-sequence emulator oracles active throughout this roadmap.
    | 
    |     Completed baseline:
    | 
    |     * `test/fixtures/vcs_animated_gallery_ram_accounting/golden.json` is the
    |       canonical machine-readable 128-byte report. It classifies every physical
    |       address from `$80` through `$FF`, including the renderer's 13 public bytes,
    |       56 private bytes, sixteen mutable row-color bytes, seven gallery-state
    |       bytes, twenty activation bytes, eight runtime bytes, and eight stack bytes.
    |     * Compiler `-X scratch` reports each fixed scratch slot's expansion scope,
    |       activation owner, size, and the current
    |       `no-intra-function-lifetime-overlay` reason. The fixture pins both
    |       sequential `next_pair()` expansions as separate six-byte groups.
    |     * Linker maps now list compiled call-graph edges, their source/bridge stack
    |       slots, one deterministic deepest weighted path, each object-level
    |       `.callstackextra` contribution, and the reconciled source/hidden/total
    |       hardware-stack bytes.
    |     * `test/vcs_animated_gallery_ram_accounting.pl` regenerates and compares the
    |       report while the independent animation emulator remains the pixel, color,
    |       control, sequence, and exact-frame oracle. No allocation or cartridge bytes
    |       changed in this item.
    | 
    | [x] 1. Add lifetime overlay between separate expressions inside one function.
    | 
    |     This is the highest-priority compiler optimization and is expected to yield
    |     broad savings well beyond the gallery.
    | 
    |     * Compute scratch liveness across the function control-flow graph instead of
    |       assigning every expression a permanent disjoint region in the function's
    |       static activation.
    |     * Allow non-overlapping expression temporaries to occupy the same bytes even
    |       when they occur in different statements, branches, loop phases, or frame
    |       phases.
    |     * Retain a scratch lease until the expression's final copy-out or side effect
    |       is complete; never reuse storage merely because code generation for one
    |       subtree has returned.
    |     * Handle joins conservatively: objects live on either predecessor remain live
    |       at the join until proven dead. Loops require fixed-point liveness rather
    |       than source-order assumptions.
    |     * Preserve address-taken, inline-assembly-referenced, volatile hardware, and
    |       ABI-visible objects. Only compiler-owned temporary storage is eligible.
    |     * Emit map/debug information showing lifetime groups and overlaid offsets.
    | 
    |     Acceptance criteria:
    | 
    |     * Sequential expressions of equal or different sizes reuse the same maximal
    |       scratch region when their lifetimes do not overlap.
    |     * Mutually exclusive branches reuse scratch safely.
    |     * Nested expressions and loop-carried values do not alias prematurely.
    |     * Existing task-15e nesting-depth/LIFO guarantees remain intact.
    |     * The gallery's VSYNC, control, and animation expressions no longer reserve
    |       disjoint bytes merely because they appear in one non-returning `main`.
    | 
    |     Completed implementation:
    | 
    |     * Scratch pools are now keyed by runtime activation owner rather than by each
    |       textual compiler context. Inline expansions inherit the caller's owner, so
    |       separate statements, branches, loops, and frame phases reuse the same
    |       active-lease depth slots.
    |     * Compiler-owned scratch cannot be named, addressed, declared volatile, or
    |       referenced by inline assembly. Its structured lease remains active through
    |       final copy-out/side effect, so no scratch value can survive a statement
    |       join or loop back-edge; nested live leases still consume deeper slots.
    |     * `-X scratch` now reports each scope's shared lifetime group, symbol, size,
    |       and acquisition count. The linker map supplies the final overlaid address.
    |     * `scratch_lifetime_overlay.pl` executes sequential, branch, loop, three-call
    |       inline, and nested-inline cases in `vcsc-sim`. The complete compile-only
    |       suite retains the existing LIFO/nesting regressions.
    |     * The gallery activation fell from 20 bytes to 7: one inline parameter plus
    |       two three-byte lifetime groups. Total RAM fell from 128/128 to 115/128,
    |       leaving 13 bytes free, with the renderer, persistent state, and eight-byte
    |       hardware-stack reservation unchanged.
    |     * The compiler does not need a general named-object CFG dataflow pass for
    |       this class of storage: compiler-owned scratch is non-addressable and its
    |       structured lease encloses final copy-out/side effects. Activation-wide
    |       depth coloring is therefore the liveness result for these temporaries;
    |       ordinary locals and ABI-visible objects remain outside this optimization.
    |     * All 438 compile-only tests and all 253 end-to-end tests pass. The slow
    |       40-cartridge composition oracle also exposed and fixed a test-harness bug:
    |       SWCHA/SWCHB now default to released inputs, so line timing is independent
    |       of reset-startup BSS clearing cost.
    | 
    | [x] 2. Stop duplicating scratch groups for repeated expansions of one inline
    |         function.
    | 
    |     * Model each inline expansion's compiler-owned temporaries by lifetime and
    |       call-site reachability, not by assigning a permanent new group per textual
    |       expansion.
    |     * Reuse one scratch footprint for repeated inline calls when the expansions
    |       cannot be active simultaneously.
    |     * Support repeated expansions in separate branches, before/after loops, and
    |       from different callers whose activations already overlay.
    |     * Do not merge persistent inline-local objects whose source semantics require
    |       distinct storage. This item concerns expression/call scratch, not ordinary
    |       static locals.
    |     * Detect nested inline expansion correctly. A caller expansion and a callee
    |       expansion that are simultaneously live still require distinct storage.
    | 
    |     Acceptance criteria:
    | 
    |     * The two current `next_pair()` expansions share one scratch group instead of
    |       consuming six bytes each.
    |     * Adding a third sequential `next_pair()` call does not grow BSS scratch.
    |     * A deliberately nested inline case still receives enough non-overlapping
    |       storage.
    |     * Generated code and emulator behavior remain unchanged except for addresses
    |       and reduced RAM ownership.
    | 
    |     Completed as a direct consequence of item 1's activation-owner lifetime
    |     coloring:
    | 
    |     * Both gallery `next_pair()` expansions use `main:0` and `main:1`, one
    |       six-byte physical footprint instead of twelve bytes.
    |     * The dedicated regression adds a third sequential inline expansion without
    |       growing BSS and retains four deeper slots for a deliberately nested inline
    |       expression whose leases overlap.
    |     * Persistent inline parameters, locals, and return objects remain distinct;
    |       only compiler-owned scratch participates in lifetime coloring.
    | 
    | [x] 3. Improve compact lowering for simple byte state updates.
    | 
    |     The gallery currently routes operations such as `sprite0 += 2`,
    |     `sprite1 := sprite0 + 1`, increments, masks, and constant comparisons through
    |     generic multi-byte expression machinery in places where direct byte
    |     instructions are sufficient.
    | 
    |     * Extend the direct uint8_t assignment/update paths for constant add/subtract,
    |       increment/decrement, mask/test, compare, and copy-plus-constant forms.
    |     * Avoid generic pointer-width or three-byte temporary sequences for byte
    |       lvalues whose width and address are known.
    |     * Fold simple post-update comparisons where doing so is smaller and preserves
    |       source evaluation semantics.
    |     * Add exact assembly and zero/additional-scratch regressions.
    | 
    |     Completed:
    | 
    |     * Ordinary directly addressable `uint8_t` lvalues now lower constant `+=`,
    |       `-=`, `&=`, `|=`, and `^=` updates directly. Unit increments/decrements use
    |       `INC`/`DEC`; other add/subtract forms use one load, carry setup, immediate
    |       arithmetic, and one store. Absolute external bindings retain required
    |       read/write side effects rather than being treated as ordinary RAM.
    |     * `dst := src + constant`, `dst := src - constant`, commuted constant-plus-
    |       source, byte logical-not assignment, truth tests, constant-mask tests, and
    |       constant comparisons use direct byte loads/branches without generic
    |       pointer-width or three-byte expression groups. Comparisons immediately
    |       following updates therefore compile as direct reload/compare branches; no
    |       speculative cross-statement register state is assumed.
    |     * `direct_u8_state_update_codegen_test.c26` locks the exact documented-opcode
    |       sequences and forbids both expression-scratch symbols and Y setup.
    |       `e2e_direct_u8_state_update_verify.c26` executes wraparound arithmetic,
    |       masks, copy-plus-constant, truth, comparison, and logical-not behavior in
    |       `vcsc-sim`. Existing absolute-ref and packed-BCD regressions remain active.
    |     * The animated gallery's `main` activation fell from seven bytes to two: one
    |       scheduler inline parameter plus one one-byte VSYNC scratch slot. Both
    |       `next_pair()` expansions now allocate zero expression scratch. Total RIOT
    |       RAM fell from 115 to 110 bytes, free RAM rose from 13 to 18 bytes, and ROM
    |       fell from 3993 to 3662 bytes. The authoritative accounting fixture is now
    |       schema 3 and records the five-byte RAM and 331-byte ROM item-3 deltas.
    |     * Compact lowering exposed an accidental timing dependency in the shared
    |       frame helpers: the old generic byte loop happened to spend nineteen extra
    |       cycles after its final WSYNC. The first item-3 pass incorrectly changed
    |       phase fixtures to bless that shift, and the public basic score, fingerprint,
    |       and wide-score cartridges regressed on real Stella output. The correction
    |       restores the established cycle-3 visible-component entry with an explicit
    |       calibrated tail in `vcs_ntsc_wait_component_scanlines()` and adds
    |       `vcs_ntsc_wait_visible_tail_scanlines()` for a final blank visible gap before
    |       overscan. Generic `vcs_ntsc_wait_scanlines()` keeps the compact lowering and
    |       deliberately has no return-cycle contract.
    |     * The original exact phase and reviewed Stella raster oracles are retained;
    |       optimizer-induced beam shifts are fixed in the helper rather than accepted
    |       by moving the oracle. Gallery accounting remains 110/128 RAM and 3662/4090
    |       ROM because the gallery does not use these score-gap helpers.
    |     * Final verification: 439 compile-only tests and 254 end-to-end tests pass.
    | 
    | [x] 4. Make `install_frames()` ordinary VCSC and establish a minimal-assembly
    |         example policy.
    | 
    |     * Express bitmap-page selection, frame-offset calculation, packed color
    |       lookup, nibble extraction, palette translation, color-table expansion,
    |       and graphics-pointer installation in the high-level language.
    |     * Add or improve compiler lowering for the exact missing pieces rather than
    |       hiding them in one example: efficient ROM pointer selection, pointer-plus-
    |       byte-offset arithmetic, indexed const loads, direct nibble extraction,
    |       compact counted loops, and byte-array stores.
    |     * Combine this work with items 1-3 so the high-level implementation does not
    |       consume more activation scratch than the hand-written routine.
    |     * Keep `install_frames()` out of line unless whole-program measurement proves
    |       inlining wins. High-level source does not imply textual duplication.
    |     * Audit all public examples. Assembly should be absent unless it is genuinely
    |       beam-critical, a direct hardware idiom, or a documented language/compiler
    |       limitation with a focused test and roadmap entry.
    |     * Add an example-source assembly inventory/allowlist so ordinary application
    |       logic cannot quietly migrate back into large `asm` blocks.
    | 
    |     Acceptance criteria:
    | 
    |     * The animated gallery contains no assembly in `install_frames()`.
    |     * Generated behavior, all source bitmap/color hashes, controls, and exact
    |       262-line frames remain unchanged.
    |     * Final linked ROM fits the supported cartridge profile and activation RAM is
    |       no larger than the assembly version after items 1-3.
    |     * The source is readable as an example of VCSC table and pointer operations,
    |       not as a disguised assembly program.
    | 
    |     Completed implementation:
    | 
    |     * `install_frames()` is now ordinary VCSC. Page selection, byte-pointer offsets,
    |       packed color extraction, palette translation, color expansion, and graphics
    |       pointer installation are expressed directly in the high-level source. The
    |       adjacent animation-phase table update is high-level VCSC as well.
    |     * The compiler gained reusable compact lowering for array-to-pointer decay,
    |       pointer-plus-byte offsets, indexed one-byte ROM/pointer loads, constant byte
    |       masks/shifts, indexed byte-array stores, and a narrowly proven X-backed
    |       unsigned-byte counted loop. A separate fallback regression requires unsafe
    |       indirect-store loop bodies to retain a materialized local.
    |     * The animation emulator still verifies every source bitmap/color hash, all 120
    |       source frames, the modulo-3 special set, controls, palette expansion, installed
    |       pointer pairs, and exact 262-line frames.
    |     * Activation RAM is unchanged at 110/128 bytes with 18 bytes free and zero
    |       `install_frames()` activation bytes. After the compiler/assembler addressing-mode
    |       handoff below, ordinary ROM is 3854/4090 bytes with 236 bytes free: 192 bytes
    |       more than the 3662-byte hand-written-assembly baseline. `install_frames()` is
    |       now 462 final linked bytes versus 236 hand-written bytes. A measured optimizer
    |       follow-up immediately below separates compiler output, peephole behavior,
    |       assembler relaxation, and the remaining code-size work.
    |     * `test/example_assembly_allowlist.pl` pins all remaining public-example assembly
    |       by source, normalized-statement hash, policy category, and focused regression.
    |       Ordinary application assembly that still reflects compiler/language limitations
    |       is recorded in the named follow-up below rather than silently grandfathered.
    |     * An attempted shorter ordinary byte-comparison branch form was rejected after an
    |       exact renderer regression showed scheduler-owned VBLANK work moving by one
    |       scanline. Compact counted loops keep their proven branch form, while ordinary
    |       comparisons retain the established control-flow shape.
    |     * The smaller surrounding application can move the standard renderer component by
    |       whole pages. Its documented absolute pages are observations rather than ABI; the
    |       static regression now enforces page alignment, relative code/RODATA placement,
    |       and exact phase/raster behavior rather than stale `$F300/$F600` addresses.
    |     * Verification before the addressing-mode follow-up: 442 compile-only tests and
    |       255 end-to-end tests passed. The follow-up below carries its own expanded gates.
    | 
    | [x] 4a. Recover high-level frame-installation ROM through the existing optimizer
    |          pipeline instead of accepting the pre-audit 540-byte routine as normal compiler cost.
    | 
    |     Final measured result:
    | 
    |     * The old hand-written `install_frames()` links at 236 bytes. The first ordinary
    |       high-level replacement linked at 540 bytes; the compiler-wide addressing-mode
    |       handoff completed immediately before this work reduced that to 462 bytes.
    |     * This optimizer pass reduces the final linked high-level routine from 462 to
    |       232 bytes with no activation RAM. The ordinary high-level implementation is
    |       therefore four bytes smaller than the hand-written predecessor.
    |     * Animated-gallery ordinary ROM is now 3624/4090 bytes with 466 free, down from
    |       3854 before this optimizer pass. RIOT RAM remains 110/128 bytes with 18 free.
    |     * With the final compiler but `-fno-peephole`, the gallery is 3635 bytes and
    |       `install_frames()` is 235 bytes. Thus the current peephole pass saves eleven
    |       bytes across the gallery and three bytes inside `install_frames()`. Most of the
    |       original ten branch/`JMP` opportunities disappeared structurally once page
    |       selectors and common suffixes were lowered more compactly upstream.
    | 
    |     Implemented compiler/optimizer changes:
    | 
    |     * Added a peephole rewrite for compiler-generated `conditional branch; JMP;
    |       true-label` scaffolding. It inverts the branch and removes the `JMP` only in
    |       pure compiler-generated procedures. Any inline assembly in the procedure
    |       disables the rewrite so size optimization cannot silently reschedule code whose
    |       cycle shape may be intentional. Pattern-level and source-level on/off regressions
    |       cover both the positive case and the inline-assembly refusal.
    |     * Propagated hard `page` alignment and unsigned-byte range facts into pointer
    |       lowering. Page-selection chains now load the selector once, compare through the
    |       candidate pages, tail-merge the pointer-high store, omit the known-zero page low
    |       byte, and omit carry propagation when the bounded low-byte additions provably
    |       cannot cross a page.
    |     * Merged conditional pointer-low updates when both branches target the same tracked
    |       page pointer and both ranges prove safe. The selected byte offset is computed in A
    |       and one common `CLC/ADC/STA` suffix performs the update.
    |     * Added a narrow cross-loop value-lifetime proof for the frame installer pattern.
    |       When the later bitmap offsets are exactly twice the earlier packed-color offsets
    |       modulo one byte, and the intervening counted loop cannot mutate the pointer or
    |       either source value, the compiler reuses the existing pointer low byte with
    |       `ASL` instead of rebuilding both offsets. No temporary RAM is introduced.
    |     * Proven nonempty register-backed counted loops are post-tested with `CPX/BCC` at
    |       the bottom. Potentially empty loops retain the entry test and back jump. Constant
    |       array stores through an X-backed loop index are routed through the direct indexed
    |       path so the optimizer never emits a reference to a nonexistent materialized loop
    |       local.
    |     * The three-frame animation phase expressions now state their packed-bit meaning
    |       directly with `animation_state & 0x0c` (and the doubled bitmap form) rather than
    |       obscuring the same value through shift pairs. The full animation oracle still
    |       proves the independent modulo-3 sequence and all 120 source frames.
    | 
    |     Measured development path from the 462-byte relaxed baseline:
    | 
    |         linked install_frames stage                                  bytes
    |         relaxed high-level baseline                                   462
    |         branch inversion + compact hard-page selection/ranges          324
    |         conditional page-low range/common-suffix lowering              294
    |         doubled packed-color -> bitmap offset reuse                     238
    |         post-tested proven-nonempty counted loops                       232
    | 
    |     The stage values above are actual linked map spans from this implementation, not
    |     projections. The final peephole-disabled span is 235 bytes because the upstream
    |     structural lowering removes most branch/JMP candidates before the peephole sees
    |     them; enabling the peephole removes the remaining three-byte candidate.
    | 
    |     Collateral measured effect:
    | 
    |     * The banked standard-renderer diagnostic keeps identical raster/timing while
    |       code generation has continued to shrink. The branch/page work first reduced
    |       F8/F6/F4 bank1 from 82 to 76 bytes and F8SC bank1 from 121 to 115 bytes. The
    |       later fixed-address lowering audit reduces bank0 from 1848 to 1827 bytes,
    |       F8/F6/F4 bank1 to 55 bytes, and F8SC bank1 to 86 bytes. Bank bridges, RAM
    |       accounting, and renderer placement contracts remain unchanged.
    | 
    |     Acceptance criteria:
    | 
    |     * [x] Measure final linked code rather than using the old hard-coded 540-byte cost.
    |     * [x] Preserve the completed compiler/assembler addressing-mode handoff and all
    |       split-memory/address-size contracts.
    |     * [x] Recover the measured branch/page-pointer losses without introducing RAM or
    |       weakening timing contracts.
    |     * [x] Reach or beat the 236-byte hand-written routine and explain the final linked
    |       difference in concrete compiler transformations. Final result: 232 bytes.
    |     * [x] Keep the gallery at or below 110 RAM bytes and preserve the full animation,
    |       exact 262-line, raster, banked-renderer, and complete compile/e2e regressions.
    |       Final verification: 447 compile-only tests and 256 end-to-end tests pass.
    | 
    | [x] 5. Overlay scratch across frame phases when contracts prove the lifetimes do
    |         not overlap.
    | 
    |     * Describe VSYNC, VBLANK, visible draw, and overscan ownership for reusable
    |       renderer/application workspaces.
    |     * Allow application/compiler scratch used only after `game_draw()` to overlay
    |       renderer preparation storage that is provably dead after visible drawing,
    |       and vice versa.
    |     * Do not overlay persistent renderer inputs needed by the next frame or state
    |       restored during `game_overscan()`.
    |     * Prefer compiler/linker metadata over example-specific absolute aliases.
    |     * Add negative tests for phase objects whose lifetimes really do overlap.
    | 
    |     Completed result:
    | 
    |     * The compiler records VSYNC, VBLANK, visible-draw, and overscan uses as
    |       reserved `__phaseuse$V1$Mxx$symbol` metadata. Template lifecycle suffixes
    |       count only on template-owned `require` hooks; fixed `vcs_ntsc_*` scheduler
    |       helpers are classified directly, and an unscoped use emits `M00` and
    |       permanently forbids phase sharing for that object.
    |     * Phase accesses alone are deliberately not an ownership proof for a
    |       file-scope value. Reusable frame-temporary workspaces must also carry the
    |       explicit internal `__phaseworkspace$V1$symbol` eligibility contract.
    |       Compiler-owned scratch emits that contract automatically when every
    |       acquisition is phase-scoped. The maintained 192-line player-color
    |       renderer explicitly marks only its 48-byte `object_masks` schedule after
    |       documenting that VBLANK reconstructs it before visible drawing.
    |     * The linker expands multiple phase accesses to their conservative
    |       contiguous frame interval, overlays only explicitly eligible uninitialized
    |       writable objects with disjoint intervals in the same physical MEMORY
    |       region, and leaves DATA, activation storage, unmarked objects, overlapping
    |       intervals, and any object with an `M00` use distinct.
    |     * Sharing groups are planned independently of member size/declaration order,
    |       but only groups with two or more members alter placement. A solitary
    |       phase-tagged object retains ordinary allocation order. This preserves
    |       stable component addresses such as the six-glyph pointer table while a
    |       smaller earlier object may still share a larger later disjoint workspace.
    |     * The animated gallery now overlays the one-byte VSYNC compiler scratch with
    |       the first byte of `game_object_masks` (VBLANK+visible). Physical RIOT RAM
    |       falls from 110/128 to **109/128 bytes**, leaving **19 bytes free**. Object
    |       bytes fall from 102 to 101; the hardware-stack reservation remains eight
    |       bytes, `main` activation falls from two bytes to one, and ROM remains
    |       **3624/4090 bytes** with 466 free.
    |     * `test/phase_overlay.pl` proves declaration-order-independent positive
    |       sharing and rejects overlapping draw+overscan storage, `main`-lifetime
    |       storage, an ordinary function merely named `_draw`, and a phase-confined
    |       file-scope object that lacks the explicit workspace-eligibility contract.
    |       The animated-gallery accounting fixture independently proves compiler
    |       scratch sharing with the renderer workspace.
    |     * Final verification after the placement-order safety fix is **447/447
    |       compile-only** and **258/258 end-to-end** tests. Exact scheduler, score,
    |       fingerprint, renderer, banked-renderer, animation, bankswitching, and
    |       split-memory oracles remain passing.
    | 
    | [x] 5a. Separate hard page containment from explicit power-of-two alignment,
    |         remove gratuitous ROM padding, and audit page-based optimizer proofs.
    | 
    |     * Add `align(N)` for objects, with `N` required to be a compile-time positive
    |       power of two. Reject non-power-of-two arguments rather than growing a
    |       general arbitrary-alignment test surface. `align(256)` is the canonical
    |       spelling for an object whose first byte must begin on a 6502 page boundary.
    |     * Keep existing `page` semantics unchanged: the complete object must fit
    |       within one 256-byte page. Do not redefine `page` to mean page-start
    |       alignment, because small page-contained objects must remain packable
    |       together instead of each consuming a separate 256-byte address range.
    |     * Define and test the interaction of `page` and `align(N)` explicitly. A
    |       declaration must not gain stronger low-byte-zero assumptions merely from
    |       `page`; containment and base alignment are separate facts except where
    |       object size itself makes alignment unavoidable.
    |     * Audit every compiler optimization and range proof that consumes page
    |       metadata, especially the hard-page pointer-selection/range lowering added
    |       for `install_frames()`. Prove that no optimization currently conflates
    |       "fits inside one page" with "starts at offset zero in a page." Add
    |       positive and negative code-generation regressions for contained objects
    |       placed at nonzero page offsets, exactly-256-byte page objects, and
    |       `align(256)` objects spanning multiple pages.
    |     * Remove impossible `page` qualifiers from the large ASCII font tables. The
    |       8x8 `*_ascii.c26` tables are 760 bytes and `big_ascii.c26` is 1520 bytes;
    |       they cannot satisfy whole-object page containment. Use `align(256)` while
    |       keeping each font one contiguous table.
    |     * Add linked font-layout regressions proving that an aligned 8x8 font never
    |       lets an eight-byte glyph cross a hardware page and an aligned 8x16 font
    |       never lets a sixteen-byte glyph cross a hardware page. Keep the existing
    |       small decimal/hex fonts `page`-contained where their sizes legitimately
    |       fit.
    |     * Audit examples and libraries for explicit ROM padding whose only purpose is
    |       alignment or making an object exactly 256 bytes. Remove such padding when
    |       the new alignment/containment contracts make it unnecessary. In
    |       particular, shrink the animated gallery's final bitmap page from its
    |       padded 256 bytes to the 192 bytes of real sprite data if no independent
    |       semantic use of the trailing 64 zero bytes exists.
    |     * Add regression coverage for ROM-size recovery so examples cannot quietly
    |       reintroduce literal alignment padding. Alignment should be a linker/layout
    |       contract, not application data.
    |     * Update the compiler, assembler/linker, font, and example documentation to
    |       state the distinction clearly: `page` means whole-object containment;
    |       `align(256)` means page-aligned start and may span pages.
    | 
    |     Acceptance criteria:
    | 
    |     * [x] `align(N)` accepts only positive powers of two and rejects other
    |       compile-time values with focused diagnostics.
    |     * [x] Existing valid `page` declarations retain their current placement and
    |       containment contract; no mass 256-byte spacing regression is introduced.
    |     * [x] Large ASCII fonts are contiguous `align(256)` objects, link
    |       successfully, and every glyph is proved not to cross a 256-byte page.
    |     * [x] The page-aware optimizer audit has positive/negative regressions that
    |       distinguish containment from base alignment.
    |     * [x] Gratuitous explicit ROM padding identified by the audit is removed and
    |       the linked ROM savings are measured without changing raster, timing, or
    |       source-data behavior.
    | 
    |     Completed result:
    | 
    |     * Added source-language `align(N)` for file-scope data-object definitions.
    |       `N` must be a compile-time positive power of two from 1 through 32768;
    |       zero, nonconstant, non-power-of-two, and local misuse has focused
    |       regression coverage, while the file-scope data-object-only restriction is
    |       enforced by declaration lowering. The compiler
    |       lowers the contract to the existing private-layout `.segmentalign`
    |       machinery rather than inserting application-data padding.
    |     * Kept `page` semantics unchanged. `page` means the complete object must fit
    |       within one hardware page; it does not imply a zero low byte. `align(256)`
    |       means the object starts at `$xx00` and may span pages. Combining them asks
    |       for both properties.
    |     * The optimizer audit found two real conflations in page-pointer lowering:
    |       compact page selection and direct page-base range lowering had treated any
    |       `page` object as low-byte-zero. They now require `align(256)` or stronger,
    |       or the special exactly-256-byte `page` case where containment itself forces
    |       a page boundary. A 224-byte `page` table is a negative code-generation
    |       regression; `page align(256)` is the corresponding positive regression.
    |     * Runtime placement now honors object alignment for DATA, BSS, and zero-page
    |       storage as well as ROM. `test/align_language_placement.pl` proves that a
    |       small `page` object may legally start at `$F010`, a 300-byte `align(256)`
    |       object starts on a page boundary while spanning pages, and `align(16)` BSS
    |       receives a 16-byte-aligned runtime address.
    |     * Converted all 760-byte conventional `*_ascii.c26` tables and the 1520-byte
    |       `big_ascii.c26` table to contiguous `align(256)` objects. Linked regressions
    |       prove every 8-byte and 16-byte glyph remains wholly within one hardware
    |       page. Small decimal/hex fonts remain legitimately `page` contained.
    |     * Removed the animated gallery's 64 literal zero padding bytes after sprite
    |       120. The final bitmap object is now the 192 bytes of real data rather than
    |       a padded 256-byte object; all four bitmap objects and both packed row-color
    |       objects explicitly use `page align(256)` where low-byte-zero pointer
    |       construction is required.
    |     * The follow-up source audit found no other example application-data padding
    |       used merely to manufacture alignment. Remaining padding references are
    |       timing/layout instructions inside maintained renderers or component layout
    |       contracts, not literal example data to be deleted by this optimization.
    |     * The gallery ROM falls from **3624/4090 to 3560/4090 bytes**, recovering the
    |       full **64 bytes** and leaving **530 bytes free**. RIOT RAM remains
    |       **109/128 bytes** with **19 free**, and `install_frames()` remains
    |       **232 linked bytes**. Exact animation assets, frame timing, raster output,
    |       bankswitching, score, renderer, and component contracts remain unchanged.
    |     * Final verification is **455/455 compile-only** and **260/260 end-to-end**
    |       tests in bounded batches. User-facing compiler documentation now describes
    |       `align(N)` and its distinction from `page`; assembler/linker documentation
    |       records that the source contract is carried by `.segmentalign` and applies
    |       to both load and writable runtime placement.
    | 
    | [x] 6. Reduce the gallery's persistent bookkeeping without changing behavior.
    | 
    |     * Derive `sprite1` from `sprite0` unless storing both is measurably smaller in
    |       ROM or faster within the frame budget.
    |     * Pack pause, Select-edge, and Fire-edge state into one flags byte.
    |     * Reassess whether animation clock and phase fields can share bits or use an
    |       immutable transition table more efficiently.
    |     * Keep the source set-03 modulo-3 phase independent from the normal modulo-4
    |       phase and preserve all controls.
    |     * Do not move persistent state into undocumented renderer scratch.
    | 
    |     Completed result:
    | 
    |     * Packed Select-ready, Fire-ready, and paused state into one `control_flags`
    |       byte. The three one-byte globals therefore become one byte without changing
    |       the edge-triggered console controls.
    |     * Packed the animation phase and frame-hold clock into one `animation_state`
    |       byte. Bits 0..1 remain the normal modulo-4 phase, bits 2..3 remain the
    |       independent source-set-03 modulo-3 phase, and the high nibble counts the
    |       eight-frame hold interval. The existing sixteen-entry transition table
    |       advances both phase fields and clears the hold clock in one assignment.
    |     * Measured the `sprite1` derivation trade instead of assuming one RAM byte was
    |       automatically worth taking. With the other packing already applied, the
    |       stored-selector build is **3545 ROM / 106 RAM** with a 232-byte
    |       `install_frames()`. Rebuilding P1 from P0's adjacent-set relation gives
    |       **3595 ROM / 105 RAM** and grows `install_frames()` to 303 bytes. The
    |       roadmap explicitly permits keeping `sprite1` when storage is measurably
    |       smaller in ROM, so the one-byte selector remains persistent.
    |     * Persistent gallery bookkeeping falls from seven bytes (`sprite0`, `sprite1`,
    |       phase, clock, two edge latches, pause) to four bytes (`sprite0`, `sprite1`,
    |       `animation_state`, `control_flags`). No persistent state is moved into
    |       renderer scratch or an undocumented phase workspace.
    |     * Total gallery RAM falls from **109/128 to 106/128 bytes**, object bytes from
    |       101 to 98, and free RAM rises from **19 to 22 bytes**. The hardware-stack
    |       reservation remains eight bytes and `main` activation remains one byte.
    |       Compact packed-state lowering also reduces ordinary ROM from **3560/4090
    |       to 3545/4090 bytes**, leaving **545 bytes free**.
    |     * `test/vcs_player_color_192_animation.cpp` now decodes the packed state and
    |       still proves all 1,875 automatic gallery frames, exact source pixels/colors,
    |       the independent modulo-3 animation, pair wrapping, Select edge behavior,
    |       fire pause/resume, and exact 262-line frames. The authoritative RAM fixture
    |       is schema 7 and records the measured `sprite1` trade.
    |     * Final verification is **455/455 compile-only** and **260/260 end-to-end**
    |       tests in bounded batches with no timing/raster oracle changes.

    | [x] 7. Reduce hardware-stack reservation by measurement, not by blanket inlining.
    | 
    |     * Report source calls and hidden assembly JSR depth separately in the map.
    |     * Evaluate flattening `prepare_one`/`set_range`, tail-jumping, or converting
    |       single-use helpers to local straight-line assembly when ROM and VBLANK
    |       timing improve.
    |     * Coordinate with main-roadmap item 31, whole-program one-call-site inlining.
    |       That item must still compare final linked size and stack depth; it is not a
    |       command to inline a large repeated routine such as `install_frames()`.
    |     * Consider a no-return startup path only as a separate measured optimization;
    |       do not rely on an ABI accident.
    |     * Keep the linker hardware-stack calculation conservative and exact.
    | 
    |     Completed result:
    | 
    |     * The authoritative gallery map before this item reported source-call depth
    |       2 = four bytes (`startup -> main` plus one ordinary `main` callee) and a
    |       four-byte `.callstackextra` allowance from the renderer, for **8 hardware-
    |       stack bytes total**. The hidden depth came from the VBLANK chain through
    |       the single-use `prepare_object_masks` wrapper and single-use `prepare_one`
    |       wrapper into `set_range`.
    |     * Measured alternatives rather than applying blanket inlining. Flattening only
    |       `prepare_object_masks` reduced the reserve to **6 bytes**. Flattening both
    |       single-use wrappers leaves only `set_range` as an assembly subroutine and
    |       reduces the reserve to the source-call floor of **4 bytes**. The renderer
    |       carries explicit `.callstackextra 0` metadata to record that the remaining
    |       assembly JSR was audited rather than silently omitted from accounting.
    |     * The final linked gallery remains **3545/4090 ROM bytes**; the helper changes
    |       do not increase final ROM. VBLANK helper overhead decreases, while exact
    |       262-line scheduler and renderer timing/raster oracles remain unchanged.
    |     * Total gallery RAM falls from **106/128 to 102/128 bytes**, with object bytes
    |       unchanged at 98 and hardware-stack bytes falling from 8 to 4. Free RAM rises
    |       from **22 to 26 bytes**. The `main` activation remains one byte and
    |       `install_frames()` remains 232 linked bytes.
    |     * Four stack bytes are the conservative minimum under the current ABI because
    |       stock startup enters `main` with JSR and the program has a genuine ordinary
    |       `main -> install_frames` / deadline-helper call depth. A no-return startup
    |       path could only be evaluated as a separate startup/ABI optimization; it was
    |       not used to obtain this result.
    |     * The authoritative animated-gallery RAM fixture is schema 8 and records the
    |       source/hidden split explicitly: source=4 bytes, hidden=0, total=4.
    | 
    | [x] 8. Remeasure the animated gallery on the existing P0/P1/Ball renderer after
    |         compiler and application-state optimization.
    | 
    |     * Retain Ball capability in `player_color_192` even though this particular
    |       gallery sets Ball height to zero.
    |     * Determine whether items 1-7 recover enough RAM for useful game state while
    |       preserving the current general renderer.
    |     * Record ROM, RAM, activation, and stack deltas item by item.
    |     * Do not create a gallery-only renderer merely because it is easy.

    |     Completed remeasurement:

    |     * A fresh build of the unchanged general `player_color_192` gallery reports
    |       **3545/4090 ROM bytes** and **102/128 RIOT-RAM bytes**, leaving **545 ROM
    |       bytes** and **26 RAM bytes** free. `main` owns one activation byte and the

    | [x] 9. Replace or redesign `game_object_masks` using an official-opcode direct-
    |         countdown technique derived from the faithful legacy renderer.
    | 
    |     * Start from the proven fact that the faithful renderer handles arbitrary P0,
    |       P1, and Ball vertical positions without a 48-byte object schedule.
    |     * Design an official-opcode visible-loop schedule that counts object Y state
    |       directly, compares against heights, and restores public state after draw.
    |     * Preserve the maintained renderer's ROM playfield, per-row P0/P1 colors,
    |       arbitrary positions/heights, component lifecycle, handoff behavior, and
    |       exact 192-line/262-line timing.
    |     * Do not import unofficial `DCP` merely because it makes the schedule easy;
    |       an unofficial-opcode variant may be measured separately but cannot silently
    |       replace the official renderer.
    |     * Compare RAM, ROM, VBLANK cost, visible cycles, and hardware-stack depth with
    |       the current mask-based renderer.
    |     * Add exact raster and edge-position regressions before considering removal of
    |       the existing implementation.

    |     Completed direct-countdown redesign:
    | 
    |     * Replaced the 48-byte `game_object_masks` schedule and its VBLANK range builder
    |       with direct P0/P1/Ball countdown state. P0/P1 retain one height+1 threshold
    |       each in the seven-byte phase workspace; Ball compares its public height
    |       against the directly decremented working Y coordinate. The Ball counter uses
    |       a two-pair VBLANK bias to account for VDELBL/GRP1 transfer timing.
    |     * The maintained public profile remains P0/P1/Ball and uses only official
    |       NMOS 6502 opcodes. No `DCP` or raw-opcode substitute was introduced. The
    |       renderer has no hidden assembly JSRs and retains `.callstackextra 0`.
    |     * Renderer private RAM falls from **56 to 10 bytes** and total renderer RAM
    |       from **69 to 23 bytes**. In the animated gallery, object RAM falls from 98
    |       to 52 bytes and total RIOT RAM from **102/128 to 56/128 bytes**, increasing
    |       free RAM from **26 to 72 bytes (56.25%)** while the four-byte hardware-stack
    |       reserve and one-byte `main` activation remain unchanged.
    |     * Gallery ROM falls from **3545 to 3290/4090 bytes**, saving 255 bytes. The
    |       standalone player-color smoke fixture falls from 1623 to 1368 ordinary ROM
    |       bytes and from 82 to 36 RIOT-RAM bytes.
    |     * The measured VBLANK span from the first saved-Y workspace write through the
    |       final pre-visible PF2 staging write falls from **920 to 468 CPU cycles**, a
    |       452-cycle reduction. Horizontal positioning consequently occurs earlier in
    |       VBLANK, but remains entirely blanked and the visible-field entry is unchanged.
    |     * Every visible two-line pair remains exactly **152 CPU cycles** and draw still
    |       owns exactly 192 visible scanlines in a 262-line NTSC frame. Steady playfield
    |       writes remain at cycles **10/17/40/47**. The first P1 half after a row
    |       transition writes the left playfield bytes one cycle earlier at **9/16**
    |       while retaining **40/47** on the right; the independent 160-pixel-per-line
    |       playfield oracle proves that this blanking-region timing change is visually
    |       identical.
    |     * Normal and terminal P0/P1/Ball rasters remain unchanged. New exact Ball edge
    |       regressions cover top-of-visible clipping and a four-pair Ball crossing the
    |       first 16-line row boundary. Those tests exposed and deliberately fix an old
    |       mask-builder defect: near the first row boundary the mask schedule could
    |       shift/clip Ball early or stretch a nominal four-pair Ball to five pairs.
    |       Direct countdown now follows the public Ball coordinate/height rule with
    |       ordinary visible-window clipping. This is an explicitly accepted renderer
    |       fix rather than preserved broken raster behavior.
    |     * The authoritative animated-gallery accounting fixture is schema 10 and pins
    |       the item-8 to item-9 RAM/ROM/VBLANK/visible-cycle/stack comparison plus the
    |       accepted edge fix. The complete animated-gallery emulator oracle passes.
    |     * Final verification is **455/455 compile-only** and **260/260 end-to-end**
    |       cases in bounded batches, including exact player-color/playfield timing,
    |       the animation oracle, recursive examples, bankswitching regressions, RAM
    |       accounting, and source-tree hygiene.

    | [x] 10. Investigate a more compact Ball/object schedule without removing Ball.
    | 
    |     * If direct countdown cannot satisfy the official-opcode schedule, separate
    |       the twelve meaningful Ball-mask bytes from VBLANK-only range-generation
    |       temporaries and P0/P1 threshold values.
    |     * Explore compact schedules, ROM-assisted start/end masks, on-the-fly row
    |       generation, alternate X/index choreography, or a small unpacked visible
    |       cache.
    |     * Measure every candidate against the exact visible-loop cycle schedule. The
    |       current four-byte stride exists for a reason; a smaller table that misses a
    |       beam deadline is not an optimization.
    |     * Preserve arbitrary P0/P1/Ball Y positions and heights if this remains a
    |       general player-color renderer optimization.

    |     schedule satisfies the visible-cycle contract while removing the 48-byte
    |     schedule entirely, so no compact Ball-mask fallback is needed.

    | [x] 10a. Propagate the delayed-Ball row-boundary repair and RAM cleanup through
    |           every maintained componentized player/Ball renderer.
    | 
    |     * Auditing the sibling renderers exposed the real row-boundary failure: VDELBL
    |       transfers the pending Ball value on `GRP1`, and each 16-line row transition
    |       performs an extra `GRP1`. The old mask renderers, and the first direct-count
    |       `player_color_192` conversion, could reach that transfer before the next Ball
    |       value had been written to ENABL. A Ball pair adjacent to the boundary was then
    |       duplicated while the following pair disappeared.
    |     * `player_color_192` and both `player_color_181` twins now stage ENABL before
    |       every row-boundary `GRP1`. The 181-line pair also drops its 43-byte mask
    |       schedule entirely and uses direct P0/P1/Ball countdowns. Its contract falls
    |       from **13 public + 51 private = 64 bytes** to **13 public + 11 private = 24
    |       bytes**. The two retained NUSIZ shadow bytes inside `object_x[5]` are private;
    |       the remaining private storage is eight phase-workspace bytes plus one
    |       playfield-position byte.
    |     * The all-five renderers cannot afford direct per-pair missile countdowns in
    |       the official-opcode beam budget, so their cycle-critical Ball/M1/M0 bit
    |       schedules remain. The fourth byte of each four-byte row, previously dead,
    |       now carries scratch/state and the playfield-position value. `all_five_192`
    |       falls from **78 to 71 component RAM bytes** (55 to 48 private), and both
    |       181-line all-five twins fall from **74 to 67 bytes** (51 to 44 private).
    |       The 181 schedule grows from 43 to 44 bytes solely to make the final fourth
    |       lane explicit; the former separate seven-byte workspace and one-byte
    |       playfield-position object disappear.
    |     * Ball Y/height inputs are no longer destructively rewritten in the all-five
    |       VBLANK path. P0/P1 state is still restored where the visible handoff uses it.
    |       The official and unofficial 181 twins retain identical visible traces; the
    |       unofficial all-five twin keeps only its one reviewed zero-page NOP pad, while
    |       the unofficial player-color twin currently emits no unofficial mnemonic at
    |       all because the removed mask helper contained the former substitutions.
    |     * New pixel-level edge cartridges pin Y=8,height=3 across the first packed row
    |       for both all-five families. The player-color 192 top/boundary oracles and the
    |       181 motion oracle likewise require the corrected delayed-Ball transfer.
    |       Internal row boundaries now produce one Ball pair per requested two scanlines
    |       with no five-pair stretch or dropped following pair.
    |     * A manual Stella pass caught a second transition regression after Ball staging:
    |       the first repair moved reflected PF writes too early. In `player_color_192`,
    |       row-ending PF2/PF1 at cycles 30/33 (and terminal 37/44) changed PF state while
    |       the TIA was still consuming the left half. The 181 player-color twins had the
    |       same class of defect at 38/41. Byte-level register reconstruction was internally
    |       consistent and therefore missed the hardware-timing failure.
    |     * A second manual Stella pass showed the first 192-only timing repair was still
    |       wrong: both public `player_color_192` examples had one visibly malformed
    |       scanline at each 16-line playfield-row boundary. The symmetric smoke fixture
    |       hid it. The final 192 schedule leaves the proven playfield writes where the
    |       old visibly-correct renderer had them and carries the direct Ball decision
    |       across the boundary instead of moving PF traffic. In the display-raster
    |       harness, steady and nonterminal row-ending writes are **10/17/40/47**, the
    |       first following P1 half is **9/16/40/47**, and the terminal row is
    |       **10/17/43/50**. The independent physical-phase harness observes the same
    |       schedule as **18/25/48/55**, **17/24/48/55**, and terminal **18/25/51/58**.
    |       The 181 schedules remain **18/25/48/55** steady, **18/25/45/48** row ending,
    |       **17/24/48/55** first following P1, and **18/25/48/55** terminal.
    |     * Playfield testing is now deliberately asymmetric. The default
    |       `vcs_player_color_192.pl` compiles the actual public interactive example and
    |       checks all 12x16 PF write rows against the Stella-proven byte/order/phase
    |       contract; `vcs_player_color_192_animation.pl` does the same for the animated
    |       gallery's full/checker/blank/checker/full playfield. Both oracles demonstrably
    |       reject the broken 15/22/51/54 boundary schedule. `make
    |       stella-player-color-192-test` independently snapshots the interactive example
    |       under Stella 7.0 and compares it to a reviewed raster.
    |     * The final factoring is **3292/4090 ROM** and **56/128 RAM** for the animated
    |       gallery, still leaving **72 bytes free**; the standalone player-color-192
    |       smoke is **1374 ROM / 36 RAM** after the later four-byte NTSC frame-closeout correction. Schema **13** records the Stella-corrected
    |       follow-up. The 181 player-color official/unofficial smoke pair remains
    |       size-identical at **1422 ROM / 39 RAM**.
    |     * Representative current links: player-color-181 smoke **1422 ROM / 39 RAM**
    |       including its fixture and four-byte stack; all-five-192 smoke **1621 ROM / 89
    |       RAM** including its fixture and eight-byte stack; all-five-181 smoke **1794 ROM
    |       / 86 RAM** including its fixture and eight-byte stack. The centered interactive
    |       player-color-181 example uses 65/128 RAM, all-five-192 uses 91/128, and centered
    |       all-five-181 uses 108/128.
    |     * Final verification is **455/455 compile-only** and **260/260 end-to-end**.
    |       The full public score/gameplay matrix and wide-score 181 compositions pin the
    |       direct-count transition schedules; the new asymmetric public-example oracle
    |       additionally rejects the exact broken 192 boundary schedule, and the reviewed
    |       Stella 7.0 snapshot independently certifies the visible player-color-192
    |       playfield raster.

    | [x] 11. Only if a generally useful capability split is still justified, create a

    |     and the complete animated gallery uses 56/128 RIOT-RAM bytes with **72 bytes
    |     free (56.25%)**. Removing Ball would therefore create another maintained

    | [x] 12. Only if a generally useful capability split is still justified, create an
    |          181-line two-sprite-only renderer.
    | 
    |     * This is the score-composable counterpart to item 11.
    |     * Preserve the 181-line renderer's score-above/score-below handoff contracts,
    |       arbitrary P0/P1 positioning, per-row colors, playfield timing, and exact
    |       262-line frame behavior.
    |     * Provide centered-score and wide-score interactive examples above and below
    |       gameplay, plus independent raster/timing/install tests.
    |     * Do not fork shared timing logic unnecessarily; derive it from the proven
    |       maintained 181-line renderer schedule.
    | 
    |     Closed as unnecessary after final measurement. The retained official 181-line
    |     P0/P1/Ball component is only **24 component RAM bytes**; the centered public
    |     score composition uses 65/128 RAM bytes with 63 free, and even the maintained
    |     wide-score compositions fit at 118/128 with ten bytes free. The unofficial
    |     twin has the same RAM contract. A second two-sprite-only score-composable
    |     family would add examples, timing contracts, installation coverage, and
    |     maintenance burden without an identified application requirement.
    | 
    | [x] 13. Establish completion gates and choose the retained architecture.
    | 
    |     * The optimized gallery must have meaningful free RAM for application/game
    |       state; merely reaching 128/128 again is failure.
    |     * Publish before/after maps and identify savings from expression lifetime
    |       overlay, inline scratch sharing, compact lowering, high-level example
    |       conversion, persistent-state packing, stack depth, and any renderer change
    |       separately.
    |     * Keep existing public renderer binaries/raster behavior stable unless a
    |       change is explicitly accepted as a renderer fix.
    |     * Run the complete compile-only and end-to-end suites, staged installation,
    |       recursive examples, and focused Stella/simulator oracles.
    |     * Update `.../context.txt`, `.../roadmap.txt` where cross-references are
    |       appropriate, renderer READMEs, compiler ABI/developer documentation, and
    |       the daily history.
    | 
    |     Completion decision and final accounting:
    | 
    |     * **Retained architecture:** keep the existing general P0/P1/Ball
    |       `player_color_192` and `player_color_181` profiles, plus the maintained
    |       all-five profiles and their deliberately separate unofficial twins. Do not
    |       add 192- or 181-line two-sprite-only profiles without a future concrete use
    |       case. Items 11 and 12 are therefore closed as unnecessary, not deferred.
    |     * The animated-gallery map moves from the measured baseline **3993/4090 ROM,
    |       128/128 RAM, 0 free** to **3292/4090 ROM, 56/128 RAM, 72 free**. The final
    |       128-byte RAM ownership is: 8 runtime scratch, 39 renderer state (13 public,
    |       10 private, 16 mutable row colors), 4 persistent gallery state, 1 `main`
    |       activation byte, 4 hardware-stack bytes, and 72 unallocated bytes.
    |     * The **72 RAM bytes recovered** reconcile exactly: 19 activation bytes,
    |       3 persistent-state bytes, 4 hardware-stack bytes, and 46 renderer bytes.
    |       Within the activation work, repeated-inline accounting attributes 12 bytes
    |       of the item-1 reduction to eliminating two duplicated six-byte `next_pair()`
    |       scratch footprints; item 2 added no further physical bytes because item 1's
    |       activation-owner lifetime coloring had already delivered that sharing.
    |       Compact byte lowering removes another five activation bytes and frame-phase
    |       overlay removes the final one.
    |     * The **701 ROM bytes recovered** also reconcile exactly: compact lowering
    |       saves 331; replacing hand-written `install_frames()` with ordinary VCSC plus
    |       its optimizer follow-up is a net 38-byte gallery saving versus the old
    |       hand-written version; removing literal alignment padding saves 64; packed
    |       persistent bookkeeping saves 15; and the final direct-countdown renderer,
    |       including all accepted Ball/playfield correctness repairs, saves 253.
    |       Expression/inline lifetime overlay, phase overlay, and stack-depth reduction
    |       add no ROM cost at their final checkpoints.
    |     * `install_frames()` remains ordinary VCSC at 232 linked bytes with zero
    |       activation bytes. The hardware stack is four bytes, the conservative floor
    |       under the current startup/call ABI. Ball remains a supported public object;
    |       the gallery merely chooses height zero. Visible pairs remain 152 CPU cycles,
    |       the frame remains 192 visible / 262 total lines, and the final playfield/
    |       delayed-Ball fixes are explicitly accepted renderer correctness repairs.
    |     * The authoritative accounting fixture is schema **14** and pins both the
    |       before/after ownership maps and this retained-architecture decision. The
    |       compiler ABI/developer documentation was audited against the final result;
    |       no additional ABI change is introduced by this closeout because activation
    |       lifetime coloring, phase workspace ownership, and hardware-stack accounting
    |       were documented when implemented.
    |     * Completion gates pass: **455/455 compile-only**, **260/260 end-to-end**,
    |       recursive public examples, staged `installcheck`, source-tree hygiene, the
    |       focused simulator/raster/accounting oracles, and the reviewed Stella 7.0
    |       player-color-192 raster certification. The RAM optimization campaign has a
    |       meaningful 56.25% application-state margin and is complete except for the
    |       separately scoped ordinary-application-assembly cleanup in item 14.
    | 
    | [x] 14. Remove remaining ordinary application assembly recorded by the example allowlist.
    | 
    |     * Replace hand-written packed-BCD score-control updates with compact VCSC
    |       once dynamic digit-weight arithmetic can be lowered without the current
    |       ROM/scratch penalty.
    |     * Replace selected-object address-table manipulation in the all-five
    |       interactive controls once compact heterogeneous byte-state selection and
    |       indirect update lowering is available.
    |     * Replace the fingerprint example's raw representation copies and manual
    |       six-glyph pointer-byte installation once the language/compiler can express
    |       those representation-level operations without obscuring the example or
    |       inflating it past its cartridge budget.
    |     * Keep direct reset-vector jumps, cycle-exact display sequences, and the
    |       fingerprint's intentional unofficial-opcode probes in assembly; those are
    |       hardware/timing idioms rather than ordinary application logic.
    |     * Use the example assembly allowlist and its focused behavioral regressions as
    |       the removal checklist. Delete allowlist debt when equivalent VCSC source is
    |       demonstrably as small and preserves the existing exact behavior or raster.
    | 
    |     Completed. Ordinary application assembly debt is now zero.
    | 
    |     * Fixed six-digit and two-plus-two score controls are ordinary VCSC. Runtime
    |       digit selection uses packed-BCD `+=`/`-=` against a same-width indexed
    |       decimal-weight table; the compiler lowers the selected 24-bit weight to a
    |       three-byte `SED`/`ADC` or `SBC` chain without `_mul8` or generic expression
    |       scratch. The faithful legacy interactive score is now declared `bcd24_t`,
    |       so it uses the same high-level control path without changing its three-byte
    |       representation.
    |     * All-five X motion is ordinary indexed byte-array VCSC. The compiler now
    |       lowers direct runtime-indexed byte comparisons and constant `+=`/`-=`
    |       updates without constructing a general lvalue. The five separately named Y
    |       fields remain intact for the renderer/debug ABI and are selected by a
    |       high-level `switch`; no address-table manipulation or indirect application
    |       assembly remains. Representative final public builds remain comfortably
    |       within 4K/128-byte limits: all-five-192 is **2210 ROM / 92 RAM**, and the
    |       centered official/unofficial all-five-181 score examples are **3229 ROM /
    |       112 RAM**.
    |     * The fingerprint's display-word copy is high-level typed access, and its six
    |       glyph-address arrays are correctly declared `uint16_t[6]` instead of raw
    |       `uint8_t[12]`. Constant-index assignments such as
    |       `upper_logo_pointers[3] := (uint16_t)&logo_font[24]` lower directly to
    |       relocatable low/high stores. The fingerprint fits at **3853 ROM / 108 RAM**;
    |       its only remaining assembly is the deliberate ARR silicon probes and the
    |       simulator self-test trap.
    |     * Three compiler regressions pin the new compact paths: indexed packed-BCD
    |       compound arithmetic, indexed unsigned-byte compare/update, and relocatable
    |       16-bit array-address assignment. The public score, all-five interaction,
    |       fingerprint, six-glyph, animation, and RAM-accounting behavior oracles all
    |       pass.
    |     * The example assembly allowlist contracts from 21 entries to **9** and now
    |       rejects any `compiler-limitation` entry. Every remaining source assembly
    |       statement is classified only as beam-critical or a direct hardware idiom:
    |       cycle-exact low-level examples, Reset-vector jumps, ARR probes, and the
    |       simulator trap.
    |     * The RAM-roadmap closeout remains **3292/4090 ROM, 56/128 RAM, 72 free** before
    |       the later shared scheduler correction. Current scheduler users pay four ROM bytes
    |       for the Stella-proven 262-line frame closeout, so the animated gallery is now
    |       **3296/4090 ROM, 56/128 RAM, 72 free** under authoritative schema 15. The

    |     The RAM-optimization roadmap is complete. Future assembly removal should be
    |     driven by a concrete language/compiler improvement, not by weakening timing
    |     code whose purpose is to expose the hardware directly.
