    | Post-closeout public-example assembly re-audit (2026-08-15)
    | ------------------------------------------------------------
    | Later roadmap work added controller and game examples after item 14's original
    | closeout, so the public-example assembly inventory was audited again from scratch.
    | Every `asm` statement under `examples/` was classified by execution context. Four
    | ordinary off-beam preparation paths were returned to high-level VCSC: four-player
    | Paddleball Ball fine-motion preparation, keypad glyph-pointer preparation, Driving
    | glyph-pointer preparation, and Tanks RNG/graphics/packed-position preparation. This
    | removed **76 source `asm` statements**, reducing the current public-example total from
    | **890 to 814**. The allowlist still contains **21 source files** because none of those
    | four examples became assembly-free; their visible positioning/raster code remains
    | cycle-critical.
    | 
    | The remaining assembly is intentionally restricted to beam-critical code or direct
    | hardware idioms. This includes RESP/HMOVE and visible kernels, the Tanks fixed 6502
    | knockback barrier scan whose compiler-generated equivalent exceeded the overscan
    | deadline, Reset-vector indirect jumps through `$FFFC`, fingerprint ARR silicon probes
    | and simulator trap, and the PAL/SECAM 50 Hz phase NOPs. No `compiler-limitation` debt
    | was reintroduced. The post-audit gates passed **492/492 compile-only**, **303/303
    | end-to-end**, all recursive public-example build shards, and staged `installcheck`.
    | 
    | Post-audit high-level codegen recovery (2026-08-15)
    | ---------------------------------------------------
    | The four examples returned to ordinary VCSC are compiler code-size benchmarks instead
    | of excuses to put their assembly back. The first recovery pass: 
    | 
    | * emits file-scope array-decay pointer initializers directly as relocatable low/high data
    |   instead of BSS plus run-time initialization;
    | * fuses adjacent `p := array; p += byte_offset` setup when the pointee/offset proof is
    |   exact, while retaining the established page-aligned specialization;
    | * keeps mechanically safe promoted unsigned-byte masks and constant shifts in 8-bit
    |   code, including direct constant `<<=`/`>>=` ASL/LSR sequences; and
    | * stores eligible non-coalesced one-byte automatic initializers directly from A instead
    |   of allocating expression scratch. Pointer-backed and packed-BCD initializers stay on
    |   the typed general path.
    | 
    | A second measured pass extends that general lowering rather than special-casing the
    | examples:
    | 
    | * direct one-byte `+`, `-`, `&`, `|`, and `^` expressions with a memory operand now
    |   stay in A and use the corresponding 6502 ALU instruction instead of generic scratch;
    | * an explicit int8-to-uint8 cast may feed that path as the same raw byte when no BCD or
    |   pointer representation conversion is involved;
    | * unsigned-byte multiplication by a constant is strength-reduced to a Horner-style
    |   ASL/ADC sequence, so Keypad can write the natural `key * 10` and delete its 13-byte
    |   ROM offset table;
    | * `if (x & 1) x := (x >> 1) ^ K; else x >>= 1` reuses the carry produced by LSR, giving
    |   CRC/LFSR-style code the natural 6502 lowering without a polynomial-specific hack; and
    | * direct global/static unsigned-byte returns copy straight into the memory return slot.
    |   Locals and parameters deliberately remain on the existing specialization path, and
    |   packed BCD is excluded.
    | 
    | Measured against the immediate post-audit high-level tree, the four public benchmarks
    | now recover **422 ROM bytes**: four-player Paddleball 3717 -> **3617** (101 RAM),
    | Keypad 1711/47 -> **1647/43**, Driving 1812/53 -> **1669/41**, and Tanks bank1 2951 ->
    | **2885**, bank0 3427 -> **3378**, RIOT 115 -> **112**, Superchip unchanged at 99.
    | Against the older hand-written-assembly versions the same programs are collectively
    | **151 ROM bytes smaller**: Paddleball is 77 smaller, Driving 74 smaller, Tanks 14
    | smaller across its two banks, while Keypad is the only holdout at 14 bytes larger. The
    | source-only blank and leaf-activation RAM savings from the first pass remain intact.
    | 
    | Four new compile-only regressions pin the second-pass byte-memory ALU, constant multiply,
    | carry-aware shift/XOR, and direct-global-return paths. The existing E2E controller/game
    | oracles exercise the optimized arithmetic semantically. A BCD E2E regression retains
    | the required packed-BCD exclusion. One superficially obvious micro-optimization remains
    | rejected after measurement: replacing `LDA index; TAY` with direct `LDY index` saves two
    | bytes locally but shifts a page-constrained all-five 4K layout enough that the linker can
    | no longer place `__zero_table`. The stable form remains intentionally larger; that
    | public cartridge stays **4013/4090**. Final gates are **499/499 compile-only**,
    | **303/303 end-to-end**, all eight example-build shards, and staged `installcheck`.
    | 
    | Post-closeout fixed-address lowering audit
    | -------------------------------------------
    | Completed. The compiler no longer routes a compile-time-constant offset into a fixed
    | linker symbol through Y merely because the object is activation storage or compiler
    | scratch. Fixed globals, parameters, return objects, locals, scratch, pointer values,
    | small fills/copies, comparisons, bitwise/BCD transforms, fixed subscripts, and call
    | staging now use direct `symbol + offset` operands. The former 256-byte cutoff on this
    | proof is removed: a fixed linker symbol plus offset remains directly addressable beyond
    | one page. Direct pointer variables are loaded from their own bytes before traversing
    | the pointee, instead of first taking the pointer variable's address and dereferencing
    | that temporary address. `ref` parameter slots are explicitly excluded from fixed-value
    | lowering: their pointer slots are direct, but reading or writing the referred object
    | still dereferences the slot and preserves directional read/write projection. Remaining
    | `symbol,Y` and `(ptrN),Y` lowering is reserved for genuine runtime array indexing and
    | pointer indirection. Existing codegen regressions
    | were updated to forbid the obsolete constant-Y forms rather than preserving them for
    | peephole tests. A dedicated regression reaches automatic-local byte offset 299 and
    | requires direct `symbol + 299` loads/stores, locking removal of the old one-page cutoff.
    | Representative linked savings from the audit are player-color-181 smoke 1432 -> 1422
    | ROM, all-five-181 smoke 1799 -> 1794, the six-glyph-wide fixture 926 -> 890, the public
    | wide-score example 1065 -> 969, wide-score 181 compositions 2668 -> 2554, and the banked
    | standard-renderer diagnostic from 1848 -> 1827 bytes in bank0 while ordinary bank1
    | falls 76 -> 55 bytes and F8SC bank1 115 -> 86 bytes. RAM and behavioral/raster contracts
    | for those fixtures are unchanged.
    | 
    | Expected direction
    | ------------------
    | The twenty-byte `main` activation is mostly static-lifetime waste, not a visible
    | renderer requirement. Lifetime overlay between separate expressions and shared
    | scratch for repeated inline expansions should be completed before deciding that
    | Ball support must be removed. Those compiler changes are likely to benefit many
    | VCSC programs, especially non-returning frame loops, and may make the existing
    | three-object renderer entirely practical for this gallery.
    | 
    | The faithful legacy renderer proves that arbitrary P0/P1/Ball vertical motion can
    | also be implemented without a 48-byte object schedule. An official-opcode direct-
    | countdown redesign should therefore be attempted before deleting Ball support.
    | 
    | The animated gallery's ordinary data-selection and color-expansion code should
    | also return to high-level VCSC once compiler lowering and scratch allocation make
    | that practical. A compiler project whose examples become assembly programs has
    | missed the point.
    | 
    | A two-sprite-only renderer can still be valuable as an intentional low-RAM
    | profile, but it should be justified as a reusable capability choice after the
    | compiler stops wasting scratch and after direct countdown is measured—not used
    | as camouflage for either problem.
    | 
    | 
    | Post-closeout multisprite RAM cleanup (2026-08-09)
    | ----------------------------------------------------
    | The modern parameterized multisprite renderer received a renderer-local cleanup after
    | its timing/flicker repairs. Five immutable P1 graphics pointers no longer occupy ten
    | RIOT-RAM bytes: fixed low offsets are loaded from ROM and the common aligned graphics
    | page high byte is loaded directly with cycle-balanced beam timing. A dead state byte
    | was removed, the P0 Y=0 restore flag is carried in bit 7 of the existing low-nibble
    | scanline mask, the two one-shot VBLANK wrapper subroutines were inlined so hidden
    | assembly stack depth falls from four bytes to two, and the exact five-value persistent
    | flicker permutation is nibble-packed into three bytes. The module contract falls from
    | 86 to **72 bytes**. The 181 interactive score compositions fall from **126/128 to
    | 110/128 RAM**, leaving 18 bytes free; 192 falls from 107 to 91 total RAM. Exhaustive
    | MOS6502/TIA timing/flicker tests and the independent Stella pixel oracle remain the
    | gates. The remaining large costs (generic score pointer workspace, runtime/startup
    | workspace, and compiler activation storage) are architectural and are not hidden by
    | renderer-specific aliasing.
    | 
    | Post-closeout application/runtime RAM cleanup (2026-08-10)
    | ------------------------------------------------------------
    | Completed. The three largest non-renderer costs exposed by the multisprite 181 score
    | composition were reduced without renderer-specific aliasing.
    | 
    | * **Stock runtime workspace: 8 -> 4 bytes.** `vcsc-rt0.s26` imports only `ptr0` and
    |   `ptr1`; the copy/zero/init walkers reconstruct active addresses from immutable linker
    |   records instead of retaining `ptr2/arg0/arg1`. This deliberately spends reset-time ROM
    |   and cycles to save persistent zero page. A 300-byte DATA/BSS regression crosses the
    |   255-byte boundary, and helper archive members still demand-load extra workspace only
    |   when imported.
    | * **Multisprite interactive `main`: 4 -> 0 activation bytes.** Direct 1..4-byte
    |   compile-time scalar stores no longer stage through expression scratch, direct byte
    |   switches compare the selector in place, and the fixed three-line NTSC VSYNC sequence
    |   no longer allocates an inline wait-count byte. The direct VSYNC path retains the
    |   historical post-third-WSYNC phase explicitly; an earlier shorter version was rejected
    |   after producing a 263-line raw frame.
    | * **Centered mutable-color six-glyph score: 18 -> 15 bytes.** The page-contained font
    |   lets digits 1/2 retain byte offsets while digits 3..6 keep complete pointers. Pointer
    |   storage is therefore 12 -> 10 bytes, and the fixed eight-row raster eliminates the RAM
    |   row counter. The schedule is cycle-balanced so every historical GRP write remains at
    |   its exact old cycle. More aggressive all-offset/hybrid variants that reached 95/97-byte
    |   cartridges were rejected because the exact score raster moved.
    | 
    | The maintained public multisprite totals are now **2613 ROM / 84 RAM / 44 free** for
    | 192 and **3409 ROM / 99 RAM / 29 free** for each 181+score order. The 181 build thus
    | recovers another **11 RAM bytes** from the prior 110-byte checkpoint and **27 bytes**
    | from the original 126-byte repaired build. These are compiler/runtime/component savings,
    | not hidden reductions to the 72-byte multisprite renderer contract.
    | 
    | The same architectural changes reduce the animated `player_color_192` gallery from the
    | historical schema-15 **3296 ROM / 56 RAM / 72 free** checkpoint to schema-16
    | **3377 ROM / 51 RAM / 77 free**: the reset walker spends 81 additional ROM bytes while
    | stock startup and fixed VSYNC recover five permanent RAM bytes. The authoritative gallery
    | ledger now classifies runtime workspace 4, renderer state 39, persistent state 4, main
    | activation 0, hardware stack 4, and free RAM 77. Banked standard-renderer diagnostics
    | likewise retain their raster/bridge contracts while reflecting the larger reset walker
    | and smaller RAM workspace; exact current bank sizes are regression-pinned rather than
    | being treated as renderer-size regressions.
    | 


2026-08-22 20:43:08 PDT, archive pre-cleanup video_standard_roadmap.txt

    Cold snapshot of .../video_standard_roadmap.txt immediately before the context-trimming pass.
    The maintained hot copy was then reduced to durable current state and unfinished work.

    | VCSC PAL + SECAM VIDEO-STANDARD ROADMAP
    | =======================================
    | 
    | Purpose
    | -------
    | Implement PAL and SECAM together as one 50 Hz video-standard workstream. Share
    | frame/timing infrastructure where the hardware contract is genuinely common,
    | but keep PAL and SECAM explicit public profiles because their color behavior is
    | not interchangeable. NTSC output must remain byte-for-byte stable.
    | 
    | Status legend: [ ] not started, [~] partial, [x] complete.
    | 
    | 0. [x] Shared 50 Hz frame infrastructure
    |    * Define the common 76-CPU-cycle scanline and measured 312-line 50 Hz frame
    |      contract: 3 VSYNC + 45 VBLANK + 228 visible + 36 overscan.
    |    * Put common scheduler mechanics in one directly-instantiated source module;
    |      expose thin `frame_pal.c26` and `frame_secam.c26` front ends with distinct
    |      public names and diagnostics.
    |    * Calibrate PAL/SECAM RIOT TIM64T phase deadlines independently of NTSC and
    |      verify phase entry/return contracts in the CPU scheduler harness.
    |    * Verify Stella recognizes stable PAL50 and SECAM50 frames and does not change
    |      any existing NTSC scheduler output.
    |    * Unit/E2E coverage: compile both front ends, exact constants, timer preloads,
    |      frame length, VBLANK boundaries, overrun diagnostics, installed files.
    | 
    | 1. [x] PAL palette and compile-time RGB matching
    |    * Add `__builtin_pal_rgb(r,g,b)` using the project-selected Stella-compatible
    |      PAL reference palette and a public `color_pal.c26` alias set.
    |    * Keep PAL color-loss behavior explicit: palette matching chooses a TIA byte;
    |      the alternating-line color-loss effect is a display property, not a fake
    |      second palette baked into the matcher.
    |    * Unit/E2E coverage: exact reference colors, nearest-color tie behavior,
    |      argument/range diagnostics, no NTSC palette regression.
    | 
    | 2. [x] SECAM palette and legal-color contract
    |    * Add a SECAM compile-time color matcher/mapping based on the eight distinct
    |      SECAM colors. Make legal mappings explicit rather than reusing PAL bytes.
    |    * Add `color_secam.c26`, including clear legal-color aliases and diagnostics
    |      for requested color/effect contracts that SECAM cannot represent faithfully.
    |    * Unit/E2E coverage: all eight colors, mapping/range diagnostics, and explicit
    |      rejection/warning cases as appropriate.
    | 
    |    Completed: `__builtin_secam_rgb()` maps through the eight-color SECAM
    |    reference set and returns canonical low even TIA bytes; `color_secam.c26`
    |    exposes only those eight public aliases. Higher-level callers therefore cannot
    |    accidentally request a ninth SECAM color through the typed palette surface.
    |    Raw numeric TIA color writes remain the documented low-level escape hatch and
    |    are intentionally not diagnosed because the compiler cannot infer their display
    |    intent. Compile/E2E tests lock all eight colors and generic builtin diagnostics.
    | 
    | 3. [x] 50 Hz audio/timing helpers
    |    * Add PAL/SECAM audio constants only where the machine clock changes the TIA
    |      result; share frame-rate helpers where both standards are identical.
    |    * Keep NTSC `sound_ntsc.c26` unchanged.
    |    * Unit/E2E coverage: note/control aliases and frame-duration helpers.
    | 
    | 4. [x] Renderer/component portability profiles
    |    * Classify every maintained visible component as timing-portable, palette-
    |      portable with recoloring, or unavailable on PAL/SECAM.
    |    * Port reusable renderers only when the 228-line visible composition and
    |      VBLANK/overscan callback budgets are measured. Do not scatter PAL/SECAM
    |      conditionals through NTSC renderer bodies.
    |    * Add PAL/SECAM-specific profile wrappers or template parameters only where
    |      their timing contracts remain independently testable.
    |    * Unit/E2E coverage: exact 312-line frames and raster equivalence where the
    |      geometry is intended to be shared.
    | 
    | 5. [x] Public examples and emulator-backed acceptance
    |    * Add minimal PAL and SECAM cartridges plus at least one representative
    |      gameplay/component composition for each standard.
    |    * Force the intended Stella display format in tests and verify frame/raster
    |      output; verify PAL color-loss expectations and SECAM eight-color behavior.
    |    * Document which source assets/colors are portable and which require a
    |      standard-specific palette.
    | 
    | 6. [x] Installation, docs, and final integration
    |    * Install all new public frame/color/sound/profile files and examples.
    |    * Update `libraries/vcs/README.md`, renderer authoring guidance, examples, and
    |      top-level install/uninstall/installcheck coverage.
    |    * Run the complete compile/E2E/installcheck matrices, with NTSC regression
    |      hashes/timing unchanged.
    |    * Mark main-roadmap items 32 and 33 complete together only when both PAL and
    |      SECAM meet their acceptance criteria.
    | 
    | Completion summary
    | ------------------
    | PAL and SECAM now share one measured 50 Hz scheduler core while retaining distinct
    | public frame, color, and sound front ends. The common frame is 312 lines: 3 VSYNC,
    | 45 VBLANK, 228 visible, and 36 overscan. PAL RGB matching uses the Stella-compatible
    | 128-entry reference palette; SECAM exposes the eight canonical display colors.
    | Shared 50 Hz sound helpers provide frame-duration constants and the public PAL/SECAM
    | front ends preserve distinct machine identities. Maintained scheduler-neutral C26
    | renderers/components are classified as timing-portable; legacy full-frame renderers
    | remain explicitly NTSC-only. Public examples are organized under separate `examples/17_video_standards/pal/`
    | and `secam/` subtrees, each with local `00_blank` and `01_all_five` numbering.
    | They use `__builtin_pal_rgb()` / `__builtin_secam_rgb()` directly and build from
    | the installed toolchain. The PAL/SECAM all-five examples use the same RGB intents
    | as the NTSC all-five example before standard-specific palette matching. Because
    | SECAM50 is not reliably distinguishable from PAL50 by timing alone, each public
    | example's `make play` target forces the corresponding Stella `-format`; `-tv` is
    | not a display-format option. The interactive all-five examples are verified under
    | Stella 7.0.
    | 
    | Current validation: 499/499 compile tests and 305/305 E2E tests (run in bounded
    | exact groups where needed); the Stella 7.0 50 Hz phase/snapshot regression passes,
    | and staged installed-tree builds cover all four reorganized video-standard examples. NTSC scheduler and renderer regressions remain
    | green. Main-roadmap items 32 and 33 close together.


2026-08-22 20:43:09 PDT, archive pre-cleanup instruction.txt

2026-08-22 20:45:43 PDT, compact developer handoff records

Reworked the developer-only `...` records into hot state versus cold history.
Pre-cleanup copies of context, roadmap, bankswitching, disassembler, enhanced
renderer notes, RAM/inlining/video roadmaps, and instruction.txt were archived
above as indented cold snapshots. The maintained hot files now contain only
durable invariants, current authoritative state, unfinished acceptance criteria,
and immediate next work.

`context.txt` fell from 97,062 bytes to 7,172 bytes. All eleven maintained hot
records total 38,376 bytes. `source_tree_hygiene.pl` now enforces a 16 KiB
context ceiling, per-file focused limits, a 64 KiB aggregate hot-record ceiling,
open-only main/bankswitching roadmaps, and absence of completed checklist entries
instead of requiring old completed prose. `example_assembly_allowlist.pl` no
longer depends on a completed RAM-roadmap sentence; its executable fixture is the
authority.

The active asymmetric handoff explicitly records the remaining state-dependent
262/263 Stella frame jitter and requires multi-frame Monte Carlo with fixed X/Y
positions and persistent priority state. `instruction.txt` now tells future chats
to keep completed work in daily history and hot records compact. The user's
release-tag reminder at its bottom is unchanged.

Focused validation after the cleanup: context_history_format.pl passes,
source_tree_hygiene.pl passes, and example_assembly_allowlist.pl passes. The
branding test requires generated libraries/runtime/libvcsc.l26; rebuilding that
from the clean source archive is blocked in this container because bison is not
installed, so no full branding result is claimed here.


2026-08-22 21:22:29 PDT, fix asymmetric held-layout frame jitter

The intermittent 262/263 Stella-frame jitter was reduced to the supplied
same-column state X={0,0,0,0,0,0}, Y={94,93,46,81,62,65}, priority=
{5,2,4,0,3,1}. In the baseline tarball the pinned real-6502 harness crossed the
VBLANK boundary and produced a 267-raw-line interval instead of the calibrated
264-raw-line / 262-Stella interval. Repeated bridge-orientation searches were the
dominant variable cost.

Reworked the bridge oracle without changing its forced geometry. It now scans
only priorities later than the current candidate, checks the rare +/-12 Y
separation before paying for X comparisons, and records a proven future endpoint
bit in setup_line[6], which is scheduler scratch until the existing sort-sentinel
clear. Tiny middle-scan, mask-test, and mask-write helpers are phase-pinned into
otherwise-unused ROM holes at F2F1, F67E, and F1F9. The main bridge oracle fills
the complete 64-byte F3C0 hole. The 63/13 TIM64T split and visible renderer are
unchanged.

Added `same-column bridge-oracle deadline` to
`vcs_enhanced_multisprite_asymmetric.pl`. The deterministic witness now passes
117 checked frames at 262 lines. The existing focused asymmetric test passes,
and a real-renderer sweep passed all 720 priority permutations of the bridge
scene.

Held-layout Monte Carlo then ran 252,200 checked frames with X/Y fixed for each
run while the real persistent priority/omission state evolved. It covered 300
layouts each for X palettes of 1, 2, 3, 4, 8, and 160 values plus 200 shifted
same/near-column layouts each for palette sizes 1 through 4. No 263-line interval
was found. Current public accounting is 4085/4090 ROM and 115/128 RAM.

Most bounded runner shards passed with a stricter temporary 15-second default

The maintained `test/test.pl` was not changed and still uses the project-wide

2026-08-22 22:40:17 PDT, fix real-Stella pre-WSYNC phase jitter

Interactive Stella disproved the earlier CPU-harness-only closure of the
262/263 jitter. A valid deterministic state X={14,0,25,88,114,140},
Y={76,67,95,95,95,95}, priority={2,3,4,5,0,1} produced 263 on all 80 checked
frames in the 63/13 build. The pre-bridge-optimization ROM also reproduced it,
so this witness predates the latest bridge-oracle change rather than being a
regression caused by that optimization.

Debugger breakpoints identified a second timing mechanism. Both good and bad
states reached the RIOT deadline before underflow, but after
`vcs_ntsc_finish_deadline()` the good state was on scanline 52 at horizontal CPU
phase 64 while the bad state was at phase 72. The compiler-generated
`WSYNC := 0` sequence was `LDA #0; STA WSYNC`. In the late state those two load
cycles pushed the following TIA store across the 76-cycle boundary, after which
WSYNC stalled for another entire line. The simplified host timing harness did
not model this intra-instruction TIA write boundary and incorrectly reported the
same bad state as 262.

A 64/12 TIM64T experiment hid the Stella symptom but reduced overscan margin and
made the reset-path CPU harness one raw line longer, so it was rejected. The
root fix keeps 63/13. Production polling reads INTIM into caller-clobbered X so
A retains the zero from a clear TIMINT read, including an INTIM-underflow race;
an asserted TIMINT is exactly $80 and its already-late path uses one-byte ASL to
normalize A. VBLANK exit consumes that zero with direct
`STA WSYNC; STA VBLANK`, eliminating the pre-WSYNC load while preserving cycle-3
visible entry. The same fix was applied to the shared 50 Hz PAL/SECAM scheduler
core. Compile contracts pin the poll and direct-store order.

The real-Stella witness passes 80/80 at 262 after the fix. Default, historical
top-left, retained-bridge, old all-X deadline, and all-Y=95 scenes also pass.
Additional held real-Stella sampling covered 384 valid frames across random X
palettes of 1, 2, 3, 4, 8, and 160 values with Y constrained to the documented
0..95 range; every frame was 262. The host timing harness gained
`--randomize-zp-held` and `--dump-zp`; live interval failures are checked before
the next frame mutates RAM so dumps identify the actual failing layout. The
focused asymmetric suite now holds dense 1..4-column randomized X/Y layouts for
16 frames apiece. Focused asymmetric, NTSC scheduler, 50 Hz scheduler, and
NTSC/PAL/SECAM compile-contract tests pass. Public asymmetric accounting is now
4085/4090 ROM and 115/128 RAM.

2026-08-22 23:45:05 PDT, repair stale exact-six regression

The 0/1/2 retained-X allocator improvement changed the below-P1 exact-six path
from an inline `LDA position_packed; BMI` test to `JSR TEMPLATE_exact_six_p0`.
The old structural regex therefore failed even though the semantic hazard rule
remained intact. The test now pins both halves of the new contract: the exact-six
helper maps a still-possible ordinary P0 lane to the deferred $80 marker, and
`TEMPLATE_ProveP0Retain` turns that marker into retained P0 ($81) only after a
later same-X predecessor proves the continuation. Without that proof the marker
naturally resolves to no usable lane.

Added a real-6502 regression for the user's staggered cluster
X={62,72,62,88,114,140}, Y={86,82,76,42,42,16}, identity priority. It requires
all six logical sprites to be actually visible and the final scheduler count to
be six across the held run. The focused asymmetric test passes. The separate C
scheduler model is now explicitly documented as the ordinary full-setup baseline
oracle; its retained-X deficit witness is intentionally historical/baseline, not
a claim about the current renderer.

The user's immediately preceding broad run passed every other E2E case (318/319),
and this change touches only tests/comments/hot records. Local compile validation
was 500/500 and the corrected focused asymmetric E2E passes. Current public
asymmetric accounting remains 4089/4090 ROM and 115/128 RAM.
